# Mythic Superhero RPG Platform
## World Constitution and Approved Design Register v1.27

**Status:** Complete approved design constitution; coefficient calibration and implementation verification pending  
**Date:** 2026-08-22  
**Purpose:** Canonical design record for the reusable campaign platform and its first alternate-Earth mythic-superhero campaign.

---

## 1. Product Foundation

The platform is a solo, text-first, dice-driven RPG, dungeon-master, world-builder, party-manager, and comic-production system. It supports unrestricted player intent, persistent world simulation, autonomous NPC and companion agents, long-term memory, traditional character progression, and conversion of actual campaign history into professional comic books.

### Relationship to The First Perception

The existing Thefirstpercepti codebase is a technical ancestor only.

Retain and generalize:

- Deterministic event ledger
- Reducers and validators
- Persistence and save snapshots
- Agent/model routing
- Structured memory
- Replay and testing foundations

Do not inherit:

- Storyline
- Plot structure
- Characters
- Locations
- Endings
- Narrative prerequisites
- Story-specific evaluation
- Lore-specific schemas

The prior setting remains isolated as legacy content. The new campaign framework must not import its fiction.

---

## 2. Approved Campaign Recipe

| Dimension | Approved decision |
|---|---|
| Core genre | Superhero science-fantasy |
| World | One alternate, near-future Earth |
| Mythologies | Greek, Roman, and Norse initially; expandable when coherent |
| Structure | Open world |
| Secondary activities | Survival, investigation, politics, urban conflict, realm travel, and dungeon delves |
| Tone | Mature tragicomedy |
| Player start | Hero, villain/outlaw, or ordinary survivor |
| Progression | Traditional Levels 1–20 plus powers, skills, equipment, party bonds, reputation, Epithets, Mantles, and Legacy |
| Horror | Primordial and cosmic horror as an escalation layer |
| Expanded locations | Mythic realms adjacent to Earth, not alternate Earths |

The structure is open world. Dungeon crawling and survival are activities inside the open world, not separate mandatory campaign structures.

### Player Freedom Contract

- Any physically or metaphysically possible action may be attempted.
- No plot-armored NPCs or protected quest-givers.
- No mandatory heroism, villainy, or redemption.
- Consequences replace prohibitions.
- Laws and factions react only to information they possess.
- The player may destroy planned content, institutions, cities, factions, or divine actors.
- Absurd equipment and tactics receive legitimate mechanics and progression.
- The world treats absurd player choices as canon rather than dismissing them as jokes.
- The game may produce death, imprisonment, transformation, conquest, apotheosis, or permanent failure.
- The AI proposes interpretations; validated rules and dice determine outcomes.

---

## 3. World Constitution

1. Mythology is objectively real. Belief shapes divine manifestation but did not create the original gods from nothing.
2. Earth is one physical reality. Alternate Earths are outside the approved initial setting.
3. Mythic realms are adjacent realities reached through gates, rituals, relics, death, dreams, Bleed Zones, Yggdrasil, or technology.
4. Greek, Roman, and Norse cosmologies have distinct rules and are not cosmetic variations.
5. Greek and Roman deities may be unified, divergent, or violently separated identities depending on the individual divine root.
6. Gods are powerful but can be wounded, imprisoned, divided, robbed, replaced, or truly killed.
7. Domains survive their owners and may become vacant, fragmented, uncontrolled, or inherited.
8. Superpowers can originate from mythology, science, mutation, psychic phenomena, monsters, relics, magic, cosmic exposure, equipment, or combinations.
9. Science can measure and manipulate myth without explaining it away.
10. Fate describes possible paths, not mandatory outcomes.
11. Death is real but jurisdictional.
12. The Veil remains damaged and continues producing aftershocks, Bleed Zones, awakenings, and incursions.
13. Canon records actions and consequences rather than authorial intent.
14. No system may silently rewrite a committed event.

---

## 4. The Recent Veilbreak

The playable present is twelve years after a global event known as the **Veilbreak**. The rupture lasted approximately seventeen minutes and is commonly called **The Seventeen**.

### Before the Veilbreak

Earth closely resembled our own near-future world. Gods and mythic realms existed behind a restrictive boundary. Rare relics, dreams, monsters, miracles, and crossings occurred, but organized society lacked a coherent public explanation.

### The Orpheus Array

The Orpheus Array was a distributed nine-facility scientific project studying consciousness, death, and immortality.

Public goals:

- Determine whether consciousness survives biological death
- Study post-mortem information and near-death phenomena
- Preserve memories in terminal or degenerative conditions

Classified goals:

- Locate consciousness after death
- Retrieve a complete identity
- Transfer consciousness into biological or synthetic vessels
- Maintain continuity of Self during transfer
- Achieve repeatable human immortality
- Recover selected deceased individuals

Its defining technology was the **Continuity Lock**, designed to maintain a person across the boundary of death.

### The Closed Concord

As humanity approached direct detection, divine factions met in a secret Closed Concord to debate whether to reinforce the Veil, reveal themselves, exploit humanity, destroy Orpheus, or restructure relations with Earth. The disagreement crossed pantheon lines and became violent.

### Approved cause

There was no mastermind.

The Veilbreak was a cascade of failures:

1. Researchers concealed the mythological properties of archaeological components.
2. Governments accelerated the immortality demonstration.
3. Safety shutdowns were disabled to protect the test subject's continuity.
4. Divine factions convened without understanding humanity's technical proximity.
5. The Closed Concord became violent.
6. Orpheus detected the divine rupture and activated the Continuity Lock.
7. Conflicting divine attempts to reseal the Veil worsened it.
8. Human militaries attacked manifestations they believed were invasions.
9. Synchronized Orpheus facilities were damaged while still active.
10. The shutdown occurred out of sequence, permanently scarring reality.

The divine conflict created the rupture. Orpheus anchored it to Earth and prevented complete closure. Extracosmic phenomena exploited the opening afterward but did not cause it.

### Twelve-year aftermath

| Period | Development |
|---|---|
| Day 0 | Veilbreak, mass death, first public gods, monsters, relics, and power awakenings |
| Years 0–2 | Emergency survival, vigilantes, military responses, displaced communities |
| Years 3–5 | Veil Accords, first hero institutions, pantheon diplomacy |
| Years 6–8 | Mythotechnology, relic markets, licensing, organized supercrime |
| Years 9–11 | Partial normalization and institutional expansion |
| Year 12 | Continued instability and evidence that the Veil never fully resealed |

## 5. Approved Divine Starting State

| Divine figure | State |
|---|---|
| Zeus | Intact, active, weakened, and claiming personal Olympian sovereignty |
| Jupiter | Independent from Zeus and claiming lawful institutional sovereignty |
| Hera/Juno | Reintegrated identity; politically formidable |
| Athena | Missing since the Veilbreak; fragmented Mantles still appear |
| Minerva | Independent and publicly active |
| Ares and Mars | Two independent heads on one immortal body; they hate each other and share pain, resources, and physical consequences |
| Hermes/Mercury | One continuous being across identities |
| Hades/Pluto | One sovereign operating through two administrative identities |
| Poseidon/Neptune | Missing beneath the oceans |
| Apollo | Divided among prophecy, healing, plague, music, radiance, and truth claimants |
| Dionysus/Bacchus | Active and able to shift between identities |
| Odin | Bound to Yggdrasil while attempting to stabilize the connected realms |
| Nido | Odin's low-intelligence brother, loose on Earth, genuinely believing he is **Oodin** |
| Freyja | Fully active and influential on Earth and among the dead |
| Thor | Alive, active, and politically independent |
| Loki | Multiple verified manifestations; original status uncertain |
| Hel | Intact and disputing soul jurisdiction with Hades/Pluto |
| Tyr | Missing and presumed dead, weakening metaphysical contracts |

Nido's repeated deeds and public recognition could eventually cause Oodin to become a distinct Name, Mantle, or genuine divine claimant. This is possible, not predetermined.

---

## 6. Souls, Death, and Resurrection

### Four-part soul model

| Component | Function |
|---|---|
| Self | Continuous conscious identity |
| Memory | Experiences, knowledge, personality, and relationships |
| Name | Social and mythic identity, titles, reputation, and Epithets |
| Shadow | Emotional and physical residue left on bodies, objects, people, and locations |

Reconstructing memories does not prove recovery of the original Self.

### Normal death sequence

1. Self separates from the body.
2. Memory and Name remain attached temporarily.
3. Shadow remains in the physical world.
4. Soul enters a Threshold period.
5. Psychopomps and jurisdictions submit claims.
6. The soul accepts, contests, bargains, escapes, or is forced into a jurisdiction.
7. Prolonged afterlife residence makes retrieval more difficult.

### Jurisdiction factors

- Belief and self-identification
- Oaths and divine contracts
- Mantles and supernatural affiliations
- Manner and location of death
- Soul preference
- Existing debts
- Divine intervention
- Prior resurrection or Orpheus exposure

### Post-death classifications

| Type | Definition |
|---|---|
| Echo | Memory and emotion without the complete Self |
| Ghost | Genuine Self remaining near the living world |
| Shade | Soul altered through prolonged afterlife existence |
| Returned | Original Self restored to a vessel |
| Replica | Reconstructed personality without proven original continuity |
| Possessed | Self occupying another vessel |
| Hollow | Animated vessel without the original Self |
| Composite | Multiple Selves, Shadows, or memory sets sharing a vessel |

### Resurrection

True resurrection requires the original Self, a viable vessel, and a passage across death. Consent is normally required for a stable return. Forced resurrection is possible but risks possession, Replicas, Composites, Hollows, hostility, or additional entities returning.

Methods include divine restoration, Orpheus reconstruction, relics, rituals, escape, cloning, possession, Mantle regeneration, and temporal intervention. Resurrection never rewinds the event ledger.

Player death can continue through afterlife play, escape, bargaining, companion recovery, ghost play, a Returned state, successor control, new-character Legacy, or optional permadeath.

---

## 7. Core Dice and Action Resolution

### Attributes

| Attribute | Function |
|---|---|
| Strength | Force, lifting, grappling, melee power |
| Agility | Dexterity, reflexes, aim, balance, stealth |
| Endurance | Health, stamina, poison, fatigue, survival |
| Intellect | Reasoning, technology, medicine, investigation |
| Awareness | Perception, intuition, tracking, environmental reading |
| Willpower | Mental defense, concentration, horror resistance, power control |
| Presence | Charisma, leadership, persuasion, deception, intimidation |

Attributes use familiar 1–20 scores. Attribute modifier is `floor((Attribute - 10) / 2)`.

### Universal check

`d20 + Attribute modifier + Mastery + Specialty + situational modifiers`

Skills use flexible Attributes according to the player's approach. Strength + Intimidation, Presence + Intimidation, and Intellect + Intimidation are all valid when fictionally supported. Specialty is zero unless one narrow, relevant Specialty applies. Section 12 defines the complete skill procedure.

### Mastery

| Rank | Bonus |
|---|---:|
| Untrained | +0 |
| Trained | +2 |
| Expert | +4 |
| Master | +6 |
| Legendary | +8 |

### Outcome degrees

| Margin | Outcome |
|---|---|
| Miss by 10+ | Catastrophic failure |
| Miss by 1–9 | Failure or failure with consequence |
| Meet DC | Success |
| Beat by 5+ | Strong success |
| Beat by 10+ | Exceptional success |

Natural 20 raises one degree and may generate Momentum. Natural 1 lowers one degree and introduces a complication. Neither makes a fundamentally impossible action possible.

### Edge and Setback

- Edge: roll 2d20 and keep the higher result.
- Setback: roll 2d20 and keep the lower result.
- They cancel each other; excessive stacking converts to other bounded benefits.

### Defenses

- Evasion: primarily Agility
- Fortitude: primarily Endurance
- Resolve: primarily Willpower
- Vigilance: primarily Awareness

### Action economy

- One Major Action
- One Move
- One Quick Action
- One Reaction
- Reasonable speech and minor interactions

The player may exchange or exceed actions through abilities, Push, Momentum, or explicit costs.

### Free-form Stunts

Any undefined action becomes a temporary Stunt with an intended outcome, method, Attribute, Mastery, difficulty, cost, failure consequence, and possible effect. Prefer one meaningful roll over several tedious checks.

### Push and Momentum

Push trades Strain, resources, defense, stability, injury risk, or collateral damage for Edge or greater effect. Momentum is alignment-neutral and supports rerolls, added effects, reactions, team combinations, and ambitious Stunts.

All rolls record seed, result, formula, DC/defense, Edge/Setback, resources, and committed outcome. The AI cannot change the difficulty after seeing the roll.

### Mathematical specification standard

Every completed mechanic must publish its:

- Canonical equation or deterministic resolution procedure
- Variable definitions, types, and legal ranges
- Order of operations and stacking order
- Rounding rule, with `floor` as the default unless explicitly overridden
- Minimums, maximums, caps, and overflow behavior
- Trigger timing, duration, recharge timing, and expiration timing
- Interaction with Edge, Setback, criticals, degree of success, scale, and Power Tier
- Impossible-action and invalid-input behavior
- Event-ledger fields required to reproduce the result
- Ruleset version and tuning-table version used for the resolution

Stable **core formulas** are separated from versioned **tuning tables**. Balance changes may adjust tuning-table values without silently changing the meaning of the mechanic. Committed campaign history retains the formula version, tuning version, random seed, inputs, intermediate values, and output used when the event occurred; later balance changes never rewrite prior canon.

During staged design, unresolved coefficients must be labeled **provisional**. A mechanic is not implementation-ready until its equations, tables, boundaries, and edge cases have passed cross-system review and automated simulation.

---

## 8. Rare Probability Control

Characters may hold a maximum of three Fate Charges by default.

| Cost | Effect |
|---:|---|
| 1 Fate | Reroll an eligible roll after seeing it and keep the preferred result |
| 2 Fate | Convert a possible action into an ordinary success |
| 3 Fate | Treat the action as a natural 20 and trigger critical effects |

Fate does not make an impossible action possible or automatically kill a target. It guarantees the applicable roll result within current capability and scale.

Fate is difficult to replenish. Sources include major Drives, dangerous oaths, catastrophic survival, major victories, irreversible sacrifice, world-front resolution, divine favor, relics, transformative events, and specialized powers.

Ability-specific charges may grant narrower rerolls, guaranteed hits, guaranteed criticals, or forced rerolls with their own recharge conditions.

Important entities may possess finite, pre-recorded Defiance Charges. Defiance cannot be invented after a result.

---

## 9. Character Resources, Damage, Recovery, and Defeat

### Universal resources

| Resource | Mechanical purpose |
|---|---|
| Vitality | Short-term capacity to remain active through exertion, near misses, pain, and minor injury |
| Composure | Resistance to terror, coercion, psychic disruption, possession, and loss of self-command |
| Reserve | Spendable fuel for powers, strenuous techniques, transformations, and extraordinary actions |
| Momentum | Encounter-flow currency earned through strong play and spent on tactical opportunities |
| Fate | Rare probability control governed by Section 8 |
| Recovery Dice | Limited self-recovery capacity used during rests or qualifying abilities |
| Wounds | Persistent actual harm that outlasts ordinary Vitality loss |

Reserve is mechanically universal but fictionally expressed by the character's source: Stamina, Focus, Charge, Mana, Favor, Rage, Battery, Ammunition, Blood, or another approved skin. A technique records both the common resource operation and its fictional expression.

Conditional tracks are added only when fiction requires them. Approved examples include Coherence, Realm Pressure, Resonance Debt, divine Favor, ammunition, transformation stability, corruption, and relic charge. Conditional tracks do not replace the universal resources unless an explicit feature says so.

### Overdraw

A character may exceed available Reserve only when the technique permits Overdraw. Before the roll, the character selects or accepts a declared cost such as Vitality loss, a Wound, instability, equipment damage, Realm Pressure, Resonance Debt, reduced defense, or loss of control. The cost is committed even if the action fails unless the ability explicitly states otherwise.

### Vitality and Wounds

Vitality is not literal meat. It represents the remaining capacity to avoid or absorb decisive harm. Wounds record persistent injury and are classified by severity:

| Severity | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Minor | Painful or limiting harm that normally permits continued action |
| Serious | Material impairment requiring meaningful treatment or recovery |
| Critical | Life-, identity-, or function-threatening harm with severe penalties |
| Mortal | Imminent death, destruction, severance, or irreversible collapse without intervention |

Wounds may be Physical, Psychic, Spiritual, Divine, or Ontological. Their fiction, penalties, treatment, duration, and comic-visible consequences must be recorded independently.

Reaching zero Vitality triggers a defeat-state evaluation; it does not automatically mean death. Possible states include Downed, unconscious, incapacitated, captured, surrendering, fleeing, dying, or destroyed. The result follows declared intent, damage family, fictional positioning, Wounds, and the target's nature.

### Composure

Composure absorbs extraordinary mental and spiritual pressure. It governs terror, psychic attack, supernatural coercion, identity disruption, and possession. Ordinary conversation, persuasion, seduction, deception, or disagreement does not inflict Composure damage unless a qualifying ability or extraordinary circumstance is present.

At zero Composure, the character receives a context-valid crisis state rather than losing all agency automatically. Examples include Panic, Compulsion, Dissociation, Possession Risk, Surrender, Rage, or Psychic Collapse. The source must declare what outcomes it can impose before resolution.

### Damage-resolution pipeline

Every harmful action resolves in this fixed order:

1. Declare intent: lethal, nonlethal, disabling, indiscriminate, or another explicit objective.
2. Declare the attack, target, Attribute, Mastery, effect, costs, damage family, and valid defenses.
3. Resolve the attack roll or other qualifying check and determine its degree.
4. Calculate raw damage or effect from the applicable formula and tuning table.
5. Apply barriers, armor, resistances, immunities, vulnerabilities, and scale in their declared order.
6. Apply remaining Vitality, Composure, Reserve, condition, environmental, or structural loss.
7. Test Wound and defeat thresholds.
8. Commit all mechanical, fictional, world-state, and visual-state changes to the event ledger.

Approved damage families are Physical, Energy, Mental, Spiritual, and Ontological. Individual effects may carry tags inside a family, such as ballistic, fire, lightning, sonic, poison, divine, memory, Name, or reality-severing.

Evasion determines whether an attack meaningfully connects. Armor and resistance determine how much of a connected effect is mitigated. Barriers are separately tracked protection. Immunities must be conditional and specifically defined; they are not permission to ignore unrelated effects.

Scale modifies achievable effect and damage rather than accuracy, intelligence, or player agency. Lower-tier characters may affect higher-tier threats through discovered vulnerabilities, preparation, Names, relics, environmental leverage, combined actions, realm law, and appropriately scaled equipment.

### Dying and destruction

The initial dying model requires three stabilization successes before three deterioration failures. Additional qualifying damage adds a deterioration failure. Exact check DCs, timing, assistance modifiers, and Power Tier interactions remain provisional until the level, combat, and healing coefficients are calibrated.

Gods and other reconstructive beings use the same immediate damage and defeat framework, then apply their recorded Anchors, reconstitution rules, Mantles, and divine-death states. No named character receives undocumented plot armor. Large cohorts may use aggregated unit rules, but their conversion to or from individual actors must be deterministic.

### Recovery and healing

Recovery occurs through Breathers, short rests, full rests, downtime, treatment, powers, relics, realm effects, or reconstitution. Serious, Critical, and Mortal Wounds do not disappear through ordinary rest alone.

Healing must identify what it restores: Vitality, body, Composure, memory, Self, soul component, Name, Anchor, or reality coherence. Restoring one category does not silently restore another.

### Comic continuity

Damage and recovery update visual canon. The event ledger records injury placement, severity, blood or energy effects, costume and equipment damage, mobility, carried objects, transformation state, environmental destruction, and healing progression so later panels cannot casually erase consequences.

The resource identities, resolution order, defeat principles, and continuity requirements above are approved. Exact numerical coefficients are calibrated after progression, combat, equipment, condition, and encounter mathematics are established.

---

## 10. Progression, XP, Power Tiers, Morality, and Legacy

### Connected progression tracks

- Character Level measures experience, survivability, and build development.
- Power Tier limits normal supernatural, technological, and technique scale.
- Build development covers Attributes, skills, Mastery, talents, powers, techniques, and equipment mastery.
- Mythic development covers Mantles, Names, Epithets, Domain Authority, and apotheosis.
- Legacy supports post-Level-20 horizontal advancement, successors, Relics, institutions, and permanent world impact.

Level never grants divine Authority, an Epithet, or Mantle Resonance automatically.

### Level XP curve

For current Level `L`:

`XP_next(L) = 250L(L + 1)`

Total XP required to reach Level `L`:

`XP_floor(L) = 250(L - 1)L(L + 1) / 3`

| Level | Total XP | XP to next | Tier |
|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 1 | 0 | 500 | Emergent |
| 2 | 500 | 1,500 | Emergent |
| 3 | 2,000 | 3,000 | Emergent |
| 4 | 5,000 | 5,000 | Emergent |
| 5 | 10,000 | 7,500 | Established |
| 6 | 17,500 | 10,500 | Established |
| 7 | 28,000 | 14,000 | Established |
| 8 | 42,000 | 18,000 | Established |
| 9 | 60,000 | 22,500 | Iconic |
| 10 | 82,500 | 27,500 | Iconic |
| 11 | 110,000 | 33,000 | Iconic |
| 12 | 143,000 | 39,000 | Iconic |
| 13 | 182,000 | 45,500 | Mythic |
| 14 | 227,500 | 52,500 | Mythic |
| 15 | 280,000 | 60,000 | Mythic |
| 16 | 340,000 | 68,000 | Mythic |
| 17 | 408,000 | 76,500 | Ascendant |
| 18 | 484,500 | 85,500 | Ascendant |
| 19 | 570,000 | 95,000 | Ascendant |
| 20 | 665,000 | 105,000-point Mastery Cycle | Ascendant |

### Universal XP value

Every threat, location, mystery, project, and objective receives a Rating `R`. Ratings normally run from 0–20, but cosmic and primordial threats may exceed 20.

`U(R) = 10 + 25R(R + 1)`

| Rating | XP unit |
|---:|---:|
| 0 | 10 |
| 1 | 60 |
| 2 | 160 |
| 3 | 310 |
| 5 | 760 |
| 10 | 2,760 |
| 15 | 6,010 |
| 20 | 10,510 |
| 25 | 16,260 |

An equal-level major accomplishment is worth approximately ten percent of the next-level requirement. The shared unit keeps combat, exploration, creation, social play, survival, and objectives mathematically comparable.

### Combat and kill XP

`XP_combat = round[U(T) * N * C * (0.8D + 0.2K + 0.2M)]`

- `T`: target Threat Rating
- `N`: repetition multiplier
- `C`: contribution multiplier
- `D = 1` when active opposition is neutralized; otherwise `0`
- `K = 1` when the target is killed or truly destroyed; otherwise `0`
- `M = 1` when meaningful additional risk or cost is accepted to capture, spare, or rescue the target; otherwise `0`
- `K + M <= 1`

| Outcome | Base award |
|---|---:|
| Active enemy defeated, escaped, or driven away | 80% of `U(T)` |
| Active enemy killed | 100% of `U(T)` |
| Active enemy captured or spared at meaningful cost | 100% of `U(T)` |
| Helpless or non-opposing person killed | 20% of `U(T)` |
| Self-created summon, clone, or staged victim | 0% |

Killing always has XP value. Mercy equals a kill only when it is meaningfully harder or more costly. Gods award vessel-defeat XP when a manifestation is destroyed and additional XP for later Anchor severance, Mantle severance, Name death, or True Death.

### Exploration XP

`XP_exploration = round[U(A) * S * N * C]`

`A` is the Area, Hazard, or Discovery Rating.

| Discovery | Scope `S` |
|---|---:|
| Route, cache, minor landmark, or mapped hazard | 0.25 |
| Meaningful new location or passage | 0.50 |
| Significant secret, settlement, ruin, or anomaly | 1.00 |
| Completed dungeon, major Bleed Zone, or realm node | 2.00 |
| World-changing route, realm, or revelation | 4.00 |

### Other XP sources

`XP_source = round[U(R) * S * N * C]`

| Activity | Typical scope `S` |
|---|---:|
| Routine contract or minor objective | 0.50 |
| Standard quest, negotiation, heist, or investigation | 1.00 |
| Major faction, political, scientific, or military outcome | 2.00 |
| Campaign-arc or world-defining objective | 4.00 |
| Useful prototype or crafted upgrade | 0.25 |
| Novel invention or permanent research result | 1.00 |
| Signature artifact or transformative invention | 2.00 |
| True Relic or civilization-changing technology | 4.00 |
| Meaningful survival challenge | 0.50 |
| Catastrophe or extraordinary expedition survived | 1.00–2.00 |

Distinct achievements stack. One expedition may award combat, exploration, survival, objective, Relic, and morality XP when each corresponds to a separate committed result.

A four-hour meaningful training block awards:

`XP_training = round[0.1U(L) * N]`

At most two training blocks count per in-world day.

### Contribution and controlled grinding

| Contribution | `C` |
|---|---:|
| Direct or materially causal | 1.00 |
| Meaningful support or off-screen contribution | 0.50 |
| Merely present or observational | 0 |

XP is not divided among party members. Each participating character is scored independently. Autonomous NPC actions earn XP only when genuinely simulated and committed to the ledger.

| Similar completed events | Repetition `N` |
|---:|---:|
| First 5 | 1.00 |
| 6–20 | 0.50 |
| 21+ | 0.25 |
| Self-manufactured exploit | 0 |

The repetition key includes activity type, target or creature family, region, and current adaptation state. A genuinely evolved opponent resets the repetition count because it is a new challenge. Legitimate grinding remains possible but inefficient.

### Level rewards and Build Points

Every level after Level 1 grants four Build Points. Levels 5, 9, 13, and 17 grant four additional Build Points.

`BP(L) = 4(L - 1) + 4 floor((L - 1) / 4)`

A Level 20 character earns 92 Build Points after character creation. Unspent Build Points remain banked and may be spent only at a valid Advancement Checkpoint.

At Levels 4, 8, 12, 16, and 20, the character receives two Attribute Points:

`AttributePoints(L) = 2 floor(L / 4)`

The player may apply +2 to one Attribute or +1 to two. A base Attribute cannot exceed 20. Each unusable Attribute Point converts to two Build Points. Performance beyond Attribute 20 comes from powers, scale, equipment, Mantles, techniques, and conditions.

Leveling recalculates resource maximums but does not remove Wounds, conditions, Realm Pressure, Resonance Debt, or other consequences. Current resources increase only by the increase in their maximum; leveling is not a full heal.

### Power Tier gates and Breakthroughs

`Tier(L) = 1 + floor((L - 1) / 4)`

| Rank | Levels | Tier |
|---:|---:|---|
| 1 | 1–4 | Emergent |
| 2 | 5–8 | Established |
| 3 | 9–12 | Iconic |
| 4 | 13–16 | Mythic |
| 5 | 17–20 | Ascendant |

Tier Rank is the normal ceiling for Potency, Control, Reach, Efficiency, individual techniques, and comparable equipment or mythotechnology. Raising the ceiling does not automatically raise any rating.

Entering Levels 5, 9, 13, or 17 requires sufficient XP, a completed Breakthrough, and an Advancement Checkpoint. A Breakthrough may be training, experimentation, transformation, crisis, invention, oath, theft, exposure, spiritual realization, or any coherent player-created approach. It never requires heroism, sacrifice, or a predetermined plot.

The deterministic Integration route requires:

`DowntimeDays = 3 * TargetTierRank`

The active Trial route uses:

`DC = 12 + 2(TargetTierRank)`

| Target tier | Trial DC |
|---|---:|
| Established | 16 |
| Iconic | 18 |
| Mythic | 20 |
| Ascendant | 22 |

- Success: clean breakthrough.
- Strong success: clean breakthrough and one Momentum.
- Exceptional success: clean breakthrough and one Fate.
- Failure: breakthrough plus one declared Breakthrough Scar or complication.
- Catastrophic failure: breakthrough plus two complications or one severe declared consequence, provided the character remains capable of continuing.

XP may be banked without loss while awaiting a Breakthrough. Banked levels apply sequentially after the gate, but later Tier boundaries still require their own Breakthrough. Temporary relics, pacts, realm effects, or Overreach may exceed the character's Tier without permanently raising it.

Campaigns may begin at any level. Recommended starting anchors are 1, 5, 9, 13, and 17. Hero, villain, and ordinary-survivor origins do not force a starting level.

### Moral Polarity and Conviction XP

Hero and villain remain social labels. Internal Moral Polarity is recorded separately:

`-100 <= M <= 100`

| Score | Moral state |
|---:|---|
| −100 to −61 | Ruinous |
| −60 to −21 | Villainous |
| −20 to +20 | Unbound |
| +21 to +60 | Heroic |
| +61 to +100 | Saintly |

Each meaningful moral decision records Polarity `P` of `-1`, `0`, or `+1`; Severity `S` from 1–5; and Agency `A` of `1` for deliberate, `0.5` for coerced or reckless, or `0` for accidental.

`DeltaM = round(P * S * A)`

`M_new = clamp(-100, 100, M_old + DeltaM)`

Morality follows informed action and foreseeable consequence, not labels invented by the AI.

`XP_conviction = round[0.25U(R) * Q * N]`

For action aligned with current polarity:

`Q = 1 + abs(M) / 100`

An opposing action uses `Q = 0.5` while shifting the character toward a new path. An Unbound character acting consistently with independence or survival uses `Q = 1`.

### Different moral rewards

- **Grace** rewards protective, merciful, sacrificial, and restorative play with Hope. Hope produces voluntary help, rescue, safer recovery, loyal support, reduced collateral consequences, difficult Fate restoration, and resilient institutions.
- **Ruin** rewards cruelty, domination, predation, betrayal, terror, and deliberate destruction with Dread. Dread produces forced surrender, tribute, coerced access, fear-based recruitment, minions, power shortcuts, dangerous Overdraw, and broken morale, while increasing rebellion, betrayal, coalitions, and hunters.
- **Unbound** rewards self-preservation, independence, improvisation, and refusal to serve either extreme with Defiance. Defiance supports escape, resistance to coercion, improvisational rerolls, broken contracts, environmental conversion, and survival without institutional support.

Hope, Dread, and Defiance derive from moral-event Severity. Their exact spending costs are defined with Drives, reputation, and faction rules. Unknown deeds can change internal morality, but public and faction rewards require information propagation.

### Bounded world adaptation

The world adapts without scaling every creature or civilian to the player.

`DeltaAdaptation = W + ceil(K / 5) + 2E + 3S + 2P`

- `W = 1` when witnesses or evidence identify the player
- `K`: ordinary casualties or defeats
- `E`: elite losses
- `S`: strategic assets destroyed or stolen
- `P = 1` for major public humiliation or exposure

| Adaptation | State |
|---:|---|
| 0–2 | Unaware |
| 3–6 | Alert |
| 7–11 | Countering |
| 12–17 | Escalating |
| 18+ | Mobilized |

Adaptation changes intelligence, tactics, countermeasures, specialists, patrols, alliances, hired hunters, propaganda, relocation, surrender, and reinforcement quality.

`ThreatBonus = floor(AdaptationStage / 2)`

This bounded numerical increase is secondary to evidence-based counters. A known fire-user may encounter heat shielding, fireproof specialists, hostage tactics, divine weather bargains, surveillance, or evacuated districts rather than ordinary enemies arbitrarily gaining levels.

Adaptation declines by one point after 30 quiet in-world days but may not fall below permanent institutional knowledge. Threat Ratings are not capped at 20, so stronger gods, monsters, realms, and Outside entities always remain possible.

### Level 20 Mastery Cycles and Legacy

At Level 20, each 105,000 post-cap XP completes a Mastery Cycle:

`MasteryCycles = floor(PostCapXP / 105000)`

Each cycle grants four Build Points restricted to horizontal development and one Legacy Mark. It grants no additional Attribute increase, Power Tier, or automatic resource scaling.

A durable Legacy Anchor normally grants one Legacy Mark. It grants two only when both world-scale and tied to an irreversible personal consequence. Each Anchor has a unique ledger identity and awards Marks once.

Legacy Rank `R` uses cumulative Marks `M`:

`Threshold(R) = R(R + 5) / 2`

| Legacy Rank | Marks required | Legacy slots |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 0 | 0 |
| 1 | 3 | 1 |
| 2 | 7 | 2 |
| 3 | 12 | 3 |
| 4 | 18 | 4 |
| 5 | 25 | 5 |

Legacy slots may preserve or transmit a Relic, teachable technique, successor bond, institution, faction relationship, Name, reputation, memory, soul connection, realm route, or durable invention. Legacy never makes an asset indestructible, guarantees resurrection, or grants universal attack bonuses.

Death does not force a campaign restart at Level 1. A new protagonist may start no higher than the median level of surviving core party members; with no survivors, the prior protagonist's level is the cap. Existing NPCs retain their actual levels.

Characters below the party Reference Level receive:

`CatchUpMultiplier = min(2, 1 + 0.25 floor(max(0, ReferenceLevel - L) / 2))`

| Levels behind | XP multiplier |
|---:|---:|
| 0–1 | 1.00 |
| 2–3 | 1.25 |
| 4–5 | 1.50 |
| 6–7 | 1.75 |
| 8+ | 2.00 |

Legacy determines what a successor may inherit; it does not erase the predecessor's death, lost relationships, reputation, equipment, soul consequences, or changed world.

---

## 11. Character Creation Architecture

Origin answers why and how the character has capabilities. Background establishes who the character was and what relationships they developed. Archetype describes how the character operates. Role, public identity, and morality remain independent choices. None is a permanent class or storyline lock.

### Creation modes

- Guided conversational creation
- Freeform concept compilation
- Expert point allocation
- Random or Fate-Born creation
- Narrative Awakening from an ordinary-survivor state

Every mode produces the same validated record and budget. Freeform concepts are translated into legal Sources, Domains, Expressions, techniques, limitations, visual signatures, and costs rather than rejected for absurdity.

### Build Point budget

Every character receives 24 starting Build Points.

`CreationBP(L) = 24 + 4(L - 1) + 4 floor((L - 1) / 4)`

| Starting level | Total creation BP |
|---:|---:|
| 1 | 24 |
| 5 | 44 |
| 9 | 64 |
| 13 | 84 |
| 17 | 104 |
| 20 | 116 |

The recommended Level 1 distribution is eight Origin BP, six Background BP, eight Archetype BP, and two free BP. This distribution is guidance rather than a wall; all points may be reallocated when the final build remains valid. Preset and AI-generated characters use the same ledger as custom builds.

### Attribute creation

Standard creation begins with all seven Attributes at 8 and grants 40 Attribute Creation Points.

| Score | Cost |
|---:|---:|
| 6 | −2 |
| 7 | −1 |
| 8 | 0 |
| 9 | 1 |
| 10 | 2 |
| 11 | 3 |
| 12 | 4 |
| 13 | 5 |
| 14 | 7 |
| 15 | 9 |
| 16 | 12 |
| 17 | 16 |
| 18 | 21 |

- Standard starting range is 6–18.
- Scores below 8 may refund no more than four total points.
- Starting Attributes cannot normally exceed 18.
- The standard fixed array is `16, 15, 14, 13, 12, 11, 8`.
- Higher-Level creation adds the Attribute Points granted by progression.
- Origins do not impose mandatory Attribute bonuses.

Fate-Born creation rolls `4d6`, drops the lowest die, and repeats seven times. If the total is below 80 or contains no score of at least 15, the player may reroll the entire set once. All dice and assignments are recorded.

### Origin contract

Every Origin records Source, Catalyst, Gift, Pressure or limitation, Entanglement, and Visual Mark. Primordial, Cosmic, and Outside Sources are ontologically distinct and never merged unless the character is explicitly Composite.

Origin grants access to a Source, not every possible effect of that Source. A Mantle-Touched Origin may purchase Touched Resonance but does not receive later Resonance or Authority automatically. Composite Origins divide their Origin budget between Sources and begin with an Interference Pressure.

Every standard Origin uses eight BP:

| Component | BP |
|---|---:|
| Core Origin Feature | 3 |
| Rank-1 Signature Technique | 2 |
| Secondary Feature | 2 |
| Narrow Origin Edge | 1 |
| Total | 8 |

Source access, Catalyst, Pressure, Entanglement, and Visual Mark cost no BP by themselves. Limitations may later discount individual techniques but cannot grant BP twice. An Origin Edge grants Edge once per scene on one narrow, Source-related check.

#### Latent or Unawakened

- **Potential Conversion:** up to eight BP function as mundane luck, adrenaline, instinct, improvisation, equipment, or survival features and may be rebuilt one-for-one at the Catalyst.
- **Survivor's Reflex:** once per scene, spend one Reserve for Edge on an escape, defense, improvised action, or environmental reaction.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 mundane or apparently coincidental technique.
- **Edge:** sensing imminent abnormal danger without automatically understanding it.
- **Pressures:** vulnerability, unknown Catalyst, selection, pursuit, or disbelief.

Awakening is optional, and the AI cannot choose the permanent power set without consent.

#### Divine Blood or Chosen

- **Divine Claim:** choose one narrow Domain adjacency. Once per scene, spend one Reserve to raise the degree of a successful non-attack Domain-related check by one, maximum Exceptional. This is not Authority.
- **Ichorous Nature:** choose one appropriate resistance or divine survival adaptation.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 Domain manifestation.
- **Edge:** divine etiquette, genealogy, ritual recognition, or pantheon law.
- **Pressures:** divine family, oath, rival heir, prophecy, worshippers, or inherited enemy.

#### Mantle-Touched

- **Touched Resonance:** begin with Touched access to one approved Mantle and its bounded instincts or manifestation.
- **Resonant Overreach:** once per full rest, increase one Mantle technique's effective rank by one for one action, then gain one Resonance Debt.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 Mantle technique.
- **Edge:** sensing disturbances directly involving the Mantle's Domain.
- **Pressures:** identity bleed, obligations, rival claimant, jurisdiction, or recognition pressure.

#### Relic-Bonded

- **Bound Relic:** the Relic contains the Signature Technique, has tracked Integrity, and may be sensed by its bearer while in the same realm.
- **Overcharge:** once per scene, Push a Relic technique without its normal Reserve surcharge by marking one Integrity damage.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 Relic technique.
- **Edge:** Relic history, authentication, attunement, or artifact behavior.
- **Pressures:** theft, curse, sentience, maintenance, ownership, or fuel.

Losing the Relic disables Relic-dependent features but creates a recoverable world-state problem rather than deleting purchased BP.

#### Veil-Exposed

- **Flux Adaptation:** select three compatible Expression tags. After a full rest, one Origin technique may exchange its active tag without changing rank or effect budget.
- **Aftershock:** once per scene, Push a Veil technique and gain one Instability or Realm Pressure.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 mutation, anomaly, or Veil effect.
- **Edge:** detecting Bleed Zones, ruptures, and Veil instability.
- **Pressures:** mutation, environmental instability, government attention, or aftershocks.

#### Orpheus Returned, Replica, or Escapee

- **Continuity Echo:** once per full rest, when reduced to zero Vitality, take one Major Action or Move before defeat evaluation completes. This cannot cancel death or destruction.
- **Partitioned Recall:** once per full rest, reroll one eligible memory, identity, consciousness, death, or Orpheus check and keep the preferred result.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 continuity, memory, soul, or reconstruction technique.
- **Edge:** diagnosing Replicas, Echoes, continuity artifacts, or soul-component irregularities.
- **Pressures:** personhood claim, memory conflict, hidden copy, Orpheus pursuit, or disputed Self.

The character is recorded as Returned, Replica, Composite, or unresolved. The game cannot casually rule that the character is not real.

#### Engineered or Mutated

- **Adaptive Physiology:** the first time each scene the character suffers a new damage tag, spend one Reserve to gain its applicable resistance until the scene ends.
- **Rapid Homeostasis:** once per rest, reroll one Recovery Die and keep the preferred result.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 biological, chemical, genetic, or physiological technique.
- **Edge:** biological diagnostics, mutation analysis, or engineered-organism identification.
- **Pressures:** instability, patent ownership, dependency, laboratory pursuit, or transmissibility.

#### Mythotechnological or Cybernetic

- **Modular System:** prepare three compatible modules within the same two-BP ceiling; one is active and may be exchanged after a full rest or with workshop access.
- **Integrated Architecture:** the Signature system cannot be conventionally disarmed, but targeted effects may disable or damage it.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 integrated mythotechnology technique.
- **Edge:** diagnosing interfaces between myth, machinery, Relics, and superscience.
- **Pressures:** maintenance, hacking, ownership, fuel, firmware, or divine incompatibility.

#### Arcane or Runic

- **Ritual Access:** attempt an unlearned but Source-consistent technique as a ritual at Rank no higher than current Tier. It requires `10 * Rank` minutes, a declared component, and `DC = 14 + 2 * Rank`. Failure invokes the declared backlash or consumes the component.
- **Prepared Pattern:** after a full rest, prepare one known ritual; its first qualifying use receives Edge.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 spell, rune, invocation, or binding.
- **Edge:** arcane sight, rune interpretation, wards, or magical residue.
- **Pressures:** components, oath, backlash, patron, forbidden text, or execution requirements.

#### Noetic or Psychic

- **Focus Transference:** once per scene, pay Composure instead of Reserve at 1:1 for one psychic technique. This cannot reduce Composure below one.
- **Mindlink:** establish a consensual mental link with up to `1 + max(0, Presence modifier)` targets within Reach.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 telepathic, telekinetic, empathic, predictive, or noetic technique.
- **Edge:** detecting mental intrusion, psychic residue, or emotional discontinuity.
- **Pressures:** feedback, involuntary reception, privacy, predators, or alien thoughts.

#### Chthonic or Monstrous

- **Predatory Renewal:** once per scene, after materially contributing to a defeat, recover Vitality or Reserve equal to current Tier Rank.
- **Death Scent:** detect nearby dying beings, loose souls, recent death, or severe spiritual injury.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 monstrous, underworld, blood, shadow, or predatory technique.
- **Edge:** Underworld survival, monstrous hierarchy, intimidation, or soul trails.
- **Pressures:** hunger, appearance, afterlife claim, transformation, or territorial instinct.

The defeated target need not be killed; this Origin does not mandate villainy.

#### Primordial, Titan, or Jotun-Blooded

- **Ancient Overreach:** once per full rest, raise the Signature Technique's effective Potency by one for one action, then accept one Realm Pressure, Wound risk, or equivalent declared consequence.
- **World-Bone Resilience:** gain Edge against forced movement, crushing environments, extreme scale, and physical-form alteration.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 elemental, colossal, natural-law, Titan, or Jotun technique.
- **Edge:** primordial laws, elder beings, giantcraft, or ancient containment.
- **Pressures:** ancient feud, incompatible scale, elemental impulse, imprisonment claim, or divine hostility.

Primordials are neither gods nor Outside entities.

#### Cosmic or Extraterrestrial

- **Alien Adaptation:** choose one hostile environment; ignore routine exposure and gain Edge against its extreme manifestations.
- **Vector Shift:** once per scene, spend one Reserve to alter a Signature Technique's Reach or personal movement by one band for that action.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 alien, stellar, gravitational, energy, or extraterrestrial technique.
- **Edge:** stellar navigation, alien systems, exotic energy, or non-Earth biology.
- **Pressures:** incompatible needs, alien jurisdiction, cultural isolation, hunters, or damaged vessel.

Cosmic does not mean Outside.

#### Outside-Touched

- **Ontological Slip:** once per scene before rolling, ignore one conventional positional or barrier tag for one action, then gain one Coherence Strain. This cannot bypass a True Name, Domain Authority, or genuine impossibility.
- **Impossible Perception:** gain Edge when detecting reality corruption, rewrites, Severance, or Outside intrusion.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 ontological, impossible, or reality-distorting technique.
- **Edge:** recognizing contradictions that reality is attempting to conceal.
- **Pressures:** Coherence loss, Outside attention, contamination, altered memories, or unstable identity.

This Origin requires a Visual Invariance Profile.

#### Mastered Human or Equipment-Based

- **Prepared Counter:** after observing a target and succeeding on Intellect or Awareness against Vigilance, gain Edge on the first attack or defense against it and learn one counterable tag.
- **Signature Kit:** prepare two compatible one-BP equipment modules and exchange them during a Breather.
- **Signature:** one Rank-1 training, weapon, vehicle, suit, or equipment technique.
- **Edge:** one narrow professional discipline.
- **Pressures:** gear loss, human limits, maintenance, employer, code, or supply.

This Origin remains competitive without secretly granting superpowers.

#### Composite

A Composite selects one three-BP Core from Source A, one two-BP Secondary from Source B, one two-BP Signature combining or alternating both Sources, and one one-BP Edge from either. It must define an Interference Pressure. Once per scene, spend one Reserve to cancel one Setback caused specifically by Source interference. Composite status waives no prerequisites.

### Hidden-origin continuity

An Origin may be Known, Suspected, or Hidden. A Hidden Origin creates a sealed canonical Truth Record at creation containing actual Source, Catalyst, responsible entities, false beliefs, available evidence, discovery conditions, and agent-access permissions. The AI cannot retroactively replace it for drama.

Every Origin records dormant, active, Pushed, injured, and transformed visual states for comic continuity.

### Ordinary-survivor and latent Potential

An ordinary survivor may remain permanently mundane, possess a latent Source, or awaken during play. Up to eight Origin BP may be designated as Potential. Before awakening, the same value manifests through luck, adrenaline, instinct, improvisation, equipment, or survival techniques. At the Catalyst event, those features may be converted one-for-one into powered techniques without additional cost. The AI cannot choose the player's permanent power set without consent.

### Background contract

A standard Background costs six BP:

| Component | BP |
|---|---:|
| First Trained competency | 1 |
| Second Trained competency | 1 |
| Knowledge or Access Tag | 1 |
| Contact Slot | 1 |
| Asset Slot | 1 |
| Background Edge | 1 |
| Obligation or entanglement | 0 |
| Total | 6 |

Access Tags permit routine access within legitimate scope; hostile, restricted, expired, or cross-jurisdictional use may require checks and generate Evidence or Exposure. Contacts have defined specialty, reach, disposition, need, and information boundaries. Assets are meaningful mundane resources and cannot conceal a free Relic or power.

A lost Contact Slot becomes a Broken Connection that may be repaired or reassigned through play. A destroyed or stolen Asset must be recovered, rebuilt, or replaced; it does not respawn, but its purchased slot is not deleted. Background Edges are usable once per scene unless stated otherwise. Background never dictates morality.

#### Academic or Archivist

- Training: choose two from research, history, languages, analysis, teaching, or investigation.
- Access: universities, libraries, archives, journals, or scholarly networks.
- Contact: professor, curator, archivist, linguist, or researcher.
- Asset: research credentials, specialist archive, office, or restricted database.
- **Citation Trail:** identify the most likely authoritative source for examined evidence or gain Edge retrieving or interpreting it; this cannot create a nonexistent source.
- Obligations: institution, publication dispute, rival, stolen text, or grant.

#### Scientist or Engineer

- Training: choose two from science, technology, engineering, crafting, systems analysis, or investigation.
- Access: laboratories, standards, patents, fabrication, or industry.
- Contact: engineer, laboratory manager, vendor, researcher, or inspector.
- Asset: advanced toolkit, laboratory bench, fabrication access, or technical library.
- **Failure Analysis:** after one minute of inspection, identify one functional tag, likely failure point, or safe test.
- Obligations: ownership, patent, prototype, funder, or rival.

#### Medical Professional

- Training: choose two from medicine, diagnosis, psychology, biology, empathy, or emergency care.
- Access: clinics, hospitals, pharmacies, records, or public health.
- Contact: physician, nurse, pharmacist, therapist, coroner, or supplier.
- Asset: trauma kit, clinic access, diagnostic equipment, or controlled supply.
- **Triage:** gain Edge on one stabilization, diagnosis, treatment-priority, or casualty-assessment check.
- Obligations: patient, licensing, confidentiality, debt, employer, or unresolved case.

#### Soldier or Mercenary

- Training: choose two from warfare, firearms, athletics, survival, vehicles, tactics, or leadership.
- Access: veteran networks, military facilities, contractors, armories, or logistics.
- Contact: squadmate, quartermaster, commander, contractor, or intelligence specialist.
- Asset: legal weapon, armor, field gear, vehicle access, or secure cache.
- **Tactical Read:** after observing a battlefield, ask one factual question about cover, chokepoints, visible threats, or routes and gain Edge on the first action exploiting the answer.
- Obligations: unit, contract, rules of engagement, evidence, teammate, or enemy commander.

#### First Responder

- Training: choose two from emergency medicine, awareness, athletics, vehicles, survival, or hazard response.
- Access: emergency scenes, dispatch, evacuation infrastructure, or municipal services.
- Contact: dispatcher, firefighter, paramedic, rescue specialist, utility worker, or emergency manager.
- Asset: emergency equipment, credentials, rescue vehicle access, or municipal maps.
- **Crisis Entry:** gain Edge on the first rescue, evacuation, stabilization, or immediate-hazard action in a scene.
- Obligations: station, survivor, failed rescue, duty, or public expectation.

#### Investigator or Law Enforcement

- Training: choose two from investigation, awareness, forensics, interrogation, influence, or weapons.
- Access: public records, cases, evidence, courts, or lawful police networks.
- Contact: detective, clerk, prosecutor, forensic specialist, attorney, or informant.
- Asset: scoped credentials, forensic kit, case files, surveillance access, or authorized weapon.
- **Chain of Evidence:** determine whether known evidence could support or refute a claim and gain Edge lawfully preserving or tracing it.
- Obligations: oversight, unresolved case, jurisdiction, informant, corruption, or wrongful conviction.

Credentials never create universal police authority.

#### Criminal, Smuggler, or Fixer

- Training: choose two from stealth, deception, theft, street knowledge, influence, forgery, or illicit logistics.
- Access: black markets, fences, smugglers, gangs, corrupt officials, or covert transport.
- Contact: fence, forger, driver, dealer, intermediary, or insider.
- Asset: safehouse, forged credentials, concealed compartment, tools, or contraband cache.
- **Illicit Route:** identify one plausible way around an official channel, checkpoint, lock, sanction, or procurement restriction; it has a stated cost or risk.
- Obligations: debt, crew, faction, partner, evidence, or territory.

#### Corporate or Political

- Training: choose two from influence, bureaucracy, finance, deception, negotiation, analysis, or leadership.
- Access: corporate offices, government, donors, regulators, or executives.
- Contact: staffer, assistant, lobbyist, regulator, consultant, donor, or union representative.
- Asset: expense account, office, credentials, legal services, meeting space, or vehicle.
- **Procedural Leverage:** identify one applicable rule, budget, decision-maker, deadline, or institutional pressure and gain Edge exploiting it.
- Obligations: employer, donor, constituency, NDA, scandal, regulator, or board.

#### Street Survivor

- Training: choose two from urban survival, awareness, stealth, improvisation, athletics, or street knowledge.
- Access: shelters, informal markets, abandoned spaces, neighborhoods, or service organizations.
- Contact: organizer, neighbor, shelter worker, dealer, mechanic, or lookout.
- Asset: stash, secure sleeping place, improvised workshop, bicycle, or safe room.
- **Always an Exit:** after surveying an urban location, identify one plausible escape route, shelter, overlooked resource, or concealed approach.
- Obligations: dependent, neighborhood, abuser, gang, favor, or housing insecurity.

#### Religious or Occult

- Training: choose two from religion, occult knowledge, ritual, empathy, influence, languages, or spiritual defense.
- Access: temples, cults, sacred archives, clergy, cemeteries, or ritual communities.
- Contact: priest, oracle, theologian, medium, defector, or supplier.
- Asset: consecrated kit, sanctuary, ritual library, sacred token, or protected meeting place.
- **Taboo and Offering:** identify a known hospitality rule, taboo, ritual requirement, or appropriate offering relevant to an entity or place.
- Obligations: congregation, deity, doctrine, rite, schism, possession, or revelation.

The Background does not prove its theology completely correct.

#### Performer, Celebrity, or Athlete

- Training: choose two from performance, athletics, influence, deception, media, or leadership.
- Access: venues, press, events, fan communities, sporting facilities, or entertainment networks.
- Contact: agent, coach, promoter, journalist, teammate, producer, or fan.
- Asset: equipment, wardrobe, venue, studio, training facility, or public platform.
- **Command Attention:** gain Edge on one crowd-facing Presence check; witnesses remember the character and Exposure increases by one until circumstances change.
- Obligations: contract, sponsor, fans, rival, scandal, team, or public identity.

#### Artisan or Tradesperson

- Training: choose two from crafting, technology, commerce, vehicles, athletics, construction, or appraisal.
- Access: workshops, unions, suppliers, construction, repair, or wholesale markets.
- Contact: supplier, foreman, mechanic, contractor, union representative, or specialist.
- Asset: tools, workshop, service van, supplier account, or fabrication space.
- **Make It Work:** gain Edge on improvised construction or repair, or halve the time of a routine mundane repair.
- Obligations: customer, union, family business, project, code violation, or supplier debt.

#### Noble or Divine Household

- Training: choose two from etiquette, history, influence, deception, languages, politics, or divine law.
- Access: estates, courts, embassies, temples, elite functions, or household archives.
- Contact: retainer, herald, relative, diplomat, priest, servant, or guard.
- Asset: signet, residence access, ceremonial equipment, trust income, vehicle, or introduction.
- **Protocol:** gain Edge navigating formal hospitality, precedence, titles, gifts, or audience procedures.
- Obligations: family, succession, alliance, scandal, enemy, or inherited duty.

Status may allow an audience request but never guarantees compliance.

#### Orpheus or Veil Operations

- Training: choose two from continuity science, mythotechnology, security, investigation, medicine, or containment.
- Access: orphaned terminology, classified procedures, abandoned infrastructure, or contractor networks.
- Contact: former colleague, whistleblower, continuity physician, contractor, or survivor.
- Asset: access shard, scanner, cache, facility map, or decommissioned containment tool.
- **Protocol Ghost:** recognize an Orpheus procedure, subsystem, maintenance path, or likely containment response; former credentials may increase Exposure.
- Obligations: cover-up, subject, employer, stolen data, facility, or Continuity Court.

#### Returned or Displaced

- Training: choose two from survival, history, awareness, empathy, deception, languages, or afterlife navigation.
- Access: Returned communities, afterlife contacts, displaced-person services, or obsolete institutions.
- Contact: ferryman, another Returned person, descendant, advocate, or soul broker.
- Asset: old identity record, soul token, inherited cache, disputed property, or passage map.
- **Out of Time:** compare incompatible memories, records, or environments to identify one changed, false, displaced, or anachronistic detail.
- Obligations: personhood, afterlife claim, family, oath, duplicate identity, or temporal dislocation.

#### Journalist or Media Worker

- Training: choose two from investigation, influence, awareness, technology, deception, writing, or production.
- Access: press events, public records, newsrooms, sources, or publishing channels.
- Contact: editor, producer, source, photographer, researcher, or public-information officer.
- Asset: recording equipment, press credentials, platform, archive subscription, or secure source channel.
- **Follow the Story:** from existing evidence, identify the likeliest missing witness, beneficiary, public-record trail, or unanswered question.
- Obligations: source protection, deadline, employer, lawsuit, suppressed story, or audience trust.

Publishing propagates information through Evidence and reputation systems.

#### Explorer or Archaeologist

- Training: choose two from survival, awareness, history, athletics, occult knowledge, vehicles, or languages.
- Access: museums, expeditions, permits, field stations, or archaeological records.
- Contact: guide, curator, sponsor, pilot, local expert, or antiquities investigator.
- Asset: expedition gear, maps, field vehicle access, survey equipment, permit, or camp.
- **Field Method:** once per scene, cancel one Setback caused specifically by unfamiliar terrain, ruins, environmental investigation, or field conditions.
- Obligations: sponsor, artifact, local community, rival, permit, or dangerous site.

#### Custom or Hybrid Background

Choose any two Trained competencies, one coherent Access Tag, one Contact, one Asset, one comparable Background Edge, and one obligation. Purchasing another complete Background costs six BP.

Resolving an obligation converts it into a completed History Anchor; later relationships evolve through play rather than retcon. Characters starting above Level 1 require `TierRank - 1` History Anchors explaining established skills, morality, reputation, enemies, and relationships without granting free mechanics.

### Archetype contract

Archetypes describe operating style rather than identity or Source. Every standard package costs eight BP:

| Component | BP |
|---|---:|
| Active Approach Hook | 3 |
| Core Archetype Talent | 2 |
| Rank-1 Signature Technique | 2 |
| Narrow Archetype Edge | 1 |
| Total | 8 |

Growth profile and defense emphasis are recommendations rather than hidden bonuses. A character may own features from multiple Archetypes, but only one Active Approach Hook is active at a time. One is selected freely when a scene begins; switching during a scene requires a Quick Action. Each Hook functions at most once per round unless stated, and no Hook creates an unrestricted additional Major Action.

#### Vanguard

- Style: engagement, pressure, grappling, and durability; Bulwark or Physical Hybrid; Fortitude.
- **Press the Line:** once per round after dealing or suffering Vitality damage at close range, or winning a physical contest, move up to half Move without spending Move. It must close distance, maintain engagement, or occupy a vacated position and triggers reactions normally.
- **Unbroken:** the first time each scene a Wound or condition would impose Setback, delay its Setback and penalties until the end of the next turn. It cannot negate incapacitation, restraint, death, or destruction.
- Signature: Rank-1 charge, grapple, slam, shockwave, endurance, or close-combat technique.
- Edge: holding position, resisting forced movement, grappling, or engaged intimidation.

#### Striker

- Style: burst damage, assassination, finishing attacks, and decisive effects; Physical or Power Hybrid; Evasion.
- **Commit:** once per round, spend a Quick Action before an attack. Success raises damage or effect magnitude one step, never above Tier ceiling. Failure grants the target Edge on its first action against the Striker before the Striker's next turn.
- **Exploit Opening:** against a target with a condition or damage suffered since the end of the Striker's previous turn, a natural 19 raises outcome degree by one.
- Signature: Rank-1 precision, execution, ambush, blast, or armor-breaking technique.
- Edge: exposed targets, attack lines, weak formations, or vulnerability windows.

#### Skirmisher

- Style: mobility, positioning, precision, and hit-and-run tactics; Balanced or Tactical Hybrid; Evasion.
- **Flow Step:** once per round after a Major Action, move up to half Move without spending Move. It triggers reactions and cannot function while Immobilized.
- **Evasive Motion:** after moving at least half Move during a turn, gain `+1 Evasion` until the next turn begins; this does not stack.
- Signature: Rank-1 dash, teleport step, acrobatic attack, disengagement, pursuit, or mobile-fire technique.
- Edge: difficult terrain, escape, pursuit, or unstable surfaces.

#### Guardian

- Style: protection, interception, barriers, and denial; Bulwark or Tactical Hybrid; Fortitude or Resolve.
- **Interpose:** once per round, use a Reaction when an ally within half Move is targeted. Move up to half Move and become the legal target of a single-target effect when physically possible. Against an area effect, interpose a valid barrier or shielding technique instead.
- **Ward:** as a Quick Action, designate one willing ally within close Reach and choose one Defense. The ally gains `+1` to that Defense while remaining within close Reach. Only one ally may be Warded and Wards do not stack.
- Signature: Rank-1 barrier, interception, shield, suppression, sanctuary, or redirection technique.
- Edge: immediate threats to a protected person, structure, or objective.

#### Controller

- Style: zones, conditions, terrain, forced movement, and battlefield manipulation; Power Hybrid or Centered; Resolve.
- **Reactive Field:** once per round, use a Reaction when a creature enters, leaves, or performs a qualifying action within an active zone to trigger one listed Reaction rider. The same rider cannot affect the same creature more than once per round.
- **Efficient Sustain:** once per round, maintain one active zone without its normally required Quick Action; Reserve and concentration costs remain.
- Signature: Rank-1 binding, hazard zone, gravity field, illusion area, weather, terrain, or forced-movement technique.
- Edge: battlefield geometry, environmental interactions, crowd motion, or containment boundaries.

#### Operator

- Style: stealth, infiltration, sabotage, traps, and preparation; Tactical Hybrid; Vigilance or Evasion.
- **Setup:** as a Quick Action, make an appropriate check against Defense or environmental DC. Success creates one Setup Tag on the target or location. The character or an ally may consume it for Edge or to cancel one Setback on a directly related action. Only one Setup Tag may exist on each target or location.
- **Contingency:** once per scene, reveal one plausible mundane tool worth no more than one BP from carried kit. It becomes spent, lost, or depleted after the scene and cannot be unique, impossible, or implausibly restricted.
- Signature: Rank-1 trap, hack, stealth system, sabotage, disguise, drone, or precision-tool technique.
- Edge: concealment, security, covert entry, surveillance, or escape planning.

#### Savant

- Style: analysis, invention, investigation, and power understanding; Centered or Power Hybrid; Vigilance.
- **Analyze:** as a Quick Action, roll Intellect or Awareness plus relevant Mastery against Vigilance or system DC. Success reveals one canonical Defense, resistance, vulnerability, Source, trigger, cost, or limitation tag. Strong success grants Edge on the next exploitation; Exceptional success permits transfer of that Edge to one ally. Failure provides no false information.
- **Applied Theory:** once per scene, cancel one Setback on an action directly exploiting a discovered tag.
- Signature: Rank-1 scan, invention, countermeasure, deduction, power-control, or forensic technique.
- Edge: research, pattern recognition, technical interpretation, or connecting evidence.

#### Commander

- Style: coordination, leadership, social control, and party action; Centered, Tactical, or Balanced; Resolve.
- **Direct:** as a Quick Action, a willing ally who can perceive the Commander may spend its Reaction to move up to half Move or perform one Quick Action. Each ally may receive Direct once per round.
- **Rally:** once per scene as a Quick Action, an ally in communication range recovers Composure equal to `2 * TierRank` or suppresses one Minor fear or hesitation through the end of its next turn. This cannot remove serious coercion, possession, or psychic injury.
- Signature: Rank-1 command, morale, coordination, pressure, formation, or team-combination technique.
- Edge: morale, coordination, formal negotiation, or leadership structure.

#### Conduit

- Style: high output, Reserve management, transformation, and Overdraw; Conduit; Resolve or Fortitude.
- **Channel:** as a Quick Action, create one Charge lasting through the end of the next turn. Consume it on a Source technique to reduce Reserve cost by one, minimum one, or gain Edge on Control. Only one Charge may be held.
- **Controlled Overdraw:** reduce the first severity-graded Overdraw consequence each scene by one step, never below Minor and never removing the cost.
- Signature: Rank-1 transformation, beam, invocation, surge, area burst, or high-output technique.
- Edge: regulation, energy flow, transformation stability, or overload detection.

#### Wildcard

- Style: improvisation, probability, chaos, absurd weapons, and environmental play; Balanced; choose one Defense at scene start.
- **Make It Weird:** as a Quick Action, designate an unattended object or environmental feature as an improvised Rank-1 tool or weapon for the scene. Choose one legal effect tag and one genuine limitation. It breaks on a natural 1 or when the scene ends.
- **Lucky Break:** once per full rest, reroll an improvised Stunt and keep the preferred result.
- Signature: Rank-1 improvised weapon, probability trick, prank device, environmental attack, or absurd item.
- Edge: unexpected uses for objects, terrain, social confusion, or collateral circumstances.

An improvised object may become a permanent named signature item by purchasing its mechanics and recording its visual bible.

#### Restorer

- Style: healing, repair, cleansing, stabilization, and reconstruction; Tactical, Centered, or Balanced; Resolve or Fortitude.
- **Rapid Aid:** once per round when a target within Move suffers damage, a condition, or equipment failure, use a Reaction to move up to half Move toward it. The next qualifying restorative technique before the end of the following turn may use a Quick rather than Major Action.
- **Not Yet:** once per scene when a target enters a Dying or equivalent destruction track, spend one Reserve to delay its next deterioration check until the end of the following round. This does not heal, stabilize, resurrect, or prevent further damage.
- Signature: Rank-1 healing, repair, cleansing, stabilization, memory restoration, necromantic maintenance, or reconstruction technique.
- Edge: diagnosing immediate physical, mental, spiritual, mechanical, or ontological damage.

Restorer implies no morality; it may preserve prisoners, repair war machines, maintain undead, or keep victims alive.

#### Hybrid or custom Archetype

Select one three-BP Active Approach Hook, one two-BP Core Talent, one two-BP Rank-1 Signature Technique, one one-BP Edge, any legal Growth profile, and a recommended Defense. Features may be mixed freely. Archetype features increase at Tier breakthroughs only when their formulas explicitly reference Tier Rank; other upgrades must be purchased.

### Resource-growth weights

Each character distributes nine Growth Weight points:

`G_V + G_C + G_R = 9`

`G_V`, `G_C`, and `G_R` govern Vitality, Composure, and Reserve growth. Normal values are 2–5.

| Profile | Vitality | Composure | Reserve |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Balanced | 3 | 3 | 3 |
| Bulwark | 5 | 2 | 2 |
| Centered | 2 | 5 | 2 |
| Conduit | 2 | 2 | 5 |
| Physical hybrid | 4 | 2 | 3 |
| Tactical hybrid | 3 | 4 | 2 |
| Power hybrid | 2 | 3 | 4 |

The Archetype recommends but does not impose the allocation. Final absolute resource coefficients are calibrated with combat and damage mathematics.

### Role, morality, and identity

Creation separately records public role, secret/public/disputed identities, starting Moral Polarity, known reputation, Heat, Infamy, faction relationships, and whether powers are public, concealed, or misunderstood.

`abs(M_start) <= 20 * TierRank`

Each 20 points of starting Polarity requires at least one canonical Moral Anchor. Public reputation additionally requires witnesses or surviving evidence.

### Complications, Drives, and connections

Every character begins with one Origin Pressure, one personal Complication, at least two Drives, and at least one meaningful connection to a person, place, faction, or institution. Complications may be tragic, practical, terrifying, humiliating, or absurd, but must have defined triggers and effects. They never give the AI unrestricted permission to sabotage the character.

### Comic creation record

Creation establishes a versioned visual and dialogue bible containing face, body, apparent age, hair, skin, identifying features, civilian clothing, costume, color language, symbols, power effects, transformation states, signature equipment, injuries, prosthetics, scars, nonhuman anatomy, speech style, vocabulary, accent notes, and elements that require a canonical event to change.

### Validation

A completed character must pass Attribute-budget, Build Point, Level/Tier, Source provenance, limitation, duplicate-effect, stacking, contact/asset, growth-allocation, morality-history, and visual-bible validation. The creation event records all choices, rolls, formulas, ruleset versions, and tuning versions. The AI cannot secretly add powers, weaknesses, history, relationships, or morality.

---

## 12. Skills and Mastery

Skills describe learned competence without dictating how a character approaches a problem. The system uses a bounded universal list, flexible Attribute pairings, ranked Mastery, and narrow Specialties so free-form actions remain mechanically consistent without reducing play to fixed buttons.

### Skill check formula

`SkillCheck = d20 + AttributeModifier + MasteryBonus + SpecialtyBonus + SituationalModifier`

Only one skill Mastery and one Specialty may contribute to a single roll. A character may receive other explicit modifiers, Edge, or Setback from circumstances, equipment, powers, teamwork, and conditions, subject to their individual stacking rules.

The player states the intended outcome and method. The system then declares the governing skill, proposed Attribute, DC or opposing Defense, stakes, costs, and consequences before the roll. The player may propose a different Attribute when the method supports it; the AI must accept a fictionally valid pairing or explain the concrete mismatch. Flexible pairing never allows the same Attribute or bonus to be counted twice.

### Mastery ranks, bonuses, and costs

Let Mastery rank `r` be an integer from 0 through 4:

`MasteryBonus(r) = 2r`

`IncrementalMasteryCost(r) = r BP` for purchasing rank `r` from rank `r - 1`

`CumulativeMasteryCost(r) = sum(i, i = 1..r) = r(r + 1) / 2 BP`

`MinimumLevel(r) = 1 + r(r - 1)`

| Rank | `r` | Bonus | Incremental BP | Cumulative BP | Minimum Level |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Untrained | 0 | +0 | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| Trained | 1 | +2 | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| Expert | 2 | +4 | 2 | 3 | 3 |
| Master | 3 | +6 | 3 | 6 | 7 |
| Legendary | 4 | +8 | 4 | 10 | 13 |

A purchase must satisfy both the minimum Level and any fictional access requirement. Training, instruction, research, recovered memory, divine revelation, or demonstrated field experience may provide access; access cannot add an unpurchased rank. Temporary effects may grant a stated bonus or effective rank but do not satisfy prerequisites unless they explicitly say so.

### Specialties

A Specialty is a narrow field inside one skill, such as Athletics (Swimming), Close Combat (Improvised Weapons), Science (Genetics), Influence (Negotiation), or Realm Navigation (Asgardian Roads).

- Each Specialty costs 1 BP.
- Its parent skill must be Trained or higher.
- A relevant Specialty grants `SpecialtyBonus = +1`; otherwise the bonus is zero.
- No more than one Specialty applies to one roll, even if several descriptions overlap.
- The maximum number of owned Specialties in a skill equals that skill's Mastery rank.
- A Specialty cannot be broader than its parent skill, substitute for a different skill, or manufacture fictional access.

### Universal skill list

#### Physical and combat skills

| Skill | Scope |
|---|---|
| Athletics | Lifting technique, climbing, swimming, jumping, sustained physical effort, and physical contests |
| Mobility | Balance, acrobatics, tumbling, escape movement, parkour, and precise bodily repositioning |
| Close Combat | Unarmed, natural-weapon, melee-weapon, and improvised close attacks, parries, and combat maneuvers |
| Ranged Combat | Firearms, bows, thrown weapons, siege aim, and other directed mundane or equipment-based ranged attacks |
| Stealth | Concealment, silent movement, shadowing, ambush positioning, and avoiding detection |
| Security | Locks, traps, alarms, intrusion hardware, bypasses, and protective-system procedure |
| Vehicles | Ground, air, sea, space, mythic, and other piloted conveyances when their operation is not fully autonomous |
| Survival | Foraging, shelter, tracking, navigation in ordinary terrain, environmental endurance, and fieldcraft |

#### Perception and knowledge skills

| Skill | Scope |
|---|---|
| Perception | Immediate sensory detection, searches, noticing danger, and reading observable detail |
| Investigation | Evidence collection, inference, reconstruction, research trails, and connecting facts over time |
| Insight | Reading emotion, motive, inconsistency, social pressure, and behavioral intent without mind reading |
| Medicine | Diagnosis, stabilization, treatment, surgery, pharmacology, and biological care |
| Science | Natural and formal sciences, experimentation, modeling, analysis, and laboratory practice |
| Technology | Computers, electronics, software, networks, advanced machinery, and technical systems |
| Craft | Fabrication, repair, artifice, trades, material work, and practical construction |
| Scholarship | History, cultures, law, languages, institutions, literature, and conventional academic knowledge |
| Occult | Rituals, symbols, entities, metaphysical laws, curses, wards, and hidden supernatural traditions |
| Realm Navigation | Veil routes, divine geographies, impossible thresholds, underworld paths, and travel through mythic spaces |

#### Social, tactical, and supernatural skills

| Skill | Scope |
|---|---|
| Streetwise | Criminal ecosystems, informal markets, urban rumor, gangs, survival networks, and illicit access |
| Influence | Persuasion, negotiation, diplomacy, etiquette, bargaining, and building willing agreement |
| Deception | Lies, disguise performance, misdirection, false identities, concealment of intent, and social cons |
| Intimidation | Threats, displays of leverage, coercive pressure, interrogation pressure, and fear-based compliance |
