| Performance | Acting, comedy, music, dance, public spectacle, audience control, and deliberate presentation |
| Leadership | Morale, coordination, command presence, delegation, inspiration, and group cohesion |
| Warfare | Tactics, strategy, battlefield assessment, logistics, military doctrine, and organized conflict |
| Power Control | Shaping, aiming, restraining, stabilizing, combining, or safely overdriving an extraordinary Source |

### Power Control boundary

Power Control is not a universal replacement for Close Combat, Ranged Combat, another relevant skill, or a power's explicit attack procedure. Every power technique must declare its primary skill and Attribute, permitted alternatives, control conditions, costs, and valid Defenses. Power Control governs manipulation of extraordinary output only when the technique or fiction calls for that control.

Examples include Willpower + Power Control to contain a divine eruption, Agility + Ranged Combat to strike with a shaped energy bolt, Intellect + Power Control to tune a portal, and Presence + Power Control to impose a Name through a Mantle. A power may permit Power Control as its attack skill, but only as an explicit property paid for and recorded in that technique.

### Untrained and restricted actions

Ordinary uses may be attempted Untrained at +0 Mastery. If an action is designated restricted because it requires professional technique, specialized doctrine, dangerous ritual knowledge, or equivalent precision, an Untrained attempt is allowed only when the character possesses the necessary tools, access, Source, and fictional opportunity. It then receives:

- `AdjustedDC = BaseDC + 5`
- Setback
- the full declared failure consequence

An attempt remains impossible when its required tools, access, Source, information, anatomy, or causal mechanism are absent. A natural 20 cannot create those prerequisites.

### Difficulty ladder

| DC | Difficulty | Intended benchmark |
|---:|---|---|
| 5 | Routine under pressure | A basic task made worth rolling by danger or time |
| 10 | Simple | Reliable for a capable person |
| 15 | Professional | Requires training, care, or favorable aptitude |
| 20 | Hard | Challenges an experienced professional |
| 25 | Extreme | Elite human or clearly superhuman execution |
| 30 | Legendary | World-class or overtly extraordinary execution |
| 35 | Mythic | Feats associated with mythic beings and domains |
| 40 | Ascendant | Reality-defining execution at the highest normal campaign tier |
| 45+ | Transcendent | Exceptional set pieces requiring explicit fictional permission and scale |

The AI selects the DC from the task itself, then applies known modifiers before the die result is revealed. It cannot raise a DC merely because the character is highly skilled. Scale, missing leverage, hostile conditions, or an unusually ambitious effect may raise difficulty; character competence does not.

### Situational modifiers, passive values, and time

Numeric situational modifiers use a bounded scale:

| Magnitude | Modifier |
|---|---:|
| Minor | +/-1 |
| Significant | +/-2 |
| Major | +/-5 |

Edge or Setback is preferred when a circumstance changes reliability rather than raw capability. Multiple modifiers from the same cause do not stack; use the single strongest applicable value. All modifiers and their sources are declared before rolling.

`PassiveValue = 10 + AttributeModifier + MasteryBonus + SpecialtyBonus + StaticModifier`

Use a passive value when a character is not actively rolling, repeated secret checks would reveal information, or the opposing actor is testing a standing capability. Edge and Setback normally add no numeric value to a passive score unless a rule explicitly converts them.

**Take 10:** when there is no immediate danger, active opposition, meaningful uncertainty spike, or consequence for delay, a character may treat the d20 as 10. Take 10 cannot trigger natural-roll effects.

**Take 20:** when repeated attempts are possible, failure does not worsen the state, adequate tools are present, and time is abundant, a character may treat the d20 as 20 after spending twenty times the normal attempt duration. This is not a natural 20 and cannot raise the outcome degree by itself.

### Contests and assistance

In an active contest, both sides roll the declared checks and compare totals. The higher total wins; a tie favors the defender or preserves the status quo. The margin between totals determines the outcome degree using the universal degree table. If only one side is actively acting, use the other's applicable Defense or passive value instead.

To assist, a helper describes a distinct contribution and rolls against:

`AssistDC = max(10, PrimaryDC - 5)`

| Helper outcome | Benefit to primary actor |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | No bonus; complication or interference |
| Failure | No bonus; time or declared consequence may still apply |
| Success | +1 |
| Strong success | +2 |
| Exceptional success | Edge |

Unless a team ability says otherwise, no more than two helpers may roll, and total assistance is capped at +2 or one Edge, whichever is better. A helper must possess the fictional access needed to contribute.

### Extended tasks

Use an extended task only when accumulating effort is itself meaningful. Set a Progress requirement before the first roll:

| Task scale | Required Progress |
|---|---:|
| Minor | 3 |
| Standard | 6 |
| Major | 10 |
| Epic | 15+ |

Each interval consumes the declared time and any stated resources:

| Outcome | Progress change |
|---|---:|
| Catastrophic failure | -1, plus a declared consequence |
| Failure | 0, plus time and any declared consequence |
| Success | +1 |
| Strong success | +2 |
| Exceptional success | +3 |

Progress cannot fall below zero. The task completes when accumulated Progress meets or exceeds the requirement. Opposition, clocks, resource depletion, detection, and changing conditions advance independently; repeating a roll does not freeze the world.

### Social-action limits

Social skills change beliefs, choices, leverage, morale, access, and relationships only to the extent supported by the fiction. They do not provide unrestricted control of another character's agency.

- Influence can produce willing agreement, compromise, or changed attitude; it cannot compel an impossible betrayal without adequate leverage and consequence.
- Deception establishes a specific false belief or concealed truth; it is not retroactive reality editing.
- Intimidation may win hesitation, information, retreat, or temporary compliance; it does not create loyalty.
- Insight reveals supported emotional or behavioral conclusions; it is not mind reading unless a power explicitly makes it so.
- Performance affects an audience through ordinary presentation; supernatural crowd control requires a power, Mantle, relic, or other explicit effect.
- Ordinary persuasion does not inflict Composure damage. An attack against Composure requires a qualifying technique, circumstance, or sustained conflict procedure.

The target's Drives, relationships, knowledge, moral boundaries, fear, leverage, and current attitude establish what outcomes are possible and what they cost. Player-character decisions remain with the player except where a transparent, rules-defined supernatural condition explicitly applies.

---

## 13. Talents and Feats

Talents and Feats provide traditional character-building choices outside Sources, powers, equipment, and Archetype packages. They support specialized competence, tactical identity, survivability, social leverage, absurd improvisation, and rare probability control without secretly granting a new Power Source or bypassing Power Tier.

### Classification, costs, and Level gates

- **Talents** cost 1–3 BP and provide trained advantages, conditional bonuses, or specialized actions.
- **Feats** cost 4–6 BP and provide character-defining rule exceptions, dramatic survivability, or controlled probability manipulation.
- **Techniques** remain active expressions of powers, equipment, Mantles, or Archetypes. A Talent or Feat cannot substitute for a Technique when an effect requires Source-scale causation.

| Cost | Classification | Default minimum Level |
|---:|---|---:|
| 1 | Minor Talent | 1 |
| 2 | Standard Talent | 1 |
| 3 | Advanced Talent | 3 |
| 4 | Defining Feat | 5 |
| 5 | Heroic Feat | 9 |
| 6 | Legendary Feat | 13 |

A listed prerequisite may raise these requirements. Features included in a validated Origin, Background, Archetype, power, Mantle, or other creation package follow that package's explicit Level rule and price rather than being silently reclassified.

### Custom-feature pricing

Begin with the closest approved catalog feature, then calculate:

`CustomCost = clamp(1, 6, BenchmarkCost + DeltaBreadth + DeltaFrequency + DeltaReliability + DeltaActionEfficiency - LimitationCredit)`

Each Delta is `-1`, `0`, or `+1` relative to the selected benchmark. `LimitationCredit` is an integer from 0 through 2. A credited limitation must constrain meaningful uses on a recurring basis; cosmetic flavor, rare contrivances, and restrictions controlled entirely by the player grant no credit.

The design record must identify the benchmark, every adjustment, final BP cost, minimum Level, prerequisites, trigger, timing, action, resource cost, frequency, range, targets, check, Defense or DC, effect, duration, stacking behavior, failure behavior, recharge, tags, and event-ledger output.

Hard pricing and permission floors apply:

- A reroll chosen after seeing the result costs at least 4 BP.
- Guaranteed ordinary success costs at least 5 BP and requires Fate or an equivalent scarce cost.
- A guaranteed natural 20 or critical costs at least 6 BP, requires Level 13, has narrow scope, and requires Fate or an equivalent scarce cost.
- Repeated unrestricted extra Major Actions are prohibited.
- Tier bypass, automatic kills, unrestricted mind control, and retroactive reality changes require powers or campaign-scale effects rather than Talents or Feats.
- A result above 6 BP is not compressed to 6; it must be divided into legal features, built as a power or Technique, or rejected.

### Purchase, stacking, and timing

- Features are purchased with whole BP and cannot be purchased twice unless marked **Ranked**.
- Identical numeric benefits do not stack; use the strongest applicable benefit unless a rule explicitly states that it is cumulative.
- Only one reroll, result replacement, or outcome-degree alteration may affect one roll.
- A trigger cannot be declared retroactively unless the feature explicitly functions after the roll or after seeing the result.
- “After seeing the result” means after the total and outcome degree are known but before mechanical and narrative consequences are committed.
- Costs declared before a roll remain spent even when the roll fails. If the rules engine rejects the action as impossible before resolution, no roll occurs and uncommitted costs are not spent.
- Once-per-round usage is tracked by round. Once-per-scene features do not recharge through artificial disengagement, brief hiding, or a manufactured micro-scene.
- Full-rest features recharge only after completing a valid full rest. A full rest does not restore Fate unless a separate rule says so.
- When player-controlled effects share a trigger, the player chooses their order. NPC-controlled effects follow declared priority and cannot be invented after the outcome is known.

### Initial universal Talent catalog

#### Skill, investigation, and exploration Talents

| Feature | BP | Prerequisite and effect |
|---|---:|---|
| **Professional Routine** | 1 | Choose an owned Specialty whose parent skill is Expert. Take 10 with that Specialty under significant time pressure, but not during immediate danger or active opposition. Ranked for different Specialties. |
| **Deep Specialty** | 2 | Choose an owned Specialty whose parent skill is Expert. Its Specialty bonus becomes +2 instead of +1. Ranked for different Specialties; only one Specialty still applies per roll. |
| **Jack of All Trades** | 2 | For restricted Untrained actions, reduce the DC surcharge from +5 to +2. Setback, tools, access, Source, and fictional prerequisites remain. |
| **Quick Study** | 2 | Once per scene after observing a successful Trained skill use, treat yourself as Trained for one attempt at the same specific task before the scene ends. This grants no access, permanent Mastery, prerequisites, powers, or copied features. |
| **Technical Improviser** | 2 | Once per scene, basic materials may substitute for missing proper tools when a plausible causal method exists. The attempt receives Setback; if improvised tools were already permitted, cancel the Setback caused solely by those tools instead. |
| **Scene Surveyor** | 2 | After one minute of observation and a successful Perception or Investigation check, create one Setup Tag tied to an observed exit, hazard, route, defensive feature, or access point. Only one Scene Surveyor Tag may exist in a location. |
| **Pathfinder** | 2 | Requires Trained Survival or Realm Navigation. Cancel one environmental Setback on the first qualifying check during each declared travel leg. Realm law, impossible access, and scale remain. |
| **Field Medic** | 3 | Requires Expert Medicine. Once per scene, perform a qualifying stabilization or emergency-care action as a Quick Action rather than a Major Action. Required tools and checks remain; this does not automatically heal a Wound. |

#### Action, teamwork, and absurd-combat Talents

| Feature | BP | Prerequisite and effect |
|---|---:|---|
| **Quick Draw** | 1 | Once per round, draw, stow, or ready one accessible ordinary item without spending an action. It cannot activate or use the item. |
| **Sure-Footed** | 1 | Cancel the first Setback each scene caused solely by ordinary unstable footing, balance, or difficult terrain. |
| **Guarded Action** | 2 | Spend a Quick Action to gain +1 to one chosen Defense until the next turn begins. It does not stack with itself. |
| **Coordinated** | 2 | A successful assistance roll from this character grants +2 instead of +1. Strong and Exceptional outcomes and the normal assistance cap remain unchanged. |
| **Set Them Up** | 3 | Once per round, a Strong or Exceptional success on a maneuver may create one Setup Tag for an ally instead of taking another optional outcome benefit. It expires after that ally's next turn. |
| **Anything Is a Weapon** | 2 | Ignore penalties caused solely by an object being an improvised weapon. Its size, durability, reach, damage, breakage, legality, availability, and absurd visual consequences remain real; it gains no supernatural property. |
| **Collateral Bargain** | 3 | Before a Stunt roll, commit a meaningful consequence—advance a valid Heat, Exposure, or Collateral clock by one, damage equipment, or endanger an Asset—to gain Edge. The cost occurs even on failure. A trivial, irrelevant, full, or fabricated clock is invalid. |

### Social, reputation, and resolve Talents

| Feature | BP | Prerequisite and effect |
|---|---:|---|
| **Read the Room** | 2 | As a Quick Action, make an Insight check. Success reveals one observable attitude, immediate pressure, Drive, or leverage; Strong success reveals two and Exceptional success three. It is not mind reading. |
| **Connected** | 2 | Gain one additional Contact slot and a related scoped Access Tag. Define the relationship and obligation. The Contact may be lost or replaced through the normal contact rules. Ranked for distinct Contacts. |
| **Reputation Leverage** | 2 | Once per scene, invoke a relevant witnessed Reputation for Edge on a social action. Doing so reveals involvement and may advance a valid Heat, Exposure, or identity clock. |
| **Unshakable** | 3 | Once per scene before a Resolve check against fear or coercion, cancel one Setback or gain +2 if no Setback applies. It does not end an existing supernatural condition. |

### Power and countermeasure Talents

| Feature | BP | Prerequisite and effect |
|---|---:|---|
| **Versatile Control** | 2 | Requires Expert Power Control. Choose one owned Technique and record one additional fictionally valid Attribute-and-skill pairing for its control check. Damage, effect, scale, cost, range, targets, and Defense do not change. Ranked for different Techniques. |
| **Countermeasure** | 3 | After discovering a canonical Source, resistance, trigger, or vulnerability tag, spend a Major Action and pass a relevant technical, occult, or tactical check. Create one Countermeasure Tag usable for Edge or to cancel one directly related Setback; only one such Tag may exist for the same discovery. |
| **Safer Overdraw** | 3 | Once per full rest, reduce one severity-graded Overdraw consequence by one step, never below Minor and never eliminating the required cost. |

### Defining and probability-control Feats

| Feature | BP | Prerequisite and effect |
|---|---:|---|
| **Diehard** | 4 | Level 5. Once per full rest, when reduced to zero Vitality without immediate death or destruction, remain active through the end of the next turn and immediately suffer a Serious Wound. Defeat is then evaluated normally unless Vitality has been restored. |
| **Second Chance** | 4 | Level 5. Once per full rest, reroll one eligible personal roll after seeing its result and keep the preferred result. |
| **Certain Success** | 5 | Level 9 and Master rank in a chosen skill. Once per full rest, spend 1 Fate after seeing an eligible result to convert a possible action using that skill into an ordinary success. Ranked only for different skills. |
| **Perfect Moment** | 6 | Level 13, Legendary rank in the parent skill, and a chosen owned Specialty. Once per full rest before rolling an action using that Specialty, spend 2 Fate to treat the roll as a natural 20. This cannot exceed capability or Tier, guarantee a kill, or make an impossible action possible. Ranked only for different Specialties. |

Fortune features do not provide free Fate restoration. They cannot combine with Fate's ordinary reroll or replacement on the same roll, and a roll affected by one probability-control feature cannot be affected by another.

### Continuity and comic capture

Every activation records the feature version, owner, trigger, timing, targets, declared costs, check or replacement, final effect, recharge state, world consequences, and any visible performance. Recurring Talents and Feats become part of the character's action and dialogue bible: stance, signature movement, improvisational style, props, tells, collateral patterns, and visual treatment must remain consistent unless canonically changed.

---

## 14. Complications and Drives

Complications and Drives turn character identity into transparent gameplay without placing the player on narrative rails. They may be noble, selfish, cruel, ridiculous, mundane, or contradictory. A motivation such as protecting everyone, achieving immortality, ruling Earth, or humiliating gods with increasingly inappropriate weapons is mechanically valid. Morality evaluates informed action and foreseeable consequence rather than the stated motivation.

### Starting requirements and boundaries

Every starting character records:

- one **Origin Pressure** tied to Source or origin;
- one **Personal Complication**;
- at least two Drives, including one Primary Drive at Intensity 3 and one Secondary Drive at Intensity 2; and
- at least one meaningful personal connection.

Complications and Drives grant no BP rebates. Additional entries are allowed, but do not increase per-scene reward limits. They cannot compel player-character behavior, silently rewrite identity, excuse an action from consequences, or authorize the AI to sabotage the character.

### Drive record

Every Drive stores its statement, desired outcome, type, Intensity, target, horizon, permitted and refused methods, Progress, qualifying evidence, Conflict Marks, completion or transformation conditions, player visibility, character knowledge, and version history.

Approved types include Bond, Creed, Ambition, Survival, Curiosity, Revenge, Dominion, Redemption, Obsession, Pleasure, and custom. Type is descriptive rather than moral or restrictive.

| Intensity | Meaning |
|---:|---|
| 1 | Interest or emerging desire |
| 2 | Serious commitment |
| 3 | Core identity motivation |

### Drive Progress and rewards

Finite Drives receive a Progress requirement:

| Horizon | Required Progress |
|---|---:|
| Short-term | 3 |
| Ongoing objective | 6 |
| Major ambition | 10 |
| Lifelong or epic purpose | 15+ |

| Qualifying Drive Beat | Progress |
|---|---:|
| Meaningful step | +1 |
| Major milestone or meaningful cost | +2 |
| Decisive or irreversible transformation | +3 |

`NewProgress = min(RequiredProgress, CurrentProgress + BeatProgress)`

Only one Progress award applies to each Drive per scene. The first mechanically meaningful Drive Beat across all of the character's Drives in a scene grants:

`DriveMomentum = 1 + CoreCost`

`CoreCost = 1` when an Intensity-3 Drive advances through genuine risk, sacrifice, or consequence; otherwise `0`. Maximum Drive Momentum is two. If the current scene does not use Momentum, Progress still applies but no substitute currency is created.

Completing a Drive is classified through the existing objective XP formula. It does not create duplicate XP when the same accomplishment has already received objective XP. On completion, the player may fulfill, extend, transform, or retire the Drive while preserving its history.

### Drive contradiction and evolution

Acting against a Drive remains legal. A qualifying direct contradiction adds:

`ConflictGain = ceil(BetrayalWeight * Agency)`

- `BetrayalWeight = 1` for a meaningful compromise.
- `BetrayalWeight = 2` for a fundamental betrayal.
- `Agency = 1` when deliberate, `0.5` when coerced or reckless, and `0` when accidental.

`ConflictMarks_new = min(3, ConflictMarks_old + ConflictGain)`

At three Conflict Marks, the Drive becomes **Fractured** and grants no further mechanical rewards until the player chooses one of the following:

- reaffirm it through meaningful action;
- rewrite it while retaining its prior history;
- abandon it and accept resulting relationship, reputation, oath, or faction consequences;
- corrupt it into a darker or otherwise transformed form; or
- fulfill it and establish a successor Drive.

The AI may present supported consequences and options, but it cannot select the character's new motivation or retcon earlier behavior.

### Complication record and categories

Every Complication records its owner, Source, category, trigger, activation mode, Severity range, allowed effects, mitigation, escalation, cooldown, resolution conditions, reward eligibility, knowledge boundaries, visual state, and version history.

Approved categories include vulnerability or weakness; dependency or addiction; oath, debt, or obligation; enemy, hunter, or divine claimant; secret or disputed identity; relationship liability; power instability; temptation or obsession; legal or reputational burden; body, soul, Mantle, or resurrection defect; absurd recurring liability; and custom Origin Pressure.

### Complication activation modes

- **Automatic:** an objective, deterministic trigger established in advance activates the recorded effect.
- **Offered:** the AI proposes an interpretive activation, which the player may accept or decline without penalty.
- **Invoked:** the player deliberately activates the Complication while its recorded trigger is valid.

Ambiguous triggers cannot be treated as Automatic. The AI cannot invent a vulnerability or activate a severe consequence merely because it would produce a dramatic story. A declined Offered activation does not prevent ordinary world events or consequences generated by other valid rules.

### Severity and Impact Points

Each activation has Severity `S` from 1 through 3 and an equal number of Impact Points. Selected effects must total no more than `S`.

| Impact cost | Example effect |
|---:|---|
| 1 | Setback on one related check, +2 DC on one action, clock +1, lost time, minor equipment problem, or ten-percent loss from one relevant resource |
| 2 | Scene-long narrow Setback, Minor condition, temporary loss of access or a feature, identity evidence revealed, endangered Contact, or clock +2 |
| 3 | Persistent significant condition, Serious Wound risk, equipment disabled until repaired, Contact or Asset captured or lost, major enemy intervention, or clock +3 |

For an Impact option that removes a percentage of a tracked resource:

`ResourceLoss = ceil(0.10 * ImpactCost * ResourceMaximum)`

`ResourceCurrent_new = max(0, ResourceCurrent_old - ResourceLoss)`

Different effects may be combined within the budget. A Complication cannot directly impose automatic death, permanent player-character rewriting, a retroactive event, or an action the player must take. Consequences above Severity 3 require a separately resolved event chain rather than a single Complication activation.

### Mitigation and effective Severity

Effective Severity determines rewards:

- a genuine immunity or invalid trigger produces `S_effective = 0`;
- material mitigation through a successful action or meaningful cost produces `S_effective = max(1, S - 1)`; and
- fully suffering the activation produces `S_effective = S`.

Cosmetic responses, costs refunded by the same setup, and consequences that do not change risk, options, resources, time, or canonical state do not qualify as mitigation or reward-bearing impact.

### Complication XP and Momentum

`XP_complication = round[0.05 * U(R) * S_effective * N]`

- `R` is the actual Event Rating rather than automatically the character's Level.
- `N` is the approved repetition multiplier.
- a self-manufactured exploit uses `N = 0`.

In a mechanically active scene:

`ComplicationMomentum = min(2, S_effective)`

Only the highest eligible Complication reward applies per causal event and per scene, although every valid consequence still occurs. The reward belongs to the Complication's owner; affected allies may receive ordinary contribution or objective rewards only through their own qualifying actions.

### Complication resolution

Because Complications grant no BP, they require no BP repayment to remove. They must instead be resolved, transformed, or rendered irrelevant through canonical play. A retired Complication cannot silently return. An Origin Pressure requires a genuine change to the character's Source, history, relationship, or metaphysical state before retirement. New complications may emerge from play only through visible events and player-accessible rules.

### Difficult Fate restoration

A Drive or Complication event restores one Fate only when every condition below is true:

- it advances or resolves an Intensity-3 Drive;
- it belongs to a Major or larger objective;
- it includes Severity-3 consequences, catastrophic survival, or irreversible sacrifice;
- it is a novel event with `N = 1`;
- the character is below maximum Fate; and
- the same milestone has not restored Fate previously.

Maximum restoration is one Fate per character per major objective. Dangerous oaths may deliberately pair a Drive with a Complication to qualify, but their recorded consequences remain real. Breaking an oath does not retroactively remove Fate already spent.

### Morality boundary

Drive and Complication rewards are alignment-neutral. The actual deed independently receives Moral Polarity change, Conviction XP, Hope, Dread, or Defiance consequences, reputation and evidence propagation, faction responses, and relationship changes. Drive completion and objective XP do not stack when they describe the same accomplishment; Complication XP may coexist because it rewards a separate, suffered cost.

### Autonomous NPCs, memory, and comics

NPCs and companions use the same record structure. Private Drives and Complications remain in canonical Truth Records and are retrieved only when relevant. An autonomous sub-agent cannot invent a motivation, know information outside its boundary, or rewrite a Drive without a committed event. Conflict, fracture, reaffirmation, and transformation are event-ledger changes rather than prompt improvisation.

Every activation records the formula version, trigger evidence, mode, declared and effective Severity, Impact effects, mitigation, XP and Momentum, Drive Progress, Conflict Marks, Fate eligibility, relationships, world consequences, and comic-facing manifestation. Dialogue tells, recurring imagery, damaged equipment, instability, absurd props, scars, and continuing consequences become part of visual and dialogue canon. Tragedy cannot vanish between panels, and comedy cannot erase canonical harm.

---

## 15. Combat: Encounter Timing, Initiative, Movement, and Momentum

Structured time begins whenever exact order matters, including combat, pursuit, rescue, collapsing environments, ritual interruption, tense infiltration, or another time-sensitive conflict. Starting structured time does not prescribe violence or require an encounter to end in death.

### Encounter-state commitment

Before Initiative, the system commits:

- participants, factions, positions, elevation, visibility, and knowledge;
- objectives and escape routes;
- lethal, nonlethal, disabling, capture, or indiscriminate intent;
- active conditions, transformations, equipment, and resources;
- hazards, collateral, witnesses, clocks, and expected reinforcements; and
- ruleset version, tuning version, and deterministic roll seed.

Hidden participants remain concealed from the player but are committed to the canonical encounter state before acting. The AI cannot invent reinforcements, hazards, or enemy positions after seeing player actions without a valid arrival, discovery, or state-change event.

### Rounds and turns

One ordinary round represents approximately six seconds. Each round resolves in this order:

1. global start-of-round triggers;
2. individual turns in descending Initiative order; and
3. end-of-round hazards, clocks, morale, reinforcements, and duration changes.

Extreme speed and time manipulation alter what a character may accomplish within structured time; they do not secretly change the common round clock.

### Surprise and readiness

The ambushing side rolls an appropriate check against each target's passive detection:

`AmbushTotal = d20 + AttributeModifier + MasteryBonus + SpecialtyBonus + SituationalModifier`

Common pairings include Agility + Stealth, Presence + Deception, and Intellect + Warfare.

| Outcome | Surprise result |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | The target detects the attempt and gains Edge on Initiative |
| Failure | No surprise |
| Success | The target becomes Unready |
| Strong success | The target is Unready and the first qualifying hostile action against it gains Edge |
| Exceptional success | Strong-success benefits plus one Setup Tag against that target |

An **Unready** character rolls Initiative with Setback and cannot use Reactions until the end of its first turn. It does not lose its entire turn.

A group ambush uses one lead roll with normal assistance and compares the result separately against each target's applicable passive value. Social betrayal may test passive Insight, physical concealment normally tests passive Vigilance, and specialized detection uses the Defense or passive value declared before the check.

### Initiative formula and order

`Initiative = d20 + InitiativeAttributeModifier + InitiativeMasteryBonus + SpecialtyBonus + SituationalModifier`

| Situation | Typical pairing |
|---|---|
| Reacting to sudden danger | Awareness + Perception |
| Explosive physical start | Agility + Mobility |
| Executing a battle plan | Intellect + Warfare |
| Beginning from concealment | Agility + Stealth |
| Vehicle pursuit | Agility or Intellect + Vehicles |

The chosen pairing must represent the actor's actual approach rather than merely its highest statistics. Turns proceed from highest Initiative total to lowest. Ties resolve by higher Awareness, then higher Agility, then player-controlled actor, then a deterministic seeded tie-breaker.

Identical minor NPCs may share one Initiative roll. Named characters and major threats roll separately. A reinforcement rolls when it actually arrives. If its Initiative position has passed, it may use otherwise available Reactions but receives its first turn at that position in the next round.

### Turn economy and exchange

At the start of each turn, a character refreshes one Major Action, one Move, one Quick Action, and one Reaction. An Unready character does not refresh its first Reaction until the end of its first turn.

Actions may be downgraded:

- Major Action to one Move or one Quick Action;
- Move to one Quick Action; and
- Quick Action cannot be upgraded.

No character gains an additional Major Action without an explicit rule. Movement may be split before and after other actions. A Reaction interrupts its trigger, resolves fully, and then the interrupted action continues when it remains valid.

### Basic actions

| Action | Cost | Function |
|---|---|---|
| Attack | Major | Make one basic attack or use a qualifying attack Technique |
| Maneuver | Major | Grapple, shove, trip, disarm, pin, distract, reposition, or attempt another nonstandard contest |
| Stunt | Major | Attempt a fictionally possible action not already defined |
| Dash | Major | Gain an additional Move up to current Speed |
| Guard | Major | Gain +2 to all Defenses until the next turn begins |
| Withdraw | Major | Move up to Speed without triggering ordinary Opportunity Attacks from visible opponents |
| Assist | Usually Major | Use the approved assistance procedure |
| Ready | Major plus Reaction | Prepare one legal action for a declared perceptible trigger |
| Aim | Quick | Gain +1 on the next ranged attack against one chosen visible target before the turn ends |
| Disengage | Quick | Choose one opponent within Reach; movement does not provoke from that opponent this turn |
| Interact | Quick or free | Manipulate an accessible object according to its complexity |
| Move | Move | Travel up to current Speed |

Brief speech and minor gestures are free when they plausibly fit within the round. Extended persuasion, command, ritual speech, or complicated instructions require an appropriate action.

### Delay and Ready

Before taking any action, a character may **Delay** by moving its Initiative slot later in the current round. The chosen position becomes its slot for the remainder of the encounter. Delay cannot interrupt another action. If the character never chooses to act before the round ends, the turn is lost and its Initiative moves to the final slot.

To **Ready**, the character declares a perceptible trigger, exact prepared action, target requirements, and range requirements. When the trigger occurs, the character spends its Reaction and resolves the action. If it does not occur before the character's next turn, the prepared action is lost.

Costs are paid when the readied action executes. “When the target begins” resolves before completion; “after the target does” resolves afterward. Ambiguous trigger language defaults to afterward.

### Reaction timing

Reactions use four timing windows:

1. before targeting or substitution;
2. before the roll;
3. after the result but before consequences; and
4. after consequences.

A feature must declare its window. Effects within the same window resolve in player-chosen order for player-controlled effects. NPC priorities must be committed before the result. A Reaction cannot create an unlimited reaction chain; reacting to another Reaction requires explicit permission.

### Position, range, and movement

Precise mode uses spaces:

`1 space = 5 feet approximately 1.5 meters`

`BaseSpeed = 6 spaces`

Text narration uses range bands backed by the same precise spatial state:

| Range band | Separation |
|---|---:|
| Engaged | 0–1 spaces |
| Near | 2–6 spaces |
| Far | 7–24 spaces |
| Distant | 25–100 spaces |
| Extreme | 101+ spaces |

Powers, vehicles, size, terrain, and conditions may alter Speed or Reach.

- Ordinary difficult terrain costs two spaces for each space entered.
- Climbing or swimming without a matching Speed costs two spaces per space and may require a check.
- Voluntary movement out of hostile Reach triggers an Opportunity Attack unless the character Disengages, Withdraws, or uses another valid exception.
- Forced movement and teleportation do not trigger ordinary Opportunity Attacks unless a feature explicitly says otherwise.
- Entering Reach does not itself trigger an Opportunity Attack.
- Ranged attacks while Engaged receive Setback unless the weapon or Technique has a close-quarters property.
- Movement cannot pass through sealed barriers, unsupported space, or occupied hostile positions without a valid method.

Spatial state records exact or banded location, elevation, engagement, cover, concealment, facing only when relevant, carried actors or objects, and environmental support. Narration cannot teleport an actor or object between unrecorded positions.

### Opportunity Attacks

When a visible hostile target voluntarily leaves Reach, a character may spend its Reaction to make one basic Close Combat attack before the target leaves. A successful attack does not automatically stop movement; stopping, tripping, grappling, or redirecting the target requires an appropriate rider, maneuver, or feature.

### Momentum maximum and lifecycle

Momentum is personal, alignment-neutral encounter currency:

`MomentumMaximum = 2 + TierRank`

| Tier Rank | Maximum Momentum |
|---:|---:|
| 1 | 3 |
| 2 | 4 |
| 3 | 5 |
| 4 | 6 |
| 5 | 7 |

Momentum begins at zero when structured conflict starts and is lost when the scene definitively ends. It cannot be transferred between characters without an explicit team ability.

Approved sources include:

- the first Strong or Exceptional risky action each round: +1;
- a successful natural 20: +1;
- qualifying Drive and Complication activations; and
- explicit Archetype, teamwork, environmental, power, or equipment effects.

One roll generates no more than one Momentum from its outcome degree and natural result. Momentum gained above the maximum is lost.

### Momentum spending

| Cost | Effect |
|---:|---|
| 1 | Add +1 after rolling but before the outcome is committed |
| 1 | Cancel one Setback before rolling |
| 2 | Reroll an eligible roll after seeing the result and keep the new result |
| 2 | After succeeding, purchase one additional legal outcome rider |
| 3 | Gain one additional Move or Quick Action on the current turn |

Only one Momentum option may modify a roll. Momentum cannot guarantee success, create a natural 20, exceed Tier, or combine with Fate or another reroll on the same roll. The additional action option may be used once per turn and cannot become a Major Action.

### Encounter conclusion

Structured time ends when exact ordering no longer matters because opposition has been defeated, escaped, surrendered, negotiated, captured, or otherwise neutralized, or because participants have separated with no immediate pursuit.

Surrender does not restrict player freedom. The player may accept, reject, imprison, release, interrogate, or continue attacking. The chosen intent receives its normal attack, morality, evidence, faction, reputation, and relationship consequences.

Ending Initiative does not erase damage, Wounds, conditions, destroyed terrain, lost equipment, witnesses, active clocks, transformations, or comic-visible state. Once-per-scene features recharge only after the encounter satisfies the approved scene-transition boundary.

### Combat event and comic record

Every structured event records Initiative inputs and order, surprise, readiness, actions, timing windows, reactions, movement paths, positions, targets, intent, Momentum gains and spending, environmental changes, visibility, dialogue, costume and equipment state, and unresolved consequences. This combat ledger is simultaneously the reproducible rules record and the source of panel choreography.

---

## 16. Combat: Attacks, Defenses, Cover, and Maneuvers

### Four Defenses

| Defense | Governing Attribute | Primary protection |
|---|---|---|
| Evasion | Agility | Strikes, projectiles, blasts, explosions, traps, and avoidable hazards |
| Fortitude | Endurance | Grapples, forced movement, poison, disease, physical transformation, and bodily disruption |
| Resolve | Willpower | Terror, psychic attacks, coercion, possession, spiritual pressure, and identity disruption |
| Vigilance | Awareness | Ambushes, feints, illusions, deception, hidden attacks, and battlefield misdirection |

Archetype Defense recommendations remain recommendations rather than hidden bonuses.

### Defense formula

`TierDefenseBonus = 2 * (TierRank - 1)`

`DefenseAdjustment = clamp(-5, 5, SumOfApplicableAdjustments)`

`Defense = max(5, 10 + DefenseAttributeModifier + TierDefenseBonus + DefenseAdjustment)`

| Tier Rank | Tier Defense Bonus |
|---:|---:|
| 1 | +0 |
| 2 | +2 |
| 3 | +4 |
| 4 | +6 |
| 5 | +8 |

Defense adjustments include Talents, powers, shields, stances, conditions, and circumstances. Bonuses from the same named source do not stack. Armor ordinarily mitigates damage rather than increasing Evasion unless it explicitly provides deflection or avoidance.

Cover and called-shot adjustments modify the attacker's target number separately:

`AttackTargetNumber = Defense + CoverBonus + CalledShotAdjustment`

The AI cannot raise a Defense because a character has become accurate, because a target is narratively important, or because a fight is expected to be difficult.

### Active Defense

Before an attack roll or Defense Check, a conscious and aware target may spend its Reaction to gain +2 to the relevant Defense for that effect:

- **Dodge:** Evasion; requires freedom to move.
- **Brace:** Fortitude.
- **Center:** Resolve.
- **Focus:** Vigilance; requires awareness of the threat.

This bonus is part of DefenseAdjustment and respects its +/-5 cap. It applies to one effect only. Guard remains stronger against repeated attacks because its +2 persists until the next turn without consuming the Reaction.

### Attack formula

`AttackModifier = clamp(-5, 5, SumOfApplicableNumericModifiers)`

`AttackTotal = d20 + AttributeModifier + MasteryBonus + SpecialtyBonus + AttackModifier`

`AttackMargin = AttackTotal - AttackTargetNumber`

Only one Attribute, one skill Mastery, and one Specialty apply. Edge and Setback operate outside the numeric modifier cap.

| Attack | Typical check | Target Defense |
|---|---|---|
| Basic melee | Strength or Agility + Close Combat | Evasion |
| Basic ranged | Agility + Ranged Combat | Evasion |
| Physical restraint | Strength + Close Combat or Athletics | Fortitude or Evasion |
| Mental assault | Willpower or Presence + declared skill | Resolve |
| Feint or combat deception | Agility or Presence + Deception | Vigilance |
| Extraordinary attack | Technique-defined pairing | Technique-defined Defense |

Power Control is used only when the Technique explicitly designates it.

### Attack results

| Outcome | Attack result |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | No direct effect; the attacker receives an Opening or another declared complication |
| Failure | No direct effect; committed action and resource costs remain spent |
| Success | Apply the attack's base effect |
| Strong success | Base effect plus one Outcome Rider |
| Exceptional success | Base effect plus two different Outcome Riders |

The same Rider cannot be selected twice unless it explicitly permits stacking.

### Natural rolls and criticals

A rolled natural 20 raises the outcome by one degree, generates no more than one Momentum from the roll, and adds one Critical Rider when the final attack outcome is Success or higher.

A natural 1 lowers the outcome by one degree and creates an Opening or equivalent complication. It is not necessarily an automatic miss against a drastically weaker target.

A Fate-created natural 20 or **Perfect Moment** produces at least an ordinary Success and one Critical Rider when the action is possible within current capability and scale.

#### Ordinary Outcome Riders

| Rider | Effect |
|---|---|
| Potency | Increase damage or effect by one step; maximum once per attack |
| Reposition | Move the attacker or target `1 + TierRank` spaces when method and scale permit |
| Minor Condition | Apply one fictionally valid Minor condition until the end of the target's next turn |
| Setup | Create one Setup Tag for the attacker or an ally |
| Disarm | Force one exposed, held ordinary item to drop within one space |
| Breach | Damage or reduce relevant cover, barrier, or protection by one step |
| Controlled Impact | Reduce one collateral consequence or applicable clock by one |
| Preserve Position | Retain concealment, cover, footing, or another position normally lost through the action |

#### Critical Riders

| Critical Rider | Effect |
|---|---|
| Critical Pressure | Add one Critical Pressure to the later Wound evaluation |
| Violent Reposition | Double one forced-movement Rider |
| Major Condition | Upgrade a qualifying Minor condition to Major through the end of the target's next turn |
| Disable | Disable one exposed non-Relic item no higher than the attacker's Tier until repaired |
| Decisive Control | Apply two different ordinary non-damage Riders |
| Signature Critical | Use a Technique-specific Critical Rider recorded in advance |

A critical does not automatically kill a target, bypass Tier, destroy a True Relic, or invent a vulnerability. Lethal intent and overwhelming effect are resolved through damage and Wounds.

For an area attack, a natural 20 raises the outcome against every affected target, but its free Critical Rider applies to only one target unless the Technique has an explicit area-critical property.

### Defense Checks

Hazards and effects without an attacking actor use a target-rolled Defense Check:

`DefenseCheck = d20 + (ApplicableDefense - 10)`

`DefenseMargin = DefenseCheck - HazardDC`

Outcome degrees use the universal margin table. An effect uses either an Attack roll or a Defense Check for one resolution stage, not both, unless it explicitly defines a secondary stage such as infection after a successful bite.

### Cover and line of effect

| Cover | Evasion bonus |
|---|---:|
| None | +0 |
| Partial | +2 |
| Major | +5 |
| Total | Direct targeting is impossible |

Cover applies only when it physically interferes with the effect's line. A telepathic assault, curse, gas cloud, or realm effect may use different line-of-effect rules.

Cover has a location, material, durability, dimensions, and damage state. It may be penetrated, bypassed, moved around, or destroyed. Narrative scenery is not indestructible unless its actual properties make it so.

### Concealment

- **Obscured:** visual targeting receives Setback.
- **Unseen but located:** attacks receive Setback; Aim and Called Shots are unavailable.
- **Unlocated:** direct targeting is impossible without detection or an area effect.
- Alternate senses negate only the concealment they can actually perceive through.

Cover and concealment may both apply.

### Called Shots

A Called Shot declares the precise target and intended result before rolling.

| Precision | Adjustment | Successful benefit |
|---|---:|---|
| Focused | +2 Target Number | One predetermined ordinary Rider |
| Precise | +5 Target Number | One predetermined Critical Rider tied to the target location |

Examples include attacking a weapon hand, knee, cybernetic control node, ritual cable, exposed eye, or known divine Anchor.

A Called Shot still applies normal damage when it is an attack. It cannot target an inaccessible or imperceptible location, turn a headshot into an automatic kill, or be used with an undirected area attack. It may stack with cover because they represent different difficulties, and it gains additional Riders normally from Strong or Exceptional success.

### Multiple targets and area attacks

A basic attack targets one actor or object. Additional targets require an area property, multiattack feature, suitable equipment, or a priced Stunt.

For an attacker-rolled area effect:

- roll once;
- compare the same AttackTotal against each target's individual Defense;
- determine the outcome separately for each target;
- apply target-specific cover, conditions, resistance, and positioning; and
- resolve friendly fire unless the effect has a selective property.

For a hazard-style area effect, each target makes the declared Defense Check against one recorded DC. No unnamed bystander receives plot immunity. Every actor and object within the committed area is evaluated under the same rules or an approved cohort abstraction.

### Core maneuver rule

A maneuver uses the normal Attack formula, declares its governing Attribute, skill, target Defense, scale requirements, and base result before rolling, and normally deals no damage unless an effect, Outcome Rider, or Technique says otherwise.

#### Grapple

Grapple requires Reach, a valid gripping method, and sufficient scale or leverage:

`GrappleCheck = d20 + StrengthOrAgilityModifier + CloseCombatMastery + ApplicableModifiers`

The target chooses Evasion or Fortitude before the roll.

On Success, the target becomes Grappled, its Speed becomes zero except through escape or forced movement, and the grappler commits at least one usable limb, appendage, field, or equivalent method. The grappler may drag the target at half Speed when scale permits.

Strong success may also restrain one limb or reposition the target. Exceptional success may immediately apply the Pin benefit as a Rider. The grappler may release the target freely.

#### Escape

A Grappled target spends a Major Action and rolls Strength + Athletics or Agility + Mobility against the grappler's Fortitude. Success ends Grappled. Strong success may also reposition the escapee or create a Setup Tag.

#### Pin

Pin requires an existing Grapple. Make another Grapple Check against Fortitude. Success applies Restrained while the Grapple continues. The grappler gives up its Move and the committed gripping method while maintaining the Pin.

#### Shove or Trip

Roll Strength or Agility + Close Combat or Athletics. The target chooses Evasion or Fortitude.

Success either pushes the target `1 + TierRank` spaces or applies Prone. Strong success applies both. Exceptional success may double the movement and apply Prone.

#### Disarm

Attack an exposed held item against Evasion with a +2 Called Shot adjustment. Success drops the item within one space. Strong success lets the attacker choose its landing space or catch it with an available hand. Relics and integrated equipment may require a vulnerability, severance condition, or higher-scale effect.

#### Feint or Distract

Roll an appropriate Deception, Performance, Close Combat, or Warfare check against Vigilance. Success creates one Setup Tag. Strong success grants Edge to the next direct exploitation. Exceptional success allows that Edge to be transferred to an ally.

The method may be tactically brilliant, socially humiliating, sexually inappropriate, completely idiotic, or any combination of them, provided it can actually distract the target.

#### Overrun

Roll Strength + Athletics against Fortitude to move through a hostile occupied position.

- Failure ends movement adjacent to the target.
- Success permits passage and displaces the target one space.
- Strong success permits passage without spending extra movement.
- Catastrophic failure leaves the attacker Prone in the nearest legal space.

Scale and anatomy remain binding.

### Free-form combat adjudication

Any unlisted attack or maneuver becomes a Stunt. Before rolling, the engine declares the intended result, Attribute and skill, target Defense, Target Number, range and line of effect, action and resource cost, failure consequence, base effect, available Outcome and Critical Riders, collateral, and evidence risks.

The AI cannot decide afterward that an accepted absurd action was merely flavor. When accepted as mechanically possible, it receives a real roll, real mechanical effect, and real consequences.

### Attack event and comic record

Every attack and maneuver records intent, targeting, formula inputs, Defense source, cover, concealment, called-shot state, roll, outcome degree, Riders, movement, costs, affected areas, bystanders, damage-stage inputs, visible contact, environmental damage, and continuity changes. A miss must have a coherent visual cause; a hit must preserve its actual trajectory, contact point, and aftermath.

---

## 17. Combat: Damage, Resources, Scale, Wounds, and Recovery

### Derived resource maximums

Let `L` be Character Level, `T` be Tier Rank, `G_V`, `G_C`, and `G_R` be the approved Growth Weights, and `SourceMod` be the Attribute modifier selected to govern Reserve.

`VitalityBase = max(1, 10 + 2 * EnduranceMod + L * (2 + G_V) + 4 * (T - 1) + VitalityPermanentBonus)`

`ComposureBase = max(1, 10 + 2 * WillpowerMod + L * (2 + G_C) + 4 * (T - 1) + ComposurePermanentBonus)`

`ReserveBase = max(1, 5 + 2 * SourceMod + L * G_R + 2 * (T - 1) + ReservePermanentBonus)`

The Reserve-governing Attribute is selected with the Source and cannot be changed opportunistically to whichever Attribute is currently highest.

### Wound reductions to maximums

Each Wound identifies the resource it impairs:

`WoundPenaltyFraction = min(0.50, 0.10 * SeriousWounds + 0.25 * CriticalWounds)`

`EffectiveResourceMaximum = max(1, floor(BaseMaximum * (1 - WoundPenaltyFraction)))`

Current resources are immediately capped when their effective maximum falls.

### Baseline calibration

The following benchmark assumes Growth Weight 3, an appropriate Attribute modifier, equal-rank attacks, and no armor, barrier, resistance, Potency Rider, healing, or defensive intervention:

| Level | Tier | Resource maximum | Average raw damage | Clean hits to zero |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 1 | 19 | 9 | 2.1 |
| 5 | 2 | 45 | 17 | 2.6 |
| 9 | 3 | 71 | 25 | 2.8 |
| 13 | 4 | 95 | 32 | 3.0 |
| 17 | 5 | 121 | 40 | 3.0 |
| 20 tank, Weight 5 | 5 | 176 | 40 | 4.4 |

This establishes fast, dangerous baseline combat while armor, resistance, barriers, recovery, defensive play, and high Vitality investment materially extend survival.

### Effect Rank and damage dice

Effect Rank `E` ranges from 0 through 5 and ordinarily cannot exceed Tier Rank:

`DamageDiceCount(E) = max(1, 2E)`

| Effect Rank | Damage dice | Average before modifiers |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 1d6 | 3.5 |
| 1 | 2d6 | 7 |
| 2 | 4d6 | 14 |
| 3 | 6d6 | 21 |
| 4 | 8d6 | 28 |
| 5 | 10d6 | 35 |

`FlatDamageModifier = clamp(-5, 5, SumOfApplicableFlatModifiers)`

`RawDamage = max(0, SumOfDamageDice + DamageAttributeModifier + FlatDamageModifier)`

A mundane punch or light weapon is normally Rank 0. Heavy weapons, powers, relics, transformations, and Techniques establish higher ranks.

A Potency Rider increases effective Effect Rank by one up to the attack's authorized ceiling:

`EffectiveRank = min(AuthorizedCeiling, BaseRank + PotencySteps + OverreachSteps)`

An unused Potency increase at the ceiling does not become free damage; the player selects another Rider.

### Baseline Reserve cost

For Source-powered Techniques:

`BaseReserveCost(E) = ceil[E(E + 1) / 4]`

| Rank | Base Reserve cost |
|---:|---:|
| 0 | 0 |
| 1 | 1 |
| 2 | 2 |
| 3 | 3 |
| 4 | 5 |
| 5 | 8 |

Area, duration, range, multi-targeting, transformation, and special modifiers may adjust this cost under the power-construction rules. A Source Technique with a positive cost cannot be reduced below one unless explicitly free. Mundane attacks normally cost no Reserve.

### Power Scale

Scale measures force, durability, and causal reach rather than accuracy or automatic area:

| Scale | Benchmark |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Mundane: humans, handheld objects, and ordinary doors |
| 1 | Enhanced: vehicles, heavy walls, and small structures |
| 2 | Superhuman: armored vehicles and buildings |
| 3 | Colossal: major structures and city blocks |
| 4 | Mythic: districts, mountains, and realm foundations |
| 5 | Ascendant: cities, regions, and planetary or divine Anchors |

A Scale-5 needle may remain a single-target attack. Area and collateral are separate properties.

An attack declares Attack Scale. A target declares Durability Scale separately for relevant damage families. Permanent Scale normally cannot exceed Tier Rank without an explicit exception.

### Scale Leverage and multiplier

Known vulnerabilities, Names, Anchors, specialized relics, realm laws, preparation, and team combinations may grant `ScaleLeverage` from 0 through 3:

`EffectiveAttackScale = AttackScale + ScaleLeverage`

`ScaleDelta = EffectiveAttackScale - DurabilityScale`

| Scale Delta | Multiplier |
|---:|---:|
| -3 or lower | 0 without an explicit breach or severance method |
| -2 | 0.25 |
| -1 | 0.50 |
| 0 | 1.00 |
| +1 | 1.50 |
| +2 | 2.00 |
| +3 or higher | 3.00 |

For nonnegative values:

`RoundHalfUp(x) = floor(x + 0.5)`

`ScaledDamage = RoundHalfUp(RawDamage * ScaleMultiplier)`

The Scale gate prevents an ordinary weapon from eventually wearing down an invulnerable god while permitting lower-tier characters to matter through discovered leverage.

### Fixed damage pipeline

Every successful damaging effect resolves in this order:

1. determine Effective Effect Rank;
2. roll Raw Damage;
3. process an applicable Barrier;
4. apply Scale against the exposed target or protection layer;
5. apply Armor after Penetration;
6. apply Resistance, Vulnerability, or conditional Immunity;
7. apply Final Damage to the declared resource or structure;
8. calculate Trauma and resolve a Wound Check;
9. resolve zero-resource defeat; and
10. commit physical, mechanical, world, and comic state.

The AI cannot rearrange the order to protect or punish a target.

### Barriers

A Barrier records current and maximum Barrier, Effect Rank, Scale, covered directions, targets and area, damage-family interactions, Armor, Resistance, vulnerabilities, duration, recharge conditions, and visual form.

Standard Barrier strength is:

`StandardBarrierMaximum = max(1, 5 * E + 2 * T + KeyAttributeModifier)`

A Barrier processes Raw Damage using its own Scale, Armor, and Resistance:

`OverflowFraction = max(0, BarrierFinalDamage - BarrierCurrentBefore) / max(1, BarrierFinalDamage)`

`RawDamageAfterBarrier = RoundHalfUp(RawDamage * OverflowFraction)`

The Barrier loses damage up to its remaining value. Only proportional Raw Damage overflow continues to the protected target, where Scale and mitigation are recalculated. A bypassed, directionally irrelevant, or internally generated effect ignores the Barrier only when its tags support doing so.

### Armor and Penetration

`EffectiveArmor = max(0, ArmorRating - Penetration)`

`PostArmorDamage = max(0, ScaledDamage - EffectiveArmor)`

Armor applies only to compatible damage families and covered locations. Armor Rating, Scale, coverage, degradation, and Penetration values are assigned under the equipment rules. A Called Shot bypasses armor only by targeting a genuinely exposed location.

### Resistance, Vulnerability, and Immunity

`NetResistanceSteps = clamp(-2, 2, VulnerabilitySteps - ResistanceSteps)`

| Net steps | Damage factor |
|---:|---:|
| -2 | x0.25 |
| -1 | x0.50 |
| 0 | x1.00 |
| +1 | x1.50 |
| +2 | x2.00 |

When PostArmorDamage is positive and no valid Immunity applies:

`FinalDamage = max(1, RoundHalfUp(PostArmorDamage * DamageFactor))`

Otherwise, `FinalDamage = 0`.

Immunities must name their family, tags, exceptions, and bypass conditions. They cannot expand from immunity to mundane fire into immunity to all Energy. Vulnerability supplies Scale Leverage only when its record explicitly says so; its damage multiplier does not automatically provide both benefits.

### Damage tracks and families

- Physical and Energy damage normally reduce Vitality.
- Mental damage normally reduces Composure.
- Spiritual damage may reduce Composure or another declared soul-related track.
- Ontological damage targets the declared resource, Anchor, Name, soul component, or reality structure.
- Drain effects may reduce Reserve.
- An effect cannot apply full damage to several tracks unless its purchased construction explicitly permits it.
- Ordinary persuasion still cannot inflict Composure damage.

### Trauma and Wound Checks

After Final Damage, set `ZeroCrossing = 1` if the relevant resource changed from above zero to zero; otherwise set it to zero.

If `FinalDamage = 0`, then `TraumaScore = 0`.

Otherwise:

`TraumaBase = floor(4 * FinalDamage / RelevantMaximum)`

`TraumaScore = clamp(0, 4, TraumaBase + CriticalPressure + ZeroCrossing)`

No Wound Check occurs at Trauma 0.

Use Fortitude for Physical and Energy Trauma, Resolve for Mental and Spiritual Trauma, and the Technique-declared Defense for Ontological Trauma:

`WoundDC = 10 + 2 * TierRank + 3 * TraumaScore`

`WoundCheck = d20 + (TraumaDefense - 10)`

| Wound Check outcome | Severity shift |
|---|---:|
| Catastrophic failure | +1 |
| Failure | 0 |
| Success | -1 |
| Strong success | -2 |
| Exceptional success | -3 |

`FinalWoundIndex = clamp(0, 4, TraumaScore + SeverityShift)`

| Index | Result |
|---:|---|
| 0 | No Wound |
| 1 | Minor Wound |
| 2 | Serious Wound |
| 3 | Critical Wound |
| 4 | Mortal Wound |

Active Defense may modify a Wound Check when the target still has its Reaction and can fictionally Brace, Center, Focus, or otherwise resist the Trauma.

### Wound consequences and consolidation

| Wound | Mechanical consequence |
|---|---|
| Minor | -1 on checks directly involving the injured function |
| Serious | Setback on directly related actions and ten-percent reduction to the declared resource maximum |
| Critical | Setback, one recorded action or functional restriction, twenty-five-percent reduction to the declared resource maximum, and deterioration risk |
| Mortal | Begin Dying, destruction, severance, or equivalent existential failure |

Two Wounds of the same severity affecting the same functional location or soul component combine into one Wound one step higher. Different injuries remain distinct. Physical, Mental, Spiritual, Divine, and Ontological Wounds identify their actual manifestation; a Critical Wound cannot remain an abstract debuff.

### Zero-resource states

- At zero Vitality, the character ordinarily becomes Downed or Incapacitated and receives the Wound evaluation. Diehard and other explicit features may delay this.
- At zero Composure, the character enters one context-valid crisis state declared by the effect, such as Panic, Dissociation, Compulsion Risk, Psychic Collapse, or Possession Risk. This does not grant unrestricted control of player agency.
- At zero Reserve, the character becomes Depleted but is not automatically unconscious. Source Techniques requiring Reserve are unavailable unless Overdraw is permitted.
- At zero Barrier or structural integrity, the Barrier collapses or the object becomes broken, breached, disabled, or destroyed according to construction and declared intent.

### Intent, defeat, and Overflow Damage

- Lethal intent permits Mortal Wounds but does not guarantee death.
- A valid controlled nonlethal attack caps resulting Wound severity at Critical and normally causes unconsciousness or incapacitation at zero Vitality.
- A Technique incapable of controlled nonlethal use cannot gain that protection through narration alone.
- Catastrophic failure, uncontrolled area effects, collateral, and inherently lethal methods may defeat the nonlethal cap.
- Disabling intent targets a declared function; it does not receive automatic severance.
- Damage beyond the remaining resource is recorded as Overflow Damage and still contributes through the full Trauma calculation.

### Dying and stabilization

A Mortal Wound begins with zero stabilization successes and zero deterioration failures. At the end of the character's turn:

`DyingDC = 10 + 2 * TierRank + 3 * MortalWounds`

`DyingCheck = d20 + (ApplicableDefense - 10)`

| Outcome | Result |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | +2 deterioration failures |
| Failure | +1 deterioration failure |
| Success | +1 stabilization success |
| Strong success | +2 stabilization successes |
| Exceptional success | Immediately Stable |

Three stabilization successes produce Stable. Three deterioration failures produce death, destruction, severance, or the entity's equivalent. Additional qualifying damage while Dying adds one deterioration failure; a critical hit against a Dying target adds two.

A Medicine or appropriate restorative action uses the same DC and progression. A Stable target remains at zero Vitality until restored above zero. Gods and reconstructive beings proceed into their recorded vessel-death, Anchor, Mantle, Name, and reconstitution rules after applicable death.

### Recovery Dice and rests

`RecoveryDiceMaximum = 2 + TierRank`

`RecoveryAmount = max(1, d8 + 2 * TierRank + RelevantAttributeModifier)`

Use Endurance for Vitality, Willpower for Composure, and the Source Attribute for Reserve.

After a ten-minute uninterrupted **Breather**, spend up to `TierRank` Recovery Dice and assign each result to Vitality, Composure, or Reserve.

During structured time, **Catch Breath** requires a Major Action and one Recovery Die:

`CombatRecovery = max(1, floor(RecoveryAmount / 2))`

A valid eight-hour **Full Rest** with adequate safety and necessities restores all Recovery Dice and restores Vitality, Composure, and Reserve to their effective maximums. It does not automatically remove Serious, Critical, Mortal, soul, Divine, or Ontological Wounds, and world clocks continue to advance. Survival conditions may reduce rest quality.

### Healing and Wound recovery

Vitality or Composure healing does not automatically heal Wounds. A restorative Technique uses:

`RawHealing = max(0, DamageDiceForRank + KeyAttributeModifier + FlatHealingModifier)`

Healing is limited by compatible Scale, anatomy, soul structure, Source, and the target's missing resource.

| Wound | Required Progress | Treatment DC | Minimum restorative Rank for rapid supernatural repair |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Minor | 3 | 15 | 1 |
| Serious | 6 | 20 | 2 |
| Critical | 10 | 25 | 4 |
| Mortal | 15 | 30 | 5 plus explicit reconstruction permission |

Treatment uses the extended-task Progress results: Failure gives zero; Success gives +1; Strong success gives +2; Exceptional success gives +3; and Catastrophic failure gives -1 plus a complication.

Minor Wounds may gain one Progress from a successful Full Rest. Serious and Critical Wounds require appropriate care, magic, technology, divine intervention, or compatible natural recovery. Mortal Wounds must first be stabilized.

A restorative Technique may contribute once to each Wound per scene unless it explicitly permits repeated reconstruction. Restoring body does not automatically restore memory, Self, Name, Shadow, Mantle, Anchor, or another soul component.

### Damage reproducibility and comic continuity

Every damage event records rolled dice and Effect Rank, Reserve and Overdraw costs, Attack and Durability Scale, Scale Leverage and multiplier, Barrier absorption and overflow, Armor and Penetration, Resistance, vulnerability and Immunity, Final Damage, Trauma, Wound DC and roll, final severity, defeat, treatment, recovery state, impact location, blood or energy, costume damage, environmental destruction, and persistent visual change.

---

## 18. Combat: Conditions, Hazards, Cohorts, Encounter Calibration, and Stunts

### Condition record

Every condition is a structured state object with:

- source and source actor, object, hazard, or realm law;
- target and affected function;
- condition family and tags;
- current Severity and maximum Severity;
- Effect Rank and Scale when applicable;
- exact mechanical effects;
- application timing and recovery timing;
- recovery Defense, check formula, and DC;
- maximum duration or causal remedy;
- escalation, stacking, and refresh rules;
- Immunity, Resistance, suppression, and bypass interactions; and
- visible, audible, behavioral, costume, environmental, and comic-continuity state.

Condition Severity uses three steps:

| Severity | Name | General weight |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Minor | Narrow impediment, brief opening, or one restricted function |
| 2 | Major | Material action loss, broad Setback, movement denial, or escalating harm |
| 3 | Severe | Near-incapacitation, hard control with explicit limits, or an immediate path to defeat |

For a condition imposed by a character, creature, hazard, or other ranked source:

`ConditionDC = 10 + 2 * SourceTierRank + EffectRank + DCModifier`

`DCModifier = clamp(-3, 3, SumOfExplicitConditionDCModifiers)`

Hazards without a Tier Rank use the hazard's specified Resolution DC. A condition must name the Defense or skill used to resist it. The source cannot switch that Defense after seeing the target's statistics.

Unless a condition says otherwise, its recovery check occurs at the end of the affected target's turn after ongoing damage. Apply the result as follows:

| Recovery outcome | Severity change |
|---|---:|
| Catastrophic failure | +1 only when the source explicitly permits escalation; otherwise 0 |
| Failure | 0 |
| Success | -1 |
| Strong success | -2 |
| Exceptional success | End the condition |

For results other than an Exceptional success:

`NewSeverity = clamp(0, SourceMaximumSeverity, CurrentSeverity + SeverityChange)`

Severity zero ends the condition. An escalating condition cannot exceed its recorded maximum Severity.

Some conditions instead have a fixed duration and no recovery check. A persistent causal condition ends only through its recorded remedy, such as extinguishing a fire, removing a restraint, curing a poison, breaking a divine command, restoring oxygen, or severing possession. A source must declare fixed or persistent duration before resolution; it cannot withhold recovery afterward because the result is inconvenient.

### Condition stacking, refreshing, and suppression

- A second application from the same source and condition family uses the greater Severity and refreshes duration; it does not add numeric modifiers unless the effect has the **Compounding** tag.
- Different sources imposing the same ordinary numeric penalty do not stack. Use the strongest value and track every source for removal.
- A condition with the **Stacking** tag may combine two applications of equal Severity into one application one step higher, up to its recorded maximum Severity.
- Edge and Setback remain bounded by their normal cancellation rules regardless of how many conditions supply them.
- A suppressed condition remains in the state ledger with its source, Severity, and remaining duration, but its mechanics pause. Suppression does not cure it unless the suppressing effect explicitly says so.
- Immunity prevents only condition families and tags it actually names. Resistance modifies an application or recovery check only as written.
- When several conditions remove the same action, the action is still removed only once; ending one condition does not restore it while another valid source remains.

### Core condition catalog

| Condition | Default mechanical state |
|---|---|
| **Exposed** | `-2` to one named Defense against the next qualifying effect, then ends. |
| **Off-Balance** | Setback on the next related physical check, then ends. |
| **Prone** | Standing costs half the character's Move. Close attacks against the target gain `+2`; the target's attacks receive Setback unless its form or Technique is suited to fighting Prone. |
| **Slowed** | Speed is halved, rounding down to a minimum of one space when movement remains possible. |
| **Immobilized** | Speed becomes zero. This does not itself prevent non-movement actions. |
| **Grappled** | The target is held under the approved Grapple rules; its movement is controlled by the grappler's valid movement. |
| **Restrained** | Speed becomes zero, Evasion is reduced by `2`, and physical attacks receive Setback. |
| **Dazed** | The target loses its next Quick Action. Multiple applications do not remove additional actions unless explicitly Compounding. |
| **Stunned** | The target has no Reaction or Quick Action and chooses either one Major Action or one Move on its turn. |
| **Blinded** | Unassisted visual tasks are impossible. Visually aimed attacks receive Setback, and attacks against the target's Evasion gain Edge when the attacker can perceive it by another valid means. |
| **Deafened** | Unassisted auditory tasks fail. Effects requiring the target to hear do not affect it unless they have another valid channel. |
| **Silenced** | Vocal powers, spoken command words, and other actions requiring intelligible speech are unavailable. |
| **Frightened** | Checks directly opposing the recorded source receive Setback. Voluntarily approaching it requires a Resolve check, but the player may always choose another legal action. |
| **Burning** | The target suffers the recorded Fire ongoing damage before its end-turn recovery or extinguish attempt. |
| **Poisoned** | Relevant Endurance checks receive Setback and the target follows the source's Affliction Stage record. |
| **Downed** | The target is Prone, has Speed zero, and cannot take ordinary actions. Explicit last-stand or recovery features may still function. |
| **Unconscious** | The target cannot act or perceive normally and has Evasion `5`. |
| **Stable** | The target makes no Dying checks but remains at zero Vitality until restored above zero. |
| **Compelled** | One explicit supernatural instruction or prohibition is recorded. It never grants unrestricted control or permission to invent additional commands. |
| **Possessed** | The possessing entity and host use a recorded contest, permissions, action access, and expulsion condition. Host agency remains explicit at every decision point. |
| **Concentrating** | The character is sustaining one recorded effect and is subject to Concentration checks. |

Fear, coercion, compulsion, possession, charm, madness, and other mental conditions do not authorize an AI to take general control of a player character. Each effect must expose its exact instruction, prohibited choices, duration, resistance method, and remaining legal alternatives.

### Ongoing damage

For an ordinary ongoing-damage condition with Effect Rank `E`:

`OngoingDiceCount = max(1, E)`

`OngoingRawDamage = SumOf(OngoingDiceCount d6)`

Ongoing damage resolves at the end of the affected character's turn before its recovery check and uses the fixed damage pipeline. It adds no Attribute modifier and cannot critically hit or generate Momentum unless the source explicitly purchases those exceptions. For the same source and damage tag, only the strongest ongoing-damage instance applies; separate causally distinct sources remain separate.

### Concentration

A character can ordinarily Concentrate on one effect. Beginning a second Concentration effect ends the first unless a feature explicitly expands that limit.

Whenever a Concentrating character takes Final Damage or suffers a listed disruption:

`ConcentrationDC = 10 + SustainedEffectRank + floor(FinalDamage / 10)`

`ConcentrationCheck = d20 + WillpowerMod + PowerControlMasteryBonus + SituationalModifiers`

Failure ends the effect after the triggering damage resolves. Catastrophic failure may also apply a source-defined backlash. Success maintains it; Strong or Exceptional success may protect it from one lesser disruption only when a feature says so. A sustained effect may additionally require a Quick Action or Reserve upkeep at the start of each turn; failure or refusal to pay ends it without a check.

### Hazard record and calibration

Every hazard records Hazard Rating `H`, source, location or zone, detection method, trigger, interval, Resolution Defense or skill, damage or condition, Effect Rank, Scale, duration, spread or escalation, avoidance, mitigation, deactivation, state changes, and comic evidence.

Hazard Rating ordinarily ranges from 1 through 20:

`HazardDetectionDC = 5 + H + ConcealmentModifier`

`HazardResolutionDC = 10 + H`

`HazardEffectRank = clamp(0, 5, ceil(H / 4))`

Unless the fiction or hazard profile establishes another value:

`HazardScale = HazardEffectRank`

Concealment Modifier normally ranges from `-3` for obvious to `+5` for deliberately or supernaturally hidden. Detection and resolution are separate checks. Failing to notice a hazard does not erase a valid defensive check unless the hazard explicitly creates surprise or unavoidable contact.

Hazards do not silently rise to match Character Level. Their Rating changes only through recorded world events, construction, maintenance, neglect, escalation, or realm law. Discovery, survival, neutralization, exploitation, and escape use the approved hazard XP formula based on `U(H)` and cannot all pay full duplicate XP for the same solved challenge.

### Environmental structures and destruction

Objects and structures use Size Units:

| Size | Size Units |
|---|---:|
| Tiny | 1 |
| Small | 2 |
| Medium | 4 |
| Large | 8 |
| Huge | 16 |
| Colossal | 32 or more |

For Durability Scale `D`:

`StructureMaximum = RoundHalfUp[5 * SizeUnits * (1 + 0.5 * D)]`

The structure also records Armor, Resistance, vulnerabilities, supports, occupied spaces, load-bearing relationships, and failure consequences. Damage uses the fixed damage pipeline against the relevant material or component.

`StructurePercentRemaining = 100 * CurrentStructure / StructureMaximum`

| Remaining structure | State |
|---:|---|
| More than 75% | Intact |
| 51%–75% | Damaged |
| 26%–50% | Compromised |
| 1%–25% | Critical |
| 0% | Destroyed |

At each state change the engine resolves recorded support, access, cover, collapse, utility, casualty, evidence, and terrain consequences. A declared load-bearing strike may change which consequence occurs but still requires sufficient Scale, damage, access, and any Called Shot. There are no plot walls: an apparently indestructible location must be supported by actual Scale, Armor, Barrier, Immunity, realm law, regeneration, or inaccessible causality.

### Falling and collisions

For an uncontrolled fall of `FallSpaces`:

`BaseFallDice = min(20, ceil(FallSpaces / 2))`

`FallDC = 10 + min(20, BaseFallDice)`

The target may use an appropriate Agility, Athletics, Acrobatics, flight, anchoring, or power-control method when fictionally available.

| Fall check outcome | Dice multiplier |
|---|---:|
| Catastrophic failure | 1.25 |
| Failure | 1.00 |
| Success | 0.75 |
| Strong success | 0.50 |
| Exceptional success | 0.25 |

`AppliedFallDice = ceil(BaseFallDice * FallOutcomeMultiplier)`

Roll `AppliedFallDice d6` as Physical damage at Scale 0 unless the fall, gravity field, velocity, destination, or realm law establishes a higher Scale. A valid arresting method may prevent the fall before this calculation. Armor, Resistance, Wounds, and structural impact remain applicable.

For a collision caused by forced movement:

`CollisionDice = min(10, ceil(ForcedMovementSpaces / 2))`

When `ForcedMovementSpaces` is positive, roll at least one collision die. Collision damage uses the Scale of the force and no Attribute modifier unless the source says otherwise. The moved target and the struck creature, object, or surface each process the collision through their own mitigation. A yielding surface, successful catch, deliberate redirection, or appropriate movement Technique may alter one side only when resolved before impact.

### Fire

Fire has Rank 0 through 5, fuel, area, duration, spread conditions, smoke, and damage-family tags.

| Fire Rank | Baseline |
|---:|---|
