| 0 | Candle, ember, or brief contact |
| 1 | Room or structure fire |
| 2 | Industrial, fuel-fed, or infernally intense fire |
| 3–5 | Supernatural, mass, realm, or ascendant fire as specifically defined |

Initial exposure resolves as a normal damaging hazard at the fire's Effect Rank. A target that catches Burning suffers:

`BurningDamage = max(1, FireRank)d6`

Extinguishing an ordinary Burning condition requires a Major Action and an appropriate method:

`ExtinguishDC = 10 + 2 * FireRank`

An assisted or overwhelming method may grant Edge or automatic success when its capacity is sufficient. Each active fire maintains a four-segment Spread Clock. At its recorded spread interval, advance the clock by one, plus one for abundant accelerant and minus one for effective containment, clamped from zero through four. At four, the fire expands to the next recorded zone, consumes appropriate fuel, resets the clock to zero, and may increase Rank only if the new fuel or supernatural source justifies it.

### Suffocation

After losing access to breathable air:

`SafeBreathRounds = max(1, 5 + EnduranceMod)`

Once those rounds expire, make an Endurance check at the end of every turn:

`SuffocationDC = 15 + 2 * PreviousSuffocationChecks`

A Success or better avoids a new mark; a Failure adds one Suffocation Mark; a Catastrophic failure adds two. Apply the highest reached state:

| Marks | Effect |
|---:|---|
| 0 | No additional effect |
| 1 | Lose 10% of maximum Vitality, rounded up |
| 2 | Lose another 20% of maximum Vitality, rounded up |
| 3 | Vitality becomes zero and the target is Downed |
| 4 or more | Suffer a Mortal Wound |

The percentage losses are damage but do not repeat when the character remains at the same mark total. One uninterrupted minute with safe breathable air clears all Suffocation Marks but does not restore lost Vitality or heal Wounds. Creatures with different respiratory needs use their recorded equivalent.

### Afflictions

Poisons, diseases, curses, radiation syndromes, nanite infections, mythic contaminations, and similar effects use Affliction Stages 0 through 3. At each recorded interval:

`AfflictionDC = 10 + HazardRating`

| Affliction check outcome | Stage change |
|---|---:|
| Catastrophic failure | +2 |
| Failure | +1 |
| Success | -1 |
| Strong or Exceptional success | Set Stage to 0 |

`NewAfflictionStage = clamp(0, 3, CurrentAfflictionStage + StageChange)`

Strong or Exceptional success overrides the formula and sets the Stage to zero unless the affliction is persistent and requires its named cure.

| Stage | Baseline effect |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Dormant, resisted, or recovered |
| 1 | Minor condition or narrow penalty |
| 2 | Major condition and possible ongoing damage |
| 3 | Severe condition, Wound risk, transformation, or terminal escalation as declared |

An affliction must record incubation, vector, immunity, treatment, recurrence, contagion, and its exact effect at every Stage. It cannot reveal an unrecorded terminal effect after infection.

### Environmental exposure

Heat, cold, radiation, divine pressure, toxic atmosphere, realm instability, and similar persistent environments record Intensity, Protection, exposure interval, and consequences.

`NetExposure = max(0, Intensity - Protection)`

At each interval, add Net Exposure to the relevant Exposure Load.

| Exposure Load | State |
|---:|---|
| 0–2 | No condition |
| 3–5 | Minor Fatigue |
| 6–8 | Major Exhaustion |
| 9 or more | Severe Exhaustion and a Trauma 2 Wound Check |

Each full rest in a compatible safe environment removes three Exposure Load. Specialized treatment, shelter, adaptation, or cleansing may remove more as specified. Returning to danger resumes from the remaining Load; narration cannot reset it.

### NPC Morale

NPCs and autonomous groups make Morale checks when a relevant trigger occurs, including leader defeat, objective loss, overwhelming horror or Scale, blocked escape, isolation, or casualties reaching 25%, 50%, or 75%. Do not repeat a check for the same unchanged trigger.

`MoralePressure = clamp(0, 15, TierGap + CasualtyPressure + LeaderPressure + ObjectivePressure + HorrorPressure + IsolationPressure - Support)`

| Component | Value |
|---|---:|
| Hostile Tier gap | `+2` per Tier, maximum `+6` |
| Casualties | `+2` at 25%, `+4` at 50%, `+6` at 75% |
| Leader defeated or fled | `+3` |
| Objective lost or revealed impossible | `+3` |
| Isolated or escape blocked | `+2` |
| Horror | `+1` through `+5` according to the source |
| Leadership, fortification, fanaticism, or credible support | `-1` through `-5` |

`MoraleDC = 10 + MoralePressure`

`MoraleCheck = d20 + (ResolveDefense - 10) + LeadershipSupport`

| Outcome | NPC response boundary |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | Rout, panic, surrender, or equivalent collapse |
| Failure | Retreat, surrender, bargain, hide, or hunker according to Drives and access |
| Success | Hold current intent |
| Strong success | Hold and gain 1 Momentum, subject to the normal maximum |
| Exceptional success | Rally and end one qualifying Minor fear condition |

The autonomous NPC agent selects among legal results using recorded Drives, loyalties, knowledge, command structure, escape routes, and self-preservation. It cannot choose the result that preserves a planned plot. Player characters never make ordinary Morale checks; fear and coercion use explicit condition rules and preserve player choice.

### Minions and cohorts

A cohort is `N` materially similar units acting as one initiative entity. It records unit profile, current count, formation, occupied area, morale, attacks, movement, Scale, and losses.

`MassBonus = min(5, floor(log2(max(1, N))))`

The cohort adds Mass Bonus to its attack roll and adds `2 * MassBonus` to Raw Damage. Mass does not increase Effect Rank or Scale and cannot breach a target that the units could not causally harm.

For a minion of Tier Rank `T` and Armor Rating `A`:

`MinionThreshold = 3 + 2 * T + A`

A successful attack must deal at least Minion Threshold Final Damage to defeat units through damage. A result below the threshold may still create valid movement, conditions, morale pressure, cover loss, or setup.

| Targeting profile | Maximum affected | Base Units |
|---|---:|---:|
| Single target | 1 | 1 |
| Cleave | 2 | 1 |
| Burst | 4 | 2 |
| Large area | 8 | 4 |
| Massive area | 16 or more as recorded | 8 |

| Attack outcome | Outcome Steps |
|---|---:|
| Success | 1 |
| Strong success | 2 |
| Exceptional success | 3 |

`UnitsDefeated = min(N, MaximumAffected, BaseUnits * OutcomeSteps + CriticalBonus)`

`CriticalBonus = 1` on a critical hit and zero otherwise.

Intent determines whether defeated units are dead, unconscious, disabled, routed, trapped, transformed, or otherwise removed. Record actual unit disposition, witnesses, bodies, evidence, morale, collateral, and identity exposure. XP uses the actual qualified units and encounter contribution; a cohort presentation does not create duplicate rewards.

### Encounter difficulty calibration

Difficulty calibration is a planning and testing instrument, not an invisible balancing force. Let the approved challenge value be:

`U(R) = 10 + 25R(R + 1)`

For each participating player character:

| Readiness state | Readiness multiplier |
|---|---:|
| Fresh | 1.00 |
| Taxed | 0.75 |
| Depleted | 0.50 |
| Crippled | 0.25 |

`PartyCapacity = SumOf[U(CharacterLevel) * ReadinessMultiplier]`

For each hostile or opposing threat:

| Threat role | Role multiplier |
|---|---:|
| Minion | 0.25 |
| Standard | 1.00 |
| Elite | 2.00 |
| Boss | 4.00 |

`EncounterThreat = SituationMultiplier * SumOf[U(ThreatRating) * RoleMultiplier]`

Situation Multiplier ordinarily ranges from `0.75` for a materially favorable situation through `1.50` for severe hostile terrain, surprise, split objectives, protected artillery, or equivalent pressure. Count each factor once; do not bury an unbounded adjustment in this value.

`DifficultyRatio = EncounterThreat / max(1, PartyCapacity)`

| Difficulty Ratio | Planning label |
|---:|---|
| 0–0.50 | Easy |
| 0.51–0.80 | Favorable |
| 0.81–1.20 | Even |
| 1.21–1.60 | Hard |
| 1.61–2.20 | Deadly |
| Greater than 2.20 | Overwhelming |

The label predicts pressure; it does not alter rolls, statistics, reinforcements, treasure, or NPC decisions after play begins. Boss phases, hidden forces, escape constraints, and active hazards must be included before the encounter or added only through a recorded world event.

### Universal Stunt budget

A Stunt is any declared action whose result is not fully supplied by an existing action, item, Talent, Technique, or environmental rule. Absurdity, profanity, comedy, genre impropriety, and unconventional props never create an inherent penalty. Causal complexity, reach, magnitude, precision, duration, speed, access, Scale, and consequences determine difficulty.

Before the roll, choose the governing Attribute and Mastery Rank and calculate:

`BaseStuntCapacity = 2 + TierRank + floor(MasteryRank / 2)`

`TotalStuntCapacity = BaseStuntCapacity + SetupCredits + CostCredits`

Setup Credits range from zero through two and require useful prior positioning, research, created Setup Tags, environmental preparation, or coordinated assistance. Cost Credits range from zero through three and require consequences paid regardless of success, such as Reserve, damaged gear, lost position, a valid clock advance, exposed identity, collateral, a consumed asset, or a Wound risk. Trivial, fabricated, already-paid, or irrelevant costs grant nothing.

Calculate Stunt Cost from every included component:

| Stunt component | Cost |
|---|---:|
| Primary effect | `1 + EffectRank` |
| Far range | `+1` |
| Distant range | `+2` |
| Extreme range | `+3` |
| Each additional discrete target | `+1` |
| Near area | `+2` |
| Far area | `+4` |
| Minor condition | `+1` |
| Major condition | `+2` |
| Severe condition | `+4` |
| Standard movement or forced movement | `+1` |
| Double standard movement | `+2` |
| Duration through the next turn | `+1` |
| Duration through the scene | `+2` |
| Persistent duration | `+4` |
| Ignore partial cover | `+1` |
| Bypass a compatible armor or Barrier layer | `+2` |
| Compress into a Quick Action or Reaction | `+2` |
| Compress into a Free Action | `+3` |
| Selective area excluding chosen targets | `+1` |
| Conceal collateral | `+1` |
| Conceal identity or authorship | `+1` |

Area cost replaces per-target cost for targets inside the declared area unless a target receives a separate additional effect. A Stunt cannot buy a component whose Source, access, or causal method the actor does not possess.

`StuntCost = SumOf(AllIncludedComponentCosts)`

`Overreach = max(0, StuntCost - TotalStuntCapacity)`

| Overreach | Resolution |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Resolve at the normal declared DC. |
| 1 | Add `+5` to the DC and apply Setback. |
| 2 | Add `+10` to the DC, apply Setback, and commit one Serious consequence that occurs regardless of success. |
| 3 or more | Impossible as one action without new leverage, preparation, access, a purchased ability, or a reduced effect. |

Stunt construction never bypasses Tier ceilings, Scale gates, Source permissions, action access, anatomy, line of effect, or established causality. A natural 20 cannot make an impossible Stunt possible.

The complete calculation, effect, target, cost, roll, failure consequence, collateral, and comic evidence are exposed before commitment. After the roll, neither player nor AI may downgrade a declared cost or consequence.

### Improvised and absurd weapons

An ordinary improvised weapon defaults to Effect Rank 0 and Scale 0, uses the closest appropriate combat skill, and normally imposes Setback when its handling materially differs from a proper weapon. It may break on a natural 1 or Catastrophic failure when its material and use justify breakage. Its shape, reach, availability, legality, contamination, witnesses, and comic silhouette remain real.

**Anything Is a Weapon** removes only the penalty caused by being improvised. It does not grant damage, Scale, durability, proficiency, range, supernatural access, or immunity to consequences.

A rubber novelty object, household appliance, sacred office chair, severed statue arm, or any similarly absurd implement can succeed when the character supplies the required skill, power, Scale, leverage, and Stunt capacity. Humor is neither a ban nor a free upgrade. An absurd object may become a signature item or Relic through the approved equipment and progression systems; it does not become equivalent to Mjolnir merely because the moment is funny.

### Condition, hazard, cohort, and Stunt event record

Every qualifying event records source, target, formulas, Severity, Effect Rank, Scale, DC, checks, outcome degree, duration, recovery, stacking, suppression, ongoing damage, hazard Rating, exposure or spread clocks, cohort count, morale inputs, Stunt Capacity and Cost, Overreach, paid costs, environmental state changes, casualties, legal and identity consequences, sensory evidence, dialogue consequences, and resulting panel-worthy beats.

---

## 19. Equipment Foundation, Inventory, Ownership, Availability, and Economy

### Equipment-system sequence

The equipment system is completed in four linked passes:

1. item records, inventory, Quality, durability, ownership, availability, and economy;
2. weapons, armor, shields, ammunition, consumables, and improvised weapons;
3. crafting, repairs, modifications, mythotechnology, and salvage; and
4. Relic attunement, Names, growth, corruption, destruction, theft, and succession.

This section establishes the approved foundation. Later equipment passes must use these identities, calculations, and state transitions rather than replacing them with isolated item rules.

### Universal item record

Every physical item instance records:

- unique instance ID and versioned template ID;
- name, category, origin, manufacturer, culture, and provenance;
- Item Rank and each Effect Rank;
- operational Scale and Durability Scale;
- Quality Grade;
- current and maximum Integrity;
- Size Units, Bulk, required hands, and Ready Slot cost;
- damage families, properties, modules, and faults;
- ammunition, charges, fuel, Reserve use, and consumables;
- training, authorization, attunement, and activation requirements;
- Availability Rating, legality, Cost Band, exact Base Value, and currency context;
- legal owner, current possessor, container, and exact location;
- modifications, maintenance, and repair history;
- blood, residue, fingerprints, divine signatures, and other evidence; and
- canonical appearance, wear, damage, transformation, and comic silhouette.

Possession, legal ownership, authorization, attunement, and mechanical entitlement are separate states. None implies the others.

### Item Rank

| Rank | Classification |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Ordinary |
| 1 | Enhanced |
| 2 | Superhuman |
| 3 | Iconic |
| 4 | Mythic |
| 5 | Ascendant |

`ItemRank = max(HighestEffectRank, HighestControlledScale, HighestModuleRank)`

Item Rank does not automatically increase accuracy, damage, Quality, Availability, or price. Each function must identify its own Effect Rank, Scale, action, resource, training, and activation requirements.

Fixed-output equipment operates at its built-in rating when its genuine activation requirements are satisfied. A stolen missile, reactor, bomb, or fixed defense does not weaken merely because its operator has a lower Character Level.

Variable, shaped, or user-channelled equipment is normally limited by the operator's Tier Rank. Attempting greater controlled output requires an explicit overclock, attunement permission, or Stunt. Possessing a powerful object never implies understanding or controlling it.

### Quality Grade

Quality Grade `Q` is an integer from `-2` through `+2`:

| Grade | Quality | Integrity multiplier | Value multiplier |
|---:|---|---:|---:|
| -2 | Crude or defective | 0.50 | 0.50 |
| -1 | Low-grade | 0.75 | 0.75 |
| 0 | Standard | 1.00 | 1.00 |
| +1 | Superior | 1.25 | 1.50 |
| +2 | Masterwork | 1.50 | 2.50 |

Positive Quality grants one approved Quality Property per positive step. Negative Quality requires one genuine Fault per negative step. Quality never grants an undocumented universal attack, Defense, Effect Rank, or Scale bonus.

Quality describes construction. Current item condition is tracked separately through Integrity.

### Item Integrity

Using the approved Size Units and Durability Scale `D`:

`ItemIntegrityMaximum = max(1, RoundHalfUp[5 * SizeUnits * (1 + 0.5 * D) * QualityIntegrityMultiplier])`

Items use the approved structural thresholds:

| Integrity remaining | Item state |
|---:|---|
| More than 75% | Intact |
| 51%–75% | Damaged |
| 26%–50% | Compromised |
| 1%–25% | Critical |
| 0% | Broken |

- **Intact:** functions according to its profile.
- **Damaged:** remains functional and gains persistent visible damage; no universal numeric penalty applies.
- **Compromised:** item-dependent checks receive Setback and one exposed module or property is disabled and recorded.
- **Critical:** only recorded basic functions remain, item-dependent checks receive Setback, and every consequential use costs one additional Integrity after resolution.
- **Broken:** the item is unusable until repaired, rebuilt, or repurposed.

An item loses Integrity only through a resolved attack, hazard, overclock, committed cost, malfunction, or canonical event. Ordinary use does not create arbitrary degradation. When an item is directly attacked, it processes damage through its own Scale, Armor, Resistance, vulnerabilities, and Integrity.

### Bulk

Bulk measures weight, awkwardness, packing, and accessibility rather than mass alone:

| Bulk | Example profile |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Negligible token or tiny component |
| 1 | Compact item or small weapon |
| 2 | Light carried kit |
| 3 | Substantial or two-handed item |
| 4 | Cumbersome equipment |
| 5 | Heavy equipment |
| 6 or more | Oversized, team-carried, or cargo |

`CarryCapacity = max(4, 8 + StrengthMod + CarryBonus)`

`BurdenLimit = floor(1.5 * CarryCapacity)`

`HardCarryLimit = 2 * CarryCapacity`

| Total Bulk | State |
|---:|---|
| At or below Carry Capacity | Normal |
| Carry Capacity + 1 through Burden Limit | Burdened: Speed `-2`; Setback on related Agility and travel-Endurance checks |
| Above Burden Limit through Hard Carry Limit | Overloaded: half Speed; no ordinary sprint; Setback on physical checks |
| Above Hard Carry Limit | Cannot be carried as inventory; must be lifted, dragged, transported, or moved through Scale |

Bulk does not determine whether super-strength can lift a vehicle or building component. Those objects use Strength, Scale, Size, leverage, and Stunt rules. Carry Bonus represents a power, form, vehicle, container, or equipment feature designed to expand a practical loadout.

Zero-Bulk objects cannot create infinite inventory. Ten ordinary units of one negligible item type occupy one Bulk unless their profile specifies another stack size. Containers may change access and organization but do not erase Bulk unless an explicit powered property says so.

### Ready loadout and access state

`ReadySlotMaximum = 4 + LoadoutBonus`

`ReadySlotCost = max(1, ceil(ItemBulk / 2))`

Held and immediately readied items consume Ready Slots. Passive worn or integrated equipment does not, although detachable weapons, ammunition, consumables, and activated modules may.

| Access state | Retrieval |
|---|---|
| Held | Already available |
| Worn | Passive, or activated according to its profile |
| Readied | Quick Action |
| Packed | Major Action |
| Cached | Requires reaching the recorded cache |
| Absent | Unavailable |

Quick Draw retains its approved once-per-round free retrieval. Dropping one held item is Free; stowing or exchanging equipment normally costs a Quick Action.

The engine locks the character's actual loadout and item locations when an encounter begins. Neither player nor AI may retroactively declare that a useful item was packed. An environmental object may still be used when it genuinely exists in the committed scene state.

### Equipment entitlement

Physical custody and character investment remain distinct:

- **Ordinary equipment:** bought, found, stolen, or crafted. Loss is genuine.
- **Asset-backed equipment:** supplied by a specific organization, facility, cache, or relationship. It remains subject to inventory, authorization, replacement time, and obligations.
- **Signature Equipment:** Build Points support an enduring capability and progression path. Losing the physical item disables it but does not erase the spent Build Points.
- **Relic:** a unique world entity with provenance, a Name or potential Name, possible will, and its own continuity. It cannot be generically respawned.
- **Integrated equipment:** physically attached to a body, suit, vehicle, or form. It resists ordinary disarming but can still be disabled or damaged through a valid method.

Selling, gifting, abandoning, or deliberately destroying Asset-backed or Signature Equipment does not generate a free replacement loop. Its entitlement becomes **Broken** until the character recovers it, pays the legitimate replacement costs, or completes an appropriate rebuild or reassignment. A replacement cannot be issued while the previous instance remains usable or deliberately liquidated without resolution.

### Exact money and Standard Value Units

The economy uses exact balances rather than an abstract Wealth roll. Every market retains its actual local currency and exchange rate. Rules calculations normalize monetary value into **Standard Value Units**, an indexed accounting measure rather than a universal in-world currency.

`NormalizedValue = RoundHalfUp(LocalCurrencyAmount * RecordedExchangeRate)`

Nonfungible payment—including favors, oaths, memories, souls, divine service, political concessions, unique barter, or access—cannot be silently converted into money. Its exact terms, parties, obligations, and completion state are recorded separately.

Cost Band `C` ranges from 0 through 15:

`CostBandBenchmark(0) = 1`

For `C >= 1`:

`CostBandBenchmark(C) = 10 * 5^(C - 1)`

| Cost Band | Benchmark Value |
|---:|---:|
| 0 | 1 |
| 1 | 10 |
| 2 | 50 |
| 3 | 250 |
| 4 | 1,250 |
| 5 | 6,250 |
| 6 | 31,250 |
| 7 | 156,250 |
| 8 | 781,250 |
| 9 | 3,906,250 |
| 10 | 19,531,250 |
| 11 | 97,656,250 |
| 12 | 488,281,250 |

The formula continues through Cost Band 15. An item template receives an exact integer Base Value; Cost Band is its procedural and comparison bracket. Rank, Scale, fame, and price remain separate variables.

### Market value and transaction price

Condition Value multipliers are:

| State | Multiplier |
|---|---:|
| Intact | 1.00 |
| Damaged | 0.80 |
| Compromised | 0.50 |
| Critical | 0.20 |
| Broken or parts-only | 0.05 |

Markets record exact current stock and target stock:

`StockRatio = CurrentStock / max(1, TargetStock)`

| Stock Ratio | Market multiplier |
|---:|---:|
| `0` | Unavailable without commissioning or a new source |
| More than 0 through 0.25 | 2.50 |
| More than 0.25 through 0.50 | 1.50 |
| More than 0.50 through 1.25 | 1.00 |
| More than 1.25 through 2.00 | 0.75 |
| Greater than 2.00 | 0.50 |

Black-market Risk multipliers are:

| Legal state | Risk multiplier |
|---|---:|
| Open or properly authorized | 1.00 |
| Unlicensed | 1.10 |
| Restricted | 1.50 |
| Prohibited | 2.50 |
| Veil-Accord or existentially controlled | 4.00 |

`PriceAdjustment = clamp(-0.25, 0.50, SumOfExplicitNegotiationAndRelationshipAdjustments)`

`CurrentMarketValue = max(1, RoundHalfUp[BaseValue * QualityValueMultiplier * ConditionValueMultiplier * MarketMultiplier * RiskMultiplier])`

`TransactionPrice = max(1, RoundHalfUp[CurrentMarketValue * (1 + PriceAdjustment)])`

A price does not create availability. Some objects have calculable value but no willing seller, lawful market, fungible price, or transferable ownership.

Vendor offers use:

`SalePrice = floor(CurrentMarketValue * ResaleRate)`

| Buyer | Resale Rate |
|---|---:|
| Qualified direct buyer | 0.60 |
| Specialist dealer | 0.40 |
| General dealer or pawn market | 0.25 |
| Fence for stolen or prohibited goods | 0.15 |

Vendors have finite stock and funds. Buying reduces stock; selling increases stock and consumes vendor funds. Restocking follows recorded world clocks. This prevents infinite buy-sell loops, instant merchant liquidity, and markets that ignore player activity.

### Availability and acquisition

Availability Rating `A` ranges from 0 through 10. Ratings 9 and 10 represent access to a unique lead or singular object rather than ordinary shelf stock.

| Legal access | DC adjustment |
|---|---:|
| Open or properly licensed | 0 |
| Required license missing | +2 |
| Restricted | +5 |
| Prohibited | +8 |
| Veil-Accord or existentially controlled | +12 |

Access Reduction is zero without special access, three for a valid Access Tag, or five for direct supplier or owner authorization. Market Pressure ranges from `-3` through `+5`.

`AcquisitionDC = clamp(5, 35, 10 + AvailabilityRating + LegalPenalty + MarketPressure - AccessReduction)`

A suitable approach may use Investigation, Influence, Streetwise, Technology, Craft, Scholarship, or another justified skill. A Contact may grant Edge but cannot manufacture inventory, money, authority, or knowledge beyond its recorded scope.

| Availability Rating | Search interval |
|---:|---|
| 0–1 | Immediate |
| 2 | 10 minutes |
| 3–4 | 1 hour |
| 5–6 | 4 hours |
| 7 | 1 day |
| 8 | 3 days |
| 9 | 1 week and a concrete lead |
| 10 | A campaign-scale lead, owner, site, or creation path |

| Outcome | Result |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | No source; consume the interval and trigger a relevant scam, Exposure, Heat, betrayal, or market complication |
| Failure | No source; consume the interval |
| Success | Locate one genuine transaction at the calculated price |
| Strong success | Success plus one acquisition benefit |
| Exceptional success | Success plus two acquisition benefits |

Available benefits are a ten-percent price reduction, half search or delivery time, cleaner provenance, reduced Evidence, or one useful fact about the item. Total price reduction remains subject to the `-25%` Price Adjustment cap.

Money is spent only after the player accepts a discovered transaction unless a deposit, bribe, auction stake, or scam risk was declared before the check. Repeating the same failed search against unchanged market state produces no new roll; a new lead, seller, market interval, approach, or material state change is required.

Theft, robbery, coercion, counterfeiting, and battlefield looting use their actual action systems. An Acquisition check cannot sanitize those choices or convert possession into ownership.

### Lifestyle without bookkeeping abuse

Lifestyle Rating `L_s` ranges from 0 through 5:

`DailyLifestyleCost(0) = 0`

For `L_s >= 1`:

`DailyLifestyleCost(L_s) = 10 * 5^(L_s - 1)`

`IncidentalLimit = floor(DailyLifestyleCost / 5)`

Lifestyle may cover routine food, local transport, lodging, hygiene, communication, and nonpersistent purchases at or below the Incidental Limit. It cannot produce permanent equipment, stockpiled consumables, resale inventory, bribes, restricted goods, crafting inputs, mission-critical resources, or proof of ownership.

Campaign premise establishes starting Lifestyle. The default is Lifestyle 1 with:

`StartingLiquidFunds = 30 * DailyLifestyleCost`

Background Assets may support a different standard of living within their recorded scope but are not unrestricted cash. A character unable or unwilling to pay its current Lifestyle cost receives the actual housing, hunger, debt, travel, relationship, and access consequences rather than an invisible punishment or automatic character failure.

### Item and economy continuity

Every item and transaction event records current holder and hand, exact location, access state, ammunition, charge, activation, Integrity, disabled modules, blood, residue, repairs, modifications, scale relative to the wielder, canonical colors and markings, legal ownership, theft status, local and normalized price, seller, buyer, payment source, stock change, evidence, dialogue names, and replacement state.

A weapon cannot change hands, ammunition cannot refill, armor cannot repair itself, money cannot appear, and a comedy prop cannot change appearance between panels without a recorded event. The comic pipeline renders the item state that existed at the event's committed timestamp.

---

## 20. Combat Equipment: Weapons, Armor, Ammunition, Consumables, and Improvised Arms

### Weapon resolution

Every weapon attack uses the approved attack and damage pipelines:

`WeaponAttackTotal = d20 + AttackAttributeMod + MasteryBonus + SpecialtyBonus + AttackModifier`

`WeaponRawDamage = DamageDice(EffectRank) + DamageAttributeMod + WeaponFlatDamage + OtherFlatModifiers`

The combined Flat Damage Modifier remains capped from `-5` through `+5` under the core damage rule.

Damage Attribute is determined by the weapon profile:

- **Muscle:** Strength, normally for melee and thrown weapons.
- **Finesse:** Strength or Agility, chosen when the attack is declared.
- **Fixed:** zero Attribute damage, normally for firearms and explosives.
- **Source:** the recorded power or equipment Source Attribute.
- **Mounted:** the vehicle or emplacement profile.

Weapons establish what an attack can do; the character's Attribute, Mastery, Specialty, modifiers, and d20 determine execution. Weapons do not grant automatic hits. Accuracy modifiers enter the normal capped Attack Modifier, while Edge and Setback follow their existing rules.

### Weapon profile

Every weapon records:

- Effect Rank and authorized ceiling;
- Attack Scale;
- damage family and tags;
- Attack Attribute and Damage Attribute mode;
- Flat Damage;
- Penetration;
- Optimal and Maximum Range;
- Reach;
- required hands and Strength;
- Bulk and Ready Slot cost;
- ammunition, capacity, and reload method;
- firing or attack modes;
- properties and limitations;
- sound, flash, residue, recoil, and collateral;
- nonlethal capability; and
- Integrity and malfunction state.

`FinalPenetration = clamp(0, 2 + 2 * EffectiveEffectRank, WeaponPenetration + AmmunitionPenetration + OtherPenetration)`

Penetration reduces only compatible Armor. It never increases Scale, bypasses a Barrier automatically, reduces Resistance, or converts unused Penetration into damage.

### Weapon range

Weapons record an Optimal Range and Maximum Range using the approved range bands:

- within Optimal Range, no range penalty applies;
- one band beyond Optimal but within Maximum Range receives Setback;
- beyond Maximum Range is impossible without a suitable Technique, targeting system, or Stunt;
- ranged attacks while Engaged retain Setback unless the weapon has **Close-Quarters**; and
- Aim remains a Quick Action granting `+1` to the next ranged attack against the selected target before the turn ends.

Thrown weapons use:

`ThrownMaximumSpaces = max(2, 4 + 2 * StrengthMod + ThrowRangeBonus)`

Scale-enhanced throwing may replace this limit only through an explicit Technique or feature.

### Core melee benchmarks

| Weapon | Rank | Scale | Flat | Penetration | Reach | Hands and Bulk | Properties |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| Unarmed | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | Natural | Muscle, Controlled |
| Knife | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 1 | 1 hand, Bulk 1 | Finesse, Concealable, Thrown, Lethal |
| Baton or club | 0 | 0 | +1 | 0 | 1 | 1 hand, Bulk 2 | Muscle, Controlled |
| Sword or axe | 0 | 0 | +2 | 1 | 1 | 1 hand, Bulk 2 | Muscle or Finesse, Lethal |
| Spear or polearm | 0 | 0 | +1 | 1 | 2 | 2 hands, Bulk 3 | Muscle, Reach, Brace |
| Chain or whip | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 2 | 1 hand, Bulk 2 | Finesse, Flexible, Entangling |
| Heavy melee weapon | 1 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 1–2 | 2 hands, Bulk 4 | Muscle, Heavy, Lethal |
| Powered melee weapon | 1 or higher | 1 or higher | Profile | Profile | Profile | Profile | Source or Fixed |

A weapon's physical method selects its genuine damage family. A sword cannot freely become Blunt while using its cutting edge; striking with its pommel uses an alternate Rank-0 profile.

### Core ranged benchmarks

| Weapon | Rank | Scale | Flat | Penetration | Optimal and Maximum | Capacity | Properties |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|---|
| Bow | 0 | 0 | +1 | 1 | Near and Far | 1 | Muscle, Quiet, two-handed |
| Heavy crossbow | 1 | 0 | 0 | 2 | Far and Distant | 1 | Fixed, Brace, Major Reload |
| Holdout pistol | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | Near and Far | 6 | Concealable, Loud |
| Service pistol | 0 | 0 | +1 | 1 | Far and Far | 15 | Loud |
| Heavy pistol | 0 | 0 | +2 | 2 | Far and Far | 6 | Loud, Recoil |
| Submachine gun | 0 | 0 | +1 | 1 | Far and Far | 30 | Burst, Automatic, Loud |
| Shotgun | 1 | 0 | 0 | 1 | Near and Far | 6 | Spread, Loud |
| Assault rifle | 1 | 0 | 0 | 2 | Far and Distant | 30 | Burst, Automatic, Loud |
| Precision rifle | 1 | 0 | +2 | 3 | Distant and Extreme | 5 | Accurate, Brace, Loud |
| Light machine gun | 1 | 0 | +1 | 2 | Far and Distant | 100 | Automatic, Heavy, Brace |
| Anti-materiel rifle | 2 | 1 | 0 | 4 | Distant and Extreme | 5 | Accurate, Heavy, Brace, Recoil |
| Rocket launcher | 2 | 1 | 0 | 2 | Far and Distant | 1 | Blast 3, Heavy, Major Reload |
| Fragmentation grenade | 1 | 0 | 0 | 1 | Thrown | 1 | Blast 2, Lethal, single-use |

Firearms use Fixed damage unless a supernatural or technological feature explicitly lets the operator channel an Attribute into projectile force. These are construction and calibration baselines, not a restriction on fictional weapon varieties.

### Weapon properties

| Property | Mechanical effect |
|---|---|
| **Accurate** | Aim grants `+2` instead of `+1`. |
| **Brace** | Attacking without spending a Move to brace or using firm support causes Setback. |
| **Burst** | Consume three rounds before rolling to gain `+1` Attack Modifier. |
| **Automatic** | Enables Spray and Suppressive Fire. |
| **Close-Quarters** | Cancels the Setback caused solely by firing while Engaged. |
| **Controlled** | Permits ordinary controlled nonlethal intent without changing the weapon profile. |
| **Entangling** | A Success may initiate an appropriate ranged Grapple instead of damage. |
| **Finesse** | Choose Strength or Agility for attack and damage when declaring the attack. |
| **Heavy** | Requires two hands and the recorded Strength Requirement. |
| **Lethal** | Its primary profile cannot use controlled nonlethal intent. |
| **Reach X** | Threatens out to the listed number of spaces. |
| **Recoil** | Firing without sufficient Strength, bracing, or compensation causes Setback. |
| **Spread** | Gain `+1` Attack Modifier at Engaged or Near range; reduce Effect Rank by one at Far range. |
| **Suppressed** | Reduces ordinary acoustic detection but does not make the attack silent or erase muzzle, impact, or forensic evidence. |
| **Thrown** | Uses the thrown-range formula and consumes or relocates the item. |
| **Unwieldy** | Receive Setback after moving more than half Speed unless properly braced. |
| **Versatile** | Switching between its recorded one- and two-handed profiles costs no action. |
| **Blast X** | Affects every valid target within an X-space radius; roll once and compare separately. |
| **Cleave** | Uses the approved two-unit cohort targeting profile when targets are adjacent and within Reach. |

Properties with the same name do not stack unless they contain an explicit numeric rating.

### Lethal and nonlethal attacks

- Controlled weapons may use the existing nonlethal Wound cap.
- A Lethal weapon requires a genuine alternate profile to attack nonlethally.
- An improvised pommel strike, weapon-butt strike, warning shot, or disabling strike normally uses Effect Rank 0 and Penetration 0 and may receive Setback when awkward.
- A projectile strike to a limb remains a lethal attack resolved through a Called Shot; it is not guaranteed safe.
- Tasers, restraint launchers, chemical darts, and similar weapons use their recorded condition or Affliction profile rather than pretending ordinary ballistic damage is nonlethal.
- Player-declared intent remains binding, but physics, Overflow Damage, uncontrolled effects, and catastrophic results remain real.

### Fire modes

Ammunition is committed before the attack roll:

| Mode | Ammunition | Effect |
|---|---:|---|
| Single | 1 | Ordinary weapon attack |
| Burst | 3 | `+1` Attack Modifier against one target |
| Spray | 10 | Attack a Blast-2 area with Setback; no Aim or selective targeting |
| Suppressive Fire | 10 | Establish a six-space suppression zone until the attacker's next turn |

For Suppressive Fire, make one Ranged Combat roll:

`SuppressionDC = clamp(10, 30, SuppressionAttackTotal)`

A creature that enters the zone, leaves cover within it, or takes an exposed consequential action makes one Evasion Defense Check against that DC. Failure suffers weapon damage at one Effect Rank lower, minimum Rank 0. Each creature resolves this at most once per suppression zone.

Total cover prevents exposure. Suppression cannot distinguish allies without an explicit selective system. One recorded natural-20 Critical Rider may affect only the first qualifying target.

### Ammunition and reloads

Every weapon records chamber state, magazine or cell instance, current rounds, capacity, ammunition type, and compatible Rank.

- A shot consumes ammunition even when it misses.
- A detachable magazine or standard energy cell requires a Quick Action to replace.
- An internal magazine reloads its recorded number of rounds with a Quick Action or fills completely with a Major Action.
- Belt-fed, crew-served, or complex systems normally require a Major Action.
- A single-use weapon becomes spent immediately after activation.
- A partial magazine remains a distinct inventory object.
- A character with one free hand and an appropriate pouch may retain a partial magazine during a Quick reload; otherwise it is dropped in the current space.
- An empty weapon cannot attack even when narration fails to mention that it was emptied.

Special ammunition replaces the relevant parts of the standard profile; it does not freely stack every benefit.

| Ammunition | Baseline adjustment |
|---|---|
| Armor-piercing | `+1` Penetration and `-1` Flat Damage |
| Expanding | `+1` Flat Damage against unarmored biological targets; Penetration becomes 0 |
| Incendiary | Adds Fire; may apply Burning through a qualifying Rider |
| Tranquilizer | Replaces ordinary damage with a species-compatible Affliction |
| Blessed, runic, or keyed | Interacts only with its recorded vulnerability, Name, or protection |
| Training or marking | Replaces lethal damage with its explicit training, paint, tracer, or tracking effect |

For ammunition exceeding the weapon's safe Rank:

`Overpressure = max(0, AmmunitionRank - WeaponItemRank)`

Each shot causes:

`OverpressureIntegrityLoss = 2 * Overpressure`

It also adds Overpressure to the weapon's Malfunction Range.

### Malfunctions

Condition Malfunction is zero while Intact or Damaged, one while Compromised, and two while Critical:

`MalfunctionRange = clamp(0, 5, max(0, -QualityGrade) + ConditionMalfunction + AmmunitionFault + Overpressure - ReliabilityReduction)`

If the kept natural attack die is at or below Malfunction Range, resolve the attack normally and then apply the weapon's recorded malfunction:

- jam or stalled mechanism;
- misfire and consumed ammunition;
- overheated or depleted system;
- dropped or displaced component;
- Integrity damage;
- uncontrolled discharge; or
- shutdown requiring repair.

An Intact, Standard-quality weapon with proper ammunition has Malfunction Range zero. A natural 1 still creates the normal Opening but does not automatically make every reliable weapon jam.

Clearing a routine jam is a Quick Action. Severe, damaged, supernatural, software, or integrated failures may require a Major Action, tools, access, or repair.

### Armor construction

Armor has Rank `R` from 0 through 5 and Frame `F` from 0 through 3:

| Frame | Name | Coverage | Base Bulk |
|---:|---|---|---:|
| 0 | Concealed | Selected torso or recorded zones | 1 |
| 1 | Light | Torso and major organs | 2 |
| 2 | Medium | Broad torso and limb coverage | 3 |
| 3 | Heavy | Full coverage when helmet and seals are present | 5 |

`ArmorRating = 1 + 2R + F`

`DefaultArmorScale = R`

`ArmorStrengthRequirement = 8 + 2F + 2R`

| Rank | Concealed | Light | Medium | Heavy |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 |
| 1 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 |
| 2 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 |
| 3 | 7 | 8 | 9 | 10 |
| 4 | 9 | 10 | 11 | 12 |
| 5 | 11 | 12 | 13 | 14 |

Armor must list compatible damage families. Ordinary ballistic armor does not automatically protect against psychic, spiritual, divine, ontological, corrosive, internal, or environmental effects.

For a covered location:

`ProtectedDurabilityScale = max(NaturalDurabilityScale, ArmorScale)`

Only the strongest compatible Armor Rating applies. Multiple ordinary suits do not stack; specialized liners grant a bonus only when their explicit property says so.

An obvious exposed location may be bypassed with a Focused Called Shot. A small or protected gap requires a Precise Called Shot. Fully sealed armor cannot be bypassed without a recorded opening, vulnerability, breach, or internal delivery method.

### Armor mobility

Let Effective Strength include only Strength and support explicitly applicable to wearing the armor:

`StrengthShortfall = max(0, ArmorStrengthRequirement - EffectiveStrength)`

`ArmorPenaltySteps = clamp(0, 3, max(0, Frame - 2 - PoweredMobility) + ceil(StrengthShortfall / 4))`

| Penalty Steps | Effect |
|---:|---|
| 0 | No universal mobility penalty |
| 1 | Speed `-1`; Setback on Stealth |
| 2 | Speed `-2`; Setback on Mobility and Stealth |
| 3 | Half Speed; Setback on physical Agility checks; Dodge unavailable |

Powered Mobility is normally zero or one and cannot exceed the armor profile's rating. Armor training or a Technique may reduce penalties but does not increase protection.

### Armor degradation

After Armor applies:

`ArmorPreventedDamage = max(0, ScaledDamage - PostArmorDamage)`

`ArmorIntegrityLoss = ceil(ArmorPreventedDamage / 2)`

This loss applies only when the damage family interacts materially with the armor. Mental pressure does not scratch a steel vest, while acid, armor-breaking attacks, hostile environmental exposure, and ontological effects follow their recorded interactions.

Integrity loss occurs after the hit is committed but before the armor's new state affects later attacks. A Breach or Disable Rider may inflict additional recorded damage or disable a specific armor property.

### Physical shields

Physical shields use Frame 0 through 2:

| Frame | Shield | Size Units | Bulk |
|---:|---|---:|---:|
| 0 | Buckler or compact shield | 2 | 1 |
| 1 | Standard shield | 4 | 3 |
| 2 | Tower or heavy shield | 8 | 5 |

`ShieldArmorRating = 1 + 2 * ShieldRank + ShieldFrame`

`ShieldStrengthRequirement = 8 + 2 * ShieldFrame + 2 * ShieldRank`

While readied and directionally relevant, a shield grants `+1` Evasion within the normal Defense Adjustment cap.

As a Quick Action, **Raise Shield** to gain Partial Cover against attacks from one declared direction until the next turn begins.

As a Reaction after a hit but before damage, **Block** places the shield into the Barrier step of the damage pipeline. The shield uses its own current Integrity, Scale, Armor, Resistance, and vulnerabilities. Proportional overflow follows the existing Barrier rule.

Block cannot stop an effect the shield cannot physically intercept. The same Reaction cannot also be spent on Active Defense.

Energy shields and projected force fields use the standard Barrier rules rather than receiving both physical-shield and Barrier benefits.

### Consumables

Every consumable records exact quantity or charges, Use Action, delivery method, Effect Rank, Scale, DC, duration, Dose Rating, shelf stability, and whether it is consumed before or after successful delivery.

Unless explicitly recoverable, a consumable is removed when committed.

| Consumable | Baseline effect |
|---|---|
| Field dressing | One use; grants Edge on the next immediate stabilization or bleeding-control check |
| Trauma kit | Five charges; supplies proper tools and `+2` on compatible emergency Medicine checks |
| Rank-1 med-injector | Two doses; heals `2d6 + IntellectMod`; once per target per scene |
| Antitoxin | Grants Edge on the next compatible Affliction check and may reduce Stage by one on Strong success |
| Smoke grenade | Blast 2; creates Obscured terrain for three rounds, subject to wind and ventilation |
| Flashbang | Blast 2; Vigilance or Fortitude Defense Check against its Condition DC; applies Dazed, with Blinded as an Outcome upgrade |
| Incendiary grenade | Rank-1 Energy damage, Blast 1, Fire Rank 1, possible Burning |
| Breaching charge | Rank-1, Scale-1 damage against an attached structure; Blast 1 and Loud |
| Tracking tag | Creates a detectable signal from its committed attachment point |
| Restraint foam | Applies a ranged Grapple or Immobilized condition at its recorded Rank |

Mundane medical supplies do not resurrect, erase Wounds, or repair soul components.

### Dose Load

Chemical and biological consumables record Dose Rating from one through three:

`DoseCapacity = max(1, 2 + EnduranceMod + DoseToleranceBonus)`

`NewDoseLoad = CurrentDoseLoad + DoseRating`

When Dose Load exceeds Dose Capacity:

`OverdoseDC = 10 + HighestActiveDoseRating + 2 * (DoseLoad - DoseCapacity)`

Make a Fortitude Defense Check. Failure applies Poisoned or advances the relevant Affliction by one Stage; Catastrophic failure advances it by two. Success prevents escalation but does not remove Dose Load.

Each consumable records its clearance interval. The default is one Full Rest. Treatment may reduce Load when causally appropriate.

### Improvised-weapon profiles

For an ordinary object not built as a weapon:

`ImprovisedEffectRank = clamp(0, 1, floor((Bulk - 1) / 3))`

`ImprovisedFlatDamage = clamp(-2, 2, Bulk - 2)`

| Object | Rank | Flat Damage | Typical handling |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Negligible or soft prop | 0 | -2 | One hand, Fragile |
| Compact object | 0 | -1 | One hand |
| Handheld solid object | 0 | 0 | One hand |
| Substantial object | 0 | +1 | One or two hands |
| Cumbersome object | 1 | +2 | Two hands, Heavy |
| Oversized object | 1 or Stunt | +2 | Scale, leverage, or team handling required |

`ImprovisedStrengthRequirement = 6 + 2 * Bulk`

A physical form may supply one genuine property such as Blunt, Sharp, Reach, Flexible, Thrown, Entangling, or Concealable. It cannot gain a property contradicted by its actual construction.

Ordinary improvised handling imposes Setback when materially different from a proper weapon. **Anything Is a Weapon** removes only that Setback.

When a wielder channels greater Scale through an improvised object:

`ImprovisedAttackScale = min(ActorSuppliedScale, ItemDurabilityScale + 1)`

`ScaleStress = max(0, ImprovisedAttackScale - ItemDurabilityScale)`

`ImpactIntegrityLoss = 2 * ScaleStress * (EffectiveEffectRank + 1)`

Fragile objects suffer an additional `2 * (EffectiveEffectRank + 1)` Integrity loss on successful contact. A natural 1 or Catastrophic failure may apply the same additional loss when the action stresses the object.

Thus a rubber novelty weapon, traffic cone, frying pan, sacred chair, or severed statue arm can produce a legitimate attack. It still needs sufficient handling, force, Scale, and durability. It can later become Signature Equipment or a Relic through actual progression and history.

### Dual wielding and mounted weapons

Holding two weapons grants access to both profiles but does not create a free additional attack. Multiple attacks require a Technique, feature, or priced Stunt.

A mounted weapon uses its recorded firing controls and may use the operator's Vehicles, Ranged Combat, Warfare, or another designated skill. Its fixed output remains independent of the operator's physical Strength, but recoil, targeting, access, crew, ammunition, firing arc, and collateral remain binding.

### Combat-equipment event record

Every use records the exact item instance, wielder, attack mode, hands, access state, ammunition before and after, Attribute, skill, range, properties, Rank, Scale, damage dice, Penetration, armor coverage, Integrity changes, malfunction inputs, reload state, dropped components, collateral, sound, residue, ownership, witnesses, and resulting comic imagery.

---

## 21. Crafting, Repair, Modification, Salvage, Production, and Mythotechnology

### Project types

The same project engine supports:

- **Repair:** restore Integrity and disabled native functions.
- **Fabricate:** build a known item from a Blueprint.
- **Modify:** install, remove, or replace modules.
- **Invent:** develop a genuinely new Blueprint.
- **Reverse-engineer:** learn reproducible functions from an existing object.
- **Salvage:** recover materials, modules, information, and evidence.
- **Improvise:** create a temporary solution with limited tools and time.
- **Manufacture:** produce validated items in batches.

Crafting produces real world objects. It does not grant an undocumented personal power, bypass Build Point advancement, or make equipment immune to theft and destruction.

### Project record

Every project records:

- project ID, owner, workers, and assistants;
- project type and intended output;
- Item Rank `R`, Complexity `C`, target Quality `Q`, and Size Units;
- Blueprint and Blueprint Fidelity;
- Attribute, skill, Specialty, and Source requirements;
- required facility, tools, materials, catalysts, permissions, and access;
- DC, required Progress, interval duration, and current Progress;
- reserved, installed, wasted, and recoverable materials;
- power, fuel, contamination, Realm Pressure, and waste;
- declared failure states and project hazards;
- intellectual-property, legal, faction, and moral consequences;
- item appearance and every visible construction change; and
- formula, tuning version, rolls, seed, and committed state.

The AI declares every known requirement before work begins and cannot add a missing ingredient, instability, curse, or defect after seeing a successful roll. A genuinely hidden contaminant or sabotaged component must already exist in committed world state and remain discoverable through appropriate evidence.

### Complexity

| Complexity `C` | Classification |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Trivial assembly or routine service |
| 1 | Simple mechanical or artisanal work |
| 2 | Professional multi-component construction |
| 3 | Complex advanced system |
| 4 | Exotic superscience or mythic engineering |
| 5 | Singular, cross-domain, or civilization-scale engineering |

Complexity measures integration difficulty rather than power. A mechanically simple divine blade may have high Rank but moderate Complexity; a mundane supercomputer may have low Rank but high Complexity.

### Materials

Every material lot records:

- material family and quantity;
- Material Rank and Quality;
- Bulk and normalized value;
- physical, scientific, mythic, divine, soul, or cosmic tags;
- source, owner, provenance, contamination, and evidence; and
- compatible uses and forbidden substitutions.

A monetary value does not make a required catalyst interchangeable. A drachma from Hades, a sample of Nidhogg venom, and commercial steel can have equal market value without being mechanically substitutable.

Substitution is classified before the roll:

| Substitute | Effect |
|---|---|
| Exact or approved equivalent | No adjustment |
| Compatible substitute | `+2` DC |
| Poor but causally functional substitute | `+5` DC and one declared Fault |
| Incompatible or missing essential component | Project is impossible |

### Facility requirements

Facility Rating ranges from 0 through 5:

| Rating | Facility |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Field tools or portable kit |
| 1 | Professional workshop |
| 2 | Advanced laboratory or fabrication shop |
| 3 | Superscience foundry or containment facility |
| 4 | Mythic forge, divine laboratory, or realm-capable installation |
| 5 | Ascendant, primordial, or civilization-scale works |

`RequiredFacilityRating = max(ItemRank, Complexity - 1, SourceFacilityRequirement)`

`FacilityGap = FacilityRating - RequiredFacilityRating`

- Gap zero or higher permits normal work.
- Gap `-1` permits work only when the method allows it; apply Setback, double interval duration, and cap target Quality at zero.
- Gap `-2` or lower is impossible without a different facility or specifically purchased portable capability.

A facility must also support the item's Size and Source. A Rank-5 microscope cannot manufacture a city-sized engine merely because its numeric Rating is sufficient.

### Fabrication calculation

Let:

`SizeStep = max(0, ceil(log2(SizeUnits)) - 2)`

Novelty Modifier is:

| Design state | Modifier |
|---|---:|
| Valid complete Blueprint | 0 |
| Approved adaptation | +2 |
| Prototype design | +5 |
| Paradigm-crossing experiment | +10 |

`FabricationDC = 10 + 2R + 2C + 2 * max(0, Q) + NoveltyModifier + SubstitutionModifier`

`FabricationProgressRequired = 3 + 2R + 2C + 2 * max(0, Q) + SizeStep`

Facility speed is:

`FacilityTimeDivisor = 1 + min(2, max(0, FacilityGap))`

`SizeTimeFactor = max(1, ceil(SizeUnits / 4))`

`CraftIntervalHours = [0.5 * 2^C * SizeTimeFactor / FacilityTimeDivisor]`

The existing extended-task outcomes apply:

- Catastrophic failure gives `-1` Progress plus a declared consequence.
- Failure gives no Progress; time and committed costs remain spent.
- Success gives `+1` Progress.
- Strong success gives `+2` Progress.
- Exceptional success gives `+3` Progress.

Progress cannot fall below zero. Work remains interruptible, and the project preserves its exact partially completed state.

### Fabrication material cost

First calculate the target item's standard-quality-adjusted value:

`TargetStandardValue = BaseValue * QualityValueMultiplier`

Process multipliers are:

| Process | Multiplier |
|---|---:|
| First prototype | 1.50 |
| Custom or ordinary one-off | 1.00 |
| Validated production Blueprint | 0.80 |

`FabricationMaterialRequirement = ceil(TargetStandardValue * 0.50 * ProcessMaterialMultiplier)`

This represents consumed materials rather than labor, licensing, facility rent, energy, shipping, bribes, or unique catalysts.

Required tagged materials are allocated to a Project Reserve. Installed material follows Progress:

`InstalledMaterial = floor(MaterialRequirement * min(CurrentProgress, RequiredProgress) / RequiredProgress)`

A Catastrophic failure wastes:

`CatastropheWaste = max(1, ceil(MaterialRequirement * 0.10))`

If the project is abandoned, uninstalled material remains recoverable. Installed material requires Salvage.

### Repair

Let:

`MissingIntegrity = TargetIntegrity - CurrentIntegrity`

`MissingIntegrityFraction = MissingIntegrity / IntegrityMaximum`

If Missing Integrity is positive:

`DamageSteps = clamp(1, 4, ceil(4 * MissingIntegrityFraction))`

Repair State Modifier is:

| State | Modifier |
|---|---:|
| Damaged | 0 |
| Compromised | +2 |
| Critical | +5 |
| Broken | +8 |

`RepairDC = 10 + 2 * ItemRank + Complexity + RepairStateModifier`

`RepairProgressRequired = 2 * DamageSteps + ItemRank + Complexity`

`RepairMaterialRequirement = ceil(BaseValue * QualityValueMultiplier * 0.50 * MissingIntegrityFraction)`

Partial repair is deterministic:

`RestoredIntegrity = floor(MissingIntegrity * CurrentProgress / RepairProgressRequired)`

`CurrentIntegrityDuringRepair = min(TargetIntegrity, StartingIntegrity + RestoredIntegrity)`

Normal repair restores the item's existing Quality and native functions. It cannot:

- replenish ammunition, charges, or fuel;
- recreate a destroyed unique catalyst;
- restore a severed Name, Self, soul component, or Mantle;
- improve Quality without a separate refit;
- remove evidence or provenance automatically.

A Broken item can be repaired only when its essential chassis, Pattern, or core remains. Otherwise it must be rebuilt through Fabrication.

### Emergency field repairs

A normal field repair requires ten uninterrupted minutes, appropriate tools, and may affect an item only once per scene:

`FieldRepairDC = 15 + 2 * ItemRank + RepairStateModifier`

On Success:

`FieldRepairAmount = max(1, d6 + IntellectMod)`

A field repair cannot raise Integrity above 51% of maximum or restore a complex disabled module. Strong success may restore one simple disabled property. Exceptional success may also remove one ordinary malfunction.

Performing this as a Major Action during combat requires an explicit Talent, Technique, Archetype feature, or suitably advanced repair system.

### Modification slots

`ModuleSlotMaximum = 1 + ItemRank + max(0, QualityGrade) + ChassisSlotBonus`

Native properties do not consume these slots. Added modules do.

| Slot cost | Module scope |
|---:|---|
| 1 | Minor property, `+1` Flat Damage or Penetration, capacity, range, reliability, concealment, sensor, or utility change |
| 2 | Major property, alternate attack mode, powered support, Barrier, area function, transformation, or one-Rank effect upgrade within the chassis ceiling |
| 3 | Scale increase, cross-Source interface, soul or Name interaction, selective mass effect, or defining system |

A module can contain only the effects recorded in its profile. One slot cannot conceal several unrelated upgrades.

`UsedModuleSlots <= ModuleSlotMaximum`

If a module increases Effect Rank, Scale, or another controlling rating, Item Rank is recalculated. A module cannot exceed the chassis by more than one Rank without rebuilding the chassis.

### Modification projects

Let `M` be Module Slot Cost and `X` be Source Mismatch, normally zero through three:

`ModificationDC = 10 + 2 * max(ItemRank, ModuleRank) + 2M + 2X`

`ModificationProgressRequired = 3 + ItemRank + 2M + X`

When fabricating the module during installation:

`ModificationMaterialRequirement = ceil(ModuleBaseValue * 0.75)`

When installing an existing intact module:

`IntegrationMaterialRequirement = ceil(ModuleBaseValue * 0.10)`

Removing a module uses the same procedure with `-2` DC. Success recovers it intact. Failure leaves it installed. Catastrophic failure damages the item or module and applies the declared integration consequence.

Changing a weapon's physical structure does not rewrite its identity silently. Serial marks, silhouette, weight, balance, sound, power signature, and visible attachments update in its comic bible.

### Quality upgrades

Improving Quality is a separate refit rather than an ordinary repair:

`QualityUpgradeDC = 12 + 2 * ItemRank + 2 * TargetQuality + Complexity`

`QualityUpgradeProgress = 4 + 2 * ItemRank + 3 * TargetQuality + SizeStep`

`QualityUpgradeMaterials = ceil[BaseValue * 0.50 * (TargetQualityValueMultiplier - CurrentQualityValueMultiplier)]`

Target Quality cannot exceed the material, Blueprint, facility, or crafter limit. A Quality upgrade grants the appropriate Quality Property only after completion.

### Blueprint Fidelity

Blueprint Fidelity ranges from 0 through 3:

| Fidelity | Capability |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Fragments, observations, or unusable data |
| 1 | Prototype construction only |
| 2 | Reliable one-off construction and repair |
| 3 | Validated production, automation, and batch manufacture |

Blueprints are versioned. Improving a Blueprint never retroactively changes an already-created item.

### Research and invention

Choose target Blueprint Fidelity `F` from 1 through 3:

`ResearchDC = 12 + 2R + 2C + NoveltyModifier + SecurityModifier`

`ResearchProgressRequired = 6 + 2R + 2C + 3F`

`ResearchMaterialRequirement = ceil(FabricationMaterialRequirement * 0.25)`

Research consumes test materials, energy, samples, computation, ritual components, or experimental subjects according to its actual method.

Completing the project creates the chosen Fidelity. A prototype still requires a separate Fabrication project.

Inventing a higher-Rank effect does not waive Tier, Source, facility, Scale, or material requirements. Player creativity determines the method and purpose; the system determines whether that method can produce the declared result.

### Reverse engineering

`ReverseEngineeringDC = 12 + 2 * ItemRank + 2 * Complexity + SecurityModifier + OpacityModifier`

`ReverseEngineeringProgress = 6 + 2 * ItemRank + 2 * Complexity`

Security and Opacity each range from zero through five.

Before work begins, the engine identifies which functions are potentially reproducible. Reverse engineering can learn mechanisms, materials, effects, weaknesses, and interfaces. It cannot automatically reproduce:

- a genuine Self;
- complete Domain Authority;
- a unique Divine Name;
- personal recognition or worship;
- a soul's identity;
- a singular historical relationship;
- Fate itself;
- an unavailable primordial substance.

Such elements may instead become requirements, leads, interfaces, or vulnerabilities.

### Batch manufacture

For `N` identical units using a Fidelity-3 Blueprint:

`BatchProgressRequired = SingleItemProgress + 2 * ceil(log2(max(1, N)))`

`BatchMaterialRequirement = N * UnitProductionMaterialRequirement`

The facility must have sufficient throughput, storage, power, and labor. Each unit receives a unique item ID, or fungible consumables receive a versioned batch ID and exact quantity.

One failed batch roll affects the batch state. It does not secretly create a separate random defect in every item. Any batch-wide Fault or contamination is recorded and discoverable.

### Automated production

Automation may execute only a Fidelity-3 Blueprint:

`AutomationPassiveValue = 10 + 2 * AutomationRating + FacilityQualityModifier`

If Automation Passive Value is below the Fabrication DC, unattended manufacture is unavailable.

Otherwise:

`AutomatedProgressPerInterval = clamp(1, 3, 1 + floor((AutomationPassiveValue - FabricationDC) / 5))`

Automation consumes exact materials, energy, maintenance, and time. It cannot invent, perform undefined substitutions, make moral decisions, or conceal production from the world simulation. Factory output changes supply, law-enforcement attention, faction strategy, and markets.

### Salvage

Condition Recovery Factors are:

| Item state | Factor |
|---|---:|
| Intact | 0.60 |
| Damaged | 0.50 |
| Compromised | 0.40 |
| Critical | 0.25 |
| Broken | 0.15 |
| Destroyed remains | 0.05 |

Salvage Outcome multipliers are:

| Outcome | Multiplier |
|---|---:|
| Catastrophic failure | 0 plus the declared hazard or destruction |
| Failure | 0.50 |
| Success | 1.00 |
| Strong success | 1.25 |
| Exceptional success | 1.50 |

Let:

`StandardMaterialValue = ceil(BaseValue * QualityValueMultiplier * 0.50)`

`SalvageValue = min(floor(0.75 * StandardMaterialValue), floor(StandardMaterialValue * ConditionFactor * OutcomeMultiplier))`

Salvage produces tagged material lots rather than cash. Selling them uses the actual market.

Potentially recoverable modules are identified before the roll:

- Failure recovers no intact module.
- Success recovers one.
- Strong success recovers two.
- Exceptional success recovers three.
- Catastrophic failure destroys or dangerously activates one candidate.

Recovery cannot exceed the modules genuinely present. Unique components, souls, Names, divine residue, dangerous fuel, and forensic evidence retain their identities.

### Mythotechnology construction

Every mythotech device retains the approved seven-part architecture:

1. Source;
2. Conduit;
3. Anchor;
4. Pattern;
5. Fuel;
6. Control System; and
7. Failure State.

It additionally records Control Rating, Containment Rating, Pressure, Pressure Capacity, Source Friction, Realm Conflict, and Operational Instability.

Source Friction is calculated from:

| Interaction | Friction |
|---|---:|
| One internally compatible Source | 0 |
| Each additional compatible Source | +1 |
| Each incompatible Source pair | +3 |
| Unkeyed Mantle, Name, or Domain interface | +3 |
| Outside or Primordial interface | +4 |
| Approved harmonizer | `-1` through `-5` |

Damage Instability is:

| Device state | Instability |
|---|---:|
| Intact | 0 |
| Damaged | +1 |
| Compromised | +3 |
| Critical | +6 |

Realm Conflict ranges from zero through five. Overclock Steps range from zero through five.

`PressureCapacity = 2 + ItemRank + ControlRating`

`ProjectedPressure = CurrentPressure + BaseActivationPressure + OverclockSteps`

`PressureOverflow = max(0, ProjectedPressure - PressureCapacity)`

`OperationalInstability = max(0, SourceFriction + RealmConflict + DamageInstability + OverclockSteps + PressureOverflow - ControlRating - ContainmentRating - max(0, QualityGrade))`

A device with Operational Instability zero functions without a Stability check.

Otherwise:

`StabilityDC = 10 + 2 * ItemRank + OperationalInstability`

`StabilityCheck = d20 + IntellectMod + ChosenRelevantMastery + SpecialtyBonus + SituationalModifiers`

The relevant Mastery is declared as Technology, Craft, Occult, Science, Power Control, or another specifically supported discipline. Only one applies.

| Outcome | Activation |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | Intended function fails; trigger the recorded Severe Failure State |
| Failure | Intended function fails or becomes partial as predefined; trigger the standard Failure State |
| Success | Function operates; commit Projected Pressure plus one |
| Strong success | Function operates; commit Projected Pressure |
| Exceptional success | Function operates; commit Projected Pressure and then reduce Pressure by one |

Every device defines its Failure States before activation. Options include overload, explosion, containment breach, possession opening, Name echo, mutation, memory damage, corruption, realm tear, hostile summoning, or shutdown. The AI cannot choose a worse failure merely because the device targeted an important character.

A compatible Breather removes:

`PressureRecovered = max(1, ControlRating)`

A Full Rest or proper maintenance clears ordinary Pressure unless the device records persistent contamination, damaged containment, or an ongoing realm conflict.

### Mythotechnology boundaries

Mythotechnology can:

- detect, contain, suppress, transport, heal, empower, transform, or attack;
- interact with souls, Names, Domains, Mantles, and realm laws when correctly keyed;
- injure or kill gods through sufficient Scale and genuine leverage;
- create artificial power interfaces and temporary empowerment; and
- become mass-produced if the world acquires the required facilities and materials.

It cannot:

- create a genuine Self from nothing;
- guarantee true resurrection;
- cleanly copy complete Domain Authority;
- perfectly predict Fate;
- ignore realm-entry requirements;
- suppress every possible power universally;
- produce unlimited safe divine energy;
- erase costs, consent, ownership, morality, or canonical consequences.

Nothing prohibits villainous, reckless, grotesque, or unethical construction. Such projects require their real materials and subjects and create corresponding morality, legal, faction, relationship, and world consequences.

### Build Points and constructed equipment

Money, materials, and Craft can create a real item without spending Build Points. If the player can build, steal, or acquire it, the player may use it subject to its actual requirements.

Build Points purchase:

- reliable character-owned Techniques;
- Signature Equipment progression;
- persistent integration with the character's build;
- special replacement or rebuilding rights; and
- abilities unavailable when the physical item is absent.

Build Points do not conjure materials or duplicate sold equipment. Conversely, refusing to spend Build Points does not make a successfully constructed ordinary item imaginary.

### Crafting XP and exploitation

Crafting XP uses the approved activity scopes:

- useful prototype or crafted upgrade uses `S = 0.25`;
- novel invention or permanent research result uses `S = 1`;
- Signature artifact or transformative invention uses `S = 2`; and
- True Relic or civilization-changing technology uses `S = 4`.

XP is granted on qualified completion and demonstrated usefulness rather than per project roll. Routine repairs, identical production runs, deliberate break-repair loops, and manufacturing items solely to dismantle them use the repetition and self-manufactured-exploit rules.

Legitimate professional production and material grinding may remain profitable, but stock, demand, labor, time, facility capacity, risk, and world adaptation remain active.

### Crafting and comic continuity

Crafting records the actual workers, dialogue, workspaces, tools, clothing, grime, injuries, prototypes, failed shapes, sparks, runes, diagrams, recovered components, mounting points, paint, scars, sounds, accidents, and final reveal.

Every modification updates the item's visual bible unless it is explicitly internal or concealed. Crafting montages may compress time for the comic, but they cannot omit the project's decisive failures, costs, discoveries, sacrifices, or visible transformation.

---

## 22. Relics: Legend, Agency, Attunement, Claims, Corruption, Destruction, and Succession

### Relic definition

A Relic is not merely an expensive or powerful item. It is a persistent mythic identity expressed through an object, construct, weapon, garment, tool, vehicle, body part, artificial vessel, or similarly coherent thing.

A true Relic is supported by:

- a **Name**, including any aliases or earned Epithets;
- deeds that establish a persistent legend;
- a channel through which that legend is **Recognized**;
- a coherent Source, Domain, law, or mythic cause;
- at least one **Anchor** preserving its continuity;
- an optional but mechanically explicit will or **Self**; and
- any obligations, purpose, hunger, prohibitions, or prices that belong to it.

Relics may be deliberately forged, inherited, appointed, awakened from divine or Primordial fragments, made from a dead being or soul component, manufactured and later awakened, or generated when an ordinary object accumulates enough deeds and Recognition to become legendary. The original object may be noble, crude, comic, obscene, disposable, or absurd. Mythic status follows what actually happened, not what a curator thinks should have happened.

### Relic identity record

Every Relic has a canonical record containing, where applicable:

- a unique item ID and Relic ID;
- its Vessel and every established form;
- Names, aliases, and Epithets;
- current Grade and accumulated Legend Marks;
- ordinary item statistics and damage state;
- Source, Domains, Expressions, and intrinsic Relic Techniques;
- unspent Relic Points and any separate bearer Build Points invested in Bonded Techniques;
- current custody, legal ownership, Claims, Attunements, Recognition, and Bond Scores;
- Agency Grade, Drives, Taboos, memories, knowledge, lies, and secrets;
- Charge, Fuel, Pressure, Corruption, Hunger, or other optional tracks;
- Anchors, destruction keys, reformation state, and interference;
- succession law and known claimants; and
- voice, appearance, transformations, scars, damage, and visual motifs for comic continuity.

Custody, ownership, Claim, Attunement, Recognition, and Bond are separate facts. Possessing a Relic does not automatically establish any of the others.

### Relic Grades and Legend Marks

| Grade | State | Typical meaning |
|---:|---|---|
| 0 | Signature or proto-Relic | Persistent important object whose legend has not fully awakened |
| 1 | Named | A true Relic with a recognized identity |
| 2 | Awakened | Its supernatural identity and capabilities are established |
| 3 | Iconic | Its legend affects factions, regions, or major institutions |
| 4 | Mythic | Its legend carries realm, world, or civilization-scale weight |
| 5 | Sovereign | It can stand as an independent mythic authority |

A qualified deed awards Legend Marks according to its demonstrated scope:

| Marks | Deed scope |
|---:|---|
| 1 | Personal or encounter-defining |
| 2 | Factional or regional |
| 3 | National, realm-wide, or world-defining |
| 4 | Civilizational or cosmological |

The cumulative Legend Mark threshold for Grade `G` is:

`RelicLegendThreshold(G) = 3G(G + 1) / 2`

| Grade reached | Cumulative Legend Marks |
|---:|---:|
| 1 | 3 |
| 2 | 9 |
| 3 | 18 |
| 4 | 30 |
| 5 | 45 |

Legend requires a persistent Recognition channel such as witnesses, testimony, records, art, rumor, worship, institutional acknowledgment, divine notice, or an enduring supernatural imprint. A unique deed normally awards Marks to a given Relic only once. Repetition, staged danger, captive-farming, self-manufactured crises, and other exploit loops use the approved repetition and anti-exploitation rules.

Comedy, obscenity, villainy, humiliation, failure, and stupidity can all produce genuine legend. They also determine what kind of legend the Relic becomes and how others recognize it.

### Becoming a true Relic

A Grade 0 object becomes Grade 1 only when all of the following are true:

- it has at least 3 Legend Marks;
- it has maintained a continuous, identifiable object history;
- it has a coherent Source or mythic cause;
- it has at least one Anchor;
- its deeds have a continuing Recognition channel; and
- a **Naming Event** establishes its persistent mythic identity.

A nickname alone is insufficient. A Naming Event may be deliberate, accidental, ceremonial, public, private but metaphysically witnessed, triumphant, tragic, or ridiculous. A rubber novelty weapon can become a true Relic if its actual history satisfies the same requirements as any sacred sword.

### Relic Points and Techniques

Upon reaching Grade `G`, the Relic gains:

`RelicPointsGained = 2 + NewGrade`

Its cumulative Relic Points are:

`CumulativeRelicPoints(G) = G(G + 5) / 2`

| Grade | Cumulative Relic Points |
|---:|---:|
| 1 | 3 |
| 2 | 7 |
| 3 | 12 |
| 4 | 18 |
| 5 | 25 |

Relic Points buy intrinsic Relic Techniques, Expressions, upgrades, or similarly native features using the approved power-building costs. These features belong to the Relic and travel with it through theft, sale, inheritance, loss, or succession. Relic Points cannot buy bearer Attributes, unrelated training, or capabilities unsupported by the Relic's Grade, Rank, Source, Domains, and established causality. Intrinsic features may become inaccessible while the Relic is Dormant, Severed, Dispersed, or Unmade.

Bearer Build Points remain separate. They may buy Bonded Techniques representing a character's persistent mastery of, relationship with, or special interface to a particular Relic. Such Techniques become dormant when that Relic is unavailable; they do not duplicate the object or transfer its ownership.

### Normal and accelerated evolution

Normal Relic advancement requires sufficient Legend Marks, qualifying deeds, persistent Recognition, and an appropriate advancement checkpoint. It does not require sacrifice.

Accelerated advancement may use an extraordinary, relevant Price under the approved accelerated Mantle standard. Once the Price is knowingly accepted and actually paid, it guarantees the intended Grade increase. Any associated trial determines stability, mutation, Hunger, new Claim conflicts, damaged Anchors, hostile attention, or similar consequences; it does not revoke the purchased Grade.

No Price can invent a false history, manufacture a Self without a causal basis, or retroactively create public belief that did not occur.

### Agency Grades and autonomous Relics

| Agency Grade | State | Capability |
|---:|---|---|
| 0 | Inert | No independent preference or action |
| 1 | Instinctive | Impulses, affinities, aversions, or reflexive resistance |
| 2 | Communicative | Bounded communication and choices within a narrow identity |
| 3 | Person-like | Persistent personality, goals, relationships, plans, and independent action |
| 4 | Sovereign | Independent mythic actor capable of long-horizon agendas and Authority |

Agency Grades 3 and 4 are eligible for autonomous sub-agent operation. The same truth and agency rules that govern NPC agents apply:

- the Relic reasons only from its actual senses, memories, knowledge, and valid inferences;
- its Drives and Taboos constrain decisions;
- it may lie, refuse, negotiate, withhold, resist, or cooperate;
- it cannot read hidden state or spend bearer resources without permission, except through an explicitly defined coercion or possession mechanic;
- narration cannot compel it or silently transfer control; and
- its dialogue, voice, behavior, and visual identity must remain consistent with its canonical record.

A person-like Relic remains a character even when a jurisdiction treats it as property.

### Bond and Recognition

Each bearer–Relic relationship has a Bond Score from `-10` to `20`.

| Bond Score | Relationship state |
|---:|---|
| -10 to -6 | Hostile |
| -5 to -1 | Rejecting |
| 0 to 2 | Tolerated |
| 3 to 6 | Recognized |
| 7 to 11 | Bonded |
| 12 to 17 | Chosen |
| 18 to 20 | Co-Sovereign |

Typical event changes are:

- `+1` for meaningful alignment, attention, or care;
- `+2` for costly loyalty;
- `+3` for a defining shared deed;
- `-1` for meaningful neglect or violation;
- `-2` for betrayal or exploitation; and
- `-3` for a defining betrayal or attempted destruction.

A single event changes Bond by no more than `3`, and trivial repetition grants no change.

Bond establishes access boundaries:

| State | Default access |
|---|---|
| Hostile | Active resistance, refusal, misinformation, or hostile functions as established |
| Rejecting | Mundane use only, when physically possible |
| Tolerated | Mundane use and explicitly public basic functions |
| Recognized | Intrinsic Relic Techniques up to Rank 1 |
| Bonded | Intrinsic Techniques up to the lower of bearer Power Tier and Relic ceiling |
| Chosen | A recorded champion privilege, overcharge option, or equivalent exceptional access |
| Co-Sovereign | Highest succession priority plus explicitly defined Authority or identity-sharing access |

Bond is not a heroism lock. A Relic may Recognize a tyrant, choose a clown, love a monster, or hate a saint when its history, Drives, and choices support that outcome.

### Attunement procedure

Attunement is a functional supernatural link and remains separate from Bond, ownership, and Claim. Each attempt records the Relic's `Resistance` and the user's `ClaimConflict`, each rated from `0` to `5`.

First calculate projected strain:

`ProjectedAttunementStrain = max(0, ProjectedRelicLoad - AttunementCapacity)`

Then determine the check Difficulty Class, required progress, and base interval:

`AttunementDC = 10 + 2 * RelicGrade + RelicResistance + ClaimConflict + 2 * ProjectedAttunementStrain`

`AttunementProgressRequired = 3 + 2 * RelicGrade + RelicResistance + ClaimConflict`

`AttunementIntervalHours = 2^(max(0, RelicGrade - 1))`

Attunement is an extended task using a pairing justified by the approach and Relic, such as Willpower plus Power Control, Presence plus Influence, Intellect plus Occult or Technology, or Awareness plus Realm Navigation. Success and failure generate progress and consequences under the approved extended-task rules. A willing Relic may Take 10 when the process is safe and unopposed. Completing Attunement unlocks only the functions allowed by Bond, Claim, Grade, and other explicit prerequisites.

### Attunement capacity and Bond Strain

Relic Load is:

`RelicLoad = 1 + floor((max(1, RelicGrade) - 1) / 2)`

| Relic Grade | Load |
|---:|---:|
| 0–2 | 1 |
| 3–4 | 2 |
| 5 | 3 |

A character's capacity is:

`AttunementCapacity = 2 + TierRank + floor(max(0, WillpowerMod) / 2) + AttunementBonus`
