If total Load exceeds capacity:

`BondStrain = max(0, TotalRelicLoad - AttunementCapacity)`

Over-attunement is permitted. While Bond Strain is greater than zero:

- each Relic Technique costs additional Reserve equal to Bond Strain;
- Relic Control, Attunement, Corruption-resistance, and Claim checks suffer Setback; and
- Breathers cannot restore Composure unless enough Relics are suppressed to remove the strain.

A bearer may suppress selected Attunements during a Full Rest. Voluntary disattunement requires a number of hours equal to the Relic's Load. Forced severance requires an explicit effect and never occurs merely because the narrator wants the item elsewhere.

### Coerced Attunement

An unwilling or resisting Relic may be forced only through a valid method. Its DC is:

`ForcedLinkDC = AttunementDC + 5`

Success creates no more than Tolerated access unless the method explicitly establishes something further. It also inflicts a `-3` Bond change, creates a Claim violation, and may trigger the Relic's resistance, Corruption pressure, curse, or retaliation. Forced Attunement never makes a sapient Relic loyal.

### Claims and contested priority

A claimant's Claim Strength ranges from `0` to `15`. Evidence may contribute:

| Evidence | Claim Strength |
|---|---:|
| Current custody | +1 |
| Maker or creator | +2 |
| Designated inheritance | +3 |
| Current Recognized bearer | +3 |
| Defining deed, restoration, or protection | +1 each, maximum +3 total |
| Law, oath, Domain, or appointment | +1 to +3 |
| Conquest, when the Relic's succession law recognizes it | +1 to +3 |
| Public, institutional, or divine Recognition | +1 to +3 |
| Relevant violation or forfeiture | -1 to -5 |

`ClaimStrength = clamp(0, 15, SumOfClaimEvidence)`

`ClaimModifier = floor(ClaimStrength / 3)`

When priority is contested:

`ClaimCheck = d20 + RelevantAttributeMod + RelevantMastery + ClaimModifier`

The check is opposed by another claimant or the Relic's Resolve, as causally appropriate. Ties preserve the status quo. Winning establishes current mythic priority only; it does not automatically grant legal ownership, physical possession, Attunement, control, or affection.

### Theft, sale, loss, and recovery

Theft or transfer changes custody. Legal ownership, Claims, Bonds, Attunements, and Recognition remain as recorded until events or explicit mechanics change them. A thief can use only functions available through the physical object's mundane operation or genuinely established access.

The world retains evidence of theft, sale, inheritance, desecration, recovery, and use. A lost Relic does not teleport back to its favorite bearer. A bearer with an active Bond may sense its broad state or direction within the same realm only if the Relic explicitly provides that function. Bearer-funded Bonded Techniques remain dormant while the object is unavailable. Recovery, replacement, rebuilding, or rebinding must occur in the fiction.

### Relic Charge and fuel

Charge is optional rather than universal. If a Relic uses it:

`RelicChargeMaximum = 3 + 2 * RelicGrade + ChargeBonus`

Each Technique specifies whether it spends Relic Charge, bearer Reserve, a split cost, or another exact fuel. Every Relic has a fixed recharge profile, including source, rate, limits, and consequences. There is no universal rest-based recharge for Relics.

### Burdens, Hungers, and curses

Every burden, Hunger, curse, prohibition, or corrupting influence records:

- its trigger and effect;
- severity and any resistance check;
- escalation rule;
- duration;
- recovery, satisfaction, or release condition;
- whether it is known, discoverable, or concealed; and
- its interaction with the Relic's Drives and Techniques.

A curse cannot adapt itself retroactively to block the player's current plan unless that adaptive capability already exists in canonical state. Hidden mechanics must preexist and remain discoverable through valid evidence. A Hunger may request or tempt; refusal causes only its recorded effects, and satisfaction grants no hidden benefit.

### Corruption

Corruption is an optional, morally neutral influence track. It may represent alien logic, divine identity bleed, Primordial contamination, possession pressure, addictive resonance, ideological rewriting, or another defined effect.

When exposure calls for resistance:

`CorruptionDC = 10 + 2 * RelicGrade + InfluenceIntensity + BondStrain`

The bearer makes a Resolve Defense Check. The resulting Corruption change is:

| Result | Change |
|---|---:|
| Catastrophic failure | +2 |
| Failure | +1 |
| Success | 0 |
| Strong success | -1 |
| Exceptional success | -2 |

`NewCorruption = clamp(0, 12, CurrentCorruption + Change)`

A character may knowingly accept a stated benefit and its stated Corruption instead of resisting when the effect allows it.

| Corruption | State | Required expression |
|---:|---|---|
| 0–2 | Trace | Signs without compulsory mechanical impairment |
| 3–5 | Marked | Minor explicit effect |
| 6–8 | Altered | Major explicit effect or Complication |
| 9–11 | Claimed | Severe pressure, altered capability, or transformation risk |
| 12 | Crisis | Predefined transformation, severance, possession attempt, bargain, or identity confrontation |

Corruption never silently turns the player character over to the AI. Loss of agency must use explicit condition mechanics and meaningful choices. A Full Rest does not erase Corruption unless a specific effect says otherwise.

### Anchors

An Anchor preserves a Relic's mythic continuity. Possible Anchors include its Vessel, Name, bearer Bond, maker, bloodline, defining deed, shrine, realm, Domain, oath, law, audience, cult, institution, fragment, or blueprint.

Each Anchor records its type, location or host, access conditions, integrity, severance method, defenses, and consequences. It has an Anchor Rating from `0` to `5`.

`RelicAnchorDefense = 10 + 2 * RelicGrade + AnchorRating`

`RelicAnchorIntegrity = max(1, 5 * (RelicGrade + AnchorRating))`

Attacking an Anchor requires a valid causal method. Knowing a True Name may grant access or leverage when the established metaphysics support it; the Name does not automatically sever every Anchor or destroy the Relic.

### Damage and destruction states

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Whole | Fully functional at current integrity |
| Damaged | Impaired but operational |
| Broken | Vessel defeated and normal functions substantially lost |
| Dormant | Identity persists but active supernatural functions are suppressed |
| Dispersed | Vessel or presence divided across recoverable components or Anchors |
| Severed | One or more essential identity relationships or Anchors have been cut |
| Unmade | True destruction has ended this Relic's continuous identity |

Reducing the Vessel to `0` Integrity normally breaks the Vessel; it does not automatically unmake the Relic. True destruction may require defeating the Vessel, satisfying destruction keys, severing Anchors, ending the Self or Name, and preventing succession or reformation, as established for that Relic.

All destruction requirements must be canonical and discoverable. The system may reveal existing requirements through play but may not invent new immunities or Anchors after the player succeeds.

### Reformation

Unless a Relic states otherwise, reformation is checked once every seven days.

`ReformationProgressRequired = 6 + 3 * RelicGrade + 2 * SeveredAnchorCount`

`ReformationRate = max(0, ActiveAnchorCount + RecognitionSupport - Interference)`

Recognition Support ranges from `0` to `3`; Interference ranges from `0` to `5`.

At each interval:

`NewReformationProgress = min(RequiredProgress, CurrentProgress + ReformationRate)`

With no active Anchor, Reformation Rate is `0`. On completion:

`ReformedIntegrity = max(1, floor(0.25 * IntegrityMaximum))`

The Relic returns in Critical condition and must be repaired or restored normally. Its surviving identity, memories, scars, and altered Anchors remain consequential. True destruction is possible. A later object modeled on an Unmade Relic is a replica, successor, echo, or new Relic rather than a retroactive continuation.

### Succession and Legacy

A Relic may recognize one or more explicit succession laws, including inheritance, personal choice, bloodline, conquest, deed, oath, public Recognition, divine Recognition, compatible vessel, or succession through a linked Name, office, Mantle, or Relic.

An heir receives only the rights actually transmitted. Succession may grant a Claim but does not automatically grant Attunement, Bond, memory access, alignment, obedience, or ownership under every jurisdiction.

A character's Legacy slot may transmit:

- physical custody of the Relic, if the character actually controls it at succession;
- `+3` designated-inheritance Claim evidence;
- one known Attunement method;
- a known recovery route, Anchor, or location; and
- memories the Relic itself retains and is willing or compelled by its established rules to disclose.

Legacy cannot clone a unique Relic, reverse its true destruction, or compel a person-like Relic to accept an heir.

### Markets, replicas, and counterfeit legend

Relics are nonfungible. Any normalized value is used only for an explicit purpose such as insurance, taxation, ransom, Craft difficulty, faction budgeting, or scenario balancing; it does not imply open-market availability.

Selling a Relic transfers custody and may transfer legal ownership where recognized. It does not automatically transfer Bond, Attunement, Claim priority, a Self's consent, or mythic Recognition.

A counterfeit may reproduce appearance, mundane statistics, or manufactured functions. It does not acquire the original Name, history, Claim network, Self, or Authority merely by looking identical.

### Event sourcing and comic continuity

Every Relic-changing event records:

- current form, holder, hand, location, and damage state;
- Names, Epithets, Grade, Legend Marks, and the deed or Recognition that changed them;
- Relic Point purchases and all resource costs;
- Claim evidence, Attunement, Load, Bond, access, and severance changes;
- the observations, memories, Drives, and Taboos supporting any autonomous decision;
- Anchors, destruction progress, reformation progress, and interference; and
- voice, dialogue, visual design, scars, transformations, scale, and panel-relevant effects.

Awakening and Grade increases should produce a deliberate visual evolution when fictionally visible. Comic adaptation must preserve the Relic's actual history and iconography, including obscene, ridiculous, humiliating, or villainous elements. It may clarify presentation but may not replace the object with a more respectable version.

---

## 23. Survival: Time, Needs, Fatigue, Rest, Camps, and Resource Gathering

### Survival design contract

- Survival pressure comes from committed world conditions rather than automatic level scaling.
- Powers, equipment, preparation, wealth, and clever absurdity can genuinely solve problems they are capable of solving.
- Supplies cannot appear retroactively, but the engine forecasts shortages before they occur.
- Safe routine periods are compressed; detailed tracking activates when scarcity, danger, logistics, or consequences matter.
- Theft, predation, cannibalism, divine bargains, monster harvesting, and other extreme solutions remain possible when causally valid. Morality, law, evidence, relationships, and contamination respond normally.

### World-time units

| Unit | Duration | Primary use |
|---|---:|---|
| Round | 6 seconds | Combat and immediate danger |
| Exploration Turn | 10 minutes | Searching, careful movement, breaching, and field actions |
| Watch | 4 hours | Travel, guard rotations, weather, and supply intervals |
| Day | 24 hours | Food, sleep, recovery, and world-front advancement |

Every action records start time, base duration, delays, interruptions, and completion time. Simultaneous party activities advance time by the longest activity rather than adding every participant's duration together.

Narrative compression still processes supply consumption, hazards, faction clocks, market changes, and NPC actions.

### Survival profile

Every character or creature records:

- breathing medium and safe-air requirements;
- Base Food Need per 24 hours;
- Base Water Need per eight hours;
- Required Rest Hours;
- valid sleep, trance, charging, maintenance, or hibernation method;
- environmental Protections and adaptations;
- immunities and explicit Need Overrides; and
- incompatible, poisonous, religiously prohibited, or otherwise restricted provisions.

The standard human baseline is:

| Requirement | Baseline |
|---|---:|
| Food | 1 Ration per 24 hours |
| Water | 1 Water Unit per 8 hours |
| Rest | 6 qualifying hours within an 8-hour rest attempt |
| Air | Continuous breathable atmosphere |

One Ration contains one standard human day of nutrition. One Water Unit is approximately one liter. Five Rations occupy one Bulk; three Water Units occupy one Bulk.

### Adjusted need

`EffectiveFoodNeed = max(0, BaseFoodNeed + ExertionFoodDemand - FoodNeedOverride)`

`EffectiveWaterNeed = max(0, BaseWaterNeed + HeatDemand + ExertionWaterDemand - WaterNeedOverride)`

- Exertion Food Demand is `+1` after at least eight hours of strenuous activity during the day.
- Exertion Water Demand is `+1` during an eight-hour interval containing at least two hours of strenuous activity.
- Net heat Intensity 1–2 adds `+1` Water Demand; Net heat Intensity 3 or greater adds `+2`.
- A Need Override cannot exceed the requirement it explicitly replaces.

An illusion of food does not nourish anyone. Conjured food, blood, divine nectar, batteries, souls, sunlight, or stranger fuel works only according to its recorded properties.

### Deprivation Loads

Food, hydration, sleep, and each environmental Exposure are tracked separately from `0` through `12`.

For food or water:

`Shortfall = max(0, EffectiveNeed - UnitsConsumed)`

If Effective Need is greater than zero:

`LoadGain = min(3, ceil[3 * Shortfall / EffectiveNeed])`

- Hunger is evaluated every 24 hours.
- Hydration is evaluated every eight hours.
- Completely satisfying the next applicable interval reduces that source's Load by `3`, minimum zero.
- Excess consumption cannot bank immunity against later deprivation unless a feature explicitly permits storage.

Sleep uses:

`SleepShortfall = max(0, RequiredRestHours - QualifyingRestHours)`

`SleepLoadGain = min(3, ceil[SleepShortfall / 2])`

A successful rest reduces Sleep Load according to its Rest Quality.

### Unified fatigue

For each Survival Load:

`SourceSeverity = clamp(0, 3, floor(SourceLoad / 3))`

| Load | Source Severity |
|---:|---:|
| 0–2 | 0 |
| 3–5 | 1 |
| 6–8 | 2 |
| 9–12 | 3 |

Let `ActiveSources` be the number of sources with Severity 1 or greater. If no source is active, `HighestSourceSeverity` is zero.

`FatigueSeverity = clamp(0, 3, HighestSourceSeverity + floor(ActiveSources / 2))`

| Severity | Condition |
|---:|---|
| 0 | No fatigue condition |
| 1 | **Minor Fatigue:** Setback on forced marches, extended exertion, strenuous Pushes, and checks resisting further survival pressure |
| 2 | **Major Exhaustion:** Minor effects, Speed `-2`, Setback on physical checks and Concentration, and no more than one Recovery Die may be spent during a Breather |
| 3 | **Severe Exhaustion:** Major effects, Speed is halved instead of reduced by two, effective Vitality, Composure, and Reserve maximums fall by 25%, and Sprint or Forced March requires an explicit Overdraw cost |

Whenever a source already at Load 9 or greater reaches another evaluation interval without relief, the character makes its approved Trauma 2 Wound Check. Different fatigue sources do not stack duplicate numeric penalties.

Reducing Fatigue Severity removes its condition effects immediately but does not restore lost resources or heal Wounds. This unified procedure defines the Minor Fatigue, Major Exhaustion, and Severe Exhaustion labels used by environmental Exposure.

### Rest Quality

A rest attempt normally occupies eight hours. Combat, travel, strenuous Crafting, active rituals, and extended guard duty do not count as qualifying rest. Up to two hours of light activity or watch duty are compatible with the standard human profile.

First determine Duration Cap:

| Qualifying rest | Duration Cap |
|---|---:|
| Meets Required Rest Hours | 3 |
| At least half, but less than required | 1 |
| Less than half | 0 |

Then calculate:

`RestQuality = min(DurationCap, clamp[0, 3, 3 + CampBonus - SafetyPenalty - ShelterPenalty - NecessityPenalty - InterruptionPenalty])`

- Camp Bonus is normally `0`, or `+1` from a valid Comfortable camp feature.
- Safety Penalty is `1` when a known approach threat lacks a watcher, alarm, concealment, or secured barrier.
- Shelter Penalty is `1` when the highest Net Exposure is 1–2 and `2` when it is 3 or greater.
- Necessity Penalty is `1` when one due air, food, water, fuel, or physiological requirement is unmet and `2` when multiple requirements are unmet.
- Interruption Penalty is `1` for one meaningful hostile or strenuous interruption and `2` for multiple interruptions.

| Quality | Outcome |
|---:|---|
| 3 | Restorative Full Rest |
| 2 | Adequate Full Rest |
| 1 | Compromised Rest |
| 0 | Failed Rest |

Quality 2–3 grants the approved Full Rest restoration and recharges eligible full-rest abilities. Quality 1 does not count as a Full Rest. Instead:

`RecoveredDice = ceil(RecoveryDiceMaximum / 2)`

`NewRecoveryDice = min(RecoveryDiceMaximum, CurrentRecoveryDice + RecoveredDice)`

Sleep Load is reduced by Rest Quality. A character can receive Full Rest reset benefits no more than once per twenty elapsed hours unless an explicit feature changes its biological or mechanical cycle.

An interruption pauses rest; it does not retroactively erase hours already completed.

### Camps and shelter

A persistent camp records:

- exact location, occupants, ownership, and access;
- capacity and sleeping spaces;
- tagged Protection by hazard family;
- concealment, alarms, guard plan, and approach routes;
- defensive terrain and fortifications;
- sanitation, light, noise, heat, fuel, and power;
- stored inventory and caches;
- damage, evidence, tracks, and waste;
- whether and by whom the camp has been discovered; and
- visual condition for comic continuity.

Actual Protection is:

`ShelterProtection = TerrainProtection + GearProtection + ConstructionProtection + PowerProtection`

Each component must apply to the relevant hazard. A warm bunker does not automatically block radiation, divine pressure, spirits, or flooding.

When camp construction is uncertain:

`CampDC = clamp(5, 30, 10 + SiteDifficulty + HighestSiteHazardIntensity + UrgencyModifier - ToolBonus)`

Site Difficulty ranges from `-3` through `+5`; Urgency Modifier and Tool Bonus each range from `0` through `5`.

| Outcome | Camp result |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | Intended protection fails; paid resources remain spent and one precommitted danger or defect manifests |
| Failure | Basic shelter with one declared deficiency |
| Success | Intended protection is established |
| Strong success | Success plus one Camp Advantage |
| Exceptional success | Success plus two Camp Advantages |

Possible quantified advantages include Concealed, Defensible, Sanitary, Efficient, Comfortable, Alarmed, or Warded. No result creates materials, wards, fuel, or technology that the party did not possess.

A completed camp remains in the world. It can be revisited, discovered, robbed, occupied, improved, damaged, or destroyed.

### Foraging, hunting, and scavenging

Resources exist as persistent nodes recording type, exact current stock, Base Yield, renewal rate, search interval, ownership, legality, contamination, hazards, and collection requirements.

When locating or extracting uncertain resources:

`GatherDC = clamp(5, 30, 10 + ScarcityRating + ConcealmentModifier - AccessBonus)`

- Scarcity Rating ranges from `0` through `10`.
- Concealment Modifier ranges from `-3` through `+5`.
- Access Bonus ranges from `0` through `5`.

`GatheredUnits = min(CurrentStock, BaseYield * OutcomeYieldMultiplier)`

| Outcome | Yield multiplier |
|---|---:|
| Catastrophic failure | 0, plus a precommitted complication |
| Failure | 0 |
| Success | 1 |
| Strong success | 2 |
| Exceptional success | 3 |

Gathered units immediately reduce node stock. Renewal follows its world clock. Searching the same unchanged area after failure grants no new roll; the character needs a new zone, lead, technique, interval, or material state change.

A visible, abundant, safe resource requires no roll—only time, containers, access, and carrying capacity.

Hunting hostile prey uses combat normally. Killing XP applies to genuine opposition; Survival XP applies only when completing the distinct provisioning challenge. Contamination and disease must already exist in the resource record and use the approved Affliction system.

### Supply forecasting

For each required resource:

`CoverageIntervals = floor(CurrentUsableUnits / PlannedNeedPerInterval)`

The interface reports the earliest projected shortage using the declared route, party composition, exertion, climate, and planned resupply. Forecasts update when those assumptions change.

Known inventory cannot become a surprise shortage. Hidden spoilage, theft, leakage, or contamination is possible only when its cause was committed and discoverable.

Paid Lifestyle satisfies routine needs while the character remains inside its actual service area. It never generates expedition stock or resalable supplies.

### Powers and player freedom

A valid ability may remove hunger, create breathable air, generate shelter, teleport past exposure, purify poison, or make survival trivial. The system accepts that victory and does not invent arbitrary anti-power weather.

Conversely, Power Tier alone grants no immunity. A god who still needs water can become dehydrated; an indestructible hero can suffocate if their physiology requires air; an immortal may survive starvation while remaining severely impaired.

The player may secure provisions by purchase, theft, extortion, hunting, magic, raiding, bargains, cannibalism, monster harvesting, or any other possible method. The survival system resolves whether it works. Separate systems resolve witnesses, morality, law, relationships, infection, and retaliation.

### Survival XP and continuity

Meaningful survival uses the approved formula:

`XP_survival = round[U(R) * S * N * C]`

Routine meals, ordinary hotel stays, and repeatedly farming the same safe resource award no XP. Surviving or permanently solving a genuine hazard may award Survival XP; discovering the place or route may separately award Exploration XP when it represents a distinct accomplishment.

The event ledger records elapsed time, light and weather, needs, consumed supplies, Loads, fatigue, rest inputs, camp state, gathering results, hazards, clothing, dirt, wetness, blood, equipment wear, carried inventory, dialogue, and visible deterioration. Comic adaptation may compress routine days but cannot erase a decisive shortage, miserable camp, desperate meal, strange survival solution, or resulting change in appearance.

---

## 24. Travel and Navigation: Routes, Pace, Vehicles, Pursuits, and Realm Crossings

### Travel freedom contract

Routes organize world state; they are not rails.

- The player may follow, abandon, destroy, create, bypass, or radically shorten a route whenever their actual capabilities permit.
- Roads, portals, vehicles, flight, teleportation, super-speed, divine passage, being fired from a cannon, or riding an unwilling monster are all legitimate travel methods.
- The engine never invents an anti-teleportation field merely to preserve an encounter.
- Bypassed content continues evolving off-screen rather than relocating in front of the player.
- Travel difficulty derives from geography, weather, security, instability, and world events—not character Level.

### Canonical world versus known world

The engine maintains two separate maps:

1. **Canonical Map:** actual locations, routes, closures, hazards, actors, resources, and current world state.
2. **Knowledge Map:** what each character presently knows, believes, suspects, or misunderstands.

The interface and agents may use only their valid Knowledge Map. The engine retains actual position even when the party is lost.

A map or route record includes:

- origin, destination, geometry, and distance;
- travel modes it supports;
- Route Quality and terrain;
- access requirements, borders, tolls, laws, and checkpoints;
- Navigation Complexity and hidden branches;
- hazards, weather exposure, resource nodes, and safe stops;
- current security and Travel Event Pressure;
- opening, closure, damage, and repair state;
- last verification time and expected volatility;
- witnesses, surveillance, evidence, and faction control; and
- realm threshold information when applicable.

### Distance and movement profiles

Earth routes use kilometers. Mythic or non-Euclidean routes use declared Traversal Units with a compatible movement rate.

Tactical Speed does not automatically determine long-distance performance. Every travel method records:

- Base Travel Rate;
- supported terrain and Route types;
- maximum sustainable Pace;
- passenger and cargo capacity;
- fuel, Reserve, Charge, Favor, or other operating cost;
- crew and skill requirements;
- environmental Protection;
- noise, visibility, divine signature, or other detectability;
- maintenance and failure conditions; and
- departure, landing, entry, and exit requirements.

Baseline ordinary rates:

| Method | Base Travel Rate |
|---|---:|
| Walking | 5 km/h |
| Mount or sustained animal travel | 8 km/h |
| Bicycle | 15 km/h |
| Off-road ground vehicle | 40 km/h |
| Road vehicle | 80 km/h |
| Rotorcraft | 200 km/h |
| Conventional aircraft | 800 km/h |

Individual templates override these values. Super-speed, magical flight, divine roads, portals, and other extraordinary methods use their actual Technique profiles.

### Travel-rate calculation

`EffectiveTravelRate = BaseTravelRate * RouteMultiplier * TerrainMultiplier * WeatherMultiplier * PaceMultiplier`

Route multipliers:

| Route | Multiplier |
|---|---:|
| Optimized express corridor | 1.25 |
| Maintained route | 1.00 |
| Worn or obstructed route | 0.85 |
| Damaged route | 0.65 |
| Pathless movement | 0.50 |
| Incompatible or impassable | 0 |

Terrain multipliers:

| Terrain relationship | Multiplier |
|---|---:|
| Ideal or ignored by method | 1.00 |
| Difficult | 0.75 |
| Extreme | 0.50 |
| Impossible for method | 0 |

Weather multipliers:

| Weather | Multiplier |
|---|---:|
| Normal | 1.00 |
| Adverse | 0.80 |
| Severe | 0.50 |
| Travel-prohibiting | 0 |

A multiplier of zero makes that method invalid unless a Technique, Stunt, vehicle property, or genuine change removes the incompatibility.

The party travels at the slowest required participant's valid rate unless slower members are carried, transported, teleported, or left behind.

Travel time is rounded up to the next Exploration Turn:

`PlannedTravelHours = ceil[6 * RouteDistance / EffectiveTravelRate] / 6`

### Travel legs

A journey is divided at meaningful decisions, route nodes, thresholds, stops, events, or a maximum of one four-hour Watch.

Each leg commits:

- method and route;
- intended destination or direction;
- Pace;
- navigator, operator, scout, and other roles;
- expected duration and resource use;
- environmental conditions;
- known and hidden route state;
- event probability and eligible event pool; and
- failure destinations or deviation possibilities.

Conditions cannot be rewritten after seeing the navigation or event roll.

### Pace

| Pace | Rate multiplier | Detection and navigation | Pace Strain | Travel signature |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|
| Cautious | 0.75 | Edge on the first Navigation or hazard-detection check each leg | 0 | -2 |
| Standard | 1.00 | No adjustment | 0 | 0 |
| Fast | 1.25 | Setback on Navigation and hazard detection | 1 | +1 |
| Forced | 1.50 | Setback on Navigation and hazard detection | 2 | +2 |

A travel method must support the selected Pace. A passenger train, scheduled flight, exhausted mount, damaged vehicle, or narrowly limited portal cannot be forced faster merely by selecting the option.

### Travel Strain

Travel introduces an Exertion Load from `0–12`, which becomes another source in the approved Unified Fatigue formula.

For each leg:

`ExtendedTravelStrain = max(0, ConsecutiveLegs - 2)`

`ProjectedTravelStrain = PaceStrain + TerrainStrain + BurdenStrain + ExtendedTravelStrain`

| Source | Strain |
|---|---:|
| Compatible terrain | 0 |
| Difficult terrain | 1 |
| Extreme terrain | 2 |
| Normal load | 0 |
| Burdened | 1 |
| Overloaded | 2 |

When Projected Travel Strain is greater than zero:

`TravelEnduranceDC = clamp(10, 30, 10 + 2 * ProjectedTravelStrain)`

Use an Endurance-based Survival, Athletics, Vehicles, Power Control, or other causally valid check.

| Outcome | Adjustment |
|---|---:|
| Catastrophic failure | +2 |
| Failure | +1 |
| Success | 0 |
| Strong success | -1 |
| Exceptional success | -2 |

`ExertionLoadGain = max(0, ProjectedTravelStrain + OutcomeAdjustment)`

A Breather removes one Exertion Load, at most once following each completed leg. Rest removes:

`ExertionLoadReduction = 2 * RestQuality`

A protected passenger does not receive walking or Burden Strain, but cramped transport, violent motion, vehicle damage, piloting stress, or exposure may create its own recorded Strain.

### Navigation

No Navigation check occurs when the route is obvious, stable, accurately marked, and meaningfully uncontested.

When uncertainty matters:

`StalenessSteps = floor(ElapsedTimeSinceVerification / max[1, RouteChangeInterval])`

`EffectiveMapQuality = max(0, MapQuality - StalenessSteps)`

`NavigationDC = clamp(5, 35, 10 + RouteComplexity + RouteConcealment + Instability + Interference - EffectiveMapQuality - GuidanceBonus)`

| Variable | Range |
|---|---:|
| Route Complexity | 0–10 |
| Route Concealment | 0–5 |
| Instability | 0–5 |
| Interference | 0–5 |
| Map Quality | 0–5 |
| Guidance Bonus | 0–5 |

Ordinary geography normally uses Survival. Roads, traffic, or dangerous vehicle handling may use Vehicles. Impossible geographies and Veil routes normally use Realm Navigation. Attributes remain approach-dependent.

Navigation results:

| Outcome | Destination progress | Elapsed-time multiplier |
|---|---:|---:|
| Catastrophic failure | 0.50 | 1.25 |
| Failure | 0.75 | 1.00 |
| Success | 1.00 | 1.00 |
| Strong success | 1.00 | 0.90 |
| Exceptional success | 1.00 | 0.80 |

`ActualDestinationProgress = PlannedLegDistance * ProgressMultiplier`

`ActualLegHours = ceil[6 * PlannedLegHours * TimeMultiplier] / 6`

A failure on a route with no possible wrong turn produces delay, obstruction, or poor progress—not imaginary disorientation. A Catastrophic failure may reach a committed deviation node or hazard only when that possibility existed before the roll.

Strong success grants one Navigation Benefit; Exceptional success grants two:

- improve the route's verified Map Quality by one, maximum five;
- identify a hidden branch or useful landmark;
- preserve concealment or erase ordinary tracks;
- locate an actual safe stopping point;
- avoid one known optional delay; or
- reveal one current route-state fact.

Benefits cannot erase mandatory tolls, active opposition, consumed fuel, or physical hazards already crossed.

### Becoming lost and reorientation

Character location knowledge has four states:

| Lost Depth | Knowledge state |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Exact |
| 1 | Uncertain |
| 2 | Lost |
| 3 | Displaced |

The engine always retains the actual location. The player receives only sensory information and valid deductions.

Reorientation uses the current Navigation DC:

`ReorientationDC = NavigationDC + 2 * LostDepth`

The route specifies the attempt interval, normally one hour in open wilderness or one Exploration Turn inside a bounded site.

| Outcome | Lost Depth change |
|---|---:|
| Catastrophic failure | +1 |
| Failure | 0 |
| Success | -1 |
| Strong success | -2 |
| Exceptional success | Set to 0 |

`NewLostDepth = clamp(0, 3, CurrentLostDepth + LostDepthChange)`

A new vantage point, beacon, guide, map, trail, captured scout, divine sign, or destructive shortcut may change the DC or make reorientation automatic.

### Expedition roles

A leg may use:

- **Navigator:** selects and maintains the route.
- **Scout:** detects hazards, actors, ambushes, and alternate paths.
- **Operator:** drives, pilots, steers, or controls the travel method.
- **Trailblazer:** clears or creates physical passage.
- **Quartermaster:** tracks supplies, fuel, cargo, and projected shortages.
- **Guard:** monitors pursuit and protects resting or distracted travelers.

Only one primary actor rolls each required function. Assistance uses the approved assistance cap.

One character may fill multiple roles. If two or more roles require simultaneous active attention, that character receives Setback on those checks unless automation, a power, or a feature handles one of them.

NPCs and autonomous companions must actually agree, be obligated, or be validly commanded to perform a role. Assignment does not suspend their agency.

### Travel events

A route has Base Event Pressure from `0–10`. Each leg calculates:

`LegFraction = min(1, PlannedLegHours / 4)`

`RawEventPressure = BaseEventPressure + TravelSignature + FactionAttention + Exposure - RouteSecurity - Concealment`

`TravelEventChance = clamp(0, 20, ceil[LegFraction * max(0, RawEventPressure)])`

Roll `d20`. An event occurs when the result is less than or equal to Travel Event Chance.

Travel events are selected through a seeded weighted pool created from actual:

- location and time;
- factions and active fronts;
- ecology and population;
- weather and hazards;
- witnesses, Heat, Notoriety, and evidence;
- unresolved quests and consequences;
- discoveries and resource opportunities; and
- route damage or congestion.

Events can be social encounters, discoveries, opportunities, requests, hazards, pursuit, strange comedy, or combat. They are not automatically hostile.

Fixed actors and hazards encountered by physically crossing their locations resolve regardless of the random-event roll. The event system cannot teleport a skipped villain onto the new route.

### Vehicles and mounts

Every vehicle or mount adds a travel profile containing:

- crew and operator requirements;
- Cruise Rate and supported Pace;
- passenger, cargo, and towing capacity;
- compatible terrain;
- fuel or physiological needs;
- operating and idle consumption;
- Protection and life support;
- sensor, communication, concealment, and signature ratings;
- Integrity, disabled systems, and maintenance state;
- takeoff, landing, docking, portal, or road requirements; and
- legal ownership, registration, theft status, and evidence.

Fuel consumption is:

`FuelConsumed = ceil(DistanceTraveled * FuelPerDistance + IdleHours * FuelPerIdleHour)`

The profile defines what one Fuel Unit represents.

Routine operation requires no roll. Dangerous operation uses:

`OperationDC = clamp(5, 35, 10 + TerrainStress + WeatherStress + PaceStress + DamageStress + ManeuverStress)`

Failure produces only declared outcomes such as delay, fuel loss, lost progress, damage, collision risk, or exposure. Vehicles do not randomly break merely because an encounter would be convenient.

Fuel reaching zero stops fuel-dependent operation. Vehicle damage persists through later travel and comic panels.

### Travel-scale pursuits

A chase uses a Lead track from `0–6`:

- `0`: the pursuer reaches contact range or forces an encounter.
- `6`: the quarry escapes the current pursuit.

Each pursuit interval both sides choose a method, Pace, route, and tactic. The quarry makes an opposed check against the pursuer using Vehicles, Athletics, Survival, Realm Navigation, Power Control, Stealth, or another valid method.

Rate advantage modifies the check:

| Quarry rate divided by pursuer rate | Modifier |
|---:|---:|
| 2.00 or greater | +5 |
| 1.25–1.99 | +2 |
| 0.80–1.24 | 0 |
| 0.50–0.79 | -2 |
| Below 0.50 | -5 |

If the quarry wins:

| Degree | Lead gained |
|---|---:|
| Success | +1 |
| Strong | +2 |
| Exceptional | +3 |

If the pursuer wins, reverse the change. A tie preserves Lead.

`NewLead = clamp(0, 6, CurrentLead + LeadChange)`

Every interval consumes real time, fuel, Reserve, supplies, and Travel Strain. Hazards affect each participant according to their actual position and method.

When one side is overwhelmingly faster and the slower side lacks concealment, terrain leverage, interception, sabotage, portals, or another valid method, raw-speed escape or capture may be automatic rather than pretending the opposed roll is meaningful.

### Teleportation and unconventional transit

Teleportation and equivalent travel record:

- range and passenger/cargo capacity;
- valid origin and destination requirements;
- Destination Lock;
- line-of-effect, familiarity, Name, Anchor, beacon, or coordinate needs;
- action time and resource cost;
- interference and failure profile; and
- whether intermediate space is crossed.

A true point-to-point effect skips intermediate travel time and hazards. It does not trigger road encounters merely because that content was prepared.

If a teleport can scatter, every possible scatter region and selection procedure is committed before rolling. Failure cannot place the party wherever the narrator most wants them.

### Realm thresholds

Every realm crossing first satisfies its existing Entry Condition, Anchor, and Toll. If those make entry impossible, no roll can override them.

When an unstable or opposed crossing is possible:

`ThresholdDC = clamp(5, 40, 10 + ThresholdRating + Instability + HostileAuthority + RealmPressure - KeyQuality - GuidanceBonus)`

The crossing declares before rolling whether failure means no passage, delay, Toll consumption, Pressure, separation, or seeded misdirection.

Realm time records:

- Earth entry timestamp;
- realm entry timestamp;
- exact time ratio or transformation rule;
- elapsed time inside;
- realm exit timestamp; and
- resulting Earth timestamp.

Time distortion cannot be decided retroactively after learning what occurred elsewhere.

### Journey compression and fast travel

Journey compression is available when:

- the route and method are sufficiently known;
- required supplies, fuel, access, and capacity exist;
- the player consents;
- no unresolved immediate choice requires input; and
- the system can reproduce every required leg, roll, seed, cost, and state transition.

The engine resolves legs in sequence and pauses compression upon:

- a qualifying Travel Event;
- a resource warning or equipment failure;
- route closure or material weather change;
- a party decision or NPC autonomy conflict;
- a discovery requiring attention;
- combat, pursuit, threshold failure, or meaningful hazard; or
- arrival.

Compression saves table time; it is not teleportation and does not freeze the world.

### Travel XP and comic continuity

Distance alone awards no XP.

- Discovering or establishing a useful route normally uses Exploration Scope `0.25`.
- A dangerous expedition may award Survival XP.
- Reaching an objective may award objective XP.
- Escaping a genuine pursuit or crossing an extraordinary threshold may qualify independently.
- Repeated safe commutes use the repetition multiplier and rapidly reach zero.

Every travel event records route, actual position, timestamps, movement method, Pace, formation, occupants, carried items, fuel, supplies, weather, light, fatigue, Navigation inputs, rolls, deviation, witnesses, pursuit state, vehicle damage, clothing, dialogue, and scenic landmarks.

Comic compression may use route maps, captions, montages, or establishing panels, but it must preserve consequential detours, shortages, arguments, encounters, vehicle changes, weather, absurd travel methods, and arrival condition.

---

## 25. Exploration and Discovery: Search, Fog of War, Clues, Mapping, and Investigation

### Exploration contract

- Canonical truth, geometry, clues, hazards, and secrets exist before their checks resolve.
- Obvious, accessible information is given automatically.
- A failed roll cannot fabricate evidence, relocate a secret, or rewrite what was present.
- Detection, access, interpretation, and verification are separate accomplishments.
- Powers and destructive methods work to their actual capabilities.
- Investigation may genuinely fail through missed evidence, destroyed records, dead witnesses, elapsed time, or superior opposition.
- The engine never invents a replacement clue merely because the player eliminated the original route.

### Persistent site structure

Every explorable Site contains:

`Site -> Zone -> Feature -> Connection`

A Site record includes:

- exact geometry, scale, elevation, and boundaries;
- Zones and their Connections;
- entrances, exits, thresholds, traversal times, locks, and access requirements;
- light, air, terrain, weather, environmental conditions, and sensory interference;
- landmarks, hazards, resources, evidence, secrets, and hidden layers;
- occupants, schedules, patrols, ownership, laws, and faction control;
- damage, alterations, tracks, contamination, and discovered state;
- generation seed, state version, and last-change timestamp; and
- visual bible and comic establishing details.

Once materialized, geometry cannot regenerate because the player failed a search or chose an unexpected direction. It changes only through actual events.

### Exploration Turns and activities

One Exploration Turn remains ten minutes. Exact shorter or longer durations remain valid; the turn is the normal environmental evaluation interval.

Standard activities include:

- **Traverse:** cross a Connection or reposition through a Zone.
- **Survey:** record major observable features and exits.
- **Search:** locate concealed Features within a declared scope.
- **Interact:** open, move, activate, breach, collect, or manipulate something.
- **Analyze:** interpret known evidence or test a hypothesis.
- **Map:** measure and record geometry.
- **Secure:** disable a hazard, establish an alarm, conceal traces, or control access.
- **Guard:** monitor an approach, actor, or known danger.
- **Assist:** make a distinct contribution under the approved assistance rules.

These options never limit free-form actions.

At the beginning of an exploration interval, the player declares aim, scope, method, roles, and any stop condition. The engine commits hidden eligibility, DCs, risks, and world activity before resolving checks.

Simultaneous party work advances time by the longest concurrent activity. When second-by-second position matters, the system switches to combat rounds.

### Senses and passive observation

Every relevant sense records:

- range and resolution;
- required medium;
- valid target types;
- obstruction and interference;
- whether it is passive or actively emitted; and
- any noise, light, psychic, divine, technological, or other signature it produces.

Plainly perceptible Features require no roll.

Static concealed Features compare their Search DC against the observer's applicable Passive Value. An actively hiding creature normally uses Stealth against Vigilance or the observer's active Perception.

A passive comparison occurs when:

- the Feature first enters valid sensory range;
- the observer gains a new vantage or sense;
- concealment or lighting materially changes; or
- the Feature changes state.

Standing in the same position does not generate repeated secret checks.

### Search scope and methods

Each search declares:

- one Zone, bounded object, body, system, record set, or comparable scope;
- the currently accessible layer;
- senses and tools used;
- physical manipulation permitted; and
- whether preserving evidence or avoiding detection matters.

Access layers are:

| Layer | State |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Observable without material manipulation |
| 1 | Concealed behind camouflage, clutter, coverings, or movable obstruction |
| 2 | Secured inside a lock, seal, system, structure, or protected boundary |
| 3 | Reachable only through dismantling, destruction, transformation, phasing, realm access, or another exceptional method |

Finding an access boundary does not automatically expose the contents behind it.

| Method | Time | Effect |
|---|---:|---|
| Rapid Scan | 1 minute | Setback; only Features detectable without substantial manipulation |
| Focused Search | 10 minutes | Standard active search of one scope and accessible layer |
| Systematic Search | 60 minutes | Edge; permits orderly manipulation and coverage of the accessible layer |
| Take 20 Search | 200 minutes from the focused baseline | Uses the existing Take 20 requirements and result |

A group uses one primary search roll with normal assistance rather than cycling every party member through identical attempts.

`SearchAttemptKey = ScopeID + AccessLayer + Method + SenseProfile + MaterialStateVersion`

An unchanged failed Search Attempt Key cannot be rerolled. A new sense, tool, access layer, vantage, method, lead, actor capability, or material-state version may create a valid new attempt.

### Search resolution

For each eligible concealed Feature:

`SearchDC = clamp(5, 35, 10 + Concealment + Obstruction + Complexity + Interference - AccessBonus - ToolBonus)`

| Variable | Range |
|---|---:|
| Concealment | 0–10 |
| Obstruction | 0–5 |
| Complexity | 0–5 |
| Interference | 0–5 |
| Access Bonus | 0–5 |
| Tool Bonus | 0–5 |

One Search total is compared independently against every eligible Feature's committed DC. The interface does not reveal how many undiscovered Features exist.

| Outcome against a Feature | Result |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | Feature remains undiscovered; apply one precommitted consequence only when the method could cause it |
| Failure | Feature remains undiscovered; time and resources remain spent |
| Success | Reveal the Feature's existence and exact perceived location |
| Strong success | Success plus one causally observable property, access point, trigger, or preservation advantage |
| Exceptional success | Success plus two such benefits |

Search benefits cannot expose a deeper inaccessible layer or replace later analysis. False information occurs only when an actual decoy, illusion, forgery, corrupted sensor, or deceptive actor exists.

### Location discovery and fog of war

Each character maintains a separate Location Discovery State:

| State | Knowledge |
|---:|---|
| 0 — Unknown | No usable entry |
| 1 — Reported | A rumor, claim, approximate area, or unverified map exists |
| 2 — Located | A usable position, approach, entrance, or coordinate is believed known |
| 3 — Entered | The character has physically or remotely crossed into the Site |
| 4 — Surveyed | Major currently observable Zones, Features, and Connections in the declared scope are recorded |
| 5 — Mapped | Geometry and major Connections in that scope have been verified at Map Quality 2 or greater |

These are epistemic states, not certifications of truth. A forged map can create a Reported or believed Located entry.

Fog-of-war presentation follows these rules:

- Unknown areas reveal no canonical geometry.
- Reported areas appear with uncertainty and source attribution.
- Located Sites show only the believed approach or marker.
- Entered areas display what was actually perceived.
- Surveyed and Mapped areas retain known geometry with timestamps.
- Dynamic actors appear only through current senses or timestamped last-known positions.
- Mapped never means safe, cleared, current, or free of secrets.
- No completion percentage may use undiscovered canonical content.

A "cleared" label is always a timestamped character or faction assessment.

### Mapping and landmarks

When geometry is uncertain:

`MappingDC = clamp(5, 35, 10 + GeometryComplexity + Distortion + Interference - VantageBonus - ToolBonus)`

| Variable | Range |
|---|---:|
| Geometry Complexity | 0–10 |
| Distortion | 0–5 |
| Interference | 0–5 |
| Vantage Bonus | 0–5 |
| Tool Bonus | 0–5 |

| Outcome | Candidate Map Quality |
|---|---:|
| Catastrophic failure | No verified improvement; a possible measurement error is marked unverified |
| Failure | No improvement |
| Success | 2 |
| Strong success | 3 |
| Exceptional success | 4 |

`CrossCheckBonus = 1` when an independent survey, instrument, or authoritative source corroborates the geometry.

`NewMapQuality = max(CurrentMapQuality, min[5, CandidateMapQuality + CrossCheckBonus])`

Map Quality 1 represents a rough sketch or credible report. Quality 5 represents current, detailed, independently verified mapping within the declared scope—not universal knowledge.

Existing travel staleness rules apply as Sites change.

Every Landmark has Rating `1–5`, exact visibility conditions, identity requirements, and current state.

`LandmarkGuidanceBonus = min(5, HighestApplicableLandmarkRating + TriangulationBonus)`

Triangulation Bonus is one when at least one additional independent Landmark applies; otherwise zero. Landmarks may be moved, obscured, counterfeited, damaged, or destroyed.

### Clues and evidence

Every clue or evidence object records:

- evidence ID, type, creator, source event, and timestamp;
- exact location, custody, owner, and chain of possession;
- observable content separated from possible interpretation;
- access layer, valid senses, tools, and collection requirements;
- Search DC and Analysis properties;
- Integrity, Relevance, and Provenance ratings from `0–5`;
- contamination, tampering, forgery, illusion, decay, and destruction state;
- Claims it may support or contradict;
- Leads it may create;
- copies, derivatives, recordings, and relation to the original; and
- characters and factions presently aware of it.

Evidence Integrity changes through:

`NewEvidenceIntegrity = clamp(0, 5, CurrentIntegrity - DamageLoss - ContaminationLoss + ValidRestoration)`

Restoration cannot recreate information that no longer survives unless an explicit reconstructive ability can do so.

Finding evidence does not automatically explain it, prove authenticity, establish legal admissibility, or reveal every conclusion it supports.

### Analysis and player-driven investigation

The player declares:

- one Question or hypothesis;
- the evidence being considered;
- the analytical method;
- the acting character and skill pairing; and
- any tools, references, experiments, or comparison samples.

Independent corroboration is:

`CorroborationBonus = min(2, max[0, IndependentCorroboratingSources - 1])`

`EvidenceQuality = clamp(0, 5, floor[(Integrity + Relevance + Provenance) / 3] + CorroborationBonus)`

Use the ratings applicable to the declared Question.

`AnalysisDC = clamp(5, 40, 10 + Complexity + Fragmentation + Deception + DomainGap - EvidenceQuality - ToolBonus)`

| Variable | Range |
|---|---:|
| Complexity | 0–10 |
| Fragmentation | 0–5 |
| Deception | 0–10 |
| Domain Gap | 0–5 |
| Tool Bonus | 0–5 |

| Outcome | Result |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | No warranted conclusion; apply one committed consequence. A false conclusion requires actual deceptive evidence |
| Failure | Provide the raw observable facts and unresolved uncertainty |
| Success | Provide one supported conclusion or Lead responsive to the Question |
| Strong success | Success plus one corroboration, contradiction, vulnerability, or useful next test |
| Exceptional success | Success plus two such additions |

A roll cannot infer information absent from the evidence or beyond the method's causal reach. If the accessible facts already make the answer certain, no roll is required.

### Case records

Investigation uses a character-facing Case Graph containing:

- Questions;
- Claims and hypotheses;
- Evidence;
- witnesses and sources;
- Sites and actors;
- Leads;
- supporting or contradicting relationships;
- missing information; and
- deadlines, opposition, and unresolved contradictions.

Entries may be marked Reported, Observed, Inferred, Corroborated, Disputed, or Refuted. These labels represent the character's evidentiary basis—not the engine certifying canonical truth.

The player may freely construct, reject, or revise hypotheses. An Investigation check does not magically select the correct culprit or "solve" a mystery.

### Testimony, research, and records

A witness can communicate only what they perceived, remember, believe, infer, or were told. They may lie, bargain, refuse, misremember, omit, or misunderstand according to actual knowledge and agency.

- Influence may secure cooperation.
- Deception may establish a false claim.
- Intimidation may produce compliance or rushed testimony.
- Insight identifies supported behavioral information; it is not a truth detector.
- Memory reading, resurrection, divine inquiry, or technological extraction uses its explicit capabilities and limits.

Archives, databases, divine records, security footage, memories, and public filings contain only records actually created and retained. Successful research cannot manufacture a nonexistent document.

Broad research uses the approved extended-task procedure. Completion retrieves and organizes the eligible sources; it does not grant omniscience.

### Secret integrity and mystery solvability

Every consequential Secret records:

- canonical truth and creation event;
- current knowledge holders;
- concealment method and responsible actors;
- evidence and clue network;
- access paths and discovery conditions;
- decoys, false beliefs, and contradictions;
- expiry, disclosure, destruction, or transformation conditions; and
- actions its protectors may take to preserve it.

A hidden fact cannot be invented after an outcome merely to justify that outcome.

Every deliberately authored, intended-to-be-solvable investigation requires:

- at least three Clue Vectors;
- at least two causally independent vectors; and
- no single mandatory roll, witness, object, or access method.

Clue Vectors may include physical evidence, testimony, records, behavior, financial or resource trails, power signatures, Realm law, absences, environmental effects, or direct observation.

Emergent mysteries may be genuinely sparse or unsolvable. If world events destroy all valid evidence, the engine records that loss rather than moving or inventing clues.

### Hazards, traps, and access

Every concealed hazard records:

- trigger and trigger area;
- Search DC and valid detection methods;
- effect and Severity;
- reset, persistence, and escalation;
- bypass, disarm, resistance, and destruction methods;
- owner, installer, purpose, and maintenance state; and
- evidence left before and after activation.

Passive detection occurs only when a valid opportunity exists. Failing to detect a hazard does not activate it unless the search method itself satisfies its trigger.

Locks, seals, walls, wards, security systems, and Realm boundaries are physical or metaphysical obstacles—not narrative permissions. They may be opened, bypassed, hacked, deceived, destroyed, teleported through, phased around, bargained with, or left alone whenever a valid method exists.

Destructive access may reveal deeper layers, destroy evidence, alert occupants, cause collapse, change topology, or create a permanently ridiculous new entrance.

### Time, opposition, and traces

Exploration never pauses the world.

Searches, analysis, mapping, breaching, and collection advance:

- patrols and schedules;
- faction projects and response clocks;
- weather, light, tides, rituals, and Realm conditions;
- evidence decay and hostile cleanup;
- hazards and structural instability;
- pursuit, reinforcements, and competing explorers; and
- physiological needs and resource consumption.

Every activity records relevant Noise, Light, Disturbance, Power Signature, tracks, residue, digital traces, witnesses, and evidence handling.

Opponents may relocate or destroy clues only through actions supported by their knowledge, access, time, and capabilities. They cannot react to a search they did not detect.

### Information sharing and agent limits

A discovery initially belongs only to its observer, recording device, linked sensor network, or other actual recipient.

Sharing requires a valid channel, language, time, and willingness. A character may transmit:

- the original record;
- an accurate copy;
- a summary;
- an interpretation;
- a deliberate lie; or
- nothing.

Recipients gain the communicated claim and provenance, not automatic access to canonical truth.

Companions, Relics, NPCs, factions, and other agents may reason only from their own Case Graph and Knowledge Map. They may withhold discoveries, reach different conclusions, or deceive the player.

A merged party map exists only to the extent members or systems actually share information.

### Powers and unconventional investigation

A valid power may:

- see through an access layer;
- locate a known Name or Anchor;
- reconstruct damaged information;
- communicate with a corpse;
- interrogate matter, machines, animals, spirits, or places;
- view a bounded past event;
- reveal lies through an explicit effect;
- map through drones or remote senses; or
- demolish the Site and inspect what remains.

Such abilities use their recorded range, targets, costs, senses, interference, and limits. The engine accepts successful bypasses and does not invent arbitrary anti-divination or anti-X-ray properties.

Power Tier alone grants no hidden knowledge.

### Exploration XP, procedural boundary, and comic continuity

Exploration XP uses the approved formula:

`XP_exploration = round[U(A) * S * N * C]`

XP is awarded for meaningful verified discovery or completion—not for every search roll, copied document, repeated map, rumor, or party member independently reading the same clue.

Distinct achievements may stack when genuinely separate:

- locating a Site;
- discovering a significant Secret;
- mapping a dangerous route;
- solving an investigation;
- neutralizing a hazard;
- surviving the expedition; and
- completing its objective.

The ledger records exact location, time, positions, activities, senses, search scope, DCs, results, revealed Features, map version, evidence custody and Integrity, hypotheses, knowledge holders, testimony, lies, hazards, traces, appearance, lighting, dialogue, and reactions.

Comic adaptation preserves discovery order. It cannot visually reveal a hidden culprit, doorway, object, or threat before the viewpoint character actually perceives it.

Procedural generation must commit a Site's seed, topology, Connections, contents, hazards, actors, clues, and hidden Features before relevant exploration checks. Once materialized, it becomes persistent world state.

---

## 26. Procedural Sites and Dungeons: Topology, Stocking, Persistence, and Replayability

### Dungeon contract

A **Dungeon** is any bounded Site receiving high-resolution exploration treatment. It may be:

- a sewer, cave, tomb, temple, fortress, laboratory, office tower, motel, prison, museum, divine court, spacecraft, pocket realm, living creature, or absurd improvised structure;
- active, abandoned, inhabited, ruined, transforming, mobile, or non-Euclidean; and
- authored, procedurally generated, or hybrid.

"Dungeon" does not imply underground, hostile, level-balanced, loot-filled, or intended to be cleared.

- Generation follows world causality, not character Level.
- A Site may be overwhelmingly dangerous, almost empty, socially occupied, or already looted.
- No boss, treasure room, mandatory route, or combat quota is required.
- Teleportation, wall destruction, negotiation, surrender, stealth, bribery, retreat, or simply leaving remain valid.
- Unvisited content continues existing and evolving rather than moving onto the player's path.

### Generation authority and inputs

Generation precedence is:

1. canonical world state;
2. authored facts and required Anchors;
3. physical, technological, mythic, and Realm causality;
4. Site generation profile;
5. seeded procedural selection.

A procedural Site records:

- Site ID and Creation Event ID;
- generation mode and version;
- Site type, purpose, builder, owner, age, and history;
- Size Class and topology profile;
- environment, Realm laws, and structural materials;
- original and current functions;
- required entrances, exits, landmarks, objectives, and Anchors;
- factions, inhabitants, ecology, and supply routes;
- security, hazard, resource, occupancy, gate, and secret densities;
- known damage, alterations, occupation, abandonment, or contamination; and
- generation input snapshot.

Random generation cannot override a higher-priority fact.

### Deterministic seeds and independent streams

`SiteSeed = Hash(CampaignSeed, SiteID, CreationEventID, GenerationVersion)`

Each subsystem receives a separate random stream:

`StreamSeed = Hash(SiteSeed, StreamName, StableNodeID)`

Required streams include:

- topology;
- Zone functions;
- Connections;
- gates and security;
- hazards;
- inhabitants;
- resources;
- secrets;
- visual dressing; and
- tactical detail.

Independent streams prevent changing one decoration table from silently relocating every enemy, door, and clue.

When a generator must retry an invalid candidate:

`AttemptSeed = Hash(StreamSeed, AttemptNumber)`

The first candidate passing validation is committed. The engine cannot request another attempt merely because the valid result is inconvenient.

### Materialization boundary

At initial Site materialization, the engine commits:

- total topology and Stable Zone IDs;
- all Connections and access states;
- required Anchors and critical systems;
- Zone functions;
- gate, hazard, secret, resource, and population allocations;
- unique items and important evidence;
- initial inhabitants and schedules; and
- the Site's starting structural state.

Fine visual dressing and tactical geometry may be materialized lazily from a Zone's independent stream, but must be committed before the first relevant observation, search, interaction, or combat check.

Once materialized, content becomes persistent world state. Leaving, reloading, resting, or failing a roll does not regenerate it.

An explicitly infinite or continuously forming Realm uses stable chunks:

`ChunkSeed = Hash(SiteSeed, ChunkCoordinatesOrPath)`

Returning to the same chunk produces the same original geometry plus its recorded subsequent history.

### Size and topology

| Size Class | Zone range |
|---|---:|
| Micro | 3–5 |
| Small | 6–12 |
| Standard | 13–24 |
| Large | 25–50 |
| Vast | 51–100 |
| Megastructure | 101+ with stable chunking |

`ZoneCount = SeededInteger(MinZones, MaxZones)`

Topology profiles include linear, branching, looped, hub-and-spoke, layered, networked, labyrinthine, fractured, vertical, and non-Euclidean structures.

Each profile records decimal values from zero through one for Loop Density, Verticality, Secret Density, and Gate Density.

`LoopConnectionTarget = RoundHalfUp(ZoneCount * LoopDensity)`

`ConnectionTarget = ZoneCount - 1 + LoopConnectionTarget`

`VerticalConnectionTarget = RoundHalfUp(ConnectionTarget * Verticality)`

`SecretZoneTarget = RoundHalfUp(ZoneCount * SecretDensity)`

`GateTarget = RoundHalfUp(EligibleConnections * GateDensity)`

Authored requirements may exceed these targets. Targets guide generation; they do not delete required content.

Connections may represent corridors, roads, doors, ladders, shafts, vents, elevators, portals, roots, dreams, ritual transitions, digestive passages, or stranger causal relationships.

### Topology validation

Before commitment, the Site must pass:

- Stable ID uniqueness;
- geometry and boundary consistency;
- Connection reciprocity unless explicitly one-way;
- at least one causally defined route to every Zone, including destructive, conditional, phased, or Realm routes;
- required entrance, exit, Anchor, utility, and functional relationships;
- sufficient physical capacity for placed creatures and objects;
- gate-and-key dependency validation;
- secret Connection commitment;
- support and collapse relationship validation; and
- non-Euclidean transformation consistency.

A genuinely unreachable Zone is allowed only when explicitly classified as Sealed, Severed, Displaced, or otherwise causally inaccessible. The validator reports that state rather than silently adding a doorway.

Generation validation checks coherence—not fairness or balance.

### Zone functions and history

Every Zone records a Primary Function and may have Secondary Functions.

Common functions include:

- threshold or reception;
- circulation or transit;
- habitation;
- sanitation;
- storage;
- utility or maintenance;
- workshop or laboratory;
- security;
- administration;
- social or ceremonial;
- medical;
- archive;
- prison;
- ritual;
- shrine or tomb;
- agriculture;
- natural habitat;
- waste processing;
- command;
- anomaly; and
- intentionally absurd personal use.

Procedural selection uses:

`EffectiveWeight_i = BaseWeight_i * ContextCompatibility_i * StateAvailability_i`

For eligible options:

`SelectionProbability_i = EffectiveWeight_i / SumOfAllEligibleWeights`

A zero Effective Weight makes an option impossible.

Function assignment respects adjacency and support requirements. Habitation needs some actual source of water, food, sanitation, or external supply. A laboratory needs power, tools, or its mythic equivalent. Missing support must be explained by abandonment, failure, outside dependence, or supernatural law.

Each Zone also records historical layers:

1. original construction;
2. major conversion or occupation;
3. damage, neglect, repair, or contamination; and
4. recent activity.

Visual dressing derives from these layers rather than unrelated random clutter.

### Connections, gates, and access

Every Connection records:

- origin and destination;
- physical or metaphysical geometry;
- traversal time and capacity;
- visibility and Search properties;
- current access state;
- barrier, lock, seal, or authority;
- one-way or conditional operation;
- noise and signature;
- ownership and permitted users;
- damage and maintenance;
- bypass and destruction methods; and
- resulting evidence.

Connection states include Open, Closed, Hidden, Locked, Sealed, Collapsed, Unstable, One-Way, Conditional, Severed, and Destroyed.

A Connection may carry several states simultaneously when coherent.

### Locks, credentials, and dependency graphs

Locks use Rating `0–10`.

`AccessDC = clamp(5, 40, 10 + 2 * LockRating + SecurityModifier + StateModifier - ToolBonus)`

| Variable | Range |
|---|---:|
| Security Modifier | -3 to +5 |
| State Modifier | -3 to +5 |
| Tool Bonus | 0–5 |

The approach determines whether the check uses Technology, Craft, Thievery, Occult, Influence, Power Control, Realm Navigation, or another valid skill.

A valid current key or credential normally works automatically. Keys may include:

- physical keys;
- codes;
- biometrics;
- badges;
- identities;
- Names;
- bloodlines;
- oaths;
- recognized offices;
- passwords;
- living guides;
- sacrifices; or
- synchronized external actions.

Failure spends time and produces no new access. Catastrophic failure jams, alerts, damages, consumes, or transforms something only when that failure state was recorded.

An unchanged failed approach cannot be rerolled without new tools, access, knowledge, leverage, assistance, or material change. Take 20 remains available when its conditions are satisfied.

The access dependency graph must prevent accidental self-locking:

- a required key cannot exist solely behind the lock it opens;
- credentials must have a valid holder, provisioning route, or external acquisition path;
- chained gates must form a valid dependency order; and
- circular or impossible access is permitted only when intentionally classified as broken, sealed, or requiring an alternate method.

Keys and credentials remain persistent objects or permissions. They can be stolen, copied, revoked, destroyed, inherited, forged, or withheld.

### Hazards and traps

Hazards use the already-approved Hazard Rating rules.

`HazardDetectionDC = 5 + HazardRating + ConcealmentModifier`

`HazardResolutionDC = 10 + HazardRating`

These specific hazard formulas override the general Search DC when detecting or resolving the hazard itself.

Each Site profile has Hazard Density from zero through one:

`HazardSlotTarget = RoundHalfUp(EligibleHazardAnchors * HazardDensity)`

Eligible anchors arise from actual context, such as:

- defended entrances;
- valuables;
- prisons;
- ritual boundaries;
- unstable machinery;
- damaged structures;
- nests;
- contaminated areas;
- environmental transitions; or
- paranoid occupants.

A deliberate trap requires a maker, purpose, trigger, construction method, maintenance state, and intended users or bypass. Accidental hazards arise from damage, environment, neglect, or Realm law.

Procedural generation cannot place a trap in an arbitrary object solely because the player opened it. Hazard Rating derives from its maker, materials, source, and current state—not party Level.

### Inhabitants instead of encounter packets

The Site contains actors, not frozen encounter rooms.

Every inhabitant or group records:

- home and work Zones;
- current exact position;
- schedule and movement routes;
- role and authority;
- needs and carried possessions;
- faction and relationships;
- knowledge and communications;
- guard, patrol, sleep, feeding, or maintenance behavior;
- Morale and escape routes; and
- response to alarms, intrusion, negotiation, casualties, and changing conditions.

An encounter occurs when positions, senses, intent, and timing bring actors together.

- Patrols move even when off-screen.
- Nearby groups may hear or observe conflict.
- Reinforcements must travel from actual positions or arrive through actual routes.
- Fleeing actors remain in the world.
- A "boss" may negotiate, relocate, sleep, surrender, pursue, escape, or already be dead.
- Random events may introduce only actors eligible through the Site's ecology, route network, factions, and current world state.

Existing encounter-calibration math may label likely collisions, but cannot alter inhabitants after materialization.

### Ecology and logistics

Every sustained population identifies:

- habitable capacity;
- food, water, air, power, Charge, worship, souls, or other needs;
- storage and supply routes;
- sleeping or dormant locations;
- waste and corpse handling;
- maintenance and repair capacity;
- reproduction, recruitment, manufacturing, summoning, or migration;
- predator, prey, host, parasite, or symbiotic relationships; and
- consequences of isolation or shortages.

Impossible logistics require a recorded supernatural, technological, external, or historical explanation.

Creatures consume real resources, leave evidence, alter terrain, compete, migrate, reproduce, starve, or abandon the Site according to their profiles. Dead bodies, destroyed machines, shed material, spoor, trash, graffiti, and ritual residue remain until something removes them.

### Resources, treasure, and ownership

Resources derive from Zone function, inhabitants, history, deliveries, losses, and actual world value.

`CurrentStock = clamp(0, StorageCapacity, BaselineStock + Deliveries - Consumption - Losses)`

Every item has an identity or batch record, location, holder, ownership, legality, Bulk, condition, and value.

- Treasure does not scale to the player.
- Unique objects cannot exist in multiple Sites.
- An already-owned Relic cannot appear through a random table.
- A completed Site does not generate a reward chest.
- Furniture, wiring, doors, vehicles, bodies, monster parts, records, art, plumbing, divine residue, and structural material may all be taken when physically possible.
- Removing property changes custody, evidence, security, markets, faction behavior, and Site function.
- Empty containers remain empty until someone restocks them.

Procedural resource tables use the same weighted eligibility rule as Zone functions. Context-incompatible and globally unavailable items receive zero weight.

### Secrets, clues, and hidden topology

Secret Zones, Connections, compartments, occupants, records, and objects are committed during materialization.

Each records:

- concealment and access layer;
- Search and Analysis properties;
- purpose and creator;
- evidence of its existence;
- Connections to the ordinary topology;
- content and current state; and
- consequences of discovery, destruction, or continued concealment.

Authored solvable mysteries retain the approved three-vector and two-independent-vector requirements.

A procedural Secret does not move after a failed search. A missed secret may remain undiscovered forever. Destroying the Site may expose it, obliterate it, scatter it, or make it inaccessible according to actual structure and damage.

### Site alert state

A Site may track Alert from zero through five:

| Alert | State |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Routine activity; no known intrusion |
| 1 | Suspicious; anomalies are being checked |
| 2 | Alerted; local security and communications activate |
| 3 | Mobilized; occupants reposition, protect assets, and coordinate |
| 4 | Lockdown; access changes and an active hunt or defense begins |
| 5 | Evacuation, purge, final defense, flight, or equivalent profile-specific crisis |

`NewAlert = clamp(0, 5, CurrentAlert + RecordedAlertChange)`

Alert changes require detected noise, evidence, witness reports, sensor results, missing personnel, failed check-ins, or another actual information path.

Alert does not create enemies. It changes the decisions, positions, access states, communications, and projects of actors already capable of responding.

Reduction requires the Site's recorded verification, stand-down, deception, repair, or time process. Hiding quietly does not automatically erase confirmed knowledge.

### Tactical geometry and combat transition

Every Zone records:

- exact dimensions or bounded abstract scale;
- elevation and vertical routes;
- occupied spaces;
- cover and concealment;
- doors, barriers, windows, and chokepoints;
- structural supports;
- hazards and difficult terrain;
- movable objects;
- lighting and sensory conditions; and
- entry positions.

When combat begins, tactical geometry is derived from the committed Zone and its stable detail stream. Once generated, it remains that Zone's geometry.

The engine cannot:

- enlarge a room to fit an encounter;
- close an exit to prevent escape;
- add cover after an NPC needs it;
- remove a destructive route;
- move combatants from their actual positions; or
- conceal reinforcements omitted from prior world state.

Damage and movement update both tactical and exploration topology.

### Objectives and completion

A Site has no universal "win condition."

Objectives belong to characters, factions, contracts, Drives, survival needs, or emergent circumstances. They may include:

- rescue;
- theft;
- assassination;
- diplomacy;
- escape;
- sabotage;
- mapping;
- investigation;
- claiming territory;
- destroying or repairing a system;
- performing a ritual;
- recovering evidence;
- recruiting an inhabitant; or
- simply surviving passage.

A Completion Record contains:

- declared objective;
- responsible actor;
- qualifying conditions;
- completion evidence;
- timestamp;
- resulting Site state; and
- unresolved consequences.

Possible Site outcomes include Objective Met, Surveyed, Neutralized, Claimed, Liberated, Abandoned, Exhausted, Transformed, Collapsed, Destroyed, and Still Active. Several may coexist.

"Cleared" remains a timestamped assessment, never permanent omniscience.

Bypassing most Zones, negotiating with the inhabitants, stealing the objective through a wall, or destroying the entire structure may legitimately complete an objective.

### Persistence, repair, and repopulation

A Site does not reset when the party leaves.

It retains:

- opened or destroyed gates;
- bodies and defeated units;
- stolen or dropped objects;
- depleted stock;
- damaged structures;
- active fires, floods, contamination, and rituals;
- altered alert state;
- witnesses and evidence;
- changed ownership; and
- player-created entrances, traps, signs, graffiti, repairs, or obscene landmarks.

At each Site update interval:

`OpenCapacity = max(0, HabitableCapacity - CurrentPopulation)`

`CandidateArrivals = AvailableRecruits + ProducedUnits + ArrivingMigrants`

`RepopulationGain = min(OpenCapacity, CandidateArrivals, SupplySupport, AccessThroughput)`

All inputs are nonnegative unit counts for that interval.

New inhabitants require an actual recruitment, migration, birth, production, summoning, resurrection, infestation, or other causal source. Defeated enemies do not respawn merely to refill rooms.

Repairs require actors, access, time, materials, tools, and knowledge. Traps rearm only when their mechanism is automatic or someone services them. Supplies return only through production or delivery.

Maps become stale rather than updating themselves.

### Destruction, transformation, and mobile Sites

Structural damage uses the approved Structure and support rules.

Destroying a wall, floor, bridge, support, portal, engine, ritual boundary, or living organ may:

- create or remove Connections;
- merge or divide Zones;
- expose hidden layers;
- release hazards or inhabitants;
- sever utilities;
- collapse dependent structures;
- move the Site;
- alter Realm geometry; or
- destroy objectives and evidence.

A Site can transform into a new version while preserving its identity and history. Mobile or living Sites record position, route, interior changes, needs, damage, agency, and who controls movement.

True non-Euclidean spaces record exact transformation rules. "Impossible geometry" is not permission to change exits after the player chooses one.

### Difficulty, XP, exploitation, and comic continuity

Site danger is evaluated from actual:

- inhabitants and likely simultaneous opposition;
- hazards and environmental exposure;
- security and access;
- topology and escape difficulty;
- survival pressure;
- Realm instability; and
- information disadvantage.

Existing Party Capacity and Encounter Threat formulas provide planning labels only. They do not rebalance the Site.

A completed significant dungeon normally uses Exploration Scope `2.00`, but only when the player resolves a genuine Site-scale discovery or objective. Entering and leaving, repeatedly clearing repopulated weak creatures, remapping known Zones, or manufacturing disposable Sites does not repeatedly grant full completion XP.

Distinct combat, survival, objective, discovery, hazard, Relic, and moral awards may still stack when they represent separate accomplishments.

The generation record preserves:

- input snapshot;
- Site and stream seeds;
- generator and content-table versions;
- validation attempts and selected output;
- full initial topology and stocking;
- every later reducer event; and
- current Site and Knowledge Map versions.

The same inputs, seed, and generation version reproduce the same initial Site. Testing may regenerate that state; live play may not reroll it. Generator updates cannot silently alter an existing Site without a recorded migration or world event.

Comic continuity records architecture, scale, recurring visual motifs, Zone geometry, lighting, damage, bodies, debris, opened routes, discovered secrets, map reveal order, inhabitants, dialogue, and player alterations. Panels may not reveal unobserved topology or hidden occupants to a viewpoint that lacked that knowledge.

---
