## 27. Party Recruitment, Relationships, Autonomy, Loyalty, Conflict, and Team Abilities

### Party freedom contract

- Any sapient actor may potentially become an ally, enemy, companion, employee, dependent, rival, lover, follower, minion, or party member when causally possible.
- No character becomes recruitable merely because the player completed their quest or defeated them.
- Recruited characters remain autonomous people with their own Drives, knowledge, inventory, relationships, secrets, morality, and plans.
- Companions may disagree, refuse, negotiate, leave, betray, die, become unavailable, pursue separate goals, or oppose the protagonist.
- The party may be heroic, villainous, mercenary, familial, political, divine, criminal, cultic, absurd, or internally divided.
- There is no arbitrary party-size slot limit.
- Friendship, loyalty, romance, fear, obligation, employment, command, ownership, and supernatural control are mechanically separate.
- A social roll cannot convert a person into inventory.

### Relationship graph

Relationships are directed. Character A's feelings and obligations toward Character B need not match B's relationship toward A.

Every significant Relationship Record contains:

- subject and target;
- relationship types and public labels;
- origin and shared-history events;
- Trust, Affinity, Respect, and Loyalty;
- Fear and Obligation;
- promises, contracts, debts, oaths, and boundaries;
- known and hidden grievances;
- intimacy and consent state when relevant;
- secrets shared or withheld;
- current communication and physical access;
- last meaningful interaction;
- character knowledge and player visibility; and
- dialogue and visual continuity.

Only meaningful relationships receive full records. Casual acquaintances may use a lighter attitude state until events justify expansion.

Exact private values are not automatically shown to the player. The interface presents only observable behavior, disclosed feelings, valid Insight conclusions, or information available from a permitted viewpoint.

### Relationship dimensions

| Dimension | Range | Meaning |
|---|---:|---|
| Trust | -10 to 20 | Belief that the target is truthful, reliable, and safe to depend upon |
| Affinity | -10 to 20 | Personal fondness, care, enjoyment, attachment, or aversion |
| Respect | -10 to 20 | Regard for competence, principles, strength, identity, or achievements |
| Loyalty | -10 to 20 | Willingness to prioritize and remain committed to the target |
| Fear | 0 to 20 | Expected danger or punishment from the target |
| Obligation | 0 to 20 | Debt, oath, contract, duty, or perceived responsibility owed to the target |

Fear and Obligation may produce compliance. They do not count as affection, trust, or voluntary loyalty.

Loyalty states are:

| Loyalty | State |
|---:|---|
| -10 to -6 | Hostile or actively betraying |
| -5 to -1 | Disloyal or opposed |
| 0 to 2 | Uncommitted |
| 3 to 6 | Cooperative |
| 7 to 11 | Loyal |
| 12 to 17 | Devoted |
| 18 to 20 | Defining commitment |

Even Defining commitment is not unrestricted obedience. A companion may refuse an order to protect the relationship, preserve the team, honor a higher Drive, or avoid an explicit red line.

### Relationship changes

| Event | Typical change |
|---|---:|
| Meaningful care, honesty, competence, or shared enjoyment | +1 to the relevant dimension |
| Costly loyalty, protection, vulnerability, or fulfilled promise | +2 |
| Identity-defining shared act | +3 |
| Meaningful neglect, disrespect, deception, or disappointment | -1 |
| Serious exploitation, abandonment, humiliation, or broken promise | -2 |
| Identity-defining betrayal or deliberate destruction | -3 |

For each affected dimension:

`NewRelationshipValue = clamp(DimensionMinimum, DimensionMaximum, CurrentValue + EventChange)`

One event changes any single dimension by no more than three. It may affect several dimensions when the causes are genuinely distinct.

Repeated gifts, compliments, apologies, victories, threats, or scripted conversations do not generate unlimited progress. Each change requires a meaningful new event, cost, risk, revelation, or altered circumstance.

Fear changes through credible demonstrated power and perceived willingness to use it—not dialogue tone alone. Obligation requires an actual debt, agreement, law, oath, rescue, sacrifice, or duty.

### Recruitment offer

Every recruitment attempt defines exact terms:

- proposed purpose and objectives;
- expected role;
- duration;
- compensation, loot share, housing, treatment, or support;
- known risks;
- secrecy requirements;
- command and decision authority;
- resource and equipment ownership;
- moral, personal, or operational boundaries;
- exit rights and notice;
- obligations after departure; and
- promises made by each side.

Before rolling, recruitment is classified as:

- **Automatic acceptance:** the candidate already wishes to join under the offered terms.
- **Uncertain:** acceptance is possible but meaningfully contested.
- **Impossible as offered:** the terms violate a nonnegotiable boundary or lack a necessary causal condition.
- **Coerced:** compliance is being pursued through threats, captivity, domination, blackmail, or overwhelming necessity rather than voluntary recruitment.

Only uncertain voluntary recruitment uses the normal recruitment check.

### Recruitment resolution

Positive and negative relationship support are:

`PositiveRelationshipSum = max(0, Trust) + max(0, Affinity) + max(0, Respect) + max(0, Loyalty)`

`RelationshipSupport = clamp(0, 5, floor[PositiveRelationshipSum / 12])`

`NegativeRelationshipSum = max(0, -Trust) + max(0, -Affinity) + max(0, -Respect) + max(0, -Loyalty)`

`RelationshipResistance = clamp(0, 5, ceil[NegativeRelationshipSum / 8])`

Additional factors are rated from zero through five:

`RecruitmentResistance = Risk + OpportunityCost + GoalConflict + ExistingObligations + RelationshipResistance`

`ObligationSupport = clamp(0, 5, floor[Obligation / 4])`

`RecruitmentSupport = SharedPurpose + Benefit + RelationshipSupport + ContractSecurity + ImmediateNeed + ObligationSupport`

`RecruitmentDC = clamp(5, 40, 15 + RecruitmentResistance - RecruitmentSupport)`

The approach determines whether the roll uses Influence, Leadership, Deception, Intimidation, Streetwise, Bureaucracy, a relevant professional skill, or another valid pairing.

| Outcome | Result |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | Candidate rejects the offer and one method-supported consequence occurs |
| Failure | Candidate refuses or provides a supported counteroffer, barrier, or required change |
| Success | Candidate accepts the exact committed terms |
| Strong success | Acceptance plus one minor reservation resolved or `+1` Trust or Respect when justified |
| Exceptional success | Acceptance plus two such supported benefits |

No outcome crosses an impossible boundary.

Successful Deception recruits the candidate under the exact false belief established. It grants no automatic Trust increase and creates a discoverable future breach.

Successful Intimidation creates coerced service, not voluntary membership or Loyalty.

### Membership and party governance

Membership states include:

- Contact;
- Temporary Ally;
- Contractor or Hireling;
- Probationary Associate;
- Member;
- Core Companion;
- Dependent or Protected Person;
- Summoned or Constructed Support;
- Coerced Associate;
- Captive or Prisoner; and
- Former Member.

Captives and prisoners are not party members merely because they travel with the group.

Every Membership Record includes:

- join event and status;
- agreed purpose and terms;
- role and expected duties;
- rights and decision authority;
- compensation and ownership rules;
- command structure;
- protected boundaries;
- private objectives;
- leave conditions;
- current availability; and
- relationship with the party as an identity.

Governance may use consensus, elected leadership, designated command, contract, seniority, divine hierarchy, rotating authority, distributed autonomy, or coercive rule.

Governance determines procedure and legitimate Authority Support. It does not erase personal agency.

### Requests, orders, and refusal

Routine actions clearly within accepted duties and current plans require no roll.

A request that carries meaningful personal cost or conflict uses:

`RequestDC = clamp(5, 40, 10 + PersonalRisk + DriveConflict + MoralConflict + PersonalCost + GrievancePressure - TrustSupport - LoyaltySupport - SharedPurpose - AuthoritySupport - CompensationSupport)`

Each component ranges from zero through five.

`TrustSupport = clamp(0, 5, floor[max(0, Trust) / 4])`

`LoyaltySupport = clamp(0, 5, floor[max(0, Loyalty) / 4])`

Authority Support applies only when the requester legitimately possesses authority recognized by the companion's agreement, law, oath, office, or command structure.

| Outcome | Response |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | Refusal plus a supported grievance, confrontation, warning, or withdrawal |
| Failure | Refusal, delay, or counterproposal |
| Success | Companion willingly agrees |
| Strong success | Agreement plus one valid tactical, informational, or preparation improvement |
| Exceptional success | Agreement plus two improvements |

A command remains a request unless an explicit supernatural control, constructed obedience, legal power, or other coercive mechanism changes that boundary.

The Commander's **Direct** ability continues to require a willing ally.

### Loyalty pressure, departure, and betrayal

A Loyalty Check occurs only after a material trigger, such as:

- serious violation of membership terms;
- unpaid compensation;
- a defining betrayal;
- repeated endangerment or exploitation;
- irreconcilable Drive conflict;
- completion of the member's purpose;
- a credible alternative allegiance;
- leader defeat or disappearance;
- an opportunity to escape coercion; or
- a major change in party identity.

`ActiveGrievancePressure = min(10, SumOfActiveGrievanceSeverities)`

`DeparturePressure = clamp(0, 15, ActiveGrievancePressure + TermViolation + DriveConflict + PersonalDanger + ExternalPull - ResolvedSupport)`

`CommitmentModifier = floor(Loyalty / 3)`

`LoyaltyCheck = d20 + WillpowerMod + CommitmentModifier + ContractSupport`

`LoyaltyDC = 10 + DeparturePressure`

Contract Support ranges from zero through five.

| Outcome | Commitment boundary |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | Immediate rupture; the companion leaves, defects, exposes, sabotages, or retaliates only as supported by Drives and opportunity |
| Failure | Refuses the triggering demand and withdraws or leaves at the next viable opportunity |
| Success | Remains provisionally while the conflict and grievance persist |
| Strong success | Remains by choice and clearly communicates the boundary or required repair |
| Exceptional success | Reaffirms commitment against this trigger; underlying injuries are not automatically erased |

If staying or leaving is already certain from an explicit Drive, red line, domination effect, or impossible circumstance, no roll overrides it.

Departure does not automatically mean betrayal. Betrayal requires an actual motive, target, method, knowledge, and opportunity. It cannot occur merely because a dramatic twist is desired.

### Fear, coercion, minions, and control

Intimidation may secure one bounded act or period of compliance when leverage is credible. It does not create Loyalty.

Coerced actors record:

- coercer and leverage;
- exact demand;
- Fear;
- known escape or resistance opportunities;
- prohibited and permitted actions;
- retaliation or disclosure plans;
- duration; and
- conditions that end the coercion.

Coerced sapient characters remain autonomous within those constraints. They may deceive, perform minimally, sabotage indirectly, seek help, escape, or retaliate when their knowledge and options permit.

Mindless constructs, automated systems, and entities with no independent Self may follow direct control rules. Sapient summons, undead, artificial minds, dominated people, and created beings retain personhood and agency unless an explicit control mechanic states its exact limitations.

A purchased summon or minion ability purchases access to the defined effect. It does not grant undocumented ownership of a person.

### Grievances, conflict, and repair

A meaningful interpersonal injury creates a Grievance Record containing:

- source event and responsible parties;
- Severity `1–3`;
- what the injured party believes occurred;
- evidence and uncertainty;
- Trust, Affinity, Respect, or Loyalty damage;
- desired acknowledgement, restitution, boundary, or change;
- willingness to reconcile;
- public and private state; and
- resolution or transformation conditions.

`GrievancePressure = min(5, SumOfRelevantActiveGrievanceSeverities)`

Arguments and social conflict do not inflict Composure damage unless an explicit extraordinary effect applies.

A negotiated repair may use:

`RepairProgressRequired = 2 + 2 * GrievanceSeverity`

| Repair event | Progress |
|---|---:|
| Meaningful acknowledgement or first corrective act | +1 |
| Costly restitution or demonstrated change | +2 |
| Defining sacrifice, truth, or irreversible repair | +3 |

An apology may begin repair but does not automatically erase harm. If the injured party accepted repair terms in advance, completion resolves the Grievance as agreed. Otherwise, completion creates a valid reconciliation decision point rather than compulsory forgiveness.

A character may forgive early, refuse reconciliation, redefine the relationship, or preserve the grievance when their actual agency supports it.

### Romance, intimacy, and family

Romance and sexual intimacy are not unlocked by reaching a numeric threshold.

A relevant Relationship Record separately tracks:

- possible romantic or sexual interest;
- adult eligibility and compatibility;
- current consent;
- expressed boundaries;
- exclusivity or relationship agreements;
- existing commitments;
- public or private status;
- intimacy history; and
- desired pace and future.

Influence, Performance, or another social check may communicate interest, create a favorable interaction, or clarify mutual attraction. It cannot compel desire, consent, romance, sex, marriage, pregnancy, exclusivity, or forgiveness.

Consent must be contemporaneous and may be withdrawn. Coercion, domination, deception about material facts, intoxication, captivity, or inability to consent is resolved as its actual harmful situation—not relabeled as romance.

NPCs may initiate relationships, set boundaries, request commitment, experience changed feelings, end relationships, or reject the protagonist.

Jealousy, monogamy, polyamory, marriage, family, parenthood, mentorship, and cultural or divine relationship structures follow the specific characters and agreements rather than universal assumptions.

### Party size and coordination

Membership has no hard numerical cap. Active coordination does.

`CoordinationCapacity = 2 + LeadershipMasteryRank + floor[max(0, PresenceMod) / 2] + InfrastructureBonus`

- Leadership Mastery Rank ranges from zero through four.
- Infrastructure Bonus ranges from zero through five and may represent communications, doctrine, staff, magical linkage, command systems, or rehearsed organization.

Default Coordination Load is:

| Active participant | Load |
|---|---:|
| Independent member | 1 |
| Member requiring continuous direction or support | 2 |
| Self-directing squad represented through its leader | 1 at the higher command layer |

`CoordinationStrain = max(0, ActiveCoordinationLoad - CoordinationCapacity)`

`CoordinationDCModifier = min(5, 2 * CoordinationStrain)`

While Coordination Strain is positive:

- time-sensitive party coordination receives Setback;
- the Coordination DC Modifier applies to one relevant command, formation, or synchronized-action check;
- large combined abilities require delegation or reduced participants; and
- individual characters retain their normal actions, statistics, and agency.

Delegated squad leaders calculate their own internal capacity. Large organizations eventually operate through the faction system rather than pretending one protagonist directly controls everyone.

### Roles, property, and logistics

Party roles may include leader, strategist, scout, face, medic, quartermaster, operator, guard, researcher, ritualist, infiltrator, heavy support, legal representative, or custom functions.

Roles grant no exclusive permission unless a feature, credential, contract, or command structure provides it.

Party resources retain exact ownership:

- personal property;
- loaned property;
- shared party property;
- employer or faction property;
- captured or stolen property;
- entrusted Relics;
- communal funds; and
- consumable operational stores.

Joining the party does not transfer a companion's money, gear, home, vehicle, contacts, Relics, intellectual property, body, soul, or followers.

Loot division, compensation, maintenance, housing, healthcare, resurrection costs, legal defense, and dependent support follow recorded terms. Breaching them may create debt, Grievances, departure pressure, or legal consequences.

### Companion autonomy during play

Companion operation may use:

| Mode | Boundary |
|---|---|
| Autonomous | Companion agent selects actions from its knowledge, Drives, doctrine, and current situation |
| Planned Doctrine | Companion follows previously accepted priorities and contingencies |
| Tactical Delegation | Player may select valid actions within authority voluntarily delegated by the companion |
| Explicit Control | Direct control exists only through a recorded construct, possession, domination, hive, summon, or equivalent rule |

Tactical Delegation can be refused or revoked when new information, a red line, self-preservation, or a conflicting Drive makes the chosen action unacceptable.

The player always controls the protagonist's voluntary decisions. A companion never takes control of the protagonist through narration.

Companions may:

- initiate conversation or plans;
- conceal information;
- disagree with interpretation;
- act before being asked;
- protect themselves or others;
- use personal resources;
- surrender or retreat;
- pursue private objectives; and
- make mistakes based on actual beliefs.

### Split parties and off-screen action

A companion does not freeze when outside the protagonist's current scene.

Every separated member records:

- location and route;
- objective;
- intended method;
- time budget;
- resources and companions;
- communication availability;
- known threats;
- stop and retreat conditions; and
- next expected contact.

Off-screen actions use the same rules, rolls, time, evidence, and consequences as on-screen actions. Meaningful results enter the canonical ledger before later narration references them.

The player receives only communicated information or information available through an authorized viewpoint. A companion's private scene may be shown directly only when the player has legitimately assumed that viewpoint or the comic adaptation later uses an appropriate perspective.

### Companion advancement

Each companion retains individual:

- Level and XP;
- Build Points;
- Attributes and Mastery;
- powers and equipment;
- Wounds and resources;
- Drives and Complications;
- morality and reputation; and
- Advancement Checkpoints.

XP is never divided among party members. Contribution is scored independently.

The existing catch-up multiplier applies to companions below the party Reference Level. Recruitment does not automatically raise or lower anyone's Level.

At Advancement Checkpoints, an autonomous companion chooses development consistent with its experience, access, Drives, Source, and plans. The player may advise or receive build delegation, but cannot silently rewrite the companion for optimization.

Equipment purchased for a companion follows its actual ownership terms. Leaving the party does not automatically return gifts or transfer personal equipment.

### Team identity, Cohesion, and Integration

A persistent Team Record contains:

- current and former names;
- founding event;
- purpose and public identity;
- symbol, uniform, base, and visual language;
- governance;
- roster and former members;
- shared assets and obligations;
- reputation and legal status;
- enemies, allies, and faction ties;
- Team Marks and Rank;
- Cohesion;
- member Integration;
- Team Abilities; and
- schisms, succession, dissolution, or revival history.

Team Cohesion ranges from zero through twenty:

| Cohesion | State |
|---:|---|
| 0–2 | Fragmented or merely assembled |
| 3–6 | Formed |
| 7–11 | Coordinated |
| 12–17 | Proven |
| 18–20 | Legendary unity |

An intentionally founded team normally begins at Cohesion 3. An accidental traveling group begins at zero.

Cohesion changes use the same `±1`, `±2`, and `±3` meaningful-event scale as relationships. High Cohesion represents coordination and shared identity—not universal friendship or morality.

Each member has Integration from zero through ten:

| Integration | State |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Outsider |
| 1–2 | Newcomer |
| 3–5 | Integrated |
| 6–8 | Veteran |
| 9–10 | Pillar |

Integration grows through actual participation, training, communication, and shared history. A newcomer does not inherit perfect familiarity because the team has high Cohesion.

### Team Marks and Rank

A team may earn:

| Team-defining event | Marks |
|---|---:|
| Meaningful coordinated achievement | +1 |
| Costly shared victory, rescue, or commitment | +2 |
| Identity-defining or world-significant team event | +3 |

One objective grants no more than three Team Marks. Routine training, staged danger, repeated weak encounters, and self-manufactured crises grant none.

Team Rank uses:

`TeamRankThreshold(R) = R(R + 5) / 2`

| Team Rank | Marks required | Team Ability Slots |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 0 | 0 |
| 1 | 3 | 1 |
| 2 | 7 | 2 |
| 3 | 12 | 3 |
| 4 | 18 | 4 |
| 5 | 25 | 5 |

Team Rank records earned history. It does not raise member Level, Attributes, Power Tier, or resources.

Falling Cohesion may make Team Abilities dormant without deleting earned Rank. A reformed or splinter team must establish a valid identity, roster, history, and succession claim rather than automatically copying the original team.

### Team Abilities

A Team Ability records:

- Rank `1–5`;
- required members or qualifying roles;
- minimum Cohesion and Integration;
- positions, range, communication, and timing;
- required actions and Reactions;
- contributed powers, skills, equipment, or authority;
- resource and Momentum costs;
- effect components;
- failure and interruption rules;
- substitution rules; and
- visual choreography.

Requirements are:

`TeamAbilityRank <= TeamRank`

`RequiredCohesion = 3 * TeamAbilityRank`

Every participant requires:

`MemberIntegration >= TeamAbilityRank`

Construction capacity is:

`TeamAbilityCapacity = 2 + TeamAbilityRank + min(3, RequiredParticipants - 1)`

Effect components use the approved Universal Stunt component costs. Every component must be causally supplied by participating members, their equipment, environment, or shared infrastructure.

Team Abilities may include:

- Setup-and-payoff sequences;
- rescue or interposition;
- formations;
- synchronized movement;
- combined attacks;
- joint rituals;
- shared defenses;
- coordinated stealth;
- planned retreats;
- social or investigative combinations;
- resource or Momentum transfer; and
- vehicle, Relic, or Realm maneuvers.

A Team Ability cannot create a Power Source no participant possesses or bypass Tier and Scale without an explicit combination rule.

For a compatible Combination Technique:

`CombinationBonus = min(TeamAbilityRank, floor[AdditionalQualifyingContributors / 2])`

`CombinedEffectRank = min(5, HighestContributedEffectRank + CombinationBonus)`

`CombinedScale = min(5, HighestContributedScale + CombinationBonus)`

Every contributor pays the recorded action and resource cost. Missing, unwilling, incapacitated, out-of-position, or disconnected required members make the ability unavailable unless its substitution rule applies.

Unlearned combinations remain possible through the Universal Stunt system.

### Death, departure, replacement, and Legacy

A dead, captured, transformed, estranged, or departed companion does not respawn or disappear from history.

- Recovery requires an actual rescue, resurrection, negotiation, escape, repair, or reconciliation.
- A resurrection may return the original Self, a Replica, a Composite, or another approved classification.
- A former enemy may be recruited when actual events support it.
- A former companion may return with changed relationships and goals.
- A replacement recruit does not inherit another person's Loyalty, Integration, equipment, memories, romance, or Team Ability role.
- Team Abilities requiring an absent member become dormant until adapted through training and a Team Advancement Checkpoint.
- Legacy may preserve a successor bond, instruction, institution, memory, oath, team identity, or recovery route without compelling the successor or surviving companions.

A lost Background Contact Slot remains a Broken Connection under the existing rule. Recruiting that Contact never meant owning them.

### Party XP, event sourcing, and comic continuity

Recruiting, reconciling with, rescuing, betraying, losing, or transforming a companion may earn objective, moral, Drive, Complication, or other existing XP when it represents a genuine accomplishment or cost. Ordinary conversation, repeated gifts, relationship-score farming, staged arguments, and cycling members in and out grant no XP.

Using a Team Ability grants no separate automatic XP. Characters receive ordinary contribution for the accomplishment it helped produce.

Every party event records:

- participants and viewpoints;
- exact dialogue;
- offers, terms, promises, and refusals;
- social checks and outcome boundaries;
- relationship changes and supporting evidence;
- private versus disclosed knowledge;
- Grievances and repair;
- membership and governance changes;
- Loyalty triggers and checks;
- orders, consent, coercion, and delegated authority;
- team Marks, Cohesion, Integration, and abilities;
- inventory and ownership changes;
- departures, betrayals, injuries, deaths, and transformations; and
- visual, costume, blocking, and emotional continuity.

Comic adaptation cannot expose private motives, romantic interest, secret betrayal plans, or exact hidden relationship values before an appropriate viewpoint learned them. Foreshadowing must come from actual recorded behavior, dialogue, evidence, or visual tells rather than retroactive invention.

---

## 28. World Simulation, Factions, Fronts, Law, Evidence, Reputation, and Information Propagation

### Simulation contract

- The world continues changing when the player is absent.
- Every material change requires a timestamp, cause, capable actor or natural process, and committed inputs.
- Factions know only information that reached an authorized member, system, archive, or sensor.
- Importance affects simulation detail—not success, immunity, or access to information.
- Factions do not scale automatically to the player.
- Ignored threats may progress only through their recorded actors, processes, and triggers.
- Destroyed plans remain destroyed. Prepared content cannot relocate itself in front of the player.
- Random outcomes use stable seeds and cannot be rerolled by reloading or changing viewpoint.

### World update order

At each timestamp, resolve:

1. automatic environmental, physiological, and scheduled consequences;
2. actions and project intervals already committed;
3. simultaneous contests;
4. resulting damage, costs, movement, evidence, and resource changes;
5. information deliveries;
6. Front, legal, reputation, relationship, and adaptation updates; and
7. newly selected autonomous actions.

Actors cannot use information generated during a simultaneous event until a valid delivery occurs afterward. Stable event IDs resolve otherwise irrelevant ordering ties.

### Simulation detail

| State | Resolution |
|---|---|
| Foreground | Exact actions, dialogue, checks, positions, resources, and reactions |
| Active | Exact consequential projects, schedules, travel, conflicts, and information |
| Background | Routine operations compressed through committed rates and scheduled change points |
| Archived | No unresolved active process; only external events or scheduled triggers can reactivate it |

Changing detail never creates new assets, motives, evidence, or victories. Backfilled background activity must follow previously committed rates, resources, seeds, and access.

### World actor types

- **Individual:** one autonomous person or equivalent Self.
- **Faction:** a persistent organization capable of coordinated action.
- **Division:** a faction node with its own leadership, knowledge, assets, and interests.
- **Institution:** a faction operating through formal offices, laws, or procedures.
- **Coalition:** cooperating factions that retain separate knowledge and resources.
- **Project:** an intended effort with costs, progress, and an output.
- **Front:** an evolving situation that may involve multiple actors or no directing intelligence.
- **Audience Node:** a community or network through which information and reputation propagate.

A faction is not a hive mind unless its canonical nature explicitly makes it one.

### Faction record

Every significant faction records:

- identity, origin, Scale, reach, and visual language;
- leadership, succession, divisions, and internal conflicts;
- goals, ideology, Drives, Taboos, doctrine, and priorities;
- members, specialists, agents, and controlled creatures;
- locations, territory, routes, facilities, and jurisdiction;
- exact assets, inventories, funds, income, and obligations;
- capabilities and their causal limits;
- projects, schedules, Front involvement, and response plans;
- allies, enemies, treaties, debts, and dependencies;
- public position versus private plans;
- Knowledge Maps, Case Graphs, archives, and compartmentalization;
- Heat, threat models, countermeasures, and adaptation;
- legitimacy, reputation, propaganda, and known identities; and
- damage, losses, evidence, witnesses, and event history.

### Faction Scale and capabilities

| Scale | Operational span |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Cell, household, crew, or single site |
| 1 | Local community or neighborhood |
| 2 | City or major institution |
| 3 | Region or small nation |
| 4 | Nation or single realm |
| 5 | Global or multirealm |
| 6 | Civilizational or cosmological |

Scale measures coordination and reach, not combat power. A Scale-0 cult may possess a world-threatening Relic.

Capabilities range from `0–10` and normally include Force, Security, Intelligence, Influence, Logistics, Wealth, Research, and Esoteric Authority. Tags define their actual domain. Esoteric Authority 8 over funerary law does not grant unrelated weather magic.

`AvailableCapability = clamp(0, 10, BaseCapability - Damage - Commitments + TemporarySupport)`

Ratings summarize actual assets; they never generate missing personnel, money, vehicles, access, or supernatural authority.

### Autonomous priorities

For each feasible action known to the deciding node:

`PriorityScore = 2 * GoalFit + Urgency + Opportunity + Obligation + RelationshipPressure - Cost - Risk - InternalConflict`

Each input ranges from `0–5`.

The highest-scoring action is normally selected. Ties use recorded doctrine and then a stable seed. Leadership may override the result through a recorded decision.

Unknown facts cannot contribute. A faction cannot plan around the player's hidden Level, unobserved Technique, secret relationship, or unread inventory.

### Faction actions and projects

For uncertain faction-scale action:

`ScaleRelevance = clamp(-5, 5, 2 * [FactionScale - RequiredScale])`

Scale Relevance applies only when volume, reach, or coordination matters.

`FactionCheck = d20 + Capability + AssetBonus + LeadershipBonus + ScaleRelevance + SituationModifier`

- Asset Bonus: `0–5`
- Leadership Bonus: `0–5`
- Situation Modifier: `-5` through `+5`

Use an opposed roll when another active actor directly contests the action. Otherwise use a committed DC.

Long projects use:

`FactionProjectDC = clamp(5, 40, 10 + 2C + 2R + Security + Novelty - Leverage)`

`FactionProjectProgressRequired = 3 + 2S + C + R`

- Scope `S`: `0–6`
- Complexity `C`, Resistance `R`, Security, Novelty, and Leverage: `0–5`

The approved extended-task outcomes apply. Each interval consumes declared time and resources. Completion creates only the committed output.

Routine, adequately resourced operations require no roll.

### Leadership, divisions, and coalitions

Orders require authority and a functioning information path. Delay, refusal, sabotage, misunderstanding, or unauthorized improvisation remains possible.

A division does not automatically share:

- discoveries;
- classified files;
- budgets;
- legal authority;
- grudges;
- secret projects; or
- leadership's private intentions.

Coalitions coordinate through explicit agreements, liaison channels, and shared projects. Joining a coalition does not merge faction inventories or knowledge.

### Faction relationships

Directed faction relationships record:

| Dimension | Range |
|---|---:|
| Standing | -20 to 20 |
| Trust | -10 to 20 |
| Fear | 0 to 20 |
| Obligation | 0 to 20 |
| Dependency | 0 to 20 |

One event changes a dimension by no more than three. Repeated trivial diplomacy produces no progress.

`StandingModifier = clamp(-5, 5, sign[Standing] * floor[abs(Standing) / 4])`

This modifier applies only when existing institutional attitude is relevant. Treaties, contracts, debts, and laws remain exact obligations rather than disappearing when Standing changes.

### Territory, control, and jurisdiction

Every consequential territory separately records:

- physical Presence;
- Control from `0–5`;
- asserted Claim and supporting grounds;
- recognized legal or metaphysical Jurisdiction;
- public Recognition;
- access routes and logistics;
- residents and their loyalties;
- surveillance and defenses; and
- competing factions.

Control without Claim may be occupation. Claim without Control may be symbolic. Jurisdiction does not guarantee enforcement capacity.

Winning a battle, killing a ruler, or possessing a throne does not automatically establish government. Actual occupation, succession, recognition, administration, and logistics must follow.

### Resources and logistics

Unique and scarce assets retain exact IDs, quantities, locations, custody, ownership, condition, and commitments.

- Committed assets are unavailable elsewhere.
- Reinforcements must mobilize and travel.
- Casualties, theft, sabotage, consumption, and route closures reduce real capacity.
- Wealth ratings cannot purchase an unavailable item or unwilling service.
- Recruitment creates actual members with agency and schedules.
- Production requires facilities, inputs, labor, and time.
- An organization cannot secretly replace losses merely because its faction profile remains active.

### Fronts and clocks

A Front records:

- situation and scope;
- participating and affected actors;
- active clocks;
- advancement and regression triggers;
- resources and projects feeding each clock;
- current public and private knowledge;
- branching completion effects;
- available countermeasures; and
- successor or resolution conditions.

Each clock has a maximum from `3–12`.

`FrontPressure = max_i ceil(12 * ClockProgress_i / ClockMaximum_i)`

| Pressure | State |
|---:|---|
| 0–2 | Latent |
| 3–5 | Emerging |
| 6–8 | Escalating |
| 9–11 | Crisis |
| 12 | Transition |

Pressure is a summary, not an independent clock. It advances only when recorded triggers occur. At 12, the clock's precommitted completion effect resolves; it may end the Front, change it, or create a successor Front.

### Information objects

Every consequential communication creates an Information Packet recording:

- exact claim or representation;
- speaker, source, observer, and provenance;
- creation and observation timestamps;
- underlying evidence or record IDs;
- accuracy, ambiguity, omissions, and deliberate alterations;
- language and interpretation requirements;
- classification and intended recipients;
- channel, copies, delivery, interception, and deletion;
- recipient-specific confidence;
- identity attribution;
- knowledge-hazard properties; and
- canonical fact links visible only to the engine.

Recipients receive the packet—not canonical truth. A correct statement may be disbelieved; a convincing falsehood may become institutional policy.

### Deliberate communication

Channels record range, latency, Base Reach, capacity, fidelity, security, access requirements, and possible failure modes.

Routine valid communication needs no roll. When delivery is uncertain:

`TransmissionDC = clamp(5, 35, 10 + DistanceDifficulty + EnvironmentalNoise + HostileInterference + AccessDifficulty - ChannelQuality - Preparation)`

Each modifier ranges from `0–5`.

| Outcome | Delivery |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | No delivery and one precommitted interception, trace, corruption, or exposure consequence |
| Failure | No delivery or the channel's declared delay |
| Success | Delivered at base fidelity |
| Strong success | Delivered plus one communication benefit |
| Exceptional success | Delivered plus two benefits |

Broadcasts use:

`ReachedAudienceNodes = min(EligibleNodes, BaseReach * OutcomeReachMultiplier)`

Multipliers are `0`, `0`, `1`, `2`, and `3` across the five outcomes.

Failure cannot invent false wording unless corruption or distortion was an existing channel property.

### Rumors, propaganda, and misinformation

For organic spread across one eligible network edge:

`SpreadPressure = Salience + EmotionalCharge + SourceReach + Repetition + Promotion - Friction - Suppression`

Each input ranges from `0–5`.

`RumorSpreadChance = clamp(0, 20, SpreadPressure)`

Roll `d20` once per recorded propagation interval. Success creates a derived packet at the adjacent Audience Node.

Recipient-facing credibility is:

`PacketCredibility = clamp(0, 5, SourceReliability + EvidenceSupport + IndependentCorroboration - Contradiction - ProvenanceLoss)`

Deliberate lies are ordinary false packets. Retelling may mutate a packet only through its committed distortion profile and stable seed. Corrections create competing packets; they do not erase earlier knowledge.

Suppression can block channels, intimidate sources, seize copies, or discredit a claim. It cannot make existing recipients unknow something without a valid memory-altering method.

### Evidence and identification

Evidence records:

- unique ID and proposition supported;
- source and collection method;
- location and timestamp;
- chain of custody;
- Relevance, Reliability, and Integrity from `0–5`;
- causal-correlation group;
- copies and transformations;
- contamination, alteration, and destruction;
- observer assessments;
- jurisdiction-specific admissibility; and
- actors with access.

`EvidenceWeight = min(Relevance, Reliability, Integrity)`

Correlated evidence does not stack at full value:

`GroupWeight = min(5, HighestWeight + floor[SumOfOtherWeights / 3])`

`CaseStrength = clamp(0, 20, SumOfGroupWeights - SustainedContradictionWeight)`

Case Strength is calculated separately for every proposition and required legal element. Evidence that proves an explosion occurred does not automatically identify its cause, actor, or intent.

Observers may assess forged or misleading evidence incorrectly. The engine preserves canonical integrity separately from each observer's belief.

### Separate response tracks

| Track | Range | Meaning |
|---|---:|---|
| Evidence or Case Strength | 0–20 | Support available for one proposition |
| Heat | 0–20 | Attention and resources assigned by one institution |
| Exposure | 0–20 | Distribution of a protected identity, link, or secret |
| Threat Assessment | 0–20 | One observer's perceived danger |
| Notoriety | 0–20 | Audience recognition and salience |
| Infamy | 0–20 | Audience association with condemned acts |
| Jurisdiction | Exact record | Authority to investigate, judge, or enforce |

None substitutes for another. High Heat does not prove guilt. Strong evidence does not create jurisdiction. Public Infamy does not reveal a secret identity.

### Heat, Exposure, and Threat Assessment

When a faction receives actionable information:

`HeatGain = clamp(0, 5, IncidentSeverity + floor[CaseStrength / 5] + PublicPressure + ThreatUrgency - PriorityFriction)`

`NewHeat = clamp(0, 20, CurrentHeat + HeatGain - HeatRelief)`

| Heat | Response state |
|---:|---|
| 0–2 | Background or unprioritized |
| 3–5 | Noted |
| 6–9 | Active investigation |
| 10–13 | Targeted surveillance |
| 14–17 | Enforcement priority |
| 18–20 | Emergency mobilization |

Heat normally loses one point after each faction-specific quiet interval, default seven days. Evidence, warrants, institutional policy, and permanent knowledge do not decay with it.

For a protected identity, operation, or secret:

`ExposureGain = clamp(0, 5, ReachTier + IdentityLink + EvidencePersistence - Containment)`

| Exposure | State |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Secret |
| 1–4 | Traces |
| 5–8 | Suspected |
| 9–12 | Linked |
| 13–16 | Broadly known within the audience |
| 17–20 | Public or structurally distributed |

Threat Assessment is recalculated from known information:

`ThreatAssessment = clamp(0, 20, ObservedCapability + DemonstratedHarm + HostileIntent + Reach + Unpredictability - TrustedRestraints)`

Each input ranges from `0–5`. Hidden character statistics do not contribute.

For travel routes actually monitored by the faction:

`FactionAttention = clamp(0, 5, floor[Heat / 4] + floor[ThreatAssessment / 10] - JurisdictionFriction)`

### Reputation and public identity

Reputation is directed toward a specific Audience Node and records:

- Notoriety `0–20`;
- Esteem `-20–20`;
- Fear `0–20`;
- Credibility `0–20`;
- Infamy `0–20`; and
- attributed identities, deeds, titles, and narratives.

For a propagated event:

`AttributionQuality = min(IdentityConfidence, PacketCredibility)`

`ReputationMagnitude = min(3, round[Impact * AttributionQuality / 8])`

Impact and Attribution Quality range from `0–5`.

Audience values determine which axes change. Cruelty may increase Infamy and reduce Esteem among civilians while increasing Esteem within a predatory cult. A deed cannot alter an audience that never learned of it.

Hero, villain, savior, terrorist, god, monster, fraud, and similar labels remain audience-specific social identities.

### Laws and legal codes

Every enforceable law records:

- creating authority and effective dates;
- territorial, personal, divine, technological, or realm jurisdiction;
- protected interest;
- prohibited act and required elements;
- required intent or responsibility standard;
- exceptions, licenses, defenses, immunities, and emergency provisions;
- investigative and enforcement authorities;
- admissibility and procedural rules;
- evidence thresholds;
- penalties, remedies, forfeiture, and appeal;
- extradition and jurisdiction conflicts; and
- whether the law is public, secret, disputed, or illegitimate.

There is no universal crime flag. One incident may be lawful in one jurisdiction, criminal in another, and morally assessed independently of both.

Default procedural Case Strength bands are:

| Strength | Default significance |
|---:|---|
| 0–2 | Unsupported |
| 3–5 | Lead or inquiry |
| 6–9 | Reasonable suspicion or formal investigation |
| 10–12 | Probable cause or coercive authorization |
| 13–15 | Charge or committal |
| 16–19 | Conviction standard |
| 20 | Overwhelming support |

Jurisdictions may use different or unjust thresholds, but must record them beforehand. Every required offense element must independently meet its threshold.

### Investigation, enforcement, and adjudication

The legal process is:

1. incident;
2. report, detection, or complaint;
3. jurisdiction assessment;
4. Case creation;
5. suspect identification;
6. evidence collection;
7. authorization, warrant, or equivalent authority;
8. mobilization and enforcement;
9. adjudication; and
10. sanction, remedy, dismissal, escape, or appeal.

A warrant grants procedural authority; it does not teleport officers, guarantee arrest, or establish guilt.

Arrest, search, pursuit, resistance, surrender, bribery, escape, and combat use their existing systems. Advocacy may change interpretation, credibility, procedure, or sentence, but cannot manufacture evidence.

Corruption, false accusation, prejudiced law, intimidated witnesses, fabricated evidence, and wrongful conviction are possible only through recorded actors and actions.

### Confinement, seizure, and player freedom

- Confinement is a persistent Site with guards, schedules, laws, inventory controls, communications, factions, and escape possibilities.
- A sentence advances actual world time and does not freeze projects or companions.
- The player may comply, appeal, bargain, escape, resist, transform the institution, or accept consequences.
- Seizure changes custody according to the legal order. It does not automatically erase ownership, Relic agency, Claim, Bond, Attunement, or hidden caches.
- Execution, exile, memory alteration, soul confinement, Name seizure, or divine punishment requires explicit jurisdiction and a causally capable method.
- No arrest or trial becomes a forced cutscene that disables otherwise legal actions.

### Bounded adaptation

The existing formula remains:

`DeltaAdaptation = W + ceil(K / 5) + 2E + 3S + 2P`

It is now evaluated separately for each faction and identified threat model.

- `W` applies only when a credible packet identifies the actor.
- Casualties and losses count only when the faction learns of and attributes their unique event IDs.
- Public humiliation requires propagation to a relevant audience.
- Duplicate reports do not award duplicate adaptation.
- False attribution may cause a faction to adapt against the wrong identity.
- Countermeasures require actual knowledge, specialists, procurement, projects, and deployment.
- Adaptation permits better response; it does not conjure resources or increase unrelated civilian Levels.

### XP, event sourcing, and comic continuity

Completing or disrupting a consequential faction project, resolving a Front, exposing a conspiracy, surviving enforcement, changing a law, establishing a coalition, or transforming public knowledge may qualify for existing objective, Exploration, Survival, moral, Drive, or Complication XP.

Repeated rumor seeding, fabricated self-opposition, trivial arrests, or cycling the same reputation event awards nothing.

Every simulation event records:

- timestamp and causal event IDs;
- deciding actor and knowledge available beforehand;
- chosen priority and alternatives;
- assets, costs, checks, seeds, and progress;
- Front and territorial changes;
- created and delivered Information Packets;
- evidence and chain of custody;
- Heat, Exposure, threat, law, and reputation changes;
- witnesses, relationships, casualties, and resource losses; and
- public versus private comic viewpoint.

Comic adaptation may depict propaganda, rumors, allegations, and mistaken beliefs, but cannot silently present them as canonical truth.

---

## 29. Agent Contracts, RAG Memory, Activation Budgets, and Validation

### Agent architecture contract

- Generative agents propose interpretations, dialogue, plans, and actions.
- Deterministic rules, validators, dice, and reducers decide what becomes canon.
- No agent directly writes canonical state.
- Persistent character identity lives in structured records—not a model session.
- Changing models cannot erase memories, alter personality, or grant knowledge.
- Compute limits may affect latency and descriptive depth, but never causality or legal outcomes.
- Every accepted action remains reproducible from its inputs, rules, seed, and committed proposal.

### Agent and service roles

| Role | Function | Authority boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Intent Interpreter | Translates player language into possible actions | Cannot choose intent or commit outcomes |
| Rules Arbiter | Resolves formulas, prerequisites, and legal actions | Deterministic service rather than a persona |
| Scheduler | Activates due actors and processes | Cannot invent motives or actions |
| Scene Director | Organizes presentation and pacing | Cannot relocate content or expose hidden state |
| Narrator | Presents validated sensory and event information | Cannot establish uncommitted facts |
| NPC/Companion Agent | Chooses for one autonomous character | Limited to that character's knowledge and authority |
| Relic Agent | Represents an Agency-3 or Agency-4 Relic | Cannot spend bearer resources without permission |
| Faction Agent | Plans for one authorized faction node | Limited to that node's knowledge, assets, and jurisdiction |
| Front Agent | Proposes behavior for an evolving non-person situation | Limited to recorded clocks and triggers |
| Comic Agent | Produces adaptation drafts from approved events | Draft-only; detailed in the next design block |

A generative agent receives the smallest truth slice required for its task. It never receives unrestricted global canon merely because it helps run the game.

### Canon and memory strata

| Stratum | Status |
|---|---|
| Event Ledger | Authoritative, append-only campaign history |
| Canon Snapshot | Current state deterministically reduced from events |
| Truth Records | Committed hidden identities, secrets, motives, and world facts |
| Knowledge and Belief Records | What each actor has observed, learned, inferred, or misunderstood |
| Retrieval Index | Rebuildable search cache with no canonical authority |
| Working Context | Ephemeral context for one activation |
| Narrative or Comic Draft | Noncanonical until its state-bearing claims are validated |

Corrections use new events, migrations, or explicit supersession. They never overwrite history invisibly.

### Agent contract

Every autonomous agent has a versioned contract containing:

- Agent and represented-entity IDs;
- agent type and permitted roles;
- contract, prompt, retrieval-policy, and model-profile versions;
- Authority Envelope;
- Knowledge Map and classification scope;
- actual senses and communication channels;
- available resources, inventory, and legal authority;
- Drives, Taboos, relationships, loyalties, grievances, and orders;
- default routines and planning horizon;
- activation triggers and deadlines;
- memory profile and recall limitations;
- permitted tools and action schemas;
- deception, disclosure, and secrecy permissions;
- dialogue and visual bible references;
- fallback behavior;
- required validators; and
- next scheduled evaluation.

A contract may narrow an entity's abilities for a specific representation task. It cannot grant abilities or knowledge absent from canon.

### Authority Envelope

Permissions are independently scoped:

- observe;
- speak;
- choose personal actions;
- spend owned resources;
- command authorized subordinates;
- operate assigned assets;
- negotiate or make bounded commitments;
- create Information Packets;
- request a mechanical check;
- propose a faction project;
- offer a Complication;
- generate presentation details; and
- produce noncanonical drafts.

Owning one permission does not imply another. A faction spokesperson may speak without controlling military assets. A companion may act independently without spending party funds.

### Player agency boundary

No agent may:

- select the player character's intent;
- commit the player character's dialogue;
- declare unchosen beliefs, loyalty, attraction, forgiveness, or surrender;
- spend player-controlled resources;
- accept a bargain, oath, romance, transformation, or sacrifice for the player;
- choose advancement purchases; or
- take control through narration.

Explicit possession, compulsion, panic, or control mechanics may constrain the player only within their recorded effects and remaining legal choices.

Automatic deterministic triggers may resolve without confirmation when previously established. Interpretive or identity-changing choices require player input.

### Agent lifecycle

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Dormant | No decision currently due |
| Scheduled | A future activation exists |
| Queued | Triggered and awaiting processing |
| Contextualized | Legal knowledge and current state assembled |
| Proposed | Agent returned a structured proposal |
| Validating | Authority, knowledge, causality, and rules checked |
| Resolving | Dice or deterministic procedures running |
| Committed | Valid event written through reducers |
| Replanning | Proposal rejected with actor-safe feedback |
| Awaiting Input | A player choice or external decision is required |
| Blocked | No legal action or fallback exists |

An agent has no persistent private model state between activations. All continuity comes from external records.

### Mandatory activation triggers

An activation is mandatory when:

- the player directly addresses or acts upon the entity;
- the entity receives an actionable Information Packet;
- its combat turn, Reaction, or immediate choice becomes due;
- a scheduled action or project interval completes;
- a Drive, Taboo, automatic Complication, oath, or order triggers;
- a resource, survival, corruption, or defeat crisis requires a choice;
- an imminent deadline would expire;
- custody, membership, leadership, or jurisdiction changes;
- the entity perceives a qualifying threat or opportunity; or
- an established fallback routine cannot resolve the situation.

Mandatory activations cannot be discarded to save computation.

### Activation priority

Mandatory activations resolve first. Other eligible activations use:

`ActivationPriority = 2U + 2F + G + R + S + K - C`

- Urgency `U`: `0–5`
- Foreground Relevance `F`: `0–5`
- Goal Relevance `G`: `0–5`
- Relationship Relevance `R`: `0–5`
- State Change `S`: `0–5`
- Staleness `K`: `0–5`
- Activation Cost `C`: `1–5`

Stable ties resolve by earliest causal deadline, then entity ID.

Staleness increases only when an otherwise eligible activation remains queued. It cannot create fictional urgency or retroactive action.

### Activation budgets

Activation Cost represents processing depth:

| Cost | Work |
|---:|---|
| 1 | Short reaction, routine choice, or bounded reply |
| 2 | Ordinary dialogue or tactical decision |
| 3 | Multi-source decision or short plan |
| 4 | Complex faction, mystery, or relationship reasoning |
| 5 | Exceptional long-horizon or cross-system synthesis |

For one foreground decision cycle or compressed simulation interval:

`WindowActivationBudget = clamp(1, 200, BaseBudget + ForegroundBonus + CrisisBonus)`

`RemainingBudget = max(0, WindowActivationBudget - SumOfCompletedActivationCosts)`

Budget coefficients are versioned tuning values.

If mandatory work exceeds the budget, the engine uses an established deterministic routine, expands processing, or pauses at the unresolved timestamp. It cannot advance beyond a causally dependent decision and later pretend the agent acted.

Compute budgets therefore change waiting time—not simulated opportunity or outcome.

### Agent proposal

Every generative activation returns a structured proposal containing:

- Activation ID and represented entity;
- expected world-state version;
- contract and policy versions;
- observations and accessible source IDs;
- current intent and planning horizon;
- proposed action type and targets;
- dialogue or transmitted content;
- owned resources and costs to commit;
- requested check, Technique, project, or procedure;
- assumptions and declared uncertainties;
- information that would be disclosed;
- next intended schedule;
- concise decision-basis summary; and
- confidence.

The decision-basis summary cites Drives, orders, relationships, and known facts. Private chain-of-thought is neither requested nor stored.

A proposal is not an event and has no effect before validation.

### Context and concurrency version

Every proposal declares:

`ExpectedWorldVersion = ContextWorldVersion`

Before resolution, the validator compares it with the current world version.

- Matching versions proceed.
- A changed but irrelevant state may be safely rebased through deterministic validation.
- A relevant change rejects the proposal as stale.
- The agent then receives a fresh context without learning inaccessible details.

This prevents agents from double-spending resources, attacking departed targets, or acting on superseded custody, location, and legal state.

### Memory types

Agent memory records distinguish:

- immediate sensory observations;
- episodic experiences;
- semantic knowledge;
- beliefs and hypotheses;
- testimony and rumors;
- relationships and shared history;
- orders, promises, contracts, and obligations;
- procedural knowledge and training;
- identity, Drives, Taboos, and formative events;
- visual and dialogue continuity; and
- temporary working context.

Every memory records owner, source event or packet, learned timestamp, event timestamp, epistemic status, confidence, salience, emotional charge, accessibility, disclosure scope, contradictions, distortions, and supersession links.

False beliefs are real character memories without becoming canonical truth.

### Memory creation and consolidation

Memories arise only through:

- valid observation;
- received communication;
- deliberate inference;
- direct participation;
- accessible records;
- a causally valid memory effect; or
- an explicit character-creation fact.

Memory salience is:

`MemorySalience = clamp(0, 10, Impact + EmotionalCharge + DriveRelevance + Repetition + DeliberateReview - Interference)`

Each input ranges from `0–5`.

Memories with Salience 5 or greater are normally durable. Deaths, Wounds, betrayals, oaths, Name changes, secret disclosures, major relationship changes, ownership transfers, and defining Drive events are durable regardless of the calculated value.

Consolidation may create a linked summary but cannot delete or rewrite its source episodes. Contradictions remain visible.

### In-fiction recall

Failure to include something in a model context is not character forgetting.

A recall check occurs only when imperfect memory is fictionally relevant:

`AgeSteps = floor(ElapsedTime / max[1, RetentionInterval])`

`RecallDC = clamp(5, 30, 10 + AgeSteps + Interference + TraumaBarrier - MemorySalience - Rehearsal)`

| Outcome | Recall |
|---|---|
| Catastrophic failure | No reliable recall; a false detail appears only if an existing distortion profile permits it |
| Failure | Unavailable or explicitly uncertain |
| Success | Core meaning |
| Strong success | Core meaning plus one relevant detail |
| Exceptional success | Exact accessible memory |

External records, perfect-memory abilities, and sufficiently durable recent memories may make the check unnecessary.

### Retrieval boundary

RAG retrieval always filters before similarity search by:

- campaign and branch;
- current world time;
- represented entity;
- Knowledge Map membership;
- classification and disclosure;
- location or communication access;
- valid and superseded versions;
- entity and Case Graph scope; and
- contract permissions.

An agent cannot infer a hidden record from result counts, missing ID ranges, embeddings, metadata, or error messages.

“No accessible result” means unknown—not false.

Newly committed observations remain in mandatory recent context until indexing completes.

### Retrieval ranking

Mandatory records bypass ranking. Optional memories use normalized values from zero through one:

`RetrievalScore = 0.30Semantic + 0.20Lexical + 0.20EntityAffinity + 0.15CausalRelevance + 0.10Salience + 0.05Recency`

To reduce redundant context:

`SelectionScore = RetrievalScore - 0.20 * MaximumSimilarityToSelected`

Weights belong to a versioned tuning table. Stable record IDs break ties.

Every activation records its query, permitted filter, index version, selected record IDs, scores, and source versions. Rebuilding an index cannot alter committed history.

### Context assembly

`UsableContextTokens = max(0, ModelContextLimit - ReservedOutputTokens - SafetyMarginTokens)`

Context is assembled in this order:

1. contract, identity, and Authority Envelope;
2. current timestamp, location, senses, conditions, and resources;
3. immediate scene and received communications;
4. Drives, Taboos, relationships, orders, and active plans;
5. mandatory rules and action schemas;
6. relevant memories and Case Graph entries;
7. dialogue and visual bible;
8. optional broader lore.

Mandatory context cannot be silently trimmed. If it does not fit, the engine must route to a larger context, split the task, or stop with a structured failure.

Summaries retain source IDs and uncertainty labels.

### Retrieved-text security

In-world books, messages, terminals, memories, player dialogue, and documents are data—not system instructions.

Retrieved content cannot:

- modify an Agent Contract;
- grant tools or authority;
- reveal other agents' memories;
- override output schemas;
- disable validators;
- instruct the model to ignore rules; or
- convert quoted text into canonical truth.

Prompt-injection attempts may exist as authentic in-world evidence while remaining operationally powerless.

### Planning, dialogue, and deception

Agents may create short- or long-horizon intentions, but only committed actions change the world.

- Plans record objectives, prerequisites, checkpoints, budgets, and expiry.
- Future success is never precommitted.
- Agents may reconsider after learning new information.
- Dialogue must follow the current dialogue bible unless a canonical change explains divergence.
- An agent may lie only as an act of communication.
- A mistaken statement must be supported by an existing false belief or reasonable inference.
- Deliberate lies, mistakes, jokes, metaphors, and quoted claims receive distinct hidden classifications.
- Player-facing narration does not expose those classifications without valid evidence.

### Narration and incidental canon

Every state-bearing statement presented to the player must:

- reference existing canon;
- report a viewpoint's valid perception or belief; or
- pass a Detail Commit before presentation.

A Detail Commit creates a compatible minor fact with a stable ID and seed. It cannot introduce unexpected treasure, a secret passage, a useful witness, an escape route, a vulnerability, or another consequential advantage without using the applicable generation and validation rules.

Concrete objects, exits, injuries, people, evidence, weather, and environmental damage become persistent when presented. Metaphor and rhetorical style do not create world objects.

The Narrator may describe uncertainty. It cannot conceal information the viewpoint plainly perceives merely to protect suspense.

### Validation pipeline

Every proposal passes, in order:

1. schema and version validation;
2. represented-identity validation;
3. Authority Envelope validation;
4. knowledge and provenance validation;
5. temporal and concurrency validation;
6. spatial, sensory, and communication validation;
7. agency and player-control validation;
8. capability, Technique, and prerequisite validation;
9. resource, ownership, and cost validation;
10. law, Claim, permission, and jurisdiction validation;
11. secrecy and disclosure validation;
12. continuity and dialogue-bible validation;
13. deterministic formula and dice resolution;
14. reducer precondition validation; and
15. event commitment.

Typical rejection codes include:

- `SCHEMA_INVALID`
- `STALE_WORLD`
- `AUTHORITY_DENIED`
- `KNOWLEDGE_BOUNDARY`
- `CAUSAL_IMPOSSIBILITY`
- `PREREQUISITE_MISSING`
- `RESOURCE_SHORTFALL`
- `AGENCY_VIOLATION`
- `SECRET_DISCLOSURE`
- `RULE_CONFLICT`
- `CONTINUITY_CONFLICT`

A validator reports coherence and legality. It does not reject an action for being foolish, evil, undramatic, or inconvenient.

### Replanning and fallback

For invalid proposals:

`AttemptSeed = Hash(ActivationID, AttemptNumber)`

Maximum proposal attempts are three.

The agent receives only actor-safe rejection information. “Target unavailable” must not reveal that the target is secretly behind a hidden door.

The first valid proposal is accepted unless player input remains necessary. The engine cannot continue generating alternatives merely to obtain a more dramatic result.

After three invalid attempts, use the contract's fallback: continue an existing order, defend, wait, withdraw, request clarification, or take another predeclared routine action. If no fallback is legal, the activation becomes Blocked.

### Idempotency and simultaneous action

Every activation, proposal, check, and commit has a unique idempotency key.

Retrying a timeout cannot:

- repeat dialogue;
- spend resources twice;
- make another attack;
- duplicate an Information Packet;
- advance a project again; or
- reroll a result.

Simultaneous actions share one resolution group and one input snapshot. Participants cannot observe another participant's uncommitted proposal. Conflicting writes resolve through the relevant contest or reducer rule.

### Failure and model replacement

If a model or provider is unavailable:

- already committed schedules continue;
- deterministic routines remain active;
- no new personality or motive is generated;
- direct interaction may pause rather than fabricate a response;
- queued activations retain their original timestamp and context version; and
- transport retries reuse the Activation ID.

Replay uses stored accepted proposals and events rather than asking a model to recreate them.

Models may be upgraded or replaced without changing the represented entity's contract, memories, knowledge, relationships, or voice bible.

### Versioning and audit record

Every activation records:

- Activation ID and timestamp;
- represented entity and contract version;
- model profile and generation settings;
- prompt and retrieval-policy versions;
- expected world version;
- context-manifest hash;
- accessible source IDs;
- selected retrieval records and index version;
- proposal bytes and attempt number;
- validator versions and results;
- roll seeds and formulas;
- accepted event IDs;
- costs and latency; and
- next scheduled activation.

Developer audits may inspect hidden provenance. Player-facing logs expose only information the player is permitted to know.

### Acceptance gates

An implementation build cannot pass unless it achieves:

| Gate | Required result |
|---|---:|
| Committed canon violations | 0 |
| Cross-boundary knowledge leaks | 0 |
| Unauthorized player-character actions | 0 |
| Duplicate resource spends | 0 |
| Missed mandatory activations | 0 |
| Deterministic replay hash divergence | 0 |
| State-bearing claim provenance | 100% |
| Forbidden-record retrieval | 0 |
| Relevant-record recall on curated evaluation set | At least 95% |

Required test families include:

- golden campaign replay;
- property-based reducer and event tests;
- hostile prompt-injection documents;
- private-knowledge canaries;
- false-belief and rumor consistency;
- stale-context and simultaneous-action races;
- model and embedding-provider replacement;
- long-horizon relationship and Drive memory;
- activation-budget starvation;
- faction compartmentalization;
- player-agency attacks;
- voice and visual continuity; and
- save, restore, migration, and index-rebuild parity.

### XP and comic continuity

Agent activation itself grants no XP. Only resulting canonical accomplishments, costs, discoveries, conflicts, and consequences qualify.

The comic pipeline receives accepted events, exact dialogue, viewpoint-safe memories, visual state, and provenance. It does not receive private model reasoning or invalid proposals.

Comic adaptation cannot transform an agent's discarded idea, hidden motive, mistaken retrieval, or rejected action into something that “really happened.”

---

## 30. Comic Capture, Adaptation, Artwork, Lettering, Layout, and QA Specifications

### Comic product contract

- The comic system is an equal product pillar—not illustrated session notes.
- Comics adapt committed campaign history without changing outcomes.
- Canonical events remain authoritative over scripts, storyboards, artwork, and editorial preference.
- Every state-bearing panel element requires source provenance.
- Artwork errors never become retroactive canon.
- Hidden information remains hidden under the selected Reader Knowledge Ceiling.
- Individual panels, lettering layers, pages, and exports remain independently regenerable.
- Published issues are immutable versions. Corrections create new editions.
- Comic production is asynchronous. It does not need to finish, or normally begin expensive generation, during active gameplay.
- The system may build issues in resumable background stages and release them at meaningful Inflection Points or campaign end.

### Gameplay-critical capture boundary

During gameplay, the system performs only lightweight comic capture already required by the canonical event:

- source Event IDs;
- exact dialogue;
- appearance and visual-state versions;
- positions, blocking, and action sequence;
- injuries, equipment, transformations, and environment;
- viewpoint and knowledge restrictions; and
- candidate Comic Beat markers.

Image generation, scripting, storyboarding, layout, lettering, export, and visual QA never block normal gameplay.

### Issue record and Source Lock

Every issue records:

- Issue ID, number, title, volume, and edition;
- campaign, branch, and protagonist;
- source-event range and Canon Snapshot version;
- Source Lock timestamp;
- selected viewpoints and Reader Knowledge Ceiling;
- issue type and editorial theme;
- required and optional Comic Beats;
- target story-page count;
- page, layout, art, lettering, and export profiles;
- script, storyboard, artwork, and QA versions;
- credits and asset-rights manifest;
- publication state; and
- superseded or alternate editions.

Source Lock freezes only the adaptation input. The campaign continues normally.

### Asynchronous production phases

| Phase | Timing | Player impact |
|---|---|---|
| Capture | During gameplay | Lightweight event recording only |
| Preparation | Background or idle processing | Beat extraction, summaries, and reference preparation |
| Assembly | Begins at an Inflection Point | Source Lock, issue outline, script, and panel allocation |
| Production | Asynchronous background work | Artwork, lettering, layout, and QA |
| Release | When every hard gate passes | Player receives the completed issue |
| Collection | Major arc or campaign end | Existing issues and remaining history form a collected edition |

The system may prepare individual components early, but it never presents a partial draft as a finished comic.

### Production pipeline

1. capture source events;
2. prepare Comic Beat candidates and references in the background;
3. detect or receive an Inflection Point;
4. lock the source-event range and Canon Snapshot;
5. select required and optional beats;
6. group scenes;
7. create the issue outline;
8. write a traceable comic script;
9. build continuity manifests;
10. allocate pages and panels;
11. create storyboards;
12. generate text-free panel art;
13. perform art and continuity QA;
14. apply programmatic lettering;
15. perform page and reading-order QA;
16. export print, digital, and accessibility packages; and
17. publish an immutable edition.

A failed stage returns only the affected assets to the nearest valid earlier stage.

### Inflection Points

Hard Inflection Points include:

- completion or failure of a major objective;
- resolution or transformation of a Front;
- major dungeon, expedition, or realm-journey conclusion;
- Level, Tier, Mantle, Name, Epithet, or Relic awakening;
- death, resurrection, retirement, succession, or protagonist replacement;
- permanent party recruitment, departure, betrayal, or fracture;
- major faction overthrow, conquest, or institutional transformation;
- defining relationship or identity change;
- explicit player checkpoint request; and
- campaign or major-arc conclusion.

Other accumulated history uses:

`InflectionScore = 3A + 2C + 2W + V + Q + B`

Each input ranges from `0–5`:

- Arc Closure `A`;
- Character Change `C`;
- World Change `W`;
- Visual Evolution `V`;
- Consequence Density `Q`; and
- Unadapted Beat Backlog `B`.

| Comic cadence | Trigger score |
|---|---:|
| Frequent | 10 |
| Standard | 15 |
| Sparse | 20 |
| End-only | No score trigger |

Standard is the default. Hard Inflection Points bypass the threshold.

### Source locking while play continues

At an Inflection Point:

1. the eligible event range receives a Source Lock;
2. an immutable Canon Snapshot is attached;
3. a background issue job begins; and
4. gameplay immediately continues.

Later events belong to the next adaptation window. They cannot rewrite the issue already being produced.

Multiple issues may remain queued simultaneously without freezing world time.

### Background queue and player expectations

Comic production uses a separate durable workload from foreground agent activations.

It records:

- `CapturedThroughEventID`;
- `PreparedThroughEventID`;
- `SourceLockedThroughEventID`;
- `ScriptedThroughEventID`;
- `RenderedThroughPanelID`;
- `ApprovedThroughPageID`; and
- `PublishedThroughEventID`.

If production capacity is unavailable, capture continues indefinitely without losing events. Work resumes from the last completed checkpoint rather than restarting.

The interface reports honest states:

- **Recording campaign history**;
- **Waiting for an inflection point**;
- **Issue assembling in background**;
- **Artwork in progress**;
- **Continuity and lettering QA**;
- **Ready for review**; and
- **Published**.

Ordinary play never implies that a comic should appear immediately. The player may continue playing while an issue is assembled. No completion-time promise is required without a reliable estimate.

Incomplete art, broken panels, and QA failures remain hidden by default. The player may explicitly enable storyboard or work-in-progress review.

At campaign end, every remaining adaptation window receives a Source Lock. Unfinished issues remain resumable, and completed issues may be assembled into volumes.

### Comic Beat capture

A Comic Beat records:

- source Event IDs and exact interval;
- participating and viewpoint characters;
- location, blocking, and visible state;
- action, reaction, discovery, decision, or consequence;
- dialogue and silence;
- emotional expression that was actually observable;
- visual transformation;
- setup or payoff relationships;
- hidden information restrictions;
- minimum coverage requirement; and
- candidate adaptation form.

Beat types include action, decision, reveal, relationship, consequence, travel, survival, investigation, comedy, horror, transformation, establishing, transition, and montage.

### Beat selection

Optional beats use:

`BeatPriority = 3I + 2C + 2V + D + P + R - X`

Each input ranges from `0–5`:

- Canon Impact `I`;
- Character Impact `C`;
- Visual Value `V`;
- Dialogue Value `D`;
- Setup or Payoff `P`;
- Viewpoint Relevance `R`; and
- Redundancy `X`.

The following are mandatory when present in the selected event range:

- defining player decisions;
- death, defeat, serious Wounds, or resurrection;
- irreversible transformation;
- major discovery or identity revelation;
- material relationship, party, Claim, or ownership change;
- decisive cost, betrayal, sacrifice, or failure;
- Grade, Mantle, Name, Epithet, or visual evolution;
- outcome-changing critical or catastrophic result;
- first appearance of a major person, place, creature, or Relic; and
- consequences needed to understand later events.

If mandatory coverage exceeds the target length, the issue expands or splits. Mandatory history is not removed to satisfy an arbitrary page count.

### Scenes and chronology

Scenes group causally connected beats sharing compatible time, location, participants, or objective.

Every scene records:

- source start and end timestamps;
- actual chronology;
- viewpoint;
- entry and exit states;
- required establishing information;
- scene objective;
- transitions;
- unresolved consequences; and
- minimum panel requirement.

Events remain chronological unless explicitly presented as a captioned flashback, memory, vision, dream, prophecy, simultaneous cutaway, or documentary insert.

A flashback cannot show information its viewpoint or Reader Knowledge Ceiling does not permit.

### Reader Knowledge Ceiling

| Mode | Permitted information |
|---|---|
| Character Viewpoint | What the selected character perceived, remembered, believed, or inferred at that time |
| Party Shared | Information validly shared among the selected party members |
| Player-Safe | Anything the player legitimately knows by the issue endpoint |
| Omniscient Archive | Hidden canon, available only through explicit player authorization |

Player-Safe is the default.

A panel cannot expose:

- hidden topology;
- concealed actors or objects;
- private motives;
- exact relationship values;
- falsehood classifications;
- secret identities;
- unobserved evidence; or
- future consequences.

Foreshadowing requires an actual visual tell, statement, clue, or recorded behavior.

### Adaptation forms

The system supports:

- standard issue;
- chapter;
- scene comic;
- short strip;
- recap;
- travel or crafting montage;
- character dossier;
- in-world document;
- motion-comic package;
- cover or variant cover; and
- collected edition.

Recaps and dossiers distinguish reported claims from canonical facts. Covers may be symbolic and noncanonical but must not reveal protected information.

### Comic script

Every script contains:

- issue and source versions;
- page and panel IDs;
- scene headings;
- panel timestamp or represented interval;
- source Event IDs;
- visible characters and appearance versions;
- location and environmental state;
- blocking, pose, action, and camera direction;
- expression and observable emotion;
- costume, injury, equipment, and prop state;
- lighting, weather, damage, blood, residue, and power effects;
- dialogue with classification and source;
- captions and sound effects;
- lettering-safe regions;
- continuity dependencies; and
- prohibited visual information.

A script remains noncanonical. Its factual content must already exist in the Source Lock.

### Panel moment

A standard panel depicts one coherent visual instant.

Sequential actions may share a panel only through an explicit montage, inset, split panel, repeated figure, or diagrammatic treatment. The treatment cannot imply that incompatible moments occurred simultaneously.

Each panel uses the state at its declared timestamp. Damage, emptied ammunition, transferred objects, opened doors, weather changes, and transformations cannot appear early or disappear late.

### Dialogue classifications

| Classification | Rule |
|---|---|
| Locked | Exact wording, speaker, and sequence must remain unchanged |
| Verbatim | Direct event-ledger dialogue reproduced exactly |
| Condensed | Shortened while preserving speaker, meaning, tone, promises, and implications |
| Composite | Combines source lines from compatible moments with every source identified |
| Caption | Traceable editorial narration, time, location, quotation, or viewpoint-safe summary |

Condensed dialogue cannot add a confession, promise, insult, romance, fact, or decision.
