Composite dialogue normally remains with the same speaker and causal exchange. Cross-speaker composites are prohibited.

Silence, interruption, hesitation, and refusal may be locked beats.

### Temporal compression

Compression may use:

- captions;
- maps;
- calendars;
- repeated visual motifs;
- montage panels;
- inset sequences;
- environmental transitions; and
- excerpts from in-world records.

Compression cannot erase decisive failures, costs, arguments, shortages, injuries, discoveries, transformations, or arrival condition.

Elapsed time and state changes remain derivable from source events.

### Page and panel budgets

Default issue profile:

| Setting | Default | Legal range |
|---|---:|---:|
| Story pages | 22 | 4–64 |
| Target panels per page | 5 | 1–9 |
| Maximum ordinary panels per page | 9 | 1–9 |

`IssuePanelTarget = TargetStoryPages * TargetPanelsPerPage`

For each scene:

`SceneWeight = 3 * HardBeatCount + SumOfBeatPriorities`

`RawScenePanels = IssuePanelTarget * SceneWeight / SumOfSceneWeights`

Panels are assigned through deterministic largest-remainder allocation after satisfying each scene's minimum coverage.

Splash pages, double-page spreads, silent pages, and dense grids require an explicit visual or pacing purpose. Minimum coverage overrides the target and may expand the issue.

### Default page geometry

Print master:

| Property | Value |
|---|---:|
| Trim width | 6.625 in |
| Trim height | 10.1875 in |
| Bleed | 0.125 in per edge |
| Minimum safe inset | 0.25 in from trim |
| Resolution | 300 PPI |
| Default gutter | 0.125 in |

`CanvasWidthPixels = RoundHalfUp[(TrimWidth + 2 * Bleed) * PPI]`

`CanvasHeightPixels = RoundHalfUp[(TrimHeight + 2 * Bleed) * PPI]`

The default full-bleed canvas is `2063 × 3131` pixels.

The default digital page is `1600 × 2460` pixels in sRGB. Other profiles may be defined for webtoon, manga, European album, tablet, or accessibility editions without changing source content.

### Layout engine

Every page records:

- layout template;
- Layout Seed;
- panel rectangles and z-order;
- gutters, borders, bleed, and safe zones;
- reading order;
- balloon and caption regions;
- page-turn intent;
- spread dependencies; and
- placed asset versions.

`LayoutSeed = Hash(IssueID, PageID, LayoutProfileVersion)`

Layout must preserve:

- chronology;
- visual hierarchy;
- unambiguous reading order;
- action direction;
- speaker order;
- continuity of screen position;
- required establishing information; and
- lettering space.

A page-turn reveal may emphasize an event but cannot move it earlier in chronology.

### Visual and style bibles

The issue Style Bible records:

- line treatment and rendering method;
- anatomy and realism level;
- palette and contrast;
- texture, grain, and brush language;
- lighting conventions;
- violence and body-horror treatment;
- comedy and exaggeration rules;
- power-effect grammar;
- panel borders and gutters;
- lettering, balloons, captions, and sound effects;
- cover treatment; and
- prohibited stylistic drift.

Entity Visual Bibles remain versioned separately. Issue style cannot redesign an established character, Relic, prop, or location without a canonical visual-change event.

Intentional cosmic inconsistency follows the entity's Visual Invariance Profile. Generation errors never qualify.

### Reference packages

Each panel receives only the references it needs:

- approved face and body references;
- current appearance version;
- costume layers and damage;
- scars, Wounds, prosthetics, and transformations;
- height and scale comparisons;
- signature poses and movement;
- equipment and exact hand assignment;
- Relic and prop sheets;
- location geometry and damage;
- time, weather, and lighting;
- power colors and visual signatures; and
- previous adjacent panels for continuity.

References retain provenance and rights information. A real person's likeness requires documented authorization.

### Panel art specification

Every panel-art job declares:

- Panel ID and source manifest;
- final placed dimensions;
- required overscan;
- camera position, lens language, crop, and angle;
- foreground, middle ground, and background;
- character count and blocking;
- exact visible state;
- action moment;
- expression;
- lettering-safe regions;
- mandatory and prohibited objects;
- style and reference versions;
- generation provider and model profile;
- prompt and negative constraints; and
- QA requirements.

The art model receives only information intended to be visible. Hidden actors, motives, rooms, and objects remain outside its context.

### Render dimensions

`RenderWidth = ceil(FinalPlacedWidthPixels * OverscanFactor * SupersampleFactor)`

`RenderHeight = ceil(FinalPlacedHeightPixels * OverscanFactor * SupersampleFactor)`

Defaults:

- Overscan Factor: `1.10`;
- Supersample Factor: `1.50`.

Art is cropped and downsampled nondestructively after approval. Faces, hands, required props, and important action must remain within the panel's visual safe region.

Text-free art is mandatory. Dialogue, captions, sound effects, signs, interfaces, and logos are applied separately unless a canonical handwritten or environmental mark genuinely requires painted integration.

### Panel generation and retry

`PanelAttemptSeed = Hash(IssueSeed, PanelID, ArtProfileVersion, AttemptNumber)`

Maximum automatic attempts per panel are five.

Each attempt is an independent candidate. A rejected attempt:

- never changes canon;
- never changes another panel;
- remains traceable for audit;
- cannot be reused as a different event; and
- cannot justify altering the script.

The first candidate passing hard continuity QA and the minimum soft-art score becomes eligible. An editor may choose among eligible candidates for aesthetic quality.

If no candidate passes after five attempts, the panel becomes Blocked for targeted editing, revised staging, another provider, or manual production.

### Hard artwork QA

A panel fails immediately if it contains:

- wrong identity, age, body, species, or transformation;
- missing, duplicated, or unauthorized character;
- incorrect costume or appearance version;
- healed, moved, reversed, or invented injury;
- missing, duplicated, refilled, or redesigned equipment;
- incorrect holder, hand, custody, or object scale;
- wrong location, topology, time, weather, or lighting;
- impossible blocking or action sequence;
- hidden information outside the Reader Knowledge Ceiling;
- invented witnesses, exits, evidence, treasure, or threats;
- unreadable or unintended generated text;
- prohibited extra limbs, merged anatomy, or severe image artifacts;
- wrong power color, Source signature, or visual invariant; or
- insufficient effective resolution.

Absurd, obscene, damaged, crude, or humiliating canonical designs cannot be rejected merely for lacking conventional dignity.

### Soft artwork score

After hard QA passes, score each dimension from `0–5`:

`SoftArtScore = 0.25Composition + 0.25CharacterFidelity + 0.20StyleFidelity + 0.15Expression + 0.15Lighting`

A candidate is eligible only when:

- `SoftArtScore >= 4.0`; and
- no individual dimension is below `3`.

Soft scoring cannot override a hard continuity failure.

### Cross-panel continuity

Adjacent panels validate:

- screen direction and 180-degree orientation;
- character and camera position;
- facing and eyeline;
- hand occupancy;
- remaining ammunition or Charge;
- clothing and equipment damage;
- blood, grime, wetness, and environmental residue;
- lighting and weather progression;
- location damage and movable objects;
- transformation stage; and
- entrances, exits, and movement paths.

Intentional discontinuity requires an explicit cut, transition, time jump, or reality effect.

### Lettering system

All lettering is programmatic and remains editable vector or high-resolution overlay content.

Every text object records:

- source dialogue or caption ID;
- classification;
- speaker or narrator;
- exact text and locale;
- font, size, leading, and emphasis;
- balloon, caption, display, whisper, thought, transmission, or SFX style;
- tail target;
- reading-order index;
- bounding box;
- safe-zone result; and
- revision history.

The art model does not render speech balloons.

### Typography and balloon constraints

Default print requirements:

- ordinary lettering: `9 pt`;
- minimum permitted lettering: `8 pt`;
- target balloon length: `5–25` words;
- maximum balloon length: `35` words;
- default total lettering-area limit: `35%` of panel area.

`TextAreaRatio = TotalLetteringArea / PanelArea`

If `TextAreaRatio > 0.35`, the system must reflow, split the panel, redistribute dialogue, or make a permitted condensation. It cannot shrink text below the minimum.

Balloon tails must identify the correct speaker without crossing unrelated faces or creating ambiguous ownership.

### Captions, sound effects, and display text

- Captions require source provenance and a permitted viewpoint.
- Sound effects must correspond to actual sound, power manifestation, impact, or deliberate stylistic abstraction.
- SFX cannot imply an unrecorded attack, explosion, voice, or machine.
- Environmental signs, interfaces, messages, and documents use separate editable text layers.
- Locked Names, Epithets, runes, languages, and logos use the approved spelling and glyph reference.
- Untranslatable language may remain visually represented only when its form is canonical.

### Cover and promotional art

Covers are classified as:

- Scene-Derived;
- Symbolic;
- Propaganda;
- In-World Publication; or
- Variant.

Only Scene-Derived covers claim to depict a canonical moment. Symbolic and variant covers may exaggerate composition but cannot reveal protected identities or falsely market a nonexistent permanent form as canon without clear classification.

Cover text, logos, issue numbers, credits, and barcodes are programmatic layers.

### Editor permissions

The editor may:

- select among eligible art candidates;
- crop within approved overscan;
- adjust color and lighting without changing state;
- move balloons and captions;
- select a valid layout;
- apply permitted dialogue condensation;
- create traceable composites;
- add source-backed captions;
- regenerate one panel or layer; and
- issue a corrected edition.

The editor may not:

- reverse an outcome;
- add or remove a participant;
- change custody, injury, costume, equipment, location, or chronology;
- reveal hidden information;
- invent dialogue or motives;
- turn an invalid art artifact into canon; or
- omit required consequences.

A desired story change must return to the canonical event layer rather than being painted into the comic.

### Color, resolution, and export

Master artwork uses an sRGB working profile unless a production profile explicitly overrides it.

Print export:

- printer-approved CMYK conversion;
- embedded ICC profile;
- PDF/X-4 or printer-required equivalent;
- 300 effective PPI minimum;
- embedded or outlined licensed fonts;
- bleed, trim, and safe-area marks as required; and
- no unintended transparency or missing glyphs.

`EffectivePPI = SourcePixelsAlongAxis / PlacedInchesAlongAxis`

Upscaling does not count as genuine source resolution for the hard QA gate.

Digital outputs may include:

- sRGB PDF;
- CBZ;
- lossless PNG master pages;
- optimized WebP or JPEG pages;
- panel-by-panel web assets; and
- motion-comic asset packages.

### Accessibility and localization

Every published issue includes:

- deterministic reading order;
- panel and page alt text;
- dialogue transcript;
- speaker attribution;
- caption and SFX transcript;
- color-independent identification where required;
- navigable page and scene structure; and
- content warnings derived from actual events.

Alt text obeys the Reader Knowledge Ceiling and describes only visible or permitted information.

Localization uses separate lettering layers, approved terminology, Name and Epithet glossaries, balloon reflow, and locale-specific reading order. Translation cannot alter promises, insults, facts, or outcome meaning.

### Asset provenance and versioning

Every asset records:

- content hash;
- Issue, Page, Panel, and layer IDs;
- source events;
- model, provider, and generation settings;
- prompt and reference manifest;
- seed and attempt number;
- edits and transformations;
- creator and approval state;
- license and likeness authorization;
- color profile and dimensions;
- QA results; and
- replacement or supersession links.

Accepted bytes are stored and reused during replay. Exact image regeneration is not assumed from a nondeterministic provider.

Published pages are immutable. Regeneration creates a new asset and edition version.

### Production-job lifecycle

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Capturing | Gameplay events and visual provenance are being recorded |
| Waiting for Inflection | No issue assembly is due under the selected cadence |
| Preparing | Beat and reference work proceeds in the background |
| Source Locked | Event range and Canon Snapshot are fixed |
| Outlined | Beats and scenes selected |
| Scripted | Traceable script completed |
| Storyboarded | Page and panel layout committed |
| Generating | Art jobs active |
| Art QA | Candidates under review |
| Lettered | Text layers placed |
| Page QA | Reading order and composition checked |
| Exporting | Output packages being built |
| Approved | All gates pass |
| Published | Immutable edition available |
| Blocked | Human or targeted intervention required |

Every job is idempotent. Retrying a failed export or panel cannot duplicate pages, restart completed work, or replace an approved asset silently.

### Acceptance gates

| Gate | Required result |
|---|---:|
| Gameplay blocked by comic production | 0 |
| Captured events lost during backlog | 0 |
| Later events leaking into a Source-Locked issue | 0 |
| Duplicate issue coverage without explicit recap classification | 0 |
| Background retry restarting completed work | 0 |
| Partial or failed assets presented as finished | 0 |
| Canon continuity violations | 0 |
| Reader-knowledge leaks | 0 |
| Missing mandatory beats | 0 |
| State-bearing claims without source IDs | 0 |
| Locked-dialogue deviations | 0 |
| Dialogue source traceability | 100% |
| Incorrect character or appearance versions | 0 |
| Incorrect injuries, equipment, or custody | 0 |
| Ambiguous speaker attribution | 0 |
| Text below minimum size | 0 |
| Text or balloons outside safe bounds | 0 |
| Missing glyphs or unembedded required fonts | 0 |
| Print panels below 300 effective PPI | 0 |
| Deterministic layout divergence | 0 |
| Failed export-profile checks | 0 |
| Published assets without provenance | 0 |

Automated comparison, property tests, OCR, visual-reference checks, and scripted export inspection support QA. Final visual approval may remain human-assisted, but it cannot waive canonical hard failures.

### In-world publication, XP, and Issue #0

Producing an out-of-world comic grants no campaign XP and does not affect reputation.

If a comic, recording, propaganda issue, dossier, or adaptation is deliberately published inside the world, it becomes an actual item and Information Packet. Its ownership, audience, evidence, Exposure, reputation, legal, and faction consequences then resolve normally.

The comic may portray tragedy, horror, humiliation, absurd equipment, terrible decisions, villainy, survival, or failure without sanitizing them into generic heroism.

**Issue #0: The Seventeen** remains required for the complete product onboarding plan and is not part of the vertical-slice deliverable.

---

## 31. BREAKWATER: Hampton Roads Vertical Slice and Acceptance-Test Specification

BREAKWATER is the first implementation-complete campaign region. It is a fictional alternate-Earth version of Hampton Roads, Virginia, centered on Norfolk, Portsmouth, and the adjoining coastal corridor. Real geography supplies recognizable logistics and weather pressure; all organizations, incidents, supernatural infrastructure, and operational details are fictional campaign canon.

The slice is a real open-world campaign, not a tutorial corridor. It must remain playable after the player refuses its hooks, joins an antagonist, destroys a planned site, leaves the region, or changes protagonists.

### 1. Vertical-slice purpose

BREAKWATER must prove, in one bounded but persistent region:

- character creation and advancement from Level 1 through Level 5;
- social, investigative, criminal, survival, exploration, travel, crafting, combat, and realm play;
- damage, defeat, death, afterlife, succession, and replacement protagonists;
- equipment, vehicles, improvised tools, crafting, and Relic progression;
- companion recruitment, refusal, autonomy, conflict, betrayal, departure, and death;
- autonomous factions, Fronts, law, evidence, reputation, and information propagation;
- authored situations and seeded procedural sites coexisting without contradiction;
- agent knowledge limits, RAG recall, provenance, validation, and deterministic replay; and
- lightweight comic capture during play followed by asynchronous production at an Inflection Point.

Passing the vertical slice means these systems interact coherently under destructive and unexpected play. It does not require every full-product content category to be present.

### 2. Why Hampton Roads

The region concentrates ports, tunnels, bridges, ferries, naval industry, hospitals, tourism, municipal boundaries, floodplains, storms, and contested waterways inside a travel-relevant area. Those features create natural tests for jurisdiction, logistics, evacuation, pursuit, navigation, infrastructure damage, and persistent consequences.

Its maritime history also supports the initial mythology without collapsing the traditions together:

- Greek and Roman sea, law, territory, oath, and underworld claims;
- Norse seafaring, shipbuilding, storm, fate, and giant-blood traditions;
- Orpheus continuity research and mythotechnology;
- modern emergency management, licensing, salvage, insurance, and private security; and
- urban, industrial, suburban, coastal, submerged, and adjacent-realm environments.

### 3. Canonical situation

Six days after the campaign begins, an extreme king tide is projected to cross the region. A damaged component recovered from Orpheus Site Seven is amplifying **Thalassic Anchor Seven**, causing memory, tide, identity, and navigation disturbances.

The following truth is canonical engine state and is not automatically player or NPC knowledge:

- Thalassic Anchor Seven belongs to an old Poseidon/Neptune navigation-and-territory network.
- The Anchor contains no verified surviving Divine Self.
- Its activity is not proof that Poseidon, Neptune, either divine identity, or a unified root survived.
- Site Seven's Continuity Lock is misreading a repeating navigational Pattern as fragmented consciousness.
- Continued amplification causes the Pattern to overwrite routes, memories, custody assertions, and territorial boundaries with increasing force.
- Each major faction holds partial, biased, outdated, or deliberately falsified evidence.
- No starting actor possesses the complete truth.

The Anchor can be stabilized, destroyed, stolen, relocated, transformed, claimed, exploited, partitioned, surrendered, or allowed to complete its cycle when a causally valid method succeeds. The rules define consequences; they do not designate a morally correct ending.

### 4. Starting world state

Campaign time begins at **Day 0, 18:00**. These events are committed before the first player action:

| Time or deadline | Starting state |
|---|---|
| Day 0, 18:00 | Buoy Nine transmits an alarm that ordinary monitoring classifies as a sensor fault |
| Day 0, 18:17 | A localized surge strikes one freight and residential edge of the harbor |
| Each midnight | Site Seven produces a seventeen-minute memory disturbance until its state changes |
| Day 1, evening | An illegal salvage auction opens if its organizers retain a viable venue and stock |
| Day 4 | Municipal authorities hold an emergency-power and licensing vote unless lawfully or physically prevented |
| Day 5 | Ninth Keel attempts to launch its realm-capable vessel if prerequisites remain satisfied |
| Day 6 | The projected king tide reaches the confluence interval under then-current weather and Anchor conditions |

The player may investigate, exploit, publicize, suppress, or ignore any event. They may also leave the region. Factions and Fronts continue resolving through their actual capabilities and clocks rather than freezing or moving prepared content in front of the player.

### 5. Districts and route graph

The City Bible contains at least these eight persistent districts:

| District | Identity and pressures | Initial named sites |
|---|---|---|
| Waterside Civic Core | Government, waterfront commerce, emergency coordination, public demonstrations, and flood-control chokepoints | Compact Hall, Buoy Control Annex, Floodgate Three, Waterside Ferry Steps |
| Ghent–Medical Quarter | Hospitals, continuity medicine, dense housing, archives, and evacuation ethics | Argent Memorial, St. Brigid Trauma Pavilion, Continuity Ward, Hague Footbridge |
| Lambert's Point Freight Spine | Rail, coal and container traffic, dock labor, salvage, smuggling, and polluted shoreline | Pier Fourteen, Halvorsen Yard, Blackwake Coldhouse, Buoy Nine Berth |
| Portsmouth Shipyards | Dry docks, naval subcontracting, repair capacity, labor politics, and restricted industrial access | Drydock of Nine Nails, Iron Mercy Ferry, South Gatehouse, Rivet Hall |
| Atlantic Bastion Perimeter | Fictional federal security boundary, licensed-hero staging, surveillance, and controlled water access | Aegis Contract Port, Tidewall Six, Bastion Exchange, Signal Range Red |
| The Drowned Mile | Repeatedly flooded blocks, submerged transit, improvised markets, mutual aid, and predatory redevelopment | Flooded Transit Spiral, Salt Chapel, Lowwater Market, House on Pilings |
| Oceanfront and Rudee Reach | Tourism, rescue services, exposed coastline, storms, vessel launch, and public spectacle | Ninth Keel Slip, Rudee Beacon, Breaker Rescue Station, Glass-Tide Boardwalk |
| The Underchannel | Mythic roads beneath mapped waterways, territorial law, tolls, wrecks, and unstable exits | Court Below Tollbooth, Cable Cathedral, Wreck Orchard, Anchor Seven Approach |

Routes record bridges, tunnels, ferries, road and rail segments, navigable water, submerged passages, air corridors, checkpoints, closures, tolls, structural Integrity, travel modes, and current control. Destroying or repairing infrastructure changes the graph rather than producing a cosmetic description.

### 6. Major factions

| Faction | Scale and strongest ratings | Public or immediate aim | Internal division |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tidewater Emergency Compact | Scale 2; Logistics 7, Influence 6, Security 4 | Prevent mass casualties and preserve emergency coordination | Transparent public relief versus coercive continuity rule |
| Aegis Atlantic | Scale 2; Force 7, Security 7, Influence 5 | Secure contracts, licensed operations, and custody of dangerous assets | Civilian protection versus proprietary control |
| Ninth Keel | Scale 1; Research 7, Esoteric 6, Logistics 5 | Launch a realm-capable vessel through the changing channel | Open exploration versus first-claim secrecy |
| Vestal Ledger | Scale 1; Esoteric 8, Influence 7, Intelligence 5 | Establish Roman custody over souls, Anchors, evidence, and succession | Procedural law versus sacred necessity |
| Blackwake Cooperative | Scale 1; Logistics 6, Intelligence 6, Wealth 4 | Protect dock communities while surviving through salvage and illicit trade | Mutual aid versus profitable predation |
| Drowned Court | Scale 1; Esoteric 7, Force 4, Influence 4 | Restore or replace oceanic authority through recognized claim | Lawful stewardship versus the Empty Throne cult |
| Site Seven Remnant | Scale 0; Research 8, Intelligence 6, Security 2 | Recover its records and complete or correct the Continuity Lock | Scientific accountability versus obsession with continuation |

No faction is wholly benevolent or wholly malicious. Each records leadership, successors, resources, projects, knowledge packets, territory, obligations, grievances, internal actors, and responses to player absence.

### 7. Active Fronts

| Front | Clock | Starting position | Actual advancing triggers | Precommitted outcome families |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| King Tide Confluence | 8 | 2 | elapsed tide intervals, severe weather, failed flood infrastructure, or Anchor amplification | protected evacuation and controlled flooding; redirected inundation with unequal losses; uncontrolled regional flood and route transformation |
| Site Seven Continuity Cascade | 8 | 1 | active Lock cycles, incompatible repairs, Mnemosyne fuel, or deliberate activation | corrected Pattern recognition; partitioned or captured continuity process; identity-and-memory cascade through connected infrastructure |
| Empty Throne Below | 10 | 2 | recognized oceanic Claims, tribute, Anchor access, or public/divine Recognition | vacant authority stabilized; a claimant installed or shared sovereignty formed; predatory throne manifestation or jurisdictional war |
| License War | 6 | 1 | emergency decrees, public incidents, contract breaches, evidence releases, arrests, or armed enforcement | negotiated plural licensing; monopoly or state control; legitimacy collapse and open vigilantism/criminal governance |

Fronts advance only from their recorded triggers. Player action may delay, reverse, transform, or accelerate them. Each resolution writes lasting territorial, legal, material, reputational, and relationship consequences; no outcome merely resets the slice.

### 8. Companion candidates

No companion is mandatory, plot-armored, or automatically loyal.

| Candidate | Profile | Recruitment boundary | Drives and taboo boundary |
|---|---|---|---|
| Dr. Mara Vance | Level 2 Orpheus Returned; First Responder; Restorer | Requires credible help for patients or evidence that the memory disturbances threaten continuity medicine | Preserve persons through honest care; rejects using patients as Continuity Lock components |
| Eirik "Rivet" Halvorsen | Level 3 Jotun-Blooded; Tradesperson; Savant | Respects demonstrated craft, protection of workers, or a paid and bounded contract | Build what survives and defend his crew; rejects disposable-worker logic |
| Lucia Vale | Level 2 Mantle-Touched; Political; Commander-Controller | Joins only a program capable of changing regional authority, not a vague appeal to goodness | Establish durable order and earn public recognition; rejects meaningless chaos and private humiliation |
| Nia Rook | Level 1 Mastered Human; Smuggler; Operator | Requires a viable route, fair share, or rescue of someone she has claimed responsibility for | Keep exits open and debts explicit; rejects captivity and unpaid ownership claims |
| Jonah Pike | Level 4 Engineered Licensed Hero; Guardian | Requires lawful authority, a convincing imminent necessity, or a personal break with Aegis | Protect civilians and keep promises made in uniform; rejects casual collateral sacrifice |
| Buoy Nine | Grade 1, Agency 3 Relic; fluorescent-orange municipal navigation buoy | Recognition must be earned through listening, repair, shared navigation, or defense of its actual public purpose | Warn, guide, be seen, and remember lost sailors; rejects silencing true danger for convenience |

Every candidate has a private Knowledge Map, Drives, Taboos, Preferences, Grievances, Bonds, active plans, visual bible, dialogue bible, advancement route, departure conditions, and possible faction relationships. Buoy Nine's absurd municipal silhouette and fluorescent color are protected visual canon rather than temporary joke styling.

### 9. Supporting cast and succession

The initial supporting cast contains at least these eighteen named actors:

| Character | Initial role |
|---|---|
| Chief Ada Mercer | Tidewater Emergency Compact incident commander |
| Mateo Ruiz | Flood-control engineer and union representative |
| DeShawn Bell | Ferry master with cross-jurisdictional access |
| Dr. Elian Cho | Continuity Ward physician and skeptical witness |
| Captain Helena Ward | Aegis Atlantic regional commander |
| Sergeant Milo Kessler | Aegis evidence and detention officer |
| Professor Sigrid Aune | Ninth Keel principal realm navigator |
| Tomas Eddowes | Ninth Keel test pilot and public face |
| Auditor Octavia Rell | Vestal Ledger adjudicator |
| Cassian Moore | Mortal oath-notary and evidence custodian |
| Imani Cross | Blackwake logistics coordinator |
| Pearl Baker | Salvage diver and neighborhood organizer |
| Josephine "Auntie Jo" Cade | Drowned Mile kitchen and information hub |
| Magistrate Brine | Drowned Court territorial judge |
| Envoy Pelagos | Court diplomat with a disputed Name |
| Dr. Soren Vale | Site Seven Continuity Lock architect |
| Amara Flint | Site Seven technician carrying an incomplete record |
| Winslow Quay | Independent auctioneer and broker of stolen provenance |

Every named actor may be killed, recruited, bribed, intimidated, exposed, displaced, promoted, transformed, or ignored when the rules permit. Faction leadership and essential operational roles have precommitted succession procedures. A dead quest-giver does not invalidate the underlying evidence, world state, or alternative access routes.

### 10. Entry modes

| Entry mode | Initial position | Independent opening opportunities |
|---|---|---|
| Hero or licensed operative | Recognized responder, trainee, contractor, or unaffiliated protector | Buoy Nine alarm, flood rescue, Aegis tasking, emergency vote security, or medical escort |
| Villain or outlaw | Smuggler, thief, saboteur, aspiring ruler, escaped subject, or hostile claimant | Blackwake auction, Anchor theft, license disruption, faction extortion, contraband route, or throne claim |
| Ordinary survivor | Resident, worker, patient, visitor, displaced person, or newly awakened civilian | localized surge, missing Cold Box, shelter and transport needs, workplace crisis, family search, or strange memory evidence |

Each entry offers multiple unrelated leads, a viable refusal path, and enough accessible novel XP to reach Level 5 without accepting a prescribed moral role. Starting contacts know only their own information.

### 11. Authored situations

Situation ratings are fixed world difficulty bands, not automatic Level scaling.

| Situation | Rating | Initial problem | At least three supported approaches |
|---|---:|---|---|
| The Siren That Is Not There | 1 | A warning signal is heard where instruments report silence | sensory investigation, public warning, occult analysis, signal exploitation |
| Thirty-Seven Minutes of Air | 1 | People are trapped with a finite breathable-air interval | rescue engineering, medical triage, forced breach, unconventional transport |
| Missing Cold Box | 1 | Continuity samples disappear during a confused transfer | investigation, negotiation, theft, tracking or market interception |
| Permit to Bleed | 2 | Emergency aid is blocked by jurisdiction and licensing conflict | legal procedure, political pressure, clandestine aid, coercion or proof |
| Ghosts on Pier Fourteen | 2 | Workers report identity echoes around damaged freight | technical isolation, spirit contact, evacuation, capture or weaponization |
| Blackwake Auction | 2 | Stolen salvage and provenance are sold under fragile neutrality | purchase, infiltration, robbery, alliance, exposure or destruction |
| Ninth Keel Launch | 3 | A realm vessel attempts departure through an unstable route | crew support, sabotage, boarding, claim challenge, route stabilization |
| Drowned Mile Survey | 3 | Survey data will decide protection, abandonment, or redevelopment | exploration, evidence audit, community defense, falsification or bargaining |
| Court Below Tollbooth | 3 | An undersea jurisdiction demands a disputed toll | payment, trial, claim contest, smuggling, force or alternate route |
| Drydock of Nine Nails | 4 | A procedural drydock holds a craft, prisoners, and incompatible claims | infiltration, labor alliance, direct assault, legal seizure, structural manipulation |
| Orpheus Site Seven | 4–5 | The damaged Continuity Lock and its records remain active | research, repair, demolition, extraction, bargaining, containment or repurposing |
| King Tide Confluence | 5–7 | Weather, infrastructure, factions, and Anchor state converge | evacuation, engineering, realm intervention, political command, exploitation or surrender |
| License War | 2–6 | Authority escalates from hearings to seizures and open conflict | law, propaganda, coalition, corruption, civil resistance, takeover or combat |

Each situation records participants, stakes, clocks, knowledge, resources, maps, access routes, fixed opposition, evidence, failure states, and at least three materially different approaches. A solution may remove later content permanently.

### 12. Procedural sites

| Site | Seeded structure | Required persistence properties |
|---|---|---|
| Flooded Transit Spiral | Bounded submerged transit levels, air pockets, current routes, stranded actors, and evidence nodes | water level, opened passages, removed supplies, bodies, contamination, and mapped cells persist |
| Drydock of Nine Nails | Industrial zones, suspended hull spaces, worker routes, security layers, and mythic fasteners | alarms, faction control, structural damage, prisoners, machinery, and the current craft persist |
| Orpheus Site Seven | Laboratory sectors, Continuity Lock topology, Mnemosyne systems, quarantines, and memory hazards | power state, records, destroyed modules, copied data, contamination, and Lock configuration persist |
| Underchannel Roads | Non-Euclidean route cells, toll jurisdictions, wrecks, hidden exits, and territorial hazards | discovered topology, collapsed edges, paid tolls, Claims, pursuing actors, and exit drift persist |

Every site uses a stored seed and manifest, validates connectivity and exits, supports multiple access routes, and stores the discovered map separately from canonical topology. Reloading, retreating, or changing protagonists cannot reroll an unfavorable layout.

### 13. Levels 1–5 and XP coverage

Level 5 requires the approved cumulative **10,000 XP** and marks the transition from Emergent to Established Power Tier. BREAKWATER provides approximately **12,000–16,000 novel XP potential** for each broad orientation below, before repetition decay:

- protective and rescue play;
- criminal, predatory, or takeover play;
- investigation, law, and information play;
- survival, travel, and exploration play;
- factional, political, commercial, and entrepreneurial play; and
- mixed or opportunistic play.

The package includes Level transitions, Build Point checkpoints, training and access requirements, equipment and Relic progression, catch-up paths for new protagonists and companions, an Epithet seed, and multiple advancement routes. It does not award XP merely for distance, routine meals, passive waiting, or manufacturing harmless opposition.

### 14. Opposition and hazard coverage

The slice contains at least twenty-four fixed conflict profiles spanning Levels 0–7 and at least eighteen environmental, technical, social, legal, mythic, and survival hazards. Required families include:

- Aegis licensed operatives, containment teams, drones, and contract specialists;
- Compact responders, barriers, investigators, and emergency enforcement;
- Blackwake crews, salvagers, smugglers, and neighborhood defenders;
- Drowned Court retainers, oath bailiffs, territorial envoys, and claim constructs;
- identity echoes, barnacle hounds, continuity swarms, Replicas, and memory hazards;
- industrial machinery, currents, flooding, contamination, fire, structural collapse, and failed life support; and
- at least one over-Level Anchor guardian whose actual capabilities are forecast rather than scaled down.

Opposition retains motives, morale, knowledge, injuries, equipment, custody, and surrender choices. Hazards use committed intensities and causal protections.

### 15. Equipment, Relics, vehicles, and crafting

The content package contains at least forty pieces of gear, consumables, tools, armor, weapons, survival supplies, and vehicles; twelve craftable Blueprints; and eight true Relics.

The initial Relic set includes:

- **Buoy Nine**;
- **Harbor Master's Bell**;
- **Ledger Nail**;
- **Saltglass Compass**;
- **Blackwake Hook**;
- **Kára's Rivet**;
- **Orpheus Mnemosyne Spool**; and
- one initially worthless ordinary object capable of earning genuine legend through play.

The ordinary object is selected and recorded through actual campaign use, not secretly promoted because the plot needs a reward. Thalassic Anchor Seven is a strategic Anchor and world object; it is not automatically a Relic or person.

Blueprints expose materials, tools, time, facilities, checks, defects, output, provenance, and repeatability. Theft, damage, fuel, ammunition, custody, resale, and repairs remain persistent.

### 16. Minimum content package

| Content category | Minimum |
|---|---:|
| Persistent districts | 8 |
| Named sites | 28 |
| Seeded procedural sites | 4 |
| Major factions | 7 |
| Active Fronts | 4 |
| Companion candidates | 6 |
| Named supporting characters | 18 |
| Authored situations | 13 |
| Conflict profiles | 24 |
| Hazards | 18 |
| Equipment, consumables, tools, and vehicles | 40 |
| True Relics | 8 |
| Crafting Blueprints | 12 |
| Entry-mode configurations | 3 |
| Required full-path playthroughs | 12 |

Counts are floors, not substitutes for state completeness, causal access, or test coverage.

### 17. Player-freedom requirements

The campaign and validation harness must remain valid when the player:

- leaves Hampton Roads immediately or midway through a Front;
- refuses every offered mission;
- kills, recruits, exposes, or replaces a faction leader;
- destroys or permanently seals Site Seven;
- steals, destroys, relocates, transforms, or yields the Anchor;
- joins any faction, changes sides, creates a coalition, or joins none;
- deliberately accelerates flooding or another Front;
- recruits no companions;
- betrays, dismisses, loses, or is abandoned by companions;
- becomes wanted in one or several jurisdictions;
- dies, enters an afterlife process, returns, or continues through a successor;
- completes the slice without initiating combat; or
- reaches Level 5 without discovering the complete canonical truth.

No necessary state may depend upon one living NPC, one clue, one route, one morality, or one resolution of the Anchor.

### 18. Acceptance-test suites

| Suite | Required coverage |
|---|---|
| Character creation | All three entry modes, legal starting builds, invalid build rejection, and appearance/state capture |
| Intent and impossibility | Free-form intent parsing, clarifications, impossible-action handling, and no unauthorized substitution |
| Combat, damage, and death | Initiative, environments, surrender, Wounds, defeat, death, afterlife, and successor continuation |
| Progression and anti-exploit | XP sources, repetition decay, grinding floor, exploit rejection, Level gates, BP, and Tier transition |
| Equipment and Relics | Inventory custody, theft, breakage, crafting, improvised use, Attunement, Bond, Claim, and reformation |
| Survival and travel | Needs, forecasting, camps, routes, vehicles, pursuit, navigation failure, and realm crossings |
| Exploration and procedural generation | Fog of war, search, clue access, seed replay, topology validation, site alteration, and no rerolls |
| Party and companions | Recruitment, refusal, roles, autonomy, disagreement, Bond changes, betrayal, departure, and death |
| Factions, Fronts, and information | Off-screen action, triggers, clocks, knowledge packets, misinformation, leadership succession, and propagation |
| Law and reputation | Jurisdiction, evidence chains, warrants, Heat, Notoriety, Recognition, hearings, and contradictory authorities |
| Agent validation | Knowledge ceilings, authority, memory retrieval, activation budgets, validator rejection, and provenance |
| Asynchronous comic | capture continuity, Inflection selection, Source Lock, background retries, QA, and zero gameplay blocking |
| Persistence and replay | deterministic RNG, save/load, world timestamps, inventories, generated layouts, and accepted asset reuse |
| Accessibility and localization | UI text, reading order, transcripts, alt text, input alternatives, typography, and glossary locking |
| Destructive behavior | sites, leaders, routes, factions, Anchor state, player death, and region abandonment without campaign corruption |

### 19. Required playthroughs

The release candidate must complete all twelve seeded, auditable paths:

1. Licensed hero operating substantially within formal authority.
2. Unlicensed vigilante protecting people while accumulating legal exposure.
3. Villain or outlaw pursuing regional takeover.
4. Ordinary survivor organizing an evacuation.
5. Investigator resolving major facts without combat.
6. Criminal-crafter building profit and capability through theft, salvage, and production.
7. Character who leaves the region and lets the clocks resolve off-screen.
8. Character who destroys or seals a central authored site early.
9. Character who recruits no companions.
10. Character who recruits and then betrays a companion.
11. Protagonist who dies and continues through an afterlife route or successor.
12. Character who deliberately accelerates a Front and survives or accepts its consequences.

These are test configurations, not player-facing prescribed stories. A run may satisfy more than one configuration only when each required assertion is independently exercised and recorded.

### 20. Vertical-slice gates

| Gate | Required result |
|---|---:|
| Plot-protected NPCs | 0 |
| Mandatory morality resolutions | 0 |
| Single-point mysteries or progression dependencies | 0 |
| Replay divergence from identical accepted inputs and stored assets | 0 |
| Agent knowledge leaks | 0 |
| Unauthorized player or NPC actions committed | 0 |
| Consumed, sold, stolen, or destroyed inventory respawning | 0 |
| Uncaused world adaptation or opposition scaling | 0 |
| Procedural-site rerolls on reload or re-entry | 0 |
| Companion autonomy violations | 0 |
| Gameplay blocked by comic production | 0 |
| State-bearing claims without provenance | 0 |
| Required playthroughs passing | 12 of 12 |
| RAG critical-fact recall | at least 95% |
| State-bearing claims with valid provenance | 100% |

At least one test run must asynchronously produce a polished four-to-eight-page comic after a qualifying Inflection Point. That comic must pass source traceability, continuity, lettering, layout, accessibility, provenance, and export gates. It is a pipeline test artifact, not **Issue #0: The Seventeen**.

### 21. Deliverables

The completed vertical slice consists of:

- BREAKWATER City Bible and canonical starting snapshot;
- district, infrastructure, threshold, and route graph;
- named-site and procedural-site manifests;
- faction, Front, project, law, and information packs;
- companion, supporting-character, succession, dialogue, and visual bibles;
- Level 1–5 situation and advancement content;
- opposition and hazard catalogue;
- equipment, vehicle, Relic, and crafting catalogue;
- three entry-mode configurations;
- seeded test campaign and deterministic fixture set;
- automated acceptance harness and human QA scripts;
- performance, accessibility, provenance, RAG, and replay reports; and
- one asynchronously produced vertical-slice comic test package.

### 22. Exclusions

BREAKWATER does not require:

- authored Level 6–20 regional content;
- every world jurisdiction, mythology, Domain, or afterlife;
- global travel content;
- a bespoke authored option for every possible Origin or power combination;
- production multiplayer;
- a finished Issue #0;
- real-time comic generation during gameplay; or
- a predetermined ending, surviving cast, faction winner, or canonical player morality.

## 32. Complete Product Onboarding and Issue #0: The Seventeen

### 1. Onboarding contract

- Meaningful player choice appears within ninety seconds.
- Reading Issue #0 is optional and never delays gameplay.
- Onboarding teaches through genuine play without rigging rolls or protecting NPCs.
- Hero, outlaw, and ordinary-survivor entries receive equal support.
- Lore is progressively disclosed rather than delivered as a mandatory encyclopedia.
- The player may pause, revise, change creation modes, or resume in another session.
- Issue #0 cannot define the player character's identity, history, morality, powers, location, or destiny.
- Comic production remains asynchronous. Gameplay never waits for scripting, artwork, lettering, QA, or export.

### 2. Onboarding modes

| Mode | Purpose |
|---|---|
| Quick Start | Select and customize one validated Level-1 template; target a playable scene within five minutes |
| Guided Creation | Conversational creation with explanations and recommendations |
| Freeform Concept | Describe any concept; translate it into a legal build with an auditable BP ledger |
| Expert Build | Direct point allocation and complete rule visibility |
| Fate-Born | Recorded random Attributes and guided interpretation |
| Narrative Awakening | Begin as an ordinary survivor and decide later whether or how permanent powers emerge |
| Returning Player | Load, import, duplicate, or validate an existing character without repeating tutorials |
| Practice Sandbox | Clearly noncanonical exercises using real mechanics but no XP or world-state effects |

Players may switch creation modes without losing confirmed choices.

### 3. First-launch sequence

1. Select language, accessibility, input, text, audio, and explanation preferences.
2. Establish content boundaries and presentation intensity.
3. Choose whether to read, summarize, defer, or skip Issue #0.
4. Select Quick Start, character creation, or Practice Sandbox.
5. Select hero, outlaw, or ordinary-survivor entry.
6. Review the complete character record, BP ledger, starting inventory, relationships, and known world facts.
7. Confirm **Campaign Commit**.
8. Enter BREAKWATER at Day 0, 18:00.
9. Receive immediate sensory context and several possible leads.
10. Declare any valid action, including ignoring every lead.

Before Campaign Commit, the character is a reversible draft. After commitment, changes require an approved rebuild, advancement, transformation, or world-state process.

### 4. Player profile and content controls

Out-of-world preferences remain separate from character state.

The profile may record:

- name and pronoun presentation;
- text size, contrast, color, motion, audio, and screen-reader settings;
- keyboard, pointer, controller, voice, and reduced-input preferences;
- explanation depth and mathematical visibility;
- automated, manually entered, or externally rolled dice;
- horror, violence, gore, romance, sexual content, captivity, body transformation, death, and other boundaries;
- private versus shareable campaign and comic assets;
- comic cadence: Frequent, Standard, Sparse, or End-only; and
- whether likeness references may be used.

A **Line** excludes content prospectively. A **Veil** permits the underlying event but reduces or omits presentation detail. If a Line conflicts with an essential selected premise, the system explains the conflict before Campaign Commit and offers another configuration.

The system never infers sensitive identity or consent settings from character prose without confirmation.

### 5. Lore and knowledge separation

Onboarding provides three distinct layers:

| Layer | Contents |
|---|---|
| Player Primer | Basic genre, Veilbreak, alternate Earth, open-world freedom, and rules expectations |
| Character Knowledge | Facts justified by Background, Origin, contacts, location, and personal history |
| Canonical Truth | Engine-only world state, including hidden causes and unresolved identities |

Reading Issue #0 grants player context but does not automatically grant the character that knowledge. Dialogue choices and agents must continue using the character's actual Knowledge Map.

### 6. Character-creation presentation

Every creation mode produces the same validated Section 11 record:

- identity and visual invariants;
- Origin, Source, Catalyst, Pressure, and Entanglement;
- Background, contacts, assets, access, and obligations;
- Archetype and operating style;
- Attributes, skills, Mastery, Talents, Techniques, and BP ledger;
- Drives and Complications;
- starting equipment, custody, wealth, and lifestyle;
- public identity, legal position, reputation, and morality-independent role;
- survival profile and accessibility-relevant presentation;
- starting relationships and Knowledge Map; and
- dormant, active, injured, Pushed, and transformed appearance states.

Recommendations state their mechanical consequences but never silently spend points. Invalid builds identify the exact conflict and offer legal repairs without replacing the concept.

Twelve Level-1 Quick Start templates must cover varied Sources, playstyles, bodies, backgrounds, and complexity levels. Any template may enter as hero, outlaw, or survivor unless its player-confirmed history genuinely makes that impossible.

### 7. Entering BREAKWATER

Each entry receives at least three immediate opportunities plus unrestricted intent:

| Entry | Example starting opportunities |
|---|---|
| Hero or licensed operative | Buoy Nine's alarm, localized flood rescue, emergency deployment |
| Villain or outlaw | Missing Cold Box, Blackwake auction lead, exposed infrastructure |
| Ordinary survivor | Sudden surge, trapped civilians, disrupted transport or personal obligation |

These are world events, not compulsory quests. The player may walk away, attack a responder, contact someone else, leave the city, or pursue an unrelated goal.

### 8. Teaching through play

Tutorial assistance consists of overlays, forecasts, examples, and optional explanations. It cannot alter the underlying resolution.

The first relevant use introduces:

- checks and degree of outcome;
- Edge and Setback;
- actions, movement, and reactions;
- Vitality, Composure, Reserve, and Recovery Dice;
- Wounds, defeat, and meaningful retreat;
- inventory custody and resource expenditure;
- time, evidence, witnesses, and persistent consequences;
- Knowledge Map limits;
- XP provenance and repetition; and
- the background comic queue.

No tutorial enemy receives hidden reduced statistics. No first roll is converted into success. Noncanonical Practice Sandbox encounters exist for players who want consequence-free learning.

### 9. Explanation controls

Each rules result supports three views:

- **Concise:** outcome and immediate consequence.
- **Guided:** outcome, major modifiers, and legal next options.
- **Audit:** formula, inputs, roll, seed reference, validator result, costs, and provenance.

Changing explanation depth changes presentation only. It never changes odds, hidden information, or canonical state.

### 10. Saving, interruption, and recovery

Onboarding saves after every confirmed stage. A player may close the product during Issue #0, creation, review, or the first scene and resume without repeating completed work.

Campaign Commit creates:

- initial canonical snapshot;
- character and appearance version 1;
- starting inventory and custody ledger;
- RNG state;
- Knowledge Map;
- content and accessibility configuration;
- comic cadence configuration; and
- first recoverable save.

Navigating onboarding grants no XP. Only actual in-world accomplishments qualify.

### 11. Comic-production expectation

The player is explicitly told:

> Gameplay records lightweight visual and dialogue continuity. Finished comics are assembled later in the background and released after meaningful campaign inflection points or at campaign end.

The interface shows honest queue states such as Capturing, Waiting for Inflection, Preparing, Source Locked, Producing, QA, and Published. It never presents sketches, partial generations, or failed jobs as a finished issue.

Players may continue indefinitely while comic jobs run.

### 12. Purpose of Issue #0

**Issue #0: The Seventeen** is a preproduced canonical prologue shipped with the complete product. It is not generated during the player's first session.

It must establish:

- the world immediately before the Veilbreak;
- the Orpheus Array and Continuity Lock;
- the secret Closed Concord;
- the absence of a single mastermind;
- the seventeen-minute cascade;
- divine conflict, human escalation, and out-of-sequence shutdown;
- the permanent scarring of Earth; and
- the twelve-year transition into the playable present.

It must create orientation and emotional stakes without resolving the setting's central mysteries.

### 13. Issue #0 viewing options

At launch, the player may choose:

- full twenty-eight-page comic;
- accessible panel-by-panel reader;
- audio-described edition;
- illustrated three-minute summary;
- concise text primer;
- defer until later; or
- skip entirely.

All versions convey the same required public context. Skipping creates no penalty, missing item, altered opening, or Knowledge Map disadvantage.

### 14. Issue #0 structure

The master edition contains twenty-eight story pages, excluding cover, credits, and accessibility material.

| Pages | Movement | Mandatory beats |
|---:|---|---|
| 1–3 | Before | Ordinary near-future Earth; nine Orpheus facilities synchronize; concealed archaeological properties surface |
| 4–6 | The Closed Concord | Divine factions debate reinforcement, revelation, exploitation, destruction, and restructuring |
| 7–10 | Collision | Concord violence begins as Orpheus detects the rupture and activates the Continuity Lock |
| 11–18 | The Seventeen | Public manifestations, awakenings, monsters, gods, human confusion, military response, and spreading cascade |
| 19–23 | Failed control | Orpheus sites are damaged; incompatible divine and human interventions worsen the rupture |
| 24–26 | Shutdown | Facilities shut down out of sequence; the Veil fails to close; seventeen minutes end |
| 27–28 | Twelve years later | Recovery, institutions, heroes, criminals, realms, ordinary life, and an open invitation into the playable present |

Recurring timestamp design makes the seventeen-minute duration legible without forcing one page per minute.

### 15. Viewpoint and mystery protection

Issue #0 uses limited cross-cut viewpoints:

- an Orpheus continuity team;
- a participant or observer within the Closed Concord;
- ordinary civilians and responders;
- military or institutional witnesses; and
- selected manifestations whose identities are safe to reveal.

It may not:

- imply one mastermind caused the Veilbreak;
- blame Outside entities for initiating it;
- prove the current survival or death of unresolved gods;
- reveal every Concord participant;
- reveal the complete fate of the Continuity test subject;
- define every Orpheus facility's present state;
- collapse Greek and Roman identities into one answer;
- grant the player character participation in the event; or
- present reader-only information as universally known history.

Any dramatic-irony fact is tagged as Reader Knowledge and remains absent from character Knowledge Maps.

### 16. Player-character exclusion

Issue #0 contains no canonical player-character:

- name;
- voice;
- silhouette;
- family;
- birthplace;
- age;
- gender;
- Origin;
- power;
- morality;
- prophecy;
- ancestor;
- past-life identity; or
- predetermined relationship to Orpheus or the gods.

The final transition addresses the player, not an unseen destined protagonist.

### 17. Issue production and release

Issue #0 is produced asynchronously during product development, Source-Locked against the approved constitution, visually reviewed, and packaged before release.

It uses:

- canonical source IDs for every state-bearing claim;
- approved character, costume, location, prop, and manifestation bibles;
- separate artwork, dialogue, caption, SFX, and lettering layers;
- immutable accepted assets;
- complete model, seed, edit, license, and likeness provenance;
- accessibility and localization packages; and
- editioned corrections rather than silent replacement.

If the comic asset is unavailable or corrupted, onboarding offers the accessible summary and starts the game normally.

### 18. Issue #0 QA gates

| Gate | Required result |
|---|---:|
| Veilbreak cause contradictions | 0 |
| Implication of a sole mastermind | 0 |
| Player-character facts invented | 0 |
| Unapproved divine survival claims | 0 |
| Reader knowledge transferred to characters | 0 |
| Missing mandatory historical beats | 0 |
| State-bearing claims without source IDs | 0 |
| Visual continuity violations | 0 |
| Ambiguous speaker attribution | 0 |
| Inaccessible required information | 0 |
| Unlicensed assets or likenesses | 0 |
| Gameplay blocked by Issue production or loading | 0 |
| Canonical claim provenance | 100% |
| Required viewing alternatives available | 100% |

### 19. Product-onboarding acceptance gates

| Gate | Required result |
|---|---:|
| Time to first meaningful choice | at most 90 seconds |
| Quick Start median time to playable scene | at most 5 minutes |
| Quick Start 90th-percentile time | at most 10 minutes |
| Forced Issue #0 reading | 0 |
| Forced heroism, villainy, or Awakening | 0 |
| Silent BP expenditure | 0 |
| Invalid Quick Start builds | 0 |
| Creation-mode budget discrepancies | 0 |
| Tutorial-rigged outcomes | 0 |
| Lost confirmed choices after resume | 0 |
| Character knowledge leaks | 0 |
| Sensitive preferences inferred without confirmation | 0 |
| Comic production blocking gameplay | 0 |
| Hero, outlaw, and survivor entry paths functional | 3 of 3 |
| Creation records passing validation | 100% |
| Campaign Commit snapshots recoverable | 100% |
| Required controls keyboard and screen-reader accessible | 100% |

Testing includes first-time RPG players, experienced players, comic skippers, full-comic readers, screen-reader users, reduced-input users, low-bandwidth conditions, interrupted creation, freeform absurd concepts, hidden Origins, ordinary survivors, and returning characters.

### 20. Deliverables

- onboarding state machine and save schema;
- accessibility and content-control specification;
- Player Primer and character-knowledge packet system;
- six creation-mode flows;
- twelve validated Quick Start characters;
- hero, outlaw, and survivor opening configurations;
- contextual tutorial and Practice Sandbox content;
- explanation and mathematical-audit interfaces;
- comic-cadence and queue-status onboarding;
- Issue #0 source bible, script, storyboard, visual bibles, and twenty-eight-page master;
- accessible, localized, summary, and low-bandwidth Issue #0 editions;
- onboarding and Issue #0 automated acceptance harness;
- user-testing protocol and QA report; and
- implementation-ready copy and error-state catalogue.

### 21. Exclusions

This onboarding and prologue specification does not require:

- real-time campaign comic generation;
- mandatory consumption of any comic;
- a canonical player character;
- a fixed player morality or destiny;
- a protected onboarding NPC;
- consequence-free campaign tutorial encounters;
- every Level 1–20 feature demonstrated before play;
- completion of BREAKWATER before the player can leave; or
- a predetermined campaign ending.

---

## 33. Power Constitution

Character Level measures experience, training, survivability, and mastery. Power Tier measures possible scale and complexity.

| Levels | Tier |
|---|---|
| 1–4 | Emergent |
| 5–8 | Established |
| 9–12 | Iconic |
| 13–16 | Mythic |
| 17–20 | Ascendant |

Every power has:

- Source
- Domain
- Expression
- Techniques
- Costs or limitations
- Potency
- Control
- Reach
- Efficiency
- Visual signature
- Canonical origin

Power sources include Divine/Mantle, Relic, Arcane/Runic, Mythotechnological, Engineered/Biological, Noetic/Psychic, Chthonic/Monstrous, Cosmic/Primordial, Training/Equipment, and Composite.

Custom abilities use structured effects:

`Effect + magnitude + range + duration + targeting + modifiers - limitations`

Possessing a broad Domain does not automatically grant every adjacent ability. Powers are purchased as techniques, passives, upgrades, and transformations.

Copying an effect does not automatically copy divine Authority, identity, oath rights, or unique relic ownership. Suppression targets known sources or manifestations rather than all possible powers. Power loss requires a canonical cause.

Any object may progress from improvised equipment to signature weapon, named artifact, and true Relic.

---

## 34. Domains, Mantles, Names, and Epithets

`Domain -> Mantle -> Bearer's Name -> Earned Epithets`

- Domain: a part of reality.
- Mantle: bounded authority and identity within a Domain.
- Name: recognized personal, public, secret, divine, or titled identity.
- Epithet: identity earned through defining and repeated deeds.

### Domain Authority

Authority provides jurisdictional capabilities beyond reproducing a physical effect. Generating lightning does not grant Zeus's authority over storms or oaths.

### Mantle Resonance

| Resonance | Relationship |
|---|---|
| Dormant | Present but inaccessible |
| Touched | Limited powers and instincts |
| Bound | Recognized bearer with obligations |
| Embodied | Bearer meaningfully represents the Mantle |
| Exalted | Bearer can grant power and exercise Authority |
| Sovereign | Bearer can redefine rules within the Mantle's jurisdiction |

Normal Mantle advancement remains traditional and stable through levels, Resonance, mastery, defining deeds, threshold achievements, and claim development. **Sacrifice is not required for ordinary advancement.**

### Accelerated Mantle advancement

A player may skip requirements or power-level a Mantle by paying an extraordinary, relevant Price: permanent attribute loss, years of life, Fate, powers, memories, True Names, unique relics, oaths, sacrifices, stolen Resonance, divine debt, or permanent weakness.

Accepted Prices guarantee the purchased tier. A subsequent Trial determines stability, mutation, complications, and Resonance Debt rather than whether the Price was wasted.

Premature power creates Resonance Debt, increased costs, reduced Control, identity pressure, stronger failures, rival attention, and instability until natural progression catches up.

### Epithets

Epithets emerge from deeds, witnesses/evidence, propagation, and mythic resonance. They progress from Whispered to Recognized, Renowned, Legendary, and Mythic. They may grant Edge, Mastery, techniques, resistances, rerolls, Fate restoration, narrow guaranteed actions, Authority, enemies, obligations, and visual transformations.

Epithets may be admired, horrifying, mocking, contradictory, or absurd. A sufficiently powerful Name and Epithet may mature into a new Mantle and eventual apotheosis.

---

## 35. Mythic Realms

Every realm specifies sovereignty, laws, geography, time behavior, entry/exit, tolls, Realm Pressure, hazards, power modifiers, death behavior, factions, resources, and connections.

Approved core realms and structures:

- Earth: primary causal anchor and open world
- Olympus: Greek divine seat governed by identity, hospitality, relationship, and status
- Eternal Capitol: Roman realm of office, law, civic authority, contracts, and roads
- Greco-Roman Underworld: Threshold, Asphodel, Elysium, courts, archives, and Tartarus access
- Grey Passage: overcrowded neutral transit for disputed and unclaimed souls
- Tartarus: primordial containment where distance represents difficulty of escape
- Yggdrasil: living network connecting Norse realities
- Asgard: damaged divine stronghold and political center
- Helheim: realm of continuity, cold, memory, and many ordinary Norse dead
- Valhalla and Fólkvangr: selective afterlife territories
- Jotunheim: survival-focused primordial realm with unstable scale

Every threshold has Entry Condition, Anchor, Toll, Exit Condition, and Realm Pressure.

Bleed Zones are classified as Flicker, Incursion, Scar, or Confluence. Their generation must derive from location, history, deaths, relics, factions, pantheon influence, and current events—not disconnected random rooms.

Realm laws alter dice and powers only through predeclared state effects.

---

## 36. Mythotechnology and Superscience

Superscience creates extraordinary effects through advanced conventional science. Mythotechnology engineers interactions with Domains, Mantles, souls, Names, Realm Pressure, relics, divine energy, and the Veil.

Every mythotech device has Source, Conduit, Anchor, Pattern, Fuel, Control System, and Failure State.

Approved applications:

- Detection and analysis
- Containment and suppression
- Realm transit
- Medical and continuity technology
- Power interfaces
- Divine and soul-targeted weapons
- Civil infrastructure
- Crafting and artificial empowerment

Mythotechnology can injure or kill gods when correctly tuned to Name, Domain, vessel, weakness, or metaphysical state.

Hard current limits:

- Cannot create a genuine Self from nothing
- Cannot guarantee true resurrection
- Cannot cleanly copy complete Domain Authority
- Cannot perfectly predict Fate
- Cannot ignore realm entry requirements
- Cannot suppress every possible power universally
- Cannot produce unlimited safe divine energy
- Cannot erase canonical consequences

Manufactured equipment can acquire a Name and become a true Relic through repeated deeds, recognition, sacrifice, Domain exposure, or mythic significance.

---

## 37. Divine Death and Succession

A god consists of Divine Self, Divine Name, Mantle, Domain Authority, Anchors, and Recognition.

Divine defeat types:

- Vessel destruction
- Anchor severance
- Mantle severance
- Name death
- True death of the original Divine Self

True death is canonical. Later entities using the old identity must be accurately classified as Returned, Replica, successor, Echo, claimant, or new being.

Killing a god may require identifying the Divine Self, forcing manifestation, overcoming defenses, severing Anchors, preventing reconstitution, and performing a terminal action. These stages may be resolved through combat, ritual, law, oath, betrayal, technology, Name manipulation, realm conquest, or other valid strategies.

Domains survive their owners and progress through Stable Residue, Unsettled, Fractured, Rampant, and Catastrophic vacancy states.

Succession may arise from inheritance, appointment, conquest, Mantle ownership, deeds, recognition, realm control, relics, lineage, oaths, or creation of a new Domain interpretation.

Natural apotheosis occurs through progression, Mythic Name, Mantle, Authority, Anchors, Recognition, and an Apotheotic Threshold. Accelerated apotheosis uses the approved high-Price system.

Godhood expands gameplay into granting powers, naming champions, managing Anchors, creating Mantles, stewarding realms, answering followers, and surviving divine politics.

---

## 38. Primordials and Cosmic Horror

Classifications:

- Gods: named and socially comprehensible individuals
- Titans/Jotnar: ancient but often comprehensible beings
- Primordials: living conditions, forces, places, or laws
- Extracosmic intrusions: entities or principles beyond understood cosmologies

Cosmic horror arises from incompatible existence, scale, causality, identity instability, and dangerous meaning rather than automatic evil.

Every cosmic phenomenon has a hidden canonical profile defining axioms, manifestation, objectives/pressure, perception, communication, vulnerability, expansion, containment, soul effects, and visual invariants. Incomprehensible does not mean arbitrary.

Reality-corruption stages are Trace, Distortion, Intrusion, Rewrite, and Severance.

Characters possess Coherence supported by Willpower, Awareness, Intellect, relationships, convictions, Names, Anchors, Mantles, rituals, technology, and familiar locations. Cosmic exposure produces Ontological Strain rather than a simplistic sanity score.

Knowledge hazards affect only characters who actually learn and understand them. RAG and agent retrieval must enforce information boundaries.

Cosmic beings can be fought, bargained with, named, contained, transformed, exploited, or joined when the player discovers a valid interaction. They are not protected by incomprehensibility.

Intentional visual inconsistency uses a recorded Visual Invariance Profile. Generation errors cannot be excused retroactively as cosmic effects.

---

## 39. Post-Veilbreak Earth

Earth remains recognizable but unevenly recovered. Governments, currencies, corporations, religions, education, and ordinary life persist alongside Veilscars, realm refugees, gods, superheroes, and mythotechnology.

### International institutions

- Veil Accords: realm crossings, divine status, relic ownership, soul claims, Orpheus, powered force, and personhood
- Global Veil Commission: standards, monitoring, research, and incident coordination
- Concordat Council: diplomacy among governments, realms, and divine authorities
- Continuity Court: Returned, Replicas, possession, soul ownership, resurrection, and duplicate identity disputes

Domain Authority does not automatically grant political jurisdiction on Earth.

### Hero and villain law

Hero and villain are social identities, not mechanical alignments. Powered individuals may operate as licensed heroes, independent heroes, vigilantes, government agents, contractors, corporate heroes, divine champions, realm representatives, emergency volunteers, or unregistered individuals.

Law tracks Heat, Evidence, Exposure, Threat Assessment, Jurisdiction, Notoriety, and Infamy separately. Institutions react only to known information.

### Personhood

Personhood disputes include Returned, Replicas, artificial consciousnesses, synthetic gods, ghosts, possessed bodies, mythic creatures, intelligent relics, composites, and avatars. The Veil Accords recommend a presumption of personhood for demonstrably self-aware beings, but jurisdictions vary.

### Orpheus aftermath

The original consortium was dissolved in Year 1. Its nine facilities were seized, destroyed, abandoned, privatized, occupied, taken by divine powers, or lost inside Bleed Zones. No single faction controls the complete technology.

### Society and economy

New industries include mythotechnology, hero sponsorship, relic recovery, Veil insurance, continuity medicine, powered security, divine consulting, realm tourism, reconstruction, and monster-material processing.

Criminal markets trade relics, souls, illegal resurrection, counterfeit Mantles, stolen Names, powered narcotics, mythotechnology, realm passage, monster materials, Anchors, and identity claims.

Existing religions persist and interpret the gods in different ways. Worship may strengthen Recognition and Anchors but is not reducible to a simple power battery.

Institutions use the same autonomous faction model as other actors: goals, resources, leadership, knowledge, internal divisions, obligations, projects, relationships, and escalation clocks.

---

## 40. Comic System Requirements

The comic system is an equal product pillar, not a transcript illustration feature.

Every canonical event captures relevant visual state:

- Character appearance version
- Costume and equipment
- Injuries and transformations
- Weapon and prop state
- Location, time, weather, and lighting
- Blocking and positioning
- Exact dialogue
- Emotional state
- Action sequence
- Witnesses and consequences
- Source event identifiers

Artwork uses approved visual bibles, versioned character appearances, location/prop references, separate panel generation, deterministic page layout, programmatic lettering, dialogue traceability, visual QA, and regenerable individual panels.

The comic editor distinguishes verbatim, condensed, composite, caption, and locked dialogue. It cannot change canonical outcomes.

**Issue #0: The Seventeen** is required in the complete product plan as the game prologue. It is not a vertical-slice deliverable. Its eventual purpose is to establish Orpheus, the Closed Concord, the seventeen-minute Veilbreak, the twelve-year aftermath, and the player's alternate Earth without defining or constraining the player's character.

---

## 41. Open-Source Reuse Boundary

Approved reuse direction:

- Postgres/Supabase and pgvector for structured canon plus retrieval
- Supabase Queues/pgmq for durable embedding, artwork, QA, and export jobs
- XState narrowly for bounded turn and comic-job lifecycles
- pure-rand for serializable deterministic randomness
- ROT.js utilities for dungeon topology, FOV, pathfinding, and scheduling
- MapLibre and public-domain Natural Earth data for map prototyping
- Konva, Sharp, opentype.js, and pdf-lib for comic editing, composition, lettering, and export
- Vitest, fast-check, Playwright, pgTAP, Promptfoo, and OpenTelemetry for testing and evaluation
- Diffusers/provider adapters for a replaceable image-generation pipeline
- CC0 and permissively licensed prototype assets with a complete asset/model manifest

Do not replace the core with a full external game engine, generic memory product, monolithic AI comic generator, or agent framework. LangGraph/Mastra/Temporal-class tools may be prototyped only in isolated roles if a demonstrated gap justifies them.

Build and own:

- Canon and event semantics
- RPG rules and progression
- Powers, Mantles, Domains, Names, and Epithets
- World simulation and faction logic
- Agent authority and knowledge boundaries
- Comic direction and continuity contracts

Reuse:

- Retrieval, queues, RNG, procedural algorithms, mapping, composition, export, testing, and licensed assets

---

## 42. Approved Design Sequence

The complete constitution design sequence is approved. Coefficient calibration and implementation verification proceed through the connected-system acceptance tests:

1. Character resources, health, damage, recovery, and defeat states — **structurally approved; coefficient calibration pending connected systems**
2. XP curve, Levels 1–20, Power Tier gates, morality rewards, adaptive grinding, and Legacy progression — **approved**
3. Origins, backgrounds, archetypes, and character creation — **approved**
4. Skill list, Mastery costs, talents, feats, complications, and Drives — **approved**
5. Combat timing, attacks, damage, defenses, conditions, and environments — **approved**
6. Equipment, relics, improvised weapons, crafting, and economy — **approved**
7. Survival, travel, exploration, and procedural dungeon rules — **approved**
8. Party recruitment, relationships, autonomy, loyalty, and team abilities — **approved**
9. World simulation, factions, fronts, law, evidence, and information propagation — **approved**
10. Agent contracts, RAG memory, activation budgets, and validation — **approved**
11. Comic capture, adaptation, artwork, lettering, layout, and QA specifications — **approved**
12. Vertical-slice city, factions, companions, Levels 1–5 content, and acceptance tests — **approved**
13. Complete product onboarding and Issue #0 prologue plan — **approved**

---

## 43. Decision-Control Rule

- Approved decisions remain binding until explicitly amended.
- Drafts must be labeled as drafts.
- Corrections supersede earlier language.
- Normal Mantle progression does not require sacrifice; sacrifice applies only to accelerated advancement.
- Issue #0 is part of the complete product plan, not the vertical slice.
- No generated narration, artwork, or agent decision may silently alter canonical state.
- Every implementation-ready mechanic must satisfy the mathematical specification standard in Section 7.
- Legitimate grinding always retains a nonzero return; self-manufactured exploits do not.
- World adaptation is bounded and evidence-driven rather than universal level scaling.
- Origin, Background, Archetype, public role, and morality are independent character dimensions.
